# M10 — Hardening

**Date:** 2026-08-13
**Status:** Ready to dispatch. Four gated phases.
**Branch:** `milestone/m10-hardening`, cut from `main` @ `f2cf359` (the PR #24
merge, *the blend fingerprint covers the embedding model*), in the worktree
`~/code/usher-m10`. **The worktree is deliberate** — `~/code/usher` is shared
with concurrent sessions, and `main` moved once during this plan's own drafting
(`26a7df4` → `f2cf359`, two follow-up PRs, fast-forward).
**Subject document:**
[`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md`](../specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md),
approved 2026-08-13. Where it and the PRD disagree, the PRD wins.

Predecessor: `docs/plans/2026-08-10-m9-api-surface.md`. Read its boundary calls
and `progress.md`'s M9 entry before this one.

Dates in this plan are **UTC**.

---

## Scope

M10 is the last milestone of v1. [PRD 09](../prd/09-roadmap.md) gives it one
line — *"Observability, failure modes, backup/restore, docs, public release"* —
and `progress.md:25` has said `not planned` beside it since the roadmap was
written.

The success condition is two sentences, and they are different claims:

1. **You depend on it.** When it breaks you can see why, and when it dies you
   can restore it.
2. **A stranger can install it.** They clone the public repository, follow the
   README, and reach a working catalog *without hammering a server they do not
   own*.

M10 owes almost no instrumentation.
[PRD 10](../prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md)'s metric catalogue is complete
and every row shipped with the milestone that owned it, exactly as
`docs/specs/2026-07-28-usher-v1-design.md:165` predicted: *"M10 assembles the
dashboards over data that is already flowing, rather than retrofitting
instrumentation at the end."* What M10 owes is **everything that consumes that
data, and everything an operator needs when it goes wrong.**

## The two findings that set the order

**1. The observability stack does not exist.**
`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:904` places it externally at
`~/code/observability/`, shared across the host and deliberately not a service
in Usher's `compose.yml`. That directory is absent, no
Grafana/Prometheus/Loki/Tempo container runs on this host, and
`.env.example:290` ships `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` **empty** while
`OTEL_SERVICE_NAME=usher` is set beside it at `:291` — which is the asymmetry
that made nine milestones of no-op export easy to miss. **Every metric PRD 10
specifies is emitted and none has ever been scraped.**

**2. The repository is already public.** Created public on 2026-08-02, MIT, and
`git tag -l` is empty. The safety cluster is therefore not release-blocking work
ahead of a future publication — it is **live risk today**.

## The four phases

| Phase | Group(s) | Contents | Droppable |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0 — Foundation** | O | The stack; Usher exporting to it for real; semconv names pinned | no |
| **1 — Safety** | S | #19, #9, #13, #20 — being polite to a server you do not own — **and the leaked `EmbyAdapter` (S10)**, which holds a session against that same server for the process lifetime | no |
| **2 — Operability** | J, K, D, F | The `m10a` migration, the scheduler, backup/restore, rotation, dashboards, alerts, the remaining failure-mode gaps, operator bugs, #8's diagnosis, runbooks | no |
| **3 — Release** | R | Version wiring, changelog, first tag, image publish, community files, README truth-telling, quickstart | **yes** |

Phase 3 is droppable without invalidating 0–2. Phases 0 and 1 are not droppable
by anything.

**Phase 0 precedes Phase 1 because of #13.** Two `KIND_CONCURRENCY` entries are
bounds presented as measurements. This project's named recurring failure mode is
a bounded measurement restated as an absolute one hop up the document chain — it
has cost a task and a milestone before. Closing #13 without the instrument that
measures it would commit that error knowingly.

## Scope — out, and why

**Seven open issues stay out.** Including them would make M10 a feature
milestone wearing a hardening label.

| Issue | Why out |
|---|---|
| **#11**, **#12** | T5/T6, the IMDb credits parsers and writer, and the `(title_id, source)` index that rides on it. Real work, and the next substantive piece — but not hardening. |
| **#21** | RRF scores partial coverage as relevance. A genuine search-quality bug, and a *ranking* change. |
| **#15**, **#16** | Query expansion, measured on one model and one 150-document corpus. `09-roadmap.md:1112` scopes these post-v1 **unless `search_queries` supplies a real evaluation set**, and it has no rows from real use yet. |
| **#14** | The declined covering index for `GET /admin/unmatched`, measured at 16.4 ms. The decline still holds. |
| **#18** | API-key auth for `POST /admin/sources`. An enhancement. |

**Authentication stays out.** M9's boundary call 1, and *Post-v1 candidates*
(`09-roadmap.md:1335`). M10 **documents** the posture; it does not build auth.
Building it would also invalidate the reasoning behind `v0.1.0` — see R's
version tasks.

**Resource limits stay advisory.** `08-operations.md:643-674` carries a red
warning that its table is sizing estimates for an operator provisioning a disk,
that **nothing reads them, no host enforces them, and no policy derives from
them**. M9's Track 2 misused it as a budget and withdrew a design (ADR-0036).
M10 does not repeat that: no cgroup limits are added to `compose.yml` on the
strength of that table, and the *Disk projection* alert must be grounded in a
measured series instead.

## Migration ownership

**M10 gets one migration, `m10a`, and Group J owns it.** No other group writes a
migration; the two `llm_calls` indexes Group D's cost panel needs travel in
`m10a` and D depends on J for them. The rule is M9's and it held: one migration
per milestone means one downgrade path to reason about.

## ADR allocation

The scheduler is a new architectural component and this project ADRs those;
Group J allocates and writes it. Group F's image-proxy work is an **amendment**
to ADR-0030 rather than a new decision, because that ADR's problem-code
vocabulary is closed by design and adding a code is exactly the thing closure
was meant to make deliberate.

## Execution protocol

Unchanged in method from M8 and M9, because it is what has been catching things.

**Per task:** an implementer subagent, then a **spec-compliance** review, then a
**code-quality** review. Each is dispatched fresh with the **full task text**,
not a pointer to this plan.

**Reviewers are told not to trust the implementer's report and to re-measure.**
Across M8's ~14 review passes every one found something, and the later ones
stopped finding wrong *code* and started finding wrong *claims* — a fabricated
test citation that shipped into two files, three false statements written into
`.claude/rules/`, and a result claimed as "measured against real Postgres" that
was wrong at the exact priority the next task needed.

**Implementers are serialised.** A mutation sweep is invalidated by any
concurrent edit anywhere the suite imports, not only by an overlapping file.
**Reviewers may run concurrently** against a frozen `git archive` copy.

**Measurement tasks** write the bar to `/tmp/m10-gate/BAR.md` *before* running,
run live, report **guess-by-guess with refutations first**, and commit the
measurement rather than the data. Typo and dataset rows are third-party data and
are never committed — `tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` enforces it.

**Commits carry no trailers**, verified by
`git log -1 --pretty='%(trailers)'` returning empty.

## The gate

Every phase ends with the standard gate (`CLAUDE.md:219-223`):

```
uv run ruff check .
uv run ruff format --check .
uv run mypy src tests
uv run lint-imports              # 10 kept, 0 broken at f2cf359
uv run pytest                    # tests/integration/ needs Docker
```

⚠️ **`lint-imports` is 10 kept at this branch point, not 9.** M9's plan quotes 9
and M9's H6 added the tenth (`independence`). A task quoting the old number is
quoting a number that was true and is not.

Plus one thing only that phase can prove:

| Phase | Phase-specific gate |
|---|---|
| **0** | A real span, a real `trace_id`-carrying log line, and a real metric sample observed in Grafana, sourced from the live catalog. The semconv convention in force, recorded. |
| **1** | All four fixes verified against the **live shared Emby**, in bounded requests, with no walk — the discipline M9's H4 and H5 used. #13's number written with its denominator. |
| **2** | Every committed panel observed rendering real data. The restore drill succeeds into a scratch `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` container. |
| **3** | The quickstart followed start to finish on a clean checkout. |

A mutation sweep per phase, logged to `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`.

## Environment facts an implementer needs

- **The dev database is `usher-postgres-1` and it has no published port.** Reach
  it at the container IP:
  `docker inspect -f '{{range .NetworkSettings.Networks}}{{.IPAddress}}{{end}}' usher-postgres-1`,
  then `USHER_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://usher:usher@<ip>:5432/usher`.
  `.env` supplies `USHER_SECRET_KEY` but **no** `USHER_DATABASE_URL`.
- **It holds M9's enriched catalog** — 1,272,367 titles, 292,199 enriched,
  130,678 cached TMDb payloads, roughly two hours of live traffic. **Do not
  casually drop it.** Drills use a scratch container.
- **Title UUIDs are per-bootstrap**, so two catalogs built from the same dump
  share no ids. Anything referencing a title id cannot be moved between
  databases. This is a hard constraint on Group K's restore design.
- `uv` for everything; never pip, never conda, never a manually activated venv.
- The user's interactive shell is **fish**. A command written for *them* to paste
  is fish syntax (`set -x VAR value`); commands an implementer runs are zsh.
## The task index

**67 tasks in four phases.** The index is built from the written sections,
not the other way round. Each task's own `**Depends on:**` line is
authoritative where it and this table disagree.

### Phase 0 — Foundation — Group O, 4 tasks

- **O1** — the observability stack as its own repository, and the collision that is not on 9090
- **O2** — point Usher at the stack, and correct "no-op exporters" to the stronger thing that is actually true
- **O3** — pin the semantic-convention names, and correct the catalogue's size from 34 to 35
- **O4** — the Phase 0 gate, and the run that proves the convention is pinned rather than merely current

### Phase 1 — Safety — Group S, 11 tasks

- **S1** — what a request to somebody else's Emby actually costs, and the seven-milestone-old number 21 places call "measured"
- **S2** — the outbound limiter, and why a requests-per-second ceiling against a slow server is a limiter that limits nothing
- **S3** — the limiter reaches every adapter that dials out, and the enumeration finds that a per-pipeline factory is a per-request bucket
- **S4** — the 429 path has never met a 429, and Phase 1 can close that honestly only against a stub
- **S5** — the first gap-closing delta of a fresh deployment is a full walk wearing a delta's name, and `push_gap_min_interval_seconds` bounds nothing about it
- **S6** — a ceiling on a gap-closing delta, and the reason a bounded walk must not be allowed to record `COMPLETED`
- **S7** — the concurrency numbers, measured: four entries at 4, two justifications, and a test named for measurements that does not assert any of them
- **S8** — the retraction guard on a library nobody here owns, and a one-request probe that answers it without a walk
- **S9** — a view of somebody else's library is not a mirror of your own, and the retraction default has to say which one it assumes
- **S10** — the leaked adapter that keeps reporting, and the `supports_push` claim above it is a counterfactual read as a fact *(moved from Group F on 2026-08-14 — it leaks a session against a server you do not own)*
- **S11** — the Phase 1 gate: five fixes, one live server, bounded requests, no walk

### Phase 2 — Operability: the migration and the scheduler — Group J, 7 tasks

- **J1** — `m10a`: two columns, three indexes, and the one whose reader is in another group
- **J2** — the suggest writer, and the two things the suggest path deliberately does not have
- **J3** — ADR-0039, and the interesting claim is that the scheduler stores nothing
- **J4** — `ports/scheduler.py` and `services/scheduler.py`, shipped with an empty registry
- **J5** — registration 1: `search_queries` retention, priced against a table that holds nine rows
- **J6** — registration 2: the neighbour rebuild, a resume cursor computed once, and the guard that stops it rebuilding a correct table
- **J7** — the overnight full walk, run once, as its own task

### Phase 2 — Operability: backup, restore and rotation — Group K, 8 tasks

- **K1** — the rebuildable/precious split becomes a manifest, and PRD 08's table is corrected in the same commit because it has already drifted twice
- **K2** — a title id is minted per bootstrap, so restore resolves natural keys and never carries a UUID it did not first find in the target
- **K3** — `usher backup`, and the two stamps that let restore refuse instead of half-applying
- **K4** — `usher restore`, which refuses in one transaction rather than half-applying, and is not "load a whole database"
- **K5** — the restore drill, against a scratch container, with the dev database untouched
- **K6** — `docs/runbooks/` gets the two remaining documents, and the README's backup sentence is prepared but not landed
- **K7** — `usher rotate-secret`, holding two ciphers, over the one table that has ciphertext in it
- **K8** — the rotation drill, and PRD 08:232's sentence corrected from "re-encrypts on next write" to what the code does

### Phase 2 — Operability: telemetry gaps, dashboards and alerts — Group D, 15 tasks

- **D0** — audit D1 and D2's panels against the live catalogue, and the interesting half is that the two dashboards fail for different reasons
- **D1** — a metric series for the suggest path, and the number PRD 10 quotes for it belongs to a tier the route does not default to
- **D2** — the `propose` span, placed where the report already times it and inherited by two roots rather than one
- **D3** — `llm_calls` gains its read, and the guard that forbade one retires in the same commit
- **D4** — "cost per play attributed to an LLM row" is marked ⏳ M9 against a column M9 shipped, and the panel it blocks is one join wide
- **D5** — the ≤6-month TMDb cache ceiling, which is a live licensing obligation with no panel, and the table PRD 10 attributes it to does not exist
- **D6** — Dashboard 1 as provisioned JSON, and the invariant harness the other four inherit
- **D7** — Dashboard 2, five panels and three stated absences
- **D8** — Dashboard 3, and two of its panels read a series whose label is not the one the panel's name implies
- **D9** — Dashboard 4, whose every panel is now backed and two of which read differently after M9
- **D10** — Dashboard 5, and the one panel on it whose failure is a licence breach
- **D11** — the alert file, and the three alerts whose series already exist — one of which must fire on a zero and not on an absence
- **D12** — the two alerts whose PRD condition names a dimension the shipped series does not carry
- **D13** — Disk projection, the only alert with no series at all, and the two documents that describe its subject are both wrong
- **D14** — Cost anomaly, whose series is a ledger nothing could read until this milestone

### Phase 2 — Operability: failure modes, operator bugs and #8 — Group F, 9 tasks

- **F2** — orphaned claims reach `/health/ready`'s body, and the number has been computed and discarded at both call sites since W1
- **F3** — ADR-0030's image amendment is answered, and closure is what makes answering it the work rather than the edit
- **F4** — #5's "one-line change" is refused, and the taxonomy argument is why the fix belongs at the call site
- **F5** — `/openapi.json` names the problem media type, and the obvious spelling produces a dangling `$ref`
- **F6** — #7 is reproduced with the whole run's output kept, because the losing assertion is the deliverable and not the rate
- **F7** — claim 3's premise is `id(session)`, and `id()` is reused by the next object in the same slot
- **F8** — #10's scoped decision, and the five counts it rests on are an M8-head measurement M9 moved
- **F9** — the decision is implemented, and `Title.popularity` accepts infinity into a `double precision` column, not the `NUMERIC` the record names
- **F10** — #8's cause, and the reason no stack was recorded is Usher's own error boundary rather than a missing flag

### Phase 3 — Release — Group R, 13 tasks

- **R1** — `--version`, and the three sources that already agree
- **R2** — `/health` names the version, and the argument against publishing it is answered rather than skipped
- **R3** — the release is `v0.1.0`, and the reason is written where somebody choosing a version number will trip over it
- **R4** — `CHANGELOG.md`, and the first entry is ten milestones rather than a placeholder
- **R5** — `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and a pull-request template that renders the gate rather than restating it
- **R6** — `SECURITY.md`, and a supported-version table one person can actually keep
- **R7** — issue templates, and a chooser that sends the two kinds of report to different questions
- **R8** — a tag-triggered release job, and the licensing guarantee that has to extend to the artifact
- **R9** — the README says what is rebuildable and what is not, and it is the shorter of the two lists that matters
- **R10** — the unauthenticated-admin posture, stated as a posture and pinned as a fact
- **R11** — the two things a client developer has to know, and neither is on the page they will read
- **R12** — PRD 04 gains the git-history paragraph, and the counts in it are three files, not one
- **R13** — the quickstart, the first tag, and the clean-checkout run that is Phase 3's exit condition

## Corrections this plan carries

The parallel drafting pass verified the spec's claims against the code rather
than inheriting them. **Fifteen were wrong.** They are listed here rather than
only beside the tasks that fix them, because a plan that silently drafts around
its own spec's errors teaches the next milestone that the spec was right.

The pattern is worth naming: **nine of the fifteen are a bounded measurement
restated as an absolute, or a number that was true at one head and quoted at
another.** That is this project's named recurring failure mode, and it now has a
fifteen-case sample.

### Corrections to the spec

| # | The spec said | The code says | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The metric catalogue is **34 rows** | **35.** 34 is a real number for something else — an AST scan finds exactly 34 declared `Meter.create_*` instruments in `src/`, and that set is the catalogue *minus* `http.server.duration`, which `FastAPIInstrumentor` supplies. Both numbers are true about different things. | O3 |
| 2 | Telemetry "exported to **no-op exporters**" | **There are no exporters.** `telemetry.py:191-197`/`:222-232` construct no exporter object at all when the endpoint is blank; the providers are real, so spans have always had valid ids and every log line has always carried a `trace_id`. The true claim — *nothing has ever left the process* — is stronger. | O2 |
| 3 | The neighbour rebuild is **21.6 h** at `halfvec(1024)` | **3.33 h.** 594.7 ms/seed is `m09e`'s cost; **`m09f` repaired it** — the same commit the spec cites for the TOAST finding. The completed walk: 130,720 seeds, 11,981 s, **91.7 ms/seed**. `08-operations.md:666` still carries the stale figure. The overnight-task conclusion survives; the number does not. | J6 |
| 4 | Schema head is **`m09d`/`m09e`** | **`m09f`.** The chain is `m08b → m09a → m09c → m09d → m09e → m09f`; **there is no `m09b`** — pre-allocated, never minted. | J1 |
| 5 | #13 is **two** concurrency entries | **Four**: `MATCH`, `WATCH_HISTORY`, `WATCH_WRITEBACK`, `DERIVE`, all at 4, under two justification bullets, over nine kinds. `.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md`'s "two of the eight" is wrong twice. | S7 |
| 6 | #20's guard is at `ports/repository/media_item.py:136` | That is the port's **docstring** (`:135-165`). The guard that raises is `db/repositories/media_item.py:482-485`. | S8 |
| 7 | The source is slow at **~1–3 s/request** | **Not this repository's number.** Its own record says 1–5 s, and that entered in commit `0c823e0` on 2026-07-28 — the *first PRD commit*, two days before an Emby adapter existed. It is now cited 16× across 13 files in `src/` and 5× in the PRDs, and **11 of those 21 render it as "measured"** with no run, sample or date. The only live readings this repo holds are H4/H5's **0.141/0.142/0.143 s**. | S1 |
| 8 | `usher restore` loads into an **empty database** | **Unachievable.** `watch_states.title_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (ADR-0010), so into an empty database the insert fails for the one table PRD 08 calls load-bearing. The honest criterion is PRD 08:586's own words — *a short restore plus a background rebuild*. | K4 |
| 9 | The `llm_calls` indexes "ship with the read method" | **Unbuildable.** A reader task in another group authoring DDL is exactly the cross-group serial spine that one-migration-per-milestone exists to refuse. The indexes ship in `m10a`; the reader depends on it. | J1, D3 |
| 10 | "**Nothing** in `src/` schedules anything" | Too strong. Two periodic loops exist (`api/lanes.py:325-339`, `:595`). Neither has a job name, a declared period, or a resumption story. The precise claim is *nothing runs a named job on a period*. | J4 |
| 11 | The TMDb cache ceiling is tracked by **`provider_cache_meta`** | **That table does not exist.** [ADR-0016](../prd/decisions/0016-raw-payloads-cache-providers-not-sources.md)`:26` refused it by name. The compliance series is `raw_payloads.fetched_at` + `ix_raw_payloads_fetched_at` (`db/models/sync.py:104-115`). | D5 |
| 12 | `pyproject.toml`'s version is read by **nothing** | **`__version__` exists and has a live consumer.** `src/usher/__init__.py:6` is `__version__ = version("usher")`, and `adapters/emby/session.py:166` uses it as `app_version`'s default — **it reaches Emby in the `MediaBrowser … Version=` header on every request.** Only `--version` and the API surface are genuinely missing. | R1 |
| 13 | The history finding is **two rows in one file**, fixed by `1196a9d` | **Three files, four rows, 113 commits, two fix commits.** `title.basics.slice.tsv` holds four real tconsts (`tt0000001`, `tt0073045`, `tt0111161`, `tt0944947`); `docs/plans/2026-07-30-m2-bootstrap.md` carries the same rows verbatim; the blobs are reachable across **113** of `origin/main`'s 678 commits; and `969dcbf` removed them from the plan 18 minutes after `1196a9d` fixed the fixtures. **The decision is unchanged** — four rows across 113 commits is still de minimis against 678 commits of cross-referenced record — but it is now argued with the real figure. | R12 |
| 14 | The `supports_push = false` degradation **never fires** | **Refuted.** `08-operations.md:298`'s sentence is a *counterfactual about the design not taken*, and the spec read the premise as the conclusion. The code resets on delivery (`services/push.py:394-403`), the ceiling is reached (`:381`, `:458`), it is covered end to end (`test_services_push.py:832-846`), and M5's sweep killed the inverse mutation in four cases. **The real residual is one row down:** `LaneSupervisor.refresh` never pops a finished lane, so its `EmbyAdapter` leaks for the process lifetime and keeps feeding `register_push_gauges` — the series PRD 10's *Push down* alert reads — while `refresh`'s docstring claims it drops crashed lanes and `crashed_sources()` has no caller in `src/`. | **S10** (moved into Phase 1) |
| 15 | Suggest measured **p50 33.6 / p95 211 / max 730 ms** | Those are [ADR-0031](../prd/decisions/0031-the-two-tier-suggest.md)`:304`'s **tier-2 whole-name** figures. The route defaults to **tier 1**, whose p95 is **2,707 ms at one character** and 112 ms at the shipped four-character minimum (`:192-193`). This is why D1's series carries a `tier` label rather than being one histogram. | D1 |

**Two claims the drafting pass re-measured and found correct**, recorded because
a correction list nobody can trust in the negative direction is half a list:
`.env.example:290` ships the endpoint empty while `:291` ships the service name
set, exactly as the spec says; and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is 46 lines,
exactly as the spec says.

### Findings that become issues rather than tasks

Three things the drafting pass measured that are **outside M10's scope** and are
filed rather than absorbed. ✅ **All four are now GitHub issues — #26, #27, #28
and #29, opened 2026-08-14** (the second entry became two issues, because its
three unbacked panels have two different causes). Filing them is the point: a finding recorded beside
the code that concerns it is one nobody schedules.

1. **A second load-sensitive test that CI is equally blind to** — [#26](https://github.com/anirudhlath/usher/issues/26).
   `tests/integration/test_adapters_search_postgres.py` failed **three RRF-fusion
   cases in one of three loaded runs**, while `test_rows_refresh` — the known
   one, issue #7 — passed three of three in the same runs. Issue #7 is about one
   named test; this is a second, and the honest framing is that the suite has a
   load-sensitivity class, not a flaky case.
2. **Dashboard 2 needs a play-event log that does not exist** — [#27](https://github.com/anirudhlath/usher/issues/27), and the row-attribution half is [#28](https://github.com/anirudhlath/usher/issues/28). Three of its
   eight panels have **no backing series at all**, and D0 opens two M11 issues
   for them — a play-event log, and row attribution on `POST /play`. See D7,
   which ships Dashboard 2 with five panels and a text panel *naming* the three
   absences, because shipping an empty panel is the failure PRD 10's preamble
   exists to prevent.
3. **`README:63` claims "38 operations"** — [#29](https://github.com/anirudhlath/usher/issues/29), labelled `question` because it is unconfirmed. across PRD 07's five tables; the
   generated `/openapi.json` reports **35**. `/docs` + `/redoc` would make 38, so
   it is probably a counting convention rather than drift — but it could not be
   confirmed, and an unconfirmed reconciliation is not a correction.
## Group O — Phase 0: Foundation — the stack, the export, and the names before any panel is authored

Four tasks, **strictly serialised**, and the ordering is forced rather than stylistic: O2 cannot
observe anything until O1 exists, O3's measured half needs no stack but its *recording* is
worthless before O2 has proved something actually leaves the process, and O4 is the gate that
writes the numbers down. Nothing in Phase 1 may start until O4 is green — the spec's own argument
(`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:101-104`) is that issue #13 is a bound presented as
a measurement, and closing it without the instrument would commit this project's named recurring
error knowingly.

**One task lands outside this repository and three land inside it.** O1 creates
`~/code/observability/` as a repository of its own —
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:904-907` decided that placement and **M10 does not reopen
it**. O2, O3 and O4 touch Usher. Files this group shares with others, declared so the orchestrator
can sequence rather than discover: `tests/unit/test_telemetry.py` (O2 only, two new cases appended),
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (O3 only, and only the two anchors named in its Files
line), and `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` (O2 and O3, disjoint sections). No task in
this group edits `src/usher/telemetry.py`; **Phase 0 adds no instrumentation**, which is the whole
point of `docs/specs/2026-07-28-usher-v1-design.md:165`'s prediction holding.

**Baseline measured at HEAD `3adb5b0` on 2026-08-13**, on this host, so every later number in the
group has something to be a delta against: `ruff check .` clean · `ruff format --check .` **602
files already formatted** · `mypy` **584 source files**, no issues · `lint-imports` **10 kept, 0
broken** · `pytest tests/unit` **4,067 passed / 4 skipped in 45.70 s** · `pytest tests/integration`
**1,232 passed / 22 skipped in 159.41 s** · PRD link check `OK`.

### Task O1 — the observability stack as its own repository, and the collision that is not on 9090

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/compose.yml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/README.md`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/.gitignore`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/otel-collector/config.yaml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/prometheus/prometheus.yml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/loki/loki.yaml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/tempo/tempo.yaml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/grafana/provisioning/datasources/datasources.yaml`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/tests/test_stack_smoke.py`, `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/pyproject.toml`. **No file in `/home/anirudhlath/code/usher-m10` is touched by this task.**

`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:904-907` places the stack **externally and shared** at
`~/code/observability/`, "not bundled into Usher's compose", and `:922` states the asymmetry M10
honours: *"The dashboards are an asset of this repository; the stack that renders them is
infrastructure."* **That directory does not exist** — `ls -d ~/code/observability` answers *No such
file or directory* (2026-08-13) — and no Grafana, Prometheus, Loki or Tempo container runs on this
host. `/home/anirudhlath/CLAUDE.md` requires every code project to live under `~/code/`, so the
placement and the convention already agree; this is a **new git repository**, not a subdirectory of
Usher and not a service in `compose.yml`.

**PRD 10:909-911's rationale for a shared stack is still true and was re-checked rather than
quoted.** Alfred is instrumented and exporting to nothing:
`/home/anirudhlath/code/alfred-deploy/alfred/.env.example` and its two `pyproject.toml` files name
`opentelemetry`/`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`, and the **live** `/home/anirudhlath/code/alfred-deploy/alfred/.env`
sets no `OTEL_*` key at all. One stack, two producers today.

**The port census is measured, not assumed, and the surprise is which port is taken.**
`docker ps --format '{{.Names}}\t{{.Ports}}'` on 2026-08-13 shows **fifteen containers, nine of
which publish a host port**: 80/81/443 (`nginx-proxy-manager`), 8000 (`vllm`), 8001 (`vllm-embed`),
8080 + 9091 + 50001/udp (`dj-ledfx-app-1`), 8081 (`alfred-alfred-1`), 8088 (`signal-cli`),
8642 + 9119 (`hermes`) and 40276 (a scratch Postgres). The other six publish nothing —
`usher-postgres-1` shows a bare `5432/tcp`, and `homeassistant`/`matter-server` are on the host
network. Cross-checked against `ss -tlnp`, the free ports among the obvious defaults
are **3000, 3100, 3200, 4317, 4318, 8888, 8889 and 9090** — and **9091 is busy**, held by
`dj-ledfx-app-1`. That is the one a habit gets wrong: "Prometheus is 9090" is right here and
"pushgateway/the collector's second port is 9091" is a collision with a running service.

**Every one of those nine publishes on `0.0.0.0`.** Measured in `ss -tlnp`'s Local Address column —
there is not one `127.0.0.1:`-scoped container publish on this host; the only loopback-bound
listeners belong to non-Docker processes — and `nginx-proxy-manager` on 80/81/443 makes this box
**internet-facing**. A bare
`ports: - "3000:3000"` therefore publishes Grafana to the internet by default, which is the exact
risk `docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:401-404` names. **Every `ports:` entry in this
stack carries an explicit bind address**, and the stack gets **no** nginx-proxy-manager proxy host.
Only two ports need a host binding at all: Grafana (a browser has to reach it) and the collector's
OTLP/gRPC receiver (Phase 0's Usher runs as a **host process**, not a container — see O2). Everything
else talks over the compose network and publishes nothing.

**Usher's exporter is gRPC, so 4317 is not optional and 4318 is not sufficient.**
`src/usher/telemetry.py:16-17` imports `opentelemetry.exporter.otlp.proto.**grpc**` for both signals;
there is no HTTP exporter anywhere in `src/`. The collector serves 4318 for Alfred and anything
later; Usher needs 4317.

**Loki is not fed by the OTLP pipeline, because Usher emits no OTLP logs at all.**
`grep -rn "LoggerProvider\|OTLPLogExporter\|_logs" src/` finds nothing; `configure_logging`
(`src/usher/telemetry.py:105-114`) adds exactly one loguru sink on `sys.stdout` with
`serialize=settings.log_json`, and `log_json` defaults to `True` (`src/usher/config.py:173`). So
the correlation PRD 10:21-32 promises — *"Every record is patched with the active `trace_id` and
`span_id`"* — is already in the JSON on stdout (`telemetry.py:63-67`), and Loki's job is to ingest
**stdout**, not OTLP. The portable feed is the collector's `filelog` receiver: one path for
containers (`/var/lib/docker/containers/*/*-json.log`, bind-mounted read-only, with the `container`
parser operator) and one for host processes (a directory under `/var/tmp/`, which O2's run tees
into). The Docker `loki` log-driver plugin would also work and is **declined**: it is a host-level
plugin install, i.e. exactly the CachyOS-specific piece the migration below argues against.

**Nothing here may be CachyOS-specific.** `/home/anirudhlath/CLAUDE.md` records the
CachyOS → Proxmox VE migration as decided-but-not-started, and PRD 10:911 already anticipates this
stack surviving it *"as an always-on LXC"*. So: compose files, bind mounts and a git repo — **no
systemd unit, no `pacman` dependency, no host package, nothing that reads `/dev/nvidia*`**. Docker
Compose on this host is **5.3.1** (measured), which is well past every `env_file: path:`/`ports:`
long-form feature this needs.

**The container-to-container path is created now and used later, deliberately.** Phase 0's
observation runs Usher from a host shell, so `127.0.0.1:4317` is all O2 needs. But the containerised
Usher (`~/code/usher`, project `usher`, network `usher_default`) will need a route to the collector,
and inventing one under time pressure in Phase 2 is how a stack ends up published on `0.0.0.0`. So
this task creates an **external** docker network named `observability`
(`docker network create observability`) and attaches the collector to it; Usher's compose joins it
in Phase 2 if a containerised run is wanted. That is a network membership, not a service — PRD
10:913-918's "Usher's only coupling is configuration" is unchanged.

**Failing test first:** `/home/anirudhlath/code/observability/tests/test_stack_smoke.py::test_a_synthetic_span_a_synthetic_metric_and_a_synthetic_log_line_each_reach_their_own_store`
— pushes one OTLP span and one OTLP metric over **gRPC to `127.0.0.1:4317`** and writes one JSON
line carrying a known `trace_id` into the watched log directory, then polls Tempo's
`/api/traces/{id}`, Prometheus's `/api/v1/query` and Loki's `/loki/api/v1/query_range` until each
answers, with a bounded timeout per store. At HEAD it fails at connection refused, because nothing
listens on 4317. **Its positive control is that each of the three assertions is on the *payload*,
not on the HTTP status**: Prometheus answers `200 {"status":"success","data":{"result":[]}}` for a
series that does not exist, and Loki answers `200` with an empty `result` for a stream that was
never ingested — so a stack that ingests nothing passes exactly like a stack that works. The case
asserts the span id it pushed, the exact sample value it recorded, and the exact log line body it
wrote, each with a `assert result, "<store> returned an empty result set"` guard naming the store
before the value is read out of it. A second case,
`test_no_stack_port_is_published_on_a_wildcard_address`, parses `docker compose ps --format json`
for this project and asserts every published binding's host IP is `127.0.0.1` — with
`assert bindings, "the port scan found no published ports"` as its own premise, because a scan that
globs nothing passes exactly like a scan that passes, and a stack that failed to start publishes
nothing.

**Acceptance:**
- `~/code/observability/` is a git repository with an initial commit, holding a single
  `compose.yml` with five services: `grafana`, `prometheus`, `loki`, `tempo`, `otel-collector`.
  It is **not** referenced from anything in `/home/anirudhlath/code/usher-m10`, and
  `git status` in the Usher worktree is clean after this task.
- Published ports, each with an explicit `127.0.0.1:` bind prefix, and no others:
  **Grafana `127.0.0.1:3000`** and **OTLP/gRPC `127.0.0.1:4317`**. Prometheus (9090), Loki (3100),
  Tempo (3200) and OTLP/HTTP (4318) are reachable on the compose network and the external
  `observability` network only. **Verified against `ss -tlnp` after `docker compose up -d`**, not
  against the compose file: the two new listeners appear as `127.0.0.1:3000` and `127.0.0.1:4317`
  and nothing new appears on `0.0.0.0`. The pre-existing wildcard binds measured at HEAD (80, 81,
  443, 8000, 8001, 8080, 8081, 8088, 8642, 9091, 9119, 40276 and the rest) are unchanged in number
  and address.
- **9091 is not used by anything in this stack**, and the README says why in one line naming
  `dj-ledfx-app-1`.
- `curl -s localhost:3000/api/health` answers `"database": "ok"`; Grafana's four datasources
  (Prometheus, Loki, Tempo, **and Postgres**) are **provisioned from files in this repository**, not
  clicked in — the Postgres one because PRD 10:10-14 puts composition, spend and taste on the
  catalog rather than on a metric, and D5 will need it. The Postgres datasource points at
  `usher-postgres-1`; its password is read from an environment variable and **the file in git holds
  no credential** (a `.gitignore`d `.env` beside `compose.yml` supplies it).
- The collector's pipeline is stated in `otel-collector/config.yaml` and each leg is proved by the
  smoke test above: traces → Tempo over OTLP, metrics → Prometheus, logs → Loki from a `filelog`
  receiver over **both** `/var/lib/docker/containers/*/*-json.log` (read-only bind mount) and a host
  log directory. **No `loki` Docker log-driver plugin is installed on the host.**
- Nothing in the repository is CachyOS-specific: `grep -rniE 'pacman|systemd|cachy|nvidia|/etc/systemd' ~/code/observability`
  returns nothing outside prose that says why. The README states the Proxmox-LXC target in one
  paragraph and cites `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:911`.
- `docker compose down && docker compose up -d` returns the stack to the same state — Prometheus,
  Loki, Tempo and Grafana each use a named volume, so a restart does not lose the smoke test's own
  data.

**Risks:**
- **The smoke test is the only thing standing between "the stack is up" and "the stack ingests".**
  Three of the four stores answer `200` with an empty body for data that was never written, so a
  status-code assertion is a green that means nothing. This is the same family as the repository's
  own scan rule and is why the acceptance names the payload.
- **Prometheus does not scrape OTLP.** Whichever bridge is chosen — `prometheusremotewrite` into a
  Prometheus started with `--web.enable-remote-write-receiver`, or the collector's `prometheus`
  exporter scraped by a `scrape_configs` entry, or Prometheus 3's native OTLP receiver — the choice
  **changes the stored series name**, which is O3's subject. Record which one shipped, in the README,
  in one sentence, because a Phase 2 panel is written against the stored name and not against
  PRD 10's.
- Bind-mounting `/var/lib/docker/containers` read-only hands the collector every container's log on
  this host, including `nginx-proxy-manager`'s. That is the intended scope for a shared stack and it
  is worth one sentence in the README rather than a surprise later.

### Task O2 — point Usher at the stack, and correct "no-op exporters" to the stronger thing that is actually true

**Depends on:** O1   **Files:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry.py` (**append only**, two new cases), `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` (**only** a new section at the end). **`/home/anirudhlath/code/usher-m10/.env` is created and is gitignored; `.env.example` is NOT edited — see below.**

`.env.example:290` ships `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=` **blank** and `:291` ships
`OTEL_SERVICE_NAME=usher`. Verified at HEAD 2026-08-13. The service name being set beside the blank
endpoint is what makes this easy to miss, exactly as
`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:48-50` says.
`src/usher/config.py:757-758` binds both to `Settings` by explicit `alias`, so neither carries the
`USHER_` prefix; `:872-888`'s `_blank_to_none` turns the empty string into `None` (the field's own
docstring names `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=` as the case it exists for); and `:912-914`
`telemetry_enabled` is `bool(self.otlp_endpoint)`.

**The spec's sentence about what has been happening is wrong in a way worth correcting, and the
truth is stronger.** `docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:50` says *"Usher's telemetry has
exported to no-op exporters for nine milestones."* **There are no no-op exporters.** Read
`src/usher/telemetry.py:191-197`: a real `TracerProvider` is installed **unconditionally**, and
`BatchSpanProcessor(OTLPSpanExporter(...))` is added **only** inside `if settings.telemetry_enabled`.
`:222-232` mirrors it — a real `MeterProvider`, with `metric_readers=readers` where `readers` is the
empty list when telemetry is disabled. So with a blank endpoint **no exporter object is constructed
at all**, and that is a property the suite already pins from both sides by monkeypatching the
exporter classes to raise on construction (`tests/unit/test_telemetry.py:96` for spans,
`:233` for metrics). The consequences are precise and two of them are counter-intuitive: **spans
have always had real, valid ids**, so `inject_trace_context` (`telemetry.py:63-67`) has been firing
and every JSON log line in every deployment already carries `trace_id` and `span_id`; **metric
points have been recorded into a provider with no reader**, so they were aggregated and never
collected. Nothing has ever left the process. That is the claim to write down.

**The endpoint's scheme is load-bearing and the wrong spelling fails silently.** Measured in the
installed `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-grpc` **1.44.0**
(`.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/opentelemetry/exporter/otlp/proto/grpc/exporter.py:317-323`):
when `insecure` is not passed and `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_INSECURE` is unset, it defaults to
`parsed_url.scheme == "http"`. `telemetry.py:195` and `:224` pass `endpoint=settings.otlp_endpoint`
and nothing else. So **`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=127.0.0.1:4317`** — the spelling a person types —
builds a **TLS** channel against a plaintext collector and every export fails, while
`http://127.0.0.1:4317` builds an insecure one; `:325-326` then discards the scheme and keeps the
netloc. PRD 10:916 already documents the correct spelling; the trap belongs to whoever edits it.

**The run's environment, measured rather than assumed.** The dev database is `usher-postgres-1`,
which `docker ps` shows as `5432/tcp` with **no host publish**; its address on network
`usher_default` is **172.26.0.2**
(`docker inspect -f '{{range .NetworkSettings.Networks}}{{.IPAddress}}{{end}}' usher-postgres-1`,
2026-08-13). Credentials are `usher`/`usher`/`usher` from `compose.yml`'s postgres `environment:`
block. It holds **1,272,401 titles** on **PostgreSQL 17.10**, `alembic_version` is **`m09f`**, and
`code_head_revision()` on `milestone/m10-hardening` is also **`m09f`** — so `/health/ready` answers
200 rather than the 503 a schema mismatch produces. `~/code/usher/.env` holds exactly two keys,
`USHER_SECRET_KEY` and `USHER_TMDB_API_KEY`; **this worktree has no `.env` at all**, so the run
supplies `USHER_DATABASE_URL` itself.

**Three traps in the run, each measured, and the second one is a delta walk against somebody else's
server.**

1. **`docker compose up` from this worktree is a different compose project.** `compose.yml`'s
   postgres bind mount is `./data/postgres`, so a `usher-m10` project starts a *second*, **empty**
   Postgres and the "live catalog" observation would be over zero titles. The 1.27M-row database
   belongs to the project rooted at `~/code/usher`. Phase 0's run is a **host process** against
   172.26.0.2.
2. **`sources` in that database holds 1 row and it is enabled** (measured). So the shipped lanes
   would start a push lane and, on reconnect, a gap-closing `DELTA` reconcile against a real Emby —
   the unbounded walk `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` records under *"Starting the shipped
   app against a real source is itself an unbounded walk, and nothing warns you"*. **`USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false`
   and `USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false` are mandatory for this run**, not advisory, and they are also
   what makes the run's request budget statable at zero.
3. **Port 8000 is taken by `vllm`.** Use **8100**, measured free and already
   `USHER_COMPOSE_HOST_PORT`'s own default in `compose.yml`.

**`.env.example` is not edited, and that is enforced rather than preferred.**
`tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py:282-292` asserts `.env.example`'s key set **equals** the
`Settings` field set, and `:294` asserts every value in it equals that field's **default** — so the
endpoint line must stay blank there. The configured endpoint goes in `.env`, which is gitignored and
which `compose.yml:24-26` hands to the container whole.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry.py::test_a_configured_endpoint_builds_one_real_exporter_over_an_insecure_channel`
— the positive mirror of the two negatives already in that file, which is the half nobody wrote.
Two arms. **Arm one** builds `Settings(..., OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT="http://127.0.0.1:4317")`,
calls `configure_tracing` and `configure_metrics` under the session's `reset_otel_tracer_provider`
/ `reset_otel_meter_provider` fixtures, and asserts the installed `TracerProvider` carries **exactly
one** span processor and the installed `MeterProvider` **exactly one** metric reader — where the
existing cases assert zero exporter *constructions*, this asserts one *installation*, so a refactor
that constructs an exporter and forgets to attach it is caught. **Arm two** is the scheme: the same
settings spelled `127.0.0.1:4317` with no scheme must produce an exporter whose channel is the
secure one, and the case asserts the *insecure* flag differs between the two spellings, citing
`exporter.py:317-323`. At HEAD the whole case fails at `AttributeError`/import, because the file has
no such test; arm one is red against a `configure_tracing` that guarded the *processor* instead of
the exporter, arm two is red against any future normalisation that prepends a scheme. It carries
this repository's required positive control — `assert processors, "no span processor was installed
at all"` and `assert readers, "no metric reader was installed at all"` — because a provider with an
empty processor list is precisely the disabled state, and an assertion phrased as *"nothing wrong is
attached"* is satisfied by nothing being attached.

**Acceptance:**
- `.env` in this worktree carries `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:4317` (with the
  scheme), `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME=usher`, `USHER_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://usher:usher@172.26.0.2:5432/usher`
  and a `USHER_SECRET_KEY`. `git status --porcelain` names **no** file outside the two in the Files
  line; `.env` does not appear, because `.gitignore` already covers it. **`.env.example:290` is
  unchanged and still blank.**
- **The observation, and all three signals come from one real request over the live catalog.** With
  `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false uv run uvicorn usher.api.app:create_app --factory --port 8100`,
  stdout tee'd into O1's watched log directory, and a single
  `GET /search?q=<something the catalog holds>`:
  1. **A trace in Tempo** whose root is the FastAPI server span for `GET /search`, with
     `SQLAlchemyInstrumentor` **statement** spans beneath it — a `connect` span is not enough, per
     `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`'s record of the instrumentor that produced no
     statement spans for three milestones.
  2. **A log line in Loki carrying that trace's `trace_id`**, matched by grepping Loki for the
     32-hex id read off the Tempo trace — not by finding *a* line with *a* `trace_id` field, which
     is what `inject_trace_context` produces for every request and proves only that the patcher runs.
  3. **A metric sample in Prometheus** for `usher.search.duration` **and** for
     `http.server.duration`, each with a non-zero count. Two, not one, because the first is Usher's
     own instrument and the second is `FastAPIInstrumentor`'s — they travel different code paths and
     O3's whole subject is that the second one's name is not Usher's to choose.
  Each of the three is recorded in `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` with the query that
  found it and the value it returned, per the spec's panel-verification rule
  (`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:392-393`).
- **The request budget against the live Emby is zero**, and it is zero because both lane switches
  were off. Stated in the write-up with the two settings named.
- `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` gains a section recording (a) that nothing had ever
  left the process and **why the "no-op exporters" phrasing is wrong** — no exporter is constructed,
  the providers are real, spans have always had valid ids and log lines have always carried
  `trace_id`; (b) the `http://` scheme rule with its `exporter.py:317-323` citation; (c) the three
  run traps above with their measured values (a second empty Postgres from the wrong worktree, one
  enabled source, port 8000 taken).
- `uv run pytest tests/unit` passes at least **4,067** plus the new cases; `mypy`, `ruff check`,
  `ruff format --check` and `lint-imports` (10 kept, 0 broken) unchanged.

**Risks:**
- **A `trace_id` in a log line proves the patcher runs, not that the correlation works.** The
  acceptance requires the id to be read off the *Tempo* trace and then found in Loki, in that
  direction, because the reverse — find a log line, then look up its trace — passes against a Tempo
  that ingested nothing if the query is written loosely.
- **`configure_tracing`/`configure_metrics` are `isinstance`-guarded and set-once per process**
  (`telemetry.py:178-190`, `:222`). A run that imports `usher` before the endpoint is in the
  environment installs a provider with no exporter and then silently refuses to reconfigure. Start
  the process with the environment already set; do not `os.environ[...] = ...` in a shell that has
  already imported the package.
- If the collector is down when Usher starts, the SDK's own retry loop logs a warning and does not
  raise — recorded in `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` already. So **"the app started" is
  not evidence the export works**, and only the three observations above are.

### Task O3 — pin the semantic-convention names, and correct the catalogue's size from 34 to 35

**Depends on:** O2   **Files:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry_metric_names.py` (new), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the `### Metrics` preamble at lines 136-139 and the semconv paragraph at lines 193-199, plus the stale citation at `:184`), `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` (**only** a new section, disjoint from O2's)

`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:193-199` warns that setting
`OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http` renames `http.server.duration` to
`http.server.request.duration`, changes its unit from `ms` to seconds, and swaps `http.target` for
`http.route` — *"any one of which empties a dashboard panel built against the names above,
silently, with no error anywhere."* **Every clause of that is now verified rather than quoted**,
against the versions this project actually resolves: `uv.lock` pins
`opentelemetry-api`/`-sdk`/`-exporter-otlp` at **1.44.0** and every
`opentelemetry-instrumentation-*` plus `opentelemetry-semantic-conventions` at **0.65b0**
(`pyproject.toml:26-31` asks only for `>=1.28` / `>=0.49b0`). Driving one request through a real
`create_app()` into an `InMemoryMetricReader`, in two separate processes:

| | metric name | unit | attributes on the point |
|---|---|---|---|
| default (nothing set) | `http.server.duration` | `ms` | `http.scheme`, `http.host`, `http.flavor`, `http.method`, `http.server_name`, `http.status_code`, `http.target` |
| `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http` | `http.server.request.duration` | `s` | `url.scheme`, `http.request.method`, `http.route`, `http.response.status_code` |

and under the opt-in **`http.server.duration` is absent entirely** — not renamed alongside, gone.
The mechanism is two predicates in the installed contrib package:
`_semconv.py:196-201` defines `_report_new(mode)` as `mode != DEFAULT` and `_report_old(mode)` as
`mode not in (HTTP, DATABASE)`, and `asgi/__init__.py:622-635` builds `duration_histogram_old`
(`ms`) and `duration_histogram_new` (`s`, with second-scaled bucket advice) off exactly those two.
The third mode, `http/dup`, satisfies both and emits **both** histograms — which is the migration
path, and the only setting under which a panel written against either name keeps working.

**One thing PRD 10 does not say and a panel needs.** `http.target` is `route.path_format`
(`asgi/__init__.py:528-551`), so PRD 10:184-186's claim that it is the route template rather than
the raw path is correct — *and* an **unmatched** path has no `http.target` key at all. Measured:
`GET /no-such-route` records an `http.server.duration` point whose attributes are
`{http.scheme, http.host, http.flavor, http.method, http.server_name, http.status_code: 404}`, with
the key absent rather than empty. **A panel that groups by `http.target` silently drops every 404 on
an unrouted path**, which is the one class of request an operator most wants to see. Recorded here
rather than discovered from a panel that under-counts.

**PRD 10:184's own citation has drifted and is corrected in this task.** It says
`FastAPIInstrumentor` is *"wired in `create_app`, `api/app.py:127`"*; at HEAD
`FastAPIInstrumentor.instrument_app(app)` is at **`src/usher/api/app.py:168`** (line 127 is a comment
about the lifespan's `try:`). The claim is right; the line number is stale.

**The spec's "34" is wrong as a row count and right as a different number, and both halves matter.**
`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:32-33` says the catalogue is *"34 rows and every one
is ✅"*. The catalogue is `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:143-177` — **35 rows, 35 distinct names,
and all 35 do carry ✅** (counted; there is no unticked row). 34 is a real measurement of something
else: an AST scan of `src/usher/` for the seven `Meter.create_*` instrument factories finds
**exactly 34 declared instrument names**, and their set is the catalogue **minus
`http.server.duration`** — the one row Usher does not declare, because `FastAPIInstrumentor` emits
it. So the honest sentence is **34 instruments Usher declares plus one it inherits, against 35
documented rows**, and the spec collapsed the two into one wrong number. PRD 10's own preamble at
:136-139 gains that sentence, so the next reader counts the right thing.

**The scan has a trap that manufactures plausible missing metrics.** 🔴 **Every number in this
paragraph as first written was wrong, and O3 re-measured them on 2026-08-14** — recorded here
rather than silently replaced, because the wrong figures did not even close arithmetically
(117 − 83 − 4 = 30, against 34 instruments that must also be in the total) and nobody noticed
until the task was executed. A naive walk over `src/usher/` for calls whose attribute name
`startswith("create_")` finds **120** sites, not 117. **79** are Alembic's `op.create_table` /
`create_index` / `create_foreign_key` under `src/usher/db/migrations/versions/`, not 83; **34**
are the instrument factories themselves; **six** are `asyncio.create_task(name=…)`, not four; and
one is `create_async_engine` (`src/usher/db/base.py:110`). **79 + 34 + 6 + 1 = 120**, with no
overlap.

**And the trap is worse than the original paragraph claimed, in the direction that argues for the
filter.** All **six** `create_task` sites carry a `name=`, and **all six render `usher.*`** —
four string literals (`usher.jobs.heartbeat` at `src/usher/services/jobs.py:317`, not `:313`;
`usher.lane.worker` at `src/usher/api/lanes.py:204`; `usher.lane.refresh` at `:206`;
`usher.lane.rows.refresh` at `:208`) plus **two f-strings a literal-only harvest cannot see at
all** (`api/lanes.py:358` → `f"usher.lane.push.{source.name}"`, `services/jobs.py:340` →
`f"usher.job.{job.kind.value}"`). Every one of them would land in a catalogue comparison looking
exactly like an undocumented metric. **Filter on the factory name, never on the prefix.**

**Where the opt-in may be set, measured, and it is stronger than PRD 10:198's "Nothing in this
project's config sets that variable".** There is no place in Usher's shipped configuration where it
*can* be set:

- **as a line in `.env`** → `ValidationError: otel_semconv_stability_opt_in — Extra inputs are not
  permitted`, from **every** entry point. `Settings.model_config` is `extra="forbid"`
  (`src/usher/config.py:144-149`) and pydantic-settings' dotenv source hands an unmatched key back
  under its full lowercased name (the mechanism `config.py:115-134` documents for
  `USHER_COMPOSE_*`). Measured directly, not reasoned.
- **in `compose.yml`'s `environment:` block** → `tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py:337-347`
  asserts that block's key set **equals** the five topology-owned names.
- **in `.env.example`** → `tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py:282-292` asserts `.env.example`'s
  key set **equals** the `Settings` field set.
- **as a process environment variable** → works. `Settings` builds normally (measured). This is the
  only door, and it is outside anything the repository ships.

So the convention in force is the **default** one, and it is held by three tests and a validation
error rather than by a comment. That is what this task records.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry_metric_names.py::test_every_metric_name_usher_emits_is_a_row_of_prd_10s_catalogue`
— two halves that must both hold. The **declared** half AST-walks `src/usher/**/*.py` for calls to
the seven `Meter` instrument factories (`create_counter`, `create_up_down_counter`,
`create_histogram`, `create_gauge`, `create_observable_counter`,
`create_observable_up_down_counter`, `create_observable_gauge`), harvests the first string literal
or `name=` keyword, parses `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`'s table rows for their 35
backticked names, and asserts `declared == catalogue - {"http.server.duration"}` — **34 == 34**. The
**measured** half builds a real `create_app()`, drives one request through an
`InMemoryMetricReader`, and asserts the FastAPI scope emitted `http.server.duration` at unit **`ms`**
with `http.target` and `http.status_code` on the point. At HEAD the file does not exist. It carries
three positive controls, because each half has a way of passing while measuring nothing:
`assert declared, "the instrument scan found nothing"` plus the named anchor
`"usher.jobs.queued" in declared`; `assert len(catalogue) == 35, "the catalogue table parse found
<n> rows"`, which is the premise a Markdown-table regex loses the moment the table is reformatted;
and `assert points, "the request produced no metric points at all"` before the unit is read.
**It must be demonstrated red twice, against both spellings of the defect it guards** — once by
deleting a row from the catalogue table (the declared half fails), and once by running the same
node under `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http` in the process environment (the measured half fails
on the name and on the unit). The second demonstration is the one that matters and is O4's
phase-specific gate.

**Acceptance:**
- The convention in force is recorded, by name and version, in
  `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`: **the default HTTP semantic conventions**, under
  `opentelemetry-sdk` **1.44.0** and `opentelemetry-instrumentation-fastapi`/`-asgi` **0.65b0**,
  with the two-row table above, the `_semconv.py:196-201` / `asgi/__init__.py:622-635` mechanism,
  the `http/dup` third mode, and the four places the opt-in cannot be set with their citations.
- `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:136-139` states **35 rows — 34 instruments Usher declares
  and one `FastAPIInstrumentor` supplies** — and `:184`'s `api/app.py:127` is corrected to
  `api/app.py:168`. The `:193-199` hazard paragraph gains one sentence: the opt-in cannot be set
  from `.env`, `.env.example` or `compose.yml`'s `environment:` without failing a test or every
  entry point, so the only way to reach it is the launching process's own environment.
- `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:193-197`'s three claims are marked as **measured**
  against the pinned versions, with the date, rather than left as a warning read from upstream docs.
- **The unmatched-path finding is written down**: `http.target` is absent, not empty, on a request
  that matched no route, so a `group by (http.target)` panel drops those points. One sentence in PRD
  10 beside the `http.target` paragraph and the measurement in the rules file.
- **The Prometheus-side renaming is measured and recorded too**, because the hazard has two
  independent causes and PRD 10 documents only one: for one histogram (`usher.search.duration`), one
  counter (`usher.ingest.items`) and one gauge (`usher.jobs.queued`), the write-up names the series
  name **as Prometheus actually stores it** after O1's OTLP bridge, beside the catalogue name. If
  the two differ — dots to underscores, a unit suffix, a `_total` — that mapping is what Phase 2's
  panels are written against, and "the panel was authored against a name that is not on the wire" is
  as easy to reach through the storage layer as through the SDK.
- `uv run pytest tests/unit` passes at least **4,067** plus the new cases; the whole gate green.

**Risks:**
- **The declared half is a scan, so it is one glob away from being a test that always passes.** The
  premise guards are not decoration: a refactor that moves instruments under a package the walk does
  not reach, or a table reformat that defeats the row regex, both produce an empty comparison of two
  empty sets.
- **The measured half depends on process-global OTel state.** `metrics.set_meter_provider` is
  set-once and every `usher` module holds a `_ProxyMeter` from import time; the case must use
  `tests/conftest.py`'s `reset_otel_meter_provider`, and `_OpenTelemetrySemanticConventionStability`
  is *also* initialised once per process from `os.environ`, which is why the opt-in demonstration
  has to be a separate process and not a `monkeypatch.setenv`.
- **34 and 35 will both look wrong to a later reader.** The PRD sentence has to say which is which in
  the same breath, or the next milestone re-derives it — this task exists because the spec's author
  did not.
