### Task O4 — the Phase 0 gate, and the run that proves the convention is pinned rather than merely current

**Depends on:** O1, O2, O3   **Files:** `/var/tmp/m10-gate/phase0/BAR.md` (written **before** any command is run, with its `sha256`), `docs/plans/progress.md` (**only** the M10 row), `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` (**only** a new Phase 0 ledger entry)

The standard gate is stated in two places that agree. `CLAUDE.md:218-224` lists `uv run ruff check .`,
`uv run ruff format --check .`, `uv run mypy src tests`, `uv run lint-imports` — annotated
**"architecture contracts — 10 kept, 0 broken"** — and `uv run pytest`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is
**46 lines with a single `check` job** and runs the same five at `:29`, `:32`, `:35`, `:38` and
`:46`. **The one apparent discrepancy is not one**: CI runs a bare `uv run mypy`, and
`pyproject.toml:127` sets `files = ["src", "tests"]`, so it is the same command as `mypy src tests`.
CI's pytest additionally carries `--cov=usher --cov-report=term-missing`. The sixth step is the PRD
link check from `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md`, scoped to `docs/prd/` plus `CLAUDE.md` and
`README.md` and deliberately **not** over `docs/plans/`.

**Measured at HEAD `3adb5b0` on 2026-08-13, before Phase 0 changed anything**, so a delta is
possible rather than assertable:

| step | result at HEAD |
|---|---|
| `uv run ruff check .` | All checks passed |
| `uv run ruff format --check .` | **602 files** already formatted |
| `uv run mypy src tests` | Success, **584 source files** |
| `uv run lint-imports` | **10 kept, 0 broken** |
| `uv run pytest tests/unit` | **4,067 passed / 4 skipped**, 45.70 s |
| `uv run pytest tests/integration` | **1,232 passed / 22 skipped**, 159.41 s |
| PRD link check | `OK` |

The kept/broken count in `CLAUDE.md:222` is **already correct at 10** — M9's H6 added the tenth
(`no aggregate port module imports another`) and updated the line, so unlike M9's A1 this gate has
no stale count to fix. Phase 0 adds no module and no contract, so **10 kept, 0 broken is also the
expected value afterwards**, and a change in that number means something went in that this phase did
not plan.

**Phase 0's own proof is two things O1–O3 produced and one thing only this task can do.** From O2:
the three observations — a span, a `trace_id`-carrying log line, and a metric sample — each read out
of Grafana against the live 1,272,401-title catalog, each with its query and its returned value.
From O3: the convention recorded. And from here: **the demonstration that the convention is
*pinned* and not merely *current*.** A gate that runs O3's test once, in an environment that happens
to be the default, has proved that today's environment is the default — which is not the claim. The
claim is that the names Usher emits are the catalogue's names, and the only way to see that
assertion has teeth is to run it in the environment where it must fail.

🔴 **Amended 2026-08-14, during O3, because this paragraph's original demonstration does not
work and both O3's implementer and its reviewer proved it.** It read: run the metric-name node
twice, and under `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http` in the process environment it must **fail**.
**It passes.** `tests/conftest.py:37-38`'s autouse `clean_environment` `monkeypatch.delenv`s every
`OTEL_*` variable before any test body runs, and
`_OpenTelemetrySemanticConventionStability._initialize()` (`_semconv.py:210-217`, behind a
process-global `_initialized` latch) is called from inside `create_app()`
(`fastapi/__init__.py:271`) — i.e. **after** the scrub. The cheapest proof needs no plant at all:
a throwaway case that prints its own environment reports `OPT_IN = None` and
`singleton initialized = False` with the variable genuinely set in the parent shell, which also
distinguishes *"never arrived"* from *"arrived and did nothing"*. The honest restatement is that
**inside pytest the measured half pins a dependency upgrade that moves the default convention, not
the deployment's environment** — still worth having, and not what this plan claimed.

**Failing test first, as amended:** the demonstration is a **subprocess**, not an environment
variable draped over pytest. Spawn a child with
`env={**os.environ, "OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN": "http"}` that builds the same `create_app()`,
drives one request through an `InMemoryMetricReader` and prints what the FastAPI scope emitted; it
must report `http.server.request.duration` at unit `s` **and `http.server.duration` absent
entirely** — gone, not renamed alongside. Run the same probe with the variable unset and with
`http/dup`; the three answers are the gate. The positive control is that the *declared* half stays
green throughout (34 == 34 is untouched by an environment variable), so a failure is attributable
to the one assertion the phase is about rather than to the process falling over.

**The in-process alternative was built and verified working and is declined**, for a reason worth
recording: it must reach into `_OpenTelemetrySemanticConventionStability._initialized`, a
**vendored private attribute**, so a dependency upgrade that renamed it would silently turn the
reset into a no-op — and catching that upgrade is the only thing the measured half actually does.
A guard whose own mechanism is broken by the event it guards against is not a guard.

The house rule applies unchanged: **write the expected verdict down before running it** — all
three verdicts, all three invocations — in `/var/tmp/m10-gate/phase0/BAR.md`, with a `sha256`
recorded at write time, and **`/var/tmp` rather than `/tmp`, because `/tmp` on this host is tmpfs
and a bar whose whole value is that it provably predates the numbers cannot live in RAM**
(`CLAUDE.md`; M9's B3 wrote its bar to `/tmp` before noticing).

**Acceptance:**
- All seven standard steps green on a clean tree, with the numbers recorded and compared
  guess-by-guess against `BAR.md`: `ruff check` clean · `ruff format --check` at **602 + the new
  test files** · `mypy` at **584 + the new test files**, no issues · `lint-imports` **10 kept, 0
  broken** — unchanged, and a change is a finding · `pytest tests/unit` at **≥ 4,067 passed / 4
  skipped** · `pytest tests/integration` at **1,232 passed / 22 skipped**, unchanged (Phase 0 adds
  no integration case) · PRD link check `OK`.
- **`uv run pytest` is run whole at least once**, not only as the two directory selections, because
  that is what CI runs at `ci.yml:46` and the two are kept in sync deliberately.
  `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` records
  `tests/integration/test_rows_refresh.py::test_the_route_serves_stale_and_the_refresh_runs_on_a_session_of_its_own`
  as intermittent on this tree (**1 failure in 5 whole-suite runs**, 0 in 5 alone) and the
  long-deselected `test_sse_end_to_end` case as **no longer flaky** — so a red there is checked
  against that entry before it is reported as a Phase 0 regression, and **the deselection is
  re-measured rather than inherited**, which is the exact mistake M9's H7 recorded.
- **Phase 0's specific proof, recorded rather than asserted:** the three Grafana observations from
  O2 quoted with their queries and returned values; the convention from O3 named with its two
  package versions; and the **three-invocation subprocess demonstration** above — unset, `http`,
  `http/dup` — with all three verdicts matching the pre-registered bar. **The amendment above is
  itself part of the proof**: record that the paragraph this gate was written against was wrong,
  and that O3 found it by running the demonstration rather than by reading it.
- A Phase 0 mutation sweep entry in `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`, per
  `docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:345`, reporting the **three-way split** — killed,
  equivalent-mutant controls surviving as designed, unintended survivors — never a single total.
  The sweep is small because Phase 0's code surface is small: three plants are enough and each is
  named in O2's and O3's acceptance (the span-processor count, the scheme predicate, the
  instrument-factory filter). The harness lives **outside the working tree** (`/var/tmp/m10-O4/`),
  because `ruff check .` and `mypy src tests` walk the whole repository and a harness at the root
  makes every gate-step control read FAIL — V1's finding, and it costs nothing to obey.
- `docs/plans/progress.md`'s M10 row moves off `| M10 | Hardening + dashboards | — | not planned |`
  to name this plan and mark Phase 0 complete. **That row and nothing else in the file.**
- `git status --porcelain` after the gate names only the files this group's four tasks declare, and
  `~/code/observability` is a separate repository with its own clean tree.

**Risks:**
- **A gate that only ran the clean process has measured the environment, not the code.** This is the
  whole reason for the subprocess run, and it is the shape `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` calls
  "a run that did not run is not a pass" arriving at an environment variable instead of a harness.
  🔴 **And the original spelling of this gate was itself an instance of it** — the environment
  variable was set, the run ran, the test passed, and what it had measured was a fixture scrubbing
  the variable rather than a convention holding. A demonstration that cannot fail is not a
  demonstration, and it took executing it to find out.
- **The three Grafana observations are not tests and never will be.** They are recorded observations
  with a query and a value, exactly as the spec's panel-verification rule requires
  (`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:392-393`) — so a reviewer re-measures them rather
  than re-running them, and the write-up has to carry enough to make that possible: the query text,
  the time window, and the value returned.
- **`lint-imports` at 10 kept is a green over whatever list it was given.** M9's H6 found that
  dropping one module from an `independence` contract's `modules` list leaves it reporting a
  confident 10 kept, 0 broken with that module unconstrained; the guard against that
  (`test_the_independence_contract_names_every_aggregate_port_module`) is already in the suite and
  is part of the 4,067. Worth knowing before reading the count as coverage.
## Group S — Phase 1, Safety: Usher is a guest on somebody else's server

Four issues, one theme. **The deployment target is an Emby this project's operator
does not own**, and M9's W1 turned the worker from a strictly sequential
`for job in claimed:` into a bounded pool — `KIND_CONCURRENCY` (`src/usher/services/jobs.py:166-177`)
puts **four** of the nine job kinds at four in flight, and three of those four are
the kinds that dial the source. That is the first real parallelism this project
has ever aimed at a third party's hardware, and it landed with **no proactive
outbound limiter of any kind**, an **unbounded delta walk on startup**, four
concurrency entries nobody measured — four, not the two the spec counts (S7) —
and a retraction guard whose ADR reasons about *"a library the operator really
did remove"*.

**One number underpins all four and it is not a measurement.** *"Emby is slow
(~1–5 s/request observed)"* enters the repository in commit **`0c823e0`,
2026-07-28 — the first PRD commit**, two days before M3 built an Emby adapter and
before any request had ever been sent. It is now cited **16 times in `src/` across
13 files** and **5 times in `docs/prd/`**, and **11 of those 21 spell it as
"measured"** (`docs/prd/01-architecture.md:314`, `03-sources-and-sync.md:42` and
`:300`, `08-operations.md:467`, `decisions/0018-…:66`, `src/usher/adapters/emby/session.py:34`
and `:216`, `src/usher/api/dto/health.py:50`, `src/usher/config.py:663`, …).
**No run, no sample, no date, and no instrument is recorded anywhere for it** —
`grep -rn "s/request\|per request" docs/prd/ .claude/rules/` finds the citations
and no measurement. Meanwhile the only live Emby request timings this repository
has ever written down are M9's H4/H5 read-backs at **0.141 s / 0.142 s / 0.143 s**
(`.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:126-131`) — seven to thirty-five times
faster. The M10 spec's own *"~1–3 s/request"* (`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:96`)
and issue #19's identical figure come from a **different** client against the same
server (a Home Assistant card), not from this repository at all. **S1 exists
because every default in this group would otherwise be derived from a
seven-milestone-old impression wearing the word "measured".**

**Live-run discipline, and it binds every task here.** Every live request in this
group goes through a throwaway driver *outside* the working tree, reading the
operator's own secrets file, redacting base URL, token, user id and device id
from everything printed — `CLAUDE.md`'s *Live verification* rule, and the shape
M9's H4/H5 used to do the whole playback and write-back chain in **23 bounded
requests with no walk**. Two consequences that are not optional: the app under
test sets `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false` and `USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false`, because
issue #9 *is* the reason (a lane starts and closes a gap before anything else
happens); and **no bound may be spelled as `max_pages`** — exhausting
`MAX_PAGES` (`src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py:217`, `:429`, `:465-468`) raises
`PortDataMalformed`, so a run bounded that way records `FAILED` and never reaches
the half it was written to exercise.

**Declared live-request budget for the whole group: ≤ 256**, against the
operator's shared Emby, all read-only except S10's one restored write:

| task | budget | what it spends it on |
|---|---|---|
| S1 | **≤ 60** | per-request cost by op class, one page at a time, never through an iterator |
| S7 | **≤ 150** | single-item reads at 1, 2 and 4 in flight |
| S8 | **≤ 6** | `Limit=1` `TotalRecordCount` probes |
| S10 | **≤ 40** | the gate's end-to-end re-verification of all four |
| S2–S6, S9 | **0** | a local stub, fakes, and the suite |

Each harness prints its own request count and **refuses to issue the budget+1st
request**. A task that needs more asks; it does not quietly spend it.

**Pre-registered bars go to `/var/tmp/m10-gate/`, not `/tmp`, and this corrects
the dispatch.** `CLAUDE.md`: *"`/tmp` on this host is tmpfs — RAM — so a
pre-registered bar never goes there … a bar whose value depends on when it was
written is not [fine in `/tmp`], because the whole point of it is that it
provably predates the numbers and a reboot erases the proof."* M9's B3 made
exactly this mistake and every pre-registered bar in that milestone had been
going there. Every bar in this group is written to `/var/tmp/m10-gate/BAR-S<N>.md`,
its `sha256` recorded in the same breath, and the harness re-hashes it at run time
and prints the digest in its own log (`scripts/measure_worker_lane.py:20-25` is
the working precedent).

**Four things this group deliberately does not build**, each with its reason, so
a later reader does not re-open them:

1. **No per-source *configured* rate.** Issue #19 asks whether the ceiling belongs
   per source — *"a self-hosted server on the same LAN and a shared server across
   the internet do not deserve the same ceiling"* — and the answer is yes for the
   **key** and no for the **value** in Phase 1. The limiter is keyed by
   `source.id`; the *rate* stays one setting, because `Source` carries no tuning
   field at all (`src/usher/domain/source.py:16-25`: id, kind, name, base_url,
   credentials_ref, device_id, enabled, supports_push, created_at, updated_at) and
   adding one is DDL the spec's data-model section closes: *"One migration, `m10a`
   … No other schema change."* Named as a post-v1 candidate with the column
   spelled out, not left to be rediscovered.
2. **No throttle on the image CDN.** `src/usher/composition.py:1400-1405` already
   declined one with a reason — *"the image CDN publishes no rate limit and is not
   the API the ~40 rps ceiling is about; a token bucket here would be a limiter
   invented against a number nobody has measured"* — and S3's enumeration confirms
   the decline rather than reversing it.
3. **No new `SyncRunStatus` member.** S6 wants a delta walk that stopped at a
   ceiling to be distinguishable from one that finished, and the cheap spelling is
   a fourth status. `SyncRunStatus` has three (`src/usher/domain/sync.py:51-53`)
   and a fourth is DDL. `FAILED` plus a named `error` string buys the whole
   property that matters — a `FAILED` run contributes no cursor — at zero schema
   cost, and S6 measures that rather than asserting it.
4. **No real 429 is provoked from a third party.** S4 closes the
   `PortRateLimited.retry_after` gap against a **local stub**. ADR-0005's courtesy
   argument and M9's S3 (86 × 502 from TMDb in two bursts of 43) are why
   `scripts/measure_worker_lane.py` was built against a stub, and probing a
   household server's ceiling is strictly worse.

Intra-group ordering: **S1 → S2 → S3 → S4**, then **S5 → S6** and **S8 → S9** in
parallel from nothing, **S7 after S3 and after group O**, **S10 last**. Files this
group shares with others, declared so the orchestrator sequences rather than
discovers: `src/usher/config.py` and `.env.example` (S2, S6 — and every group that
adds a setting), `src/usher/composition.py` (S3, S7), `src/usher/api/deps.py` (S3),
`docs/prd/01-architecture.md`'s concurrency table (S7), `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md`
(S2, S6, S9), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`'s metric catalogue (S2, S8 —
each adds one row, and group O's panels are authored against them).

### Task S1 — what a request to somebody else's Emby actually costs, and the seven-milestone-old number 21 places call "measured"

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `scripts/measure_source_latency.py` (new), `docs/prd/01-architecture.md` (**only** the `~1–5 s/request` clause at :314 and the concurrency-table footnote at :311-316), `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md` (**only** :42 and :300), `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** :467), `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md` (a new measurement section)

Every number Phase 1 is about to choose — the limiter's default rate, whether a
rate limiter binds at all, whether concurrency 4 is polite — is derived from
*"Emby is slow (~1–5 s/request observed)"*. **That string enters the repository in
`0c823e0` on 2026-07-28, the first PRD commit**, before `src/usher/adapters/emby/`
existed (M3, 2026-07-30) and before any request had been sent to any Emby from
this project. `git log -S "1–5 s/request observed" --all -- docs/` returns exactly
that one commit. It is now load-bearing in **16 places in `src/` across 13 files**
and **5 in `docs/prd/`**, and **11 of the 21 render it as "measured" or
"measures"** — `src/usher/adapters/emby/session.py:216` says *"PRD 01 measures at
1-5 s per call"*, `src/usher/api/dto/health.py:50` and
`src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py:360` say the same, and PRD 01:314 — the
document all of them cite — attributes it to *"the old table"*, quoting an earlier
revision of itself. **There is no run behind it anywhere.**

**And the one live observation this repository does hold contradicts it by an
order of magnitude.** M9's H5 read an item back from the operator's real Emby
three times and recorded **0.141 s / 0.142 s / 0.143 s**
(`.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:126-131`) — that is an *upper* bound on a
`GET /Users/{u}/Items/{id}` round trip, since the figure is measured from when the
worker pass returned. Every other live Emby number in that file is about the
websocket (`Sessions` median 38.7 s, `:325-328`), not about a request.

**So the question is not "is 1–5 s right", it is "which op class is it about".**
The instrument already exists and has since M3: `EmbySession._send` records
`usher.source.request.duration` with `{source, op}` on **every** outbound Emby
request, in a `finally` (`src/usher/adapters/emby/session.py:361-364`), and PRD 10
lists it ✅ M3 (`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:148`). **Nine milestones
have emitted that histogram and nobody has ever read it**, which is Phase 0's
whole point arriving one task early. The hypothesis this task is written against —
declared in the bar, not after: **the distribution is bimodal**, with single-item
reads (`op=get_item`, `op=watch_history`) an order of magnitude cheaper than a
200-item page carrying the full `Fields` set (`op=list`,
`src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py:430-444`), and *"1–5 s"* if it is about
anything is about the page.

**The run is bounded by construction and never touches an iterator.** A page cost
is measured by calling `EmbySession.json_body("GET", "/Users/{id}/Items", params={…
"StartIndex": n, "Limit": "200", "EnableTotalRecordCount": "true"}, op="list")`
**directly** — one request, no `list_items`, no generator, so
`CLAUDE.md`'s *"the bound has to be in the iterator"* rule is satisfied by there
being no iterator. Budget **≤ 60 requests**: four op classes
(`verify` via `/System/Info/Public`, `get_item`, `list` at page depth 0, `list` at
a scattered `StartIndex`) × 12 repetitions, plus ≤ 12 discarded warm-up. Read-only
throughout — no `POST`, no `DELETE`, nothing under `/PlayedItems` or `/UserData`.
Ids for `get_item` come from `media_items` this deployment already holds, so no
search and no discovery request is spent finding one.

Quiet-check imported rather than re-derived, per `CLAUDE.md`: two-sided
idle-sampled CPU drift and the argv-token foreign-process census from
`scripts/measure_suggest_tiers.py`. A one-minute load average rises from the run's
own work and would condemn every clean run; `pgrep -f pytest` counts the shell
that mentions the word.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_measure_source_latency.py::test_the_harness_refuses_to_issue_more_requests_than_its_declared_budget`
— drives the harness's request loop against a stub transport with
`--budget 5` and asserts the sixth call raises before it reaches the transport,
naming the budget in the message. At HEAD the module does not exist. Its positive
control fires first: `assert sent == 5, "the harness sent nothing and a budget it
never approached is not a budget"` — a harness that issued zero requests passes an
"it did not exceed the budget" assertion exactly as a correct one does. Second
arm: the same harness with `--budget 0` sends **nothing at all**, which is what a
dry run has to mean.

**Acceptance:**
- `usher.source.request.duration` is read out of Phase 0's Prometheus **and**
  re-derived independently from the harness's own wall-clock timings, and the two
  agree to within 5% on the median — two instruments, because one instrument
  measuring itself is not a measurement (S3's live TMDb run agreed to 0.5% between
  a probe and `raw_payloads.fetched_at` deltas, which is the precedent).
- A table of **median, mean, p95 and max per `op`**, each with its `n`, written
  into `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md` with its date and the sample stated
  rather than laundered — *"one household, one evening"*, and explicitly not a
  constant. The mean is reported beside the median because a right-tailed
  distribution wants the mean for any `Σ` extrapolation (M9's S2 finding).
- **Every one of the 21 `1–5 s` citations is reconciled**, and the reconciliation
  is a `grep`-checkable claim: either the clause is corrected in place with the new
  number and its date, or it is left with the word *"measured"* removed and a
  pointer to the new section. `grep -rn -- "1–5 s\|1-5 s" src/ docs/prd/ | grep -c "measur"` is **0** afterwards, or the
  survivors are the ones the new run really did measure. ⚠️ The 16 `src/` sites are
  docstrings and comments, so this is a prose diff over 13 files and no behaviour;
  it must not be bundled with S2's mechanism.
- The bar at `/var/tmp/m10-gate/BAR-S1.md` predates the first request, its
  `sha256` is in the commit body, and the write-up is **refutations first**: each
  prediction, then what happened, then the confirmations.
- Total live requests issued: **≤ 60**, printed by the harness and quoted in the
  write-up beside the window (`HH:MM:SSZ → HH:MM:SSZ`) and the statement that the
  server was idle from the last one.
- The write-up names what is **still** unverified rather than implying it: this
  server under sustained concurrency (S7), and any other Emby build.

### Task S2 — the outbound limiter, and why a requests-per-second ceiling against a slow server is a limiter that limits nothing

**Depends on:** S1   **Files:** `src/usher/adapters/http.py`, `src/usher/config.py` (the source block at :202-208), `.env.example` (the source block at :63-67), `docs/prd/decisions/0039-the-outbound-limiter-is-per-source-and-spaces-requests.md` (new), `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md` (the *Durable client authentication* / adapter-tuning material at :36-45), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (one row in the metric table at :143-177), `tests/unit/test_adapters_http.py`, `tests/unit/test_config.py`, `tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py`

`src/usher/adapters/http.py` is **207 lines**, and its module docstring quotes PRD
01's promise of *"the httpx client lifecycle, retry/backoff, and rate-limit
handling that the Emby and TMDb adapters both need"* (`:4-6`). **What the file
contains is entirely reactive**: `retry_after_seconds` (`:71-97`) parses both RFC
9110 forms of a `Retry-After` header, and `port_error_for`'s first arm (`:169-172`)
turns a 429 into `PortRateLimited`. Both are about a limit that has **already been
hit**. Nothing in `src/` paces an outbound call to a source at all — `.env.example`
offers `USHER_SOURCE_PAGE_SIZE`, `USHER_SOURCE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and
`USHER_SOURCE_REAUTH_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (`:63-67`), which are size, timeout and how
long a rejected credential is remembered.

**Token bucket or minimum interval, and the reason is the shape of the traffic
rather than taste.** `TmdbClient` already has a bucket
(`src/usher/adapters/tmdb/client.py:104-136`), one second of burst, the lock held
across the wait so N coroutines cannot each independently decide they may go
(`:107-111`). That is right for TMDb, whose median request is **0.0588 s** over
130,334 live requests (`.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md`, M9's S3): a burst of
30 against a CDN-backed public API is absorbed. It is the wrong shape here. A
household server is a machine somebody is watching television on, and the failure
the operator has already hit on this exact server — recorded in issue #19 — is a
Home Assistant card that had to cap concurrent loads at 3 because prefetching
*"floods the server and starves visible posters"* for real users. **A bucket
permits precisely that flood after any idle period; a minimum-interval gate cannot
express it.** So: `_MinInterval`, a monotonic next-slot gate under a lock held
across the wait, `1/rate` seconds apart, **no burst credit**, `rate=0` meaning
unlimited (the shape `push_gap_min_interval_seconds`' `ge=0` already uses,
`src/usher/config.py:674`).

🔴 **And the finding that decides how this is written up: at the latency PRD 01
claims, a requests-per-second limiter is inert.** Four concurrent requests each
taking 2 s is 2 rps; a ceiling of 2 rps never fires, and the operator has
configured a limiter that does nothing while looking configured — the exact defect
`push_max_items_per_event`'s `le=500` bound was written against
(`src/usher/config.py:660-667`). **At the latency S1 actually measures, it is the
opposite**: single-item reads at 0.14 s × 4 in flight is ~28 rps and the gate binds
on every request. So the honest statement, which goes in the ADR and in the
setting's own comment, is that **the rate limiter and the concurrency ceiling
(#13/S7) bound different regimes and neither substitutes for the other** — and
which regime this deployment is in is S1's answer, not a guess. Same shape as
`api/deps.py:449-450`'s *"a rate limiter that limits nothing"*, arriving through
latency instead of through instance count.

**The default is derived, in one sentence, from S1.** Little's law over the op
class that dominates a walk: a page at S1's measured p95, times the concurrency
S7 settles, gives the rate at which Usher is already going as fast as the server
answers — and the default is set **below** it, so the setting is a courtesy
margin rather than a re-statement of the server's own speed. Written with its
denominator, exactly as the spec's Phase 1 gate requires of #13.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_adapters_http.py::test_two_calls_are_spaced_and_a_burst_is_not_permitted_after_an_idle_period`
— an injected clock and sleep (the `TmdbClient` pattern, `:113-124`), five
concurrent `take()` calls after 10 s of simulated idleness, asserting the granted
instants are `[0, 1/rate, 2/rate, 3/rate, 4/rate]` and **not** `[0, 0, 0, 0, 0]`.
At HEAD the class does not exist. Its positive control is the discriminating half:
the identical scenario against a **`_TokenBucket` of the same rate** is asserted to
grant all five at once — so the case proves the two designs differ rather than
asserting that one of them works, and a later "simplification" back to a bucket
fails it. Second arm: `rate=0` grants all five immediately and calls `sleep` zero
times, because a disabled limiter that still awaits is a limiter you cannot turn
off.

**Acceptance:**
- `USHER_SOURCE_REQUESTS_PER_SECOND` exists on `Settings` beside its three
  siblings, named `source_*` and **not** `emby_*` — `src/usher/config.py:197-201`
  states that rule and why (*"config.py is not an adapter, and a setting named for
  one media server would be the first source-specific concept to escape
  `adapters/`"*). It ships in `.env.example` at its default value, which
  `tests/unit/test_deployment_config.py::test_env_example_documents_every_setting`
  (:282) and `::test_env_example_ships_the_defaults` (:294) both require —
  a setting added without the example line is a red, not a discovery.
- The default carries its derivation in the field's own comment: S1's p95 for the
  op class, the concurrency it is paired with, the arithmetic, and the sentence
  that it is a **courtesy margin below** what the server would serve. No round
  number without that paragraph.
- ADR-0039 states the four decisions and their evidence: minimum interval over
  token bucket (the flood, plus issue #19's HA-card observation); keyed per source
  and why the *value* is not (S3, and the `Source` model's ten fields at
  `src/usher/domain/source.py:16-25`); the regime finding above; and that the bound
  is **per process, per source** — `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`'s W1
  entry already records that *"`USHER_JOB_CONCURRENCY` and
  `USHER_TMDB_REQUESTS_PER_SECOND` are both per process, against a rate limit that
  is per client"*, and a second `usher work` container doubles this one too. Said
  out loud, not discovered.
- One new metric row in PRD 10's table (`:143-177`), emitted rather than
  documented: **`usher.source.throttle.wait`**, a histogram labelled `source`,
  recording the seconds a caller spent inside the gate. Zero is a real reading —
  it is how an operator sees the limiter is not binding — so it is recorded on
  **every** call and not only when it waits. PRD 10's own preamble at `:135-137`
  is why this row is added in the same commit as the emitter: *"a documented metric
  nothing emits is a dashboard panel that is permanently empty, and nothing
  distinguishes that from a healthy zero"*.
- `uv run mypy src tests` clean under `strict`; `uv run lint-imports` still
  **10 kept, 0 broken** — measured at HEAD `3adb5b0` on 2026-08-13. The limiter
  lives in `usher.adapters.http`, which the tenth contract's neighbour *"the shared
  http helpers import no concrete adapter"* already covers, and it must not acquire
  an import of `usher.config`.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the lock released before the sleep
  (five waiters all compute the same slot — a burst of five, which is the defect
  the class exists for); `1/rate` replaced by `rate` (a factor-of-`rate²` error
  that every equality-on-spacing case sees); the `rate=0` arm deleted. The
  equivalent-mutant control is the two `__init__` attribute writes swapped —
  disjoint attributes on a freshly constructed object, neither reading the other —
  and it is measured **against all five gate steps separately**, not against pytest
  alone.

### Task S3 — the limiter reaches every adapter that dials out, and the enumeration finds that a per-pipeline factory is a per-request bucket

**Depends on:** S2   **Files:** `src/usher/adapters/emby/session.py`, `src/usher/adapters/factory.py`, `src/usher/composition.py` (`adapter_factory` at :314-328, its call site at :464), `src/usher/api/deps.py` (`get_source_adapter_factory` at :296-304), `src/usher/api/app.py` (the lifespan block at :60-100), `src/usher/cli.py`, `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_session.py`, `tests/unit/test_adapters_factory.py`, `tests/unit/test_composition.py`, `tests/unit/test_outbound_call_sites.py` (new), `docs/prd/01-architecture.md`

**Every outbound call site in `src/usher/adapters/`, enumerated by grep rather
than by memory** (`grep -rn "client.stream\|client.send\|client.get\|client.post\|build_request" src/usher/adapters/`),
with what each one gets and why:

| site | upstream | today | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| `emby/session.py:352-355` (`_send`) | the source | **nothing** | the gate |
| `emby/push.py` (`/embywebsocket`) | the source | nothing | **nothing** — a socket held open is not a request; the reconnect *backoff* is its limiter (`_backoff`, `services/push.py:493`) |
| `tmdb/client.py:164` | `api.themoviedb.org` | `_TokenBucket`, 30 rps (`:104-136`, `config.py:272`) | unchanged — ADR-0005, measured, and not this issue |
| `tmdb/provider.py:245,301,342,343,349,420` | — | through the client above | unchanged |
| `images/provider.py:84` | `image.tmdb.org` | **deliberately none** | **unchanged** — `composition.py:1400-1405` declined it with a reason and this task confirms rather than reverses |
| `bulk/download.py:183` | IMDb / MovieLens / TMDb exports | none | **unchanged** — one streamed file per dataset, not a request stream |
| `bulk/wikidata.py:189` | WDQS | none | **unchanged**, and named: a bootstrap phase run by hand, not by a lane |
| `llm/openai_compatible.py:194` | `USHER_LLM_BASE_URL` | none | **unchanged** — `curate` is capped at 1 in flight (`services/jobs.py:174`) and PRD 06 budgets one completion per household per day |
| `embedding/openai_compat.py:249` | `USHER_EMBEDDING_MODEL`'s endpoint | none | **unchanged** — `index` is capped at 1 |

**Five of the nine get nothing, and every one of the five has a stated reason
rather than an omission** — that is the deliverable as much as the wiring is,
because `.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md` records what happens when a
decision about an upstream is left implicit.

**`EmbySession._send` is the whole of the Emby surface and that is verified, not
assumed.** All four public entry points — `request` (`:366`), `ok` (`:411`),
`json_body` (`:425`), `anonymous_json` (`:437`) — and `_authenticate_locked`
(`:240`) call it, and it is the only place `self._client` is touched. It is
already the metering point (`usher.source.request.duration` in a `finally`,
`:361-364`), so the gate goes immediately above `build_request` and the wait shows
up in S2's new histogram rather than inside the existing one.

🔴 **The placement is the finding, and the obvious one is wrong.** A gate held on
the `EmbySession` instance is per-**adapter**, and a gate held on
`ConfiguredSourceAdapterFactory` (`src/usher/adapters/factory.py:25-77`) is
per-**factory** — and `composition.adapter_factory` is called from
`build_pipeline` (`src/usher/composition.py:464`), i.e. **once per unit of work**.
The server opens a pipeline per lane task and W1's worker opens a scope per claim
and per job; `api/deps.py:296-304` builds one per request. So a bucket on either
is a bucket per request, which is `api/deps.py:520-523`'s own recorded defect
verbatim — *"a request-scoped `TmdbClient` gives every concurrent request a fresh
bucket, so N in-flight requests get N × 30 rps"* — and `api/app.py:65-71` is the
shape that fixed it there: one owner, at lifespan, on `app.state`. **The limiter is
therefore a process-wide registry keyed by `source.id`**, built once at each
composition root and handed *into* `adapter_factory`, so every adapter for one
source shares one gate however many pipelines exist.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_composition.py::test_two_adapters_for_one_source_share_one_gate_and_two_sources_do_not`
— builds two pipelines from one composition root, opens an adapter for the same
source through each, and asserts the two sessions hold the **same** gate object
while a second source's is a different one. At HEAD `adapter_factory` mints a fresh
factory per pipeline, so the two are distinct and the case fails on identity. Its
positive control is the second half: `assert gate_a is not gate_c, "two sources
sharing one gate is a limiter that halves itself per source"` — an implementation
that returned one global gate passes the first assertion and fails this one, and a
case carrying only the first would ratify it. Paired with a behavioural arm in
`tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_session.py::test_every_send_passes_the_gate_including_the_authenticating_one`,
which drives all five entry points and counts gate acquisitions against requests —
because `_authenticate_locked` is the one that is easy to miss and is also the one
a wrong password turns into a request per call (`src/usher/config.py:204-207`).

**Acceptance:**
- `tests/unit/test_outbound_call_sites.py::test_no_outbound_http_call_escapes_a_recorded_decision`
  parses every module under `src/usher/adapters/` for calls to
  `send`/`stream`/`get`/`post`/`request`/`build_request` on an `httpx` client and
  asserts each resolved site is in a **closed table in the test file** naming its
  upstream and its limiter (or its recorded decline). A new adapter with a new
  outbound call is a red, not a discovery. It carries a premise guard —
  `assert len(found) >= 9, "the scan found nothing and a scan that globs nothing passes exactly like one that passes"` —
  and a named anchor (`usher.adapters.emby.session`), which is the control this
  repository requires of every scan.
- The five declines are written where the code is, not only in the test: each
  module's docstring or the composition-root comment says *which* upstream it
  talks to and why it is unthrottled, in the shape `composition.py:1400-1405`
  already uses.
- `usher work`, the server's worker lane, the push lane and `usher sync` all reach
  the same gate for the same source **in one process**, demonstrated through the
  real composition roots rather than argued — and the write-up states plainly that
  a second process is a second gate, which is a capacity decision an operator makes
  and not a correctness one.
- `uv run lint-imports` **10 kept, 0 broken**; `usher.adapters.factory` gains no
  import of `usher.config` (the factory takes values, per its own docstring at
  `:28-32`).
- No behavioural change with the rate left at its default in any existing case:
  `uv run pytest tests/unit` collects and passes at least the **4,071** cases
  collected at HEAD `3adb5b0` (measured 2026-08-13) plus the new ones.

### Task S4 — the 429 path has never met a 429, and Phase 1 can close that honestly only against a stub

**Depends on:** S3   **Files:** `tests/integration/test_rate_limited_end_to_end.py` (new), `tests/fakes/emby_server.py`, `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md` (**only** the `PortRateLimited.retry_after` carried-debt bullet at :1005-1030), `.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md`

PRD 09's carried-debt entry for `PortRateLimited.retry_after` is marked ✅ **paid
by M9's D9** (`docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:1013-1030`): a keyword-only
`retry_after_seconds` on `JobQueue.fail`, `GREATEST(:retry_after_seconds, 0)`
inside `_FAIL`'s `make_interval`, and `JobWorker._fail` reading the hint through
`isinstance(exc, PortRateLimited)`. The same entry closes with the sentence this
task is about: **⚠️ *"The field this closes has still never been exercised by a
real 429 … so its behaviour is pinned by one case and by no observation."***
Three live runs corroborate it and none of them is weak: M9's T2 (393 TMDb
requests, no 429, no `retry-after` on the one 400), M9's S3 (**130,334 requests,
zero 429s, and no `Retry-After` on any of 193 non-200s**), and M9's H4/H5 (23 Emby
requests, no 429, `run_after` NULL on the only queued row —
`.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:148-158`). And M9's H7 measured the cover:
ignoring the hint at `JobWorker._fail` fails **exactly one case out of 5,221**.

**The decision, stated rather than left implicit: Phase 1 does not close it, and
provoking a real 429 is refused with a reason.** The only servers this project
talks to are a third party's Emby and TMDb; deliberately hammering either until it
rate-limits is precisely the behaviour this whole group exists to prevent, and
ADR-0005 chose ~25 rps as courtesy against a *stated* ~40 rather than discovering
the ceiling by hitting it (`src/usher/adapters/tmdb/client.py:3-9`). What Phase 1
*can* do — and what the carried-debt entry's honest state is — is prove the chain
end to end against a **stub that answers 429 with a `Retry-After`**, so that the
one case D9 left is joined by one that exercises adapter → `PortRateLimited` →
`JobWorker._fail` → `jobs.run_after` on real Postgres.

That is a strictly smaller claim than the entry currently invites, and the entry is
edited to say so: **the mechanism is verified, the upstream behaviour is not**, and
the second half cannot be verified without a server that rate-limits Usher, which
would be evidence that S2 failed.

**Failing test first:** `tests/integration/test_rate_limited_end_to_end.py::test_a_429_from_a_source_defers_the_job_by_the_interval_the_upstream_asked_for`
— `FakeEmbyServer` answers `429` with `Retry-After: 120` for one `list` request; a
real `match` job is claimed and failed through the shipped `JobWorker` against real
Postgres; the case asserts `run_after - clock_timestamp()` is **≥ 120 s** and that
the same job under a 429 carrying **no** header lands on the ordinary jittered
backoff, which is a strictly smaller interval. At HEAD `FakeEmbyServer` cannot
answer 429 at all, so the case fails on the fake before it fails on the assertion —
which is why the fake's arm lands first and is asserted separately
(`assert response.status_code == 429, "the fake did not rate-limit and a chain that was never provoked proves nothing"`).
Both arms, because *"backed off"* is satisfied by any backoff and the whole content
of D9 is *which* one.

**Acceptance:**
- The 429 path is exercised over **two** distinct `Retry-After` forms — an integer
  and an HTTP-date — because `retry_after_seconds` (`src/usher/adapters/http.py:71-97`)
  handles both and the second is the one that used to raise `ValueError` in two
  separate copies of this code.
- **Zero live requests.** The word *"provoked"* does not appear in any commit body
  in this task except beside the word *"stub"*.
- PRD 09's bullet gains a ⚠️ paragraph distinguishing *the mechanism is pinned by
  two cases and real Postgres* from *no upstream this project talks to has ever sent
  one*, with the three run counts (393 / 130,334 / 23) as its evidence, and it
  stays in carried debt rather than being ticked.
- `.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md` gains the general form, which is the
  transferable half: **a refusal path that has never fired is pinned by its
  construction and not by an observation, and the honest closing note names which
  of the two the reader is getting.** Nearest relative in that file is *"a refusal
  justified by 'this cannot happen' is one measurement away from firing
  constantly"*, arriving from the opposite direction.

### Task S5 — the first gap-closing delta of a fresh deployment is a full walk wearing a delta's name, and `push_gap_min_interval_seconds` bounds nothing about it

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/api/lanes.py:401-411`, `src/usher/services/reconcile.py` (`_cursor_for` at :188-208, docstring only), `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py`, `tests/unit/test_services_push.py`, `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md` (**only** the Reconnect-delta row at :333 and the paragraph at :352-372)

`LaneSupervisor._close_gap` (`src/usher/api/lanes.py:401-411`) is four lines of
body: `reconcile.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.DELTA, adapter)` then
`watch.sync(...)`, in that order and for a stated reason. Its **only** caller is
`PushSupervisor._gap` (`src/usher/services/push.py:474-491`), which is called from
two places: `run()` at `:390`, **immediately after every successful
`adapter.events()` connection**, and `:406`, when an applied event reports
`deferred_to_delta`. A lane is started per enabled source by
`LaneSupervisor._start_lane` (`:341-361`), which the refresher task calls before it
first sleeps — so **the first thing a freshly started Usher does against every
enabled source is close a gap**, from `uvicorn usher.api.app:create_app --factory`
with default settings and no command of its own.

**What `push_gap_min_interval_seconds` actually bounds, since the spec asks:
cadence, and nothing else.** It is a `float` defaulting to **60.0** with `ge=0`
(`src/usher/config.py:674`, and `:668-673` explains the `ge=0`), threaded through
`LaneSupervisor._start_lane` at `:356` into `PushSupervisor.__init__`'s
`gap_min_interval_seconds` (`services/push.py:353`), and consulted at `:483`:
`if gate.at is not None and now - gate.at < self._gap_min_interval: return`. It
answers *"how often may a flapping socket trigger a delta"*. It says nothing about
how **large** any one delta is, and — the half that matters here —
**`gate.at` is `None` until a gap has run, so the first one is never skipped**
(`services/push.py:478-482` says so in prose, and
`tests/unit/test_services_push.py:972` pins it). It is a correct guard against the
wrong hazard.

🔴 **And the delta is unbounded because its cursor may be `None`.**
`ReconcileService._cursor_for` (`src/usher/services/reconcile.py:188-208`) returns,
for a `DELTA`, `max()` over `latest_completed_cursor` for **both** item lanes — and
`latest_completed_cursor` is `SELECT started_at … WHERE status = 'completed'`
(`src/usher/db/repositories/sync.py:54-59`), so **a source that has never
completed an item-lane run has no cursor and `_cursor_for` answers `None`**. The
walk is then `adapter.list_items(since=None)` (`reconcile.py:218`) — no
`MinDateLastSaved` parameter is sent at all (`adapters/emby/adapter.py:440-441`) —
which against the one measured library is **1,126,674 items, 5,634 pages** at the
default `USHER_SOURCE_PAGE_SIZE=200` (`.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:818`,
`adapters/emby/adapter.py:209-215`). That is the entire library, requested by a
process nobody typed a command into. It is exactly what M9's H4/H5 set
`USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false` to avoid, and an earlier probe walked **1,126,789
items** against this household before it was killed (issue #9).

**This task fixes the cursorless case and only that case**, because it is the one
with no legitimate reading: a delta with no cursor is not a delta. The gap-closer
refuses it, logs at **WARNING** naming the source and what an operator should run
(`usher sync --kind full`), and returns — leaving the socket up and the push lane
delivering, which is the whole point of the lane. A bound on a delta that *does*
have a cursor is S6, and it is a genuinely different decision.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py::test_a_source_with_no_completed_run_is_not_gap_closed_and_the_operator_is_told`
— a lane started against a source whose `sync_runs` table holds nothing; asserts
`reconcile` was **not** called, that a WARNING was emitted naming the source and
the command, and — the arm that makes it a test of the *cursor* rather than of
*emptiness* — that a source with a `FAILED` run and no completed one is refused
identically, while a source with one `COMPLETED` run **is** gap-closed. At HEAD all
three are gap-closed and the first two fail. Its positive control is the third arm
(`assert reconciled == [source_c], "a refusal that refuses everything is not a guard, it is an off switch"`),
which fires first.

**Acceptance:**
- The refusal is at the gap-closer, not inside `ReconcileService`: `usher sync
  --kind delta` on a fresh source is an operator asking for exactly this and must
  keep working. Demonstrated by a case, not by a comment.
- The WARNING is one line, carries the source **name** (never the base URL, never a
  token — PRD 08's credentials rule, and `reconcile.py:164-167` is the local
  precedent), and names the command. `usher work`'s and the lane's log both reach
  it, because the lane is where it happens.
- PRD 03's Reconnect-delta row (`:333`) gains the condition. The row currently
  reads *"Items changed since the last cursor"* and is silent about there being no
  cursor; the correction says what happens then and why it is a refusal rather than
  a walk.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the refusal moved *after*
  `reconcile` (the walk happens and the warning still prints — which every
  "warned" assertion passes, so the case has to assert **`reconcile` was not
  called**); the predicate widened from *"no completed run"* to *"no run at all"*
  (a `FAILED`-only source is walked again, which is the state a killed probe leaves
  behind); the WARNING downgraded to DEBUG (invisible at the shipped
  `USHER_LOG_LEVEL`). The equivalent-mutant control is one sentence of
  `_close_gap`'s docstring reworded — checked first against
  `grep -rln "getdoc\|__doc__\|ast.unparse\|getsource" tests/`, since several
  scans read `api/` and the control is worthless if one of them reads this module.

### Task S6 — a ceiling on a gap-closing delta, and the reason a bounded walk must not be allowed to record `COMPLETED`

**Depends on:** S5   **Files:** `src/usher/api/lanes.py`, `src/usher/services/reconcile.py`, `src/usher/config.py`, `.env.example`, `tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py`, `tests/integration/test_services_reconcile.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py`, `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (the degradation table around :298)

S5 removes the cursorless case. What is left is a delta *with* a cursor that is
still large — a source Usher has not reached for a month, a library the owner
re-scanned (PRD 03 records `LibraryChanged` naming thousands of items, and
`push_max_items_per_event` defers exactly that to a delta,
`src/usher/config.py:660-667`), or a `deferred_to_delta` outcome arriving on a busy
evening. PRD 03's measured shape for this server: a **30-day** cursor returned
**28,934** items for the item lane and 29,005 for the watch lane
(`docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md:266-271`) — 145 pages, which at S1's measured
page cost is the number this ceiling has to be chosen against rather than guessed.

🔴 **The trap, and it is why this is its own task: bounding the walk and letting
the run complete loses items permanently.** `latest_completed_cursor` is
`started_at` of the newest **completed** run in the lane
(`src/usher/db/repositories/sync.py:54-59`), so a delta that stopped at a ceiling
and recorded `COMPLETED` advances the cursor to its own start instant, and
**everything past the ceiling is never requested again by any delta**. The cover
would be the nightly full reconcile — which `_cursor_for` correctly makes
cursorless (`reconcile.py:201-202`) — except that **nothing in `src/` schedules
anything**: M9's boundary call 6 (`docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:718-723`) says so, the
M10 spec puts a scheduler in Phase 2 with two registered jobs neither of which is a
reconcile, and `usher sync --kind` defaults to `full` (`src/usher/cli.py:1616`)
precisely because a human runs it. **So on a shipped deployment with no cron, a
truncated-and-completed delta is a hole with no closer.**

The zero-DDL answer: a bounded run records **`FAILED`** with a named `error`, which
`_cursor_for` already ignores. Two things make that honest rather than a lie:
`ReconcileService.reconcile` never raises and already turns a `UsherPortError` into
a `FAILED` row with `str(exc)` in `sync_runs.error` (`reconcile.py:158-177`); and
**the items the walk did see are not lost**, because `_flush` commits per batch
(`reconcile.py:230-247`, the commit at `:245`, *"a crash costs the batch in flight,
never the walk"*). So a ceiling costs the cursor advance and nothing else. A fourth
`SyncRunStatus` member would say it better and is DDL (`src/usher/domain/sync.py:51-53`),
which the spec's data-model section closes — named as a candidate, not taken.

**And the ceiling may not be spelled as `max_pages`.** `MAX_PAGES = 10_000`
already exists (`src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py:217`, loop at `:429`) as a
dead-man's switch against a server that ignores `StartIndex`, and exhausting it
raises `PortDataMalformed` (`:465-468`) — *"Emby's item listing never ended; the
server appears to ignore StartIndex"*. Reusing it would report a deliberate,
correct stop as a broken upstream, in the one message an operator acts on, and
`CLAUDE.md`'s live-verification rule already forbids the shape for the same reason.
The ceiling is counted in **items** in the reconciler's own loop, where the count
already exists (`run.items_seen`, `reconcile.py:238`).

**Failing test first:** `tests/integration/test_services_reconcile.py::test_a_delta_that_hits_its_ceiling_records_failed_so_the_next_delta_does_not_skip_what_it_missed`
— a fake source with 500 items past the cursor and a ceiling of 200; asserts the
run is `FAILED` with a ceiling-shaped `error`, that **200 items are committed and
readable**, and then — the arm the whole task is about — runs a **second** delta
and asserts it re-requests from the *original* cursor rather than from the
truncated run's `started_at`. At HEAD no ceiling exists, so the case fails on the
first assertion; against the tempting implementation (bound + `COMPLETED`) it
passes the first two and fails the third, which is the only one that can tell them
apart. Real Postgres rather than the fake arm, because the property under test is
`latest_completed_cursor`'s `WHERE status = 'completed'` and a dict has no such
predicate.

**Acceptance:**
- `USHER_PUSH_GAP_MAX_ITEMS` on `Settings` with a comment that derives its default
  from PRD 03's measured 30-day delta (28,934 items) and S1's page cost, and states
  the trade in one sentence: **a ceiling buys a bounded startup and costs a
  reconcile an operator must run.** `ge=0`, zero meaning unlimited, matching
  `push_gap_min_interval_seconds`' own deliberate exception (`config.py:668-674`).
  In `.env.example`, or `test_env_example_documents_every_setting` (:282) is red.
- The ceiling applies to the **gap-closer's** delta and not to `usher sync --kind
  delta`, which is an operator asking for the whole thing — the same split S5 makes,
  pinned by a case in `tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py`.
- An operator-visible line at **WARNING** when it fires, naming the source, the
  items seen, the ceiling, and `usher sync --kind full` — and PRD 08's degradation
  table (`:298`) gains the row, because a bounded delta is a documented degradation
  and PRD 08 is where those live.
- The `error` string is distinguishable from `MAX_PAGES`' message by a machine, not
  only by a human: asserted by a case, so a later reader cannot collapse the two.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the ceiling recorded as
  `COMPLETED` (fails **only** the third arm above — which is the measurement that
  says that arm is carrying the task); the ceiling compared `>=` instead of `>`
  (off by one batch); the ceiling applied to the *watch* lane as well as the item
  lane without saying so. The equivalent-mutant control is the two independent
  `span.set_attribute` calls in `reconcile` swapped — a fact about the code (an
  OTel span's attributes are a map and both are side-effect-free reads), the shape
  already measured in `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`.

### Task S7 — the concurrency numbers, measured: four entries at 4, two justifications, and a test named for measurements that does not assert any of them

**Depends on:** S3, and **group O** (the observability stack — the measuring is the point)   **Files:** `/var/tmp/m10-gate/BAR-S7.md` (the bar, written first), `scripts/measure_source_lane.py` (new, or an arm of S1's harness), `src/usher/services/jobs.py:118-177`, `docs/prd/01-architecture.md` (the concurrency table at :275-320), `.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md` (the W1 entry's per-kind table), `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md`, `tests/unit/test_config.py:223-266`

**Which entries the issue means, counted rather than recalled — and both the spec
and the rules file get it wrong.** `KIND_CONCURRENCY` (`src/usher/services/jobs.py:166-177`)
has **nine** entries, one per `JobKind`. **Four of them are `4`**: `MATCH`,
`WATCH_HISTORY`, `WATCH_WRITEBACK` and `DERIVE`. They are covered by **two**
justification bullets in the comment block above — `:135-140` for the three
Emby-facing kinds (*"a **household** media server, not a CDN-backed public API, and
**this repository has never measured one under concurrent load**"*) and `:156-165`
for `derive` (*"⚠️ **The one number here that is not measured**"*). So:

- The M10 spec's *"Two entries from W1 are bounds presented as numbers"*
  (`docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:98`) is **two justifications and
  four entries**.
- `.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md`'s W1 entry says *"Two of the eight are
  bounds rather than measurements"* — wrong twice: there are **nine** kinds and
  **four** unmeasured entries.
- 🔴 **`derive`'s own comment says it is "the one number here that is not
  measured", eleven lines below a bullet admitting the same thing about three other
  kinds** — and `docs/prd/01-architecture.md:311-316` carries the mirror-image
  contradiction, calling the `match`/`watch_*` row *"the one number here that is a
  bound rather than a measurement"* while its own table two rows down describes
  `derive` as *"the pool's share, not a measured throughput"* (`:284`). Two
  documents each naming a different single unmeasured number, while both flag both.
  Corrected in this task, in both places, to *four entries and two reasons*.

🔴 **And the case that would have caught it is named for the claim it does not
make.** `tests/unit/test_config.py:223`'s
`test_the_worker_concurrency_settings_have_the_measured_defaults` asserts
`set(KIND_CONCURRENCY) == set(JobKind)` and pins `ENRICH`, `INDEX`, `CURATE` and
`BOOTSTRAP` by value (`:262-265`) — **and asserts nothing at all about `MATCH`,
`WATCH_HISTORY`, `WATCH_WRITEBACK`, `DERIVE` or `SYNC`.** The four entries the
issue is about are exactly the four the "measured defaults" case leaves unpinned,
so any of them can be changed to any value and the suite stays green. This is D4's
`TICKET_TTL_SECONDS` and B9's `CAST_LIMIT` finding a third time, in its weakest
form: the constant is not merely pinned by a derived assertion, it is **not pinned
at all**.

**This is a measurement task and it follows the house rule.** The bar — the
predictions, the bands, and what would count as *failure* rather than *refutation*
— goes to **`/var/tmp/m10-gate/BAR-S7.md`** before the first request, with its
`sha256` recorded and re-hashed by the harness at run time
(`scripts/measure_worker_lane.py:20-25`). ⚠️ **The dispatch said `/tmp/m10-gate/BAR.md`
and that is corrected here**: `/tmp` on this host is tmpfs, so a reboot erases the
proof that the bar predates the numbers, which is the only property a bar has —
`CLAUDE.md` states the rule and records that M9's B3 broke it. The write-up is
**guess-by-guess with refutations first**, and what is committed is **the
measurement, not the data**.

**What is measured, and the two halves are different questions.** `derive` is pure
Postgres — a JSONB read and three writes, no network, no model — so it is measured
against a **throwaway `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`** at 1, 2, 4 and 8 in flight on one
pool, which is the run `jobs.py:163-165` names and nobody has run. **Zero live
requests.** The three Emby-facing kinds are measured against **the operator's real
server**, and every rule in this group applies: read-only single-item reads
(`op=get_item`) over ids `media_items` already holds, at 1, 2 and 4 in flight,
**≤ 150 requests total**, no walk, no write, `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false` and
`USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false`, driven from outside the tree.

**The success criterion is not a throughput number**, for W1's own reason
(`.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md`, the W1 entry): a faster number does not
distinguish *"the pool got better"* from *"the box got faster"*. The question is
**what four concurrent requests cost the server relative to one** — per-request
latency at 1, 2 and 4, and whether the p95 degrades. W1 measured a **37%
per-worker throughput loss** going from one worker to three against TMDb and
per-worker throughput *rising* when a worker died; if a household server shows the
same shape, the polite number is smaller than 4 and the measurement says so.
**Observed overlap, not a count** — peak concurrent in-flight requests and the
intersection-over-union of the request windows, `CLAUDE.md`'s fourth evidence rule,
because "four jobs finished" is also what a serialised loop produces. Group O's
Prometheus reading of `usher.source.request.duration` is the second instrument, and
the two must agree.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_config.py::test_the_four_concurrency_entries_that_are_bounds_are_pinned_by_value_and_say_which_measurement_moved_them`
— asserts `KIND_CONCURRENCY[MATCH] == KIND_CONCURRENCY[WATCH_HISTORY] ==
KIND_CONCURRENCY[WATCH_WRITEBACK]` and each equals the **literal** this task
settles, likewise `DERIVE`, likewise `SYNC == 1`; every number a literal and none
derived from `KIND_CONCURRENCY` itself, which is the tell the three prior instances
of this bug shared. At HEAD it fails on the first entry, because nothing pins it —
which is the point, and the red is the discovery rather than a regression. Its
positive control is that the existing case at `:223` still passes unchanged, so the
two are demonstrably about different entries.

**Acceptance:**
- Each of the four entries is either **moved with a measurement** or **kept with a
  recorded decision naming the run and its denominator**, which is exactly what
  issue #13 asks (*"each has a measurement behind it, or a recorded decision that
  the bound is correct and why"*). The number is written **with its denominator**,
  which the M10 spec makes Phase 1's gate condition.
- The comment block at `src/usher/services/jobs.py:118-165` no longer contains two
  sentences each claiming to be about *the* one unmeasured number, and
  `docs/prd/01-architecture.md:275-320` matches it clause for clause. The rules
  file's *"two of the eight"* is corrected to four of nine.
- The live half's write-up carries: the window (`HH:MM:SSZ → HH:MM:SSZ`), the
  statement that the server was idle from the last request, the exact request count
  against the ≤ 150 budget, the status distribution, and **peak in-flight plus
  IoU** per setting. A sequential control run (concurrency 1) is reported beside
  them, because a limiter or a lane that never overlapped is the reading that
  invalidates the other two.
- **The quiet-check is the imported one**, and a run that fails it is discarded and
  said to have been discarded — W1 discarded a baseline for two foreign `pytest`
  processes from a sibling worktree at load average 7.04, which is the failure mode
  the census exists for. This box runs fourteen containers and a compositor; the
  check is not ceremony here.
- What is **still** unverified is named rather than implied: any other Emby build,
  this server under a *paging* load rather than single-item reads, and N > 1
  processes.

### Task S8 — the retraction guard on a library nobody here owns, and a one-request probe that answers it without a walk

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/services/reconcile.py`, `src/usher/ports/ingest.py` (`SweepResult` at :232-244), `src/usher/db/repositories/media_item.py:471-490`, `src/usher/telemetry.py`, `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (one metric row), `tests/contract/media_item_repository_contract.py`, `tests/integration/test_services_reconcile.py`, `scripts/measure_source_drift.py` (new)

ADR-0015's guard refuses a sweep that would retract more than
`sync_max_retract_fraction` of a source, default **0.25**
(`src/usher/config.py:217`, `ge=0.0`, `le=1.0`), overridable per run through
`build_pipeline(max_retract_fraction=…)` (`src/usher/composition.py:474-477`) and
from the CLI as `usher sync --allow-full-retraction` → `1.0`
(`src/usher/cli.py:338`, the flag at `:1617-1624`). ⚠️ **The spec locates the guard
at `src/usher/ports/repository/media_item.py:136`, and that is the port's
docstring** (`:135-165`); the code that raises is
`PostgresMediaItemRepository.mark_unseen_unavailable` at
`src/usher/db/repositories/media_item.py:471-490`, and the refusal is the four
lines at `:482-485` — a **count comparison rather than a division**, so an empty
source does not divide by zero and a run with nothing to retract never consults
the ceiling at all.

**Two things about how the refusal reaches a human, and both are quieter than the
ADR implies.** `AvailabilitySweepRefused` (`src/usher/ports/ingest.py:253`) is a
`UsherPortError`, so `ReconcileService.reconcile`'s `except UsherPortError`
(`reconcile.py:158`) turns it into a `FAILED` `sync_runs` row plus one ERROR log
line and **never raises** — the method's own docstring promises exactly that
(`:131`). So it never reaches the CLI boundary at all, which is precisely why
`src/usher/cli.py:141-145` keeps it out of `OPERATOR_ERRORS`: *"no measured path
reaches this boundary with one, and ADR-0026 asks for evidence per family before
the tuple grows."* **If Phase 1 measures the guard firing on a shared server, that
sentence stops being true and the tuple has its evidence** — which is S9's
decision, not this task's.

🔴 **And one sentence of ADR-0015 is false against the code, stated narrowly.**
Its *Decision* section argues for measuring what a run would change rather than
what is already unavailable, *"because a refused sweep fails the sync run and the
**upsert** half of the next walk never commits either."* The upsert half **does**
commit: `ReconcileService._flush` commits per batch (`reconcile.py:230-247`, the
commit at `:245`, *"a crash costs the batch in flight, never the walk"*), and the
sweep runs after the whole walk returned. What a refusal actually costs is the
retraction and the run's `COMPLETED` status — and therefore that run's contribution
to the delta cursor (`db/repositories/sync.py:54-59`). The ADR's *conclusion* is
unaffected; the reason given for it is wrong and is corrected in place rather than
left standing.

**The bounded probe, which is the part that makes this a Phase 1 task rather than
a wait for a churn event.** Issue #20 asks for measurement *"across at least one
genuine churn event"*, which nobody can schedule. But the guard's numerator and
denominator can be **estimated in one request**: the adapter already sends
`EnableTotalRecordCount=true` on every page (`src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py:438`)
and reads `TotalRecordCount` back purely as a walk terminator (`:459-464`), then
discards it. One request with `Limit=1` therefore answers *"how many items does
this source hold right now"* for zero walking, and comparing it against
`count(media_items WHERE source_id = … AND available)` gives the drift a full walk
would have to retract. ⚠️ **It is a necessary condition and not a sufficient one,
and the write-up must say so**: a count is not a set, so an owner who removed 300
items and added 300 shows zero drift and would still trip a 0.25 ceiling on a real
walk. It bounds the guard from **below**, which is the direction that matters for
"does this fire" — and it costs ≤ 6 requests (one per `IncludeItemTypes` scope) run
daily by an operator for as long as they like.

**Failing test first:** `tests/integration/test_services_reconcile.py::test_a_refused_sweep_reports_both_numbers_where_an_operator_can_see_them`
— a source seeded so a full walk would retract 40% against a 0.25 ceiling; asserts
the run is `FAILED`, that the refusal's message carries **`would_retract`,
`total` and `ceiling`** (all three already on `AvailabilitySweepRefused`), and that
the new counter recorded the fraction. At HEAD the exception carries the numbers
and **nothing publishes them**: `sync_runs.items_retracted` stores only the
numerator (`src/usher/ports/ingest.py:239`) and there is no retraction series
anywhere in PRD 10's 34-row catalogue (`:143-177`). Positive control first:
`assert result_before.retracted == 0 and count_available_after == count_available_before, "a sweep that changed nothing is what a refusal and a no-op both look like"` —
the case asserts the ceiling was genuinely approached before believing the refusal.

**Acceptance:**
- **`usher.sync.retraction.fraction`**, a histogram labelled `source` and
  `outcome` (`swept` | `refused`), recorded on **every** finished full walk
  including the ones that retract nothing — because *"this library shed nothing"*
  and *"this guard never ran"* are the same silence, which is
  `.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md`'s *"a filter is invisible without a
  counter"* arriving at a sweep. One row added to PRD 10's table in the same
  commit as the emitter.
- `scripts/measure_source_drift.py` prints, per source and with its request count:
  the source's live `TotalRecordCount`, Usher's available count, the drift as a
  fraction, and **the ceiling it would have hit**. ≤ 6 live requests, read-only, no
  walk, refuses past its budget. Its docstring states the count-is-not-a-set caveat
  in its own words rather than deferring to this plan.
- The first live reading is recorded in `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md`
  with its date and the sample (*one household, one reading*), and explicitly as a
  **lower bound** on what a walk would retract.
- ADR-0015 gains an *Evidence* paragraph with the reading and a corrected
  *Decision* sentence, per the finding above. The ADR's status does not change —
  this is evidence added and a wrong reason removed, not a reversal.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the counter published only on a
  refusal (invisible to every case that seeds a refusal — the zeros are the whole
  point, and one case has to seed a clean sweep); `outcome` collapsed to one label;
  the fraction computed from `retracted` rather than from `would_retract` on the
  refusal path (they differ only when the guard fires, which is the one state the
  metric exists for).

### Task S9 — a view of somebody else's library is not a mirror of your own, and the retraction default has to say which one it assumes

**Depends on:** S8   **Files:** `docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md`, `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md`, `src/usher/config.py:213-217`, `src/usher/cli.py:141-160`, `README.md`, `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py`

Issue #20's *"possibly the more interesting half"* is a modelling claim and the
PRD does not currently make it: **on a shared server the library is not a stable
object Usher is mirroring — it is another person's, and Usher's catalog is a view
of it at a point in time.** Several behaviours read differently under the two
readings, and all three are already built: retraction (ADR-0015's ceiling assumes
the operator authorises removals), availability (PRD 08's *"availability goes
stale, not wrong"* assumes staleness is the only failure), and watch write-back
(M9's D7/D8 write to an account on a server the operator does not administer —
H5 restored its test write **byte-for-byte** precisely because of that).

**The decision this task makes, with S8's reading as its evidence.** Three
outcomes are legitimate and the issue says so: confirm the default, move it, or
design a shared-source mode. **A shared-source *mode* is out** — it is a
`Source`-level configuration and `Source` has ten fields, none of them tuning
(`src/usher/domain/source.py:16-25`), so it is DDL the spec's data-model section
closes. What is in scope is the **default**, the **documentation**, and **whether
the refusal reaches a human**.

**The third of those is the one with a mechanism, and S8 establishes its
premise.** A refused sweep is a `FAILED` `sync_runs` row and an ERROR log line and
nothing else (`reconcile.py:158-177`); it never reaches `usher sync`'s exit status
because `reconcile` never raises. `usher sync-status` reports recent runs
(`src/usher/cli.py:1625`), so the row is *reachable* — but an operator who does not
run it sees a sync that printed nothing wrong and a catalog whose availability
silently stopped being updated. **On a shared server that is the expected steady
state rather than an incident**, which is the whole difference the two readings
make. If S8's reading shows the guard would fire, `AvailabilitySweepRefused` has
the *"evidence per family"* ADR-0026 asks for and joins `cli.OPERATOR_ERRORS`
(`src/usher/cli.py:151-160`) — and `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py::test_the_port_taxonomy_is_split_and_the_base_class_is_not_in_the_tuple`
reads the set off `__subclasses__()`, so the addition is a decision that cannot
arrive silently. If the reading shows it would not, the tuple stays as it is and
the sentence at `cli.py:141-145` gains the date of the reading that confirmed it.
**Either way the outcome is recorded, which is what issue #20 asks for.**

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py::test_a_refused_sweep_is_reported_at_the_boundary_the_operator_actually_watches`
— drives `usher sync` against a pipeline whose sweep refuses, and asserts the
command's own output names the two numbers and the flag, with a **non-zero exit
status** if and only if S9's decision is to promote it. At HEAD the command prints
its ordinary per-source report and exits 0, because the refusal was absorbed three
layers down. ⚠️ **The case is written against whichever branch S9 decides**, and
the decision is made from S8's reading before the case is written — writing the
case first and then choosing the branch that makes it pass is how a measurement
becomes a curve fit.

**Acceptance:**
- ADR-0015 gains a *Consequences* paragraph naming the shared-library case
  explicitly, in the vocabulary PRD 03 adopts: an owned library and a **view** of
  somebody else's, and which of the two the 0.25 default assumes.
- PRD 03 gains the distinction once, near the sync contract, and PRD 08's failure
  table gains the row for *a refused sweep on a source that churns*, with what an
  operator does about it.
- `README.md` states it, because the M10 spec's Phase 3 requires the README to
  stop being silent about postures a stranger cannot infer — and *"pointing this at
  a server you do not own is the intended deployment"* is exactly such a posture.
  One sentence, not a section.
- The `sync_max_retract_fraction` default either **moves with the reading behind
  it** or **stays with the reading behind it**. A number that stayed because nobody
  looked and a number that stayed because somebody measured are the same value and
  different claims; the comment at `src/usher/config.py:213-216` says which.
- No schema change. The per-source override is named as the post-v1 candidate with
  its column spelled out, and the reason it is not taken here.
