### Task S10 — the leaked adapter that keeps reporting, and the `supports_push` claim above it is a counterfactual read as a fact

> ⚠️ **Moved from Group F to Phase 1 on 2026-08-14, and the reason is the
> subject rather than the tidiness.** A `LaneSupervisor` that never pops a
> finished lane leaks an `EmbyAdapter` **holding a session against a server
> this deployment does not own**, for the process lifetime — which is the same
> class as #19 and #9, not a failure-mode gap. Two consequences follow. It now
> sits in the phase that gates everything after it; and it lands beside S5/S6,
> which edit the *same file* (`api/lanes.py`, `_close_gap`), so both edits to
> that module are inside one phase and one serialisation rather than
> straddling a gate. **F2 still depends on it** and now depends across a phase
> boundary, which is strictly stronger than the within-phase edge it had.


**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** the `Push socket drops` row at `:298`), `docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md` (**only** the failure-mode bullet at `:155–157`), `src/usher/api/lanes.py:298–323` (`refresh`) and `:247–255` (`crashed_sources`), `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py`

**The spec is wrong and this task's first deliverable is saying so.**
`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:155–157` reads *"the counter resets on
delivery, not on connection, so a documented degradation path is
unreachable."* PRD 08's actual sentence at `:298` is a **conditional about the
design that was not taken**: *"The failure counter resets on delivery, not on
connection — a proxy that upgrades and then buffers connects perfectly every
time, so a counter reset **by connecting** never reaches the ceiling and this
row silently never fires."* The clause after the dash is the argument **for**
resetting on delivery, not a description of what shipping code does. The spec
read the premise as the conclusion.

**The code does what PRD 08 says and it is covered.** `services/push.py:379`
initialises `failures = 0`; `:394–403` resets it **only inside the
`async for event` loop and only `if delivering`**, with a nine-line comment
naming the buffering proxy; `:427` and `:442` increment on a `UsherPortError`
and on an iterator that ends quietly; `:381`'s `while failures <
self._max_failures` exits at the ceiling and `:458` calls
`_set_push_available(source, False)`, which `api/lanes.py:413–434` turns into
`sources.supports_push = false` plus a commit. `config.py:659` is
`push_max_consecutive_failures: int = Field(default=5, ge=1)`, quoting PRD 08's
sentence at `:655–658`. `tests/unit/test_services_push.py:832–846` drives the
whole path — `assert lane.push_available == [False]` with `sleeps == [5.0,
10.0]`, *"the last failure hits the ceiling and must not sleep"* — and M5's
final sweep recorded the inverse mutation as the milestone's headline plant:
**moving `failures = 0` from delivery to connection fails 4 cases**
(`.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`). A path with a passing end-to-end case and
a killed inverse mutation is not an unreachable path.

**What *is* wrong is one row down, and it is a resource leak plus a metric that
outlives its lane.** `LaneSupervisor.refresh` (`lanes.py:316–323`) stops a lane
only when its source has gone or been disabled (`if source_id not in wanted`)
and starts one only when the source has no entry at all (`if source_id not in
self._lanes`). **A finished task is never popped**, so a lane that reached the
ceiling leaves (a) a completed `asyncio.Task` in `self._lanes` forever, (b) its
`SourceAdapter` — an `EmbyAdapter` holding a live `httpx.AsyncClient` — in
`self._open_adapters` for the process lifetime, and (c) that adapter still
feeding `push_snapshots()` (`lanes.py:273–287`), which `api/app.py:116` hands to
`register_push_gauges`. So `usher.source.push.delivering` keeps publishing a
series for a lane that stopped existing, which is **the series PRD 10's "Push
down" alert reads** — one of the seven alerts Phase 2 ships. `refresh`'s own
docstring at `:299–300` says it drops the lanes of sources that have *"gone,
been disabled, or crashed"*; the third clause is false, and it is the one that
matters here. `crashed_sources()` (`:247–255`) computes exactly this state and
**has no caller in `src/`** — `grep -rn crashed_sources src/ tests/` finds the
definition and three assertions in `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py`. Its docstring
says it exists *"so a case can tell…"*, which is an accurate description of a
seam with no product on the other side, and the same shape as `build_cipher`'s.

**The lane is deliberately not restarted and that stays.** PRD 08's remedy is
*"lean on the nightly walk"*; a `refresh` that replaced a finished lane would
reconnect forever against the proxy the ceiling exists for. What changes is that
the adapter is released and the state is named. Releasing it is also what makes
`GET /admin/sources/{id}/status` honest: `push_available()` (`:289–294`) answers
`None` — *"not probed"* — when no adapter is open, which is the truthful answer
for a source this process has stopped watching, against today's `False` read off
a dead ledger.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py::test_a_lane_that_reached_the_failure_ceiling_releases_its_adapter_and_is_named_as_stopped`
— drives a supervisor whose lane task completes (the existing `_drain(lambda:
supervisor.crashed_sources() == ["A"])` idiom at `test_api_lanes.py:697` is the
premise, asserted first so a lane that never finished cannot pass the case),
then calls `refresh()` with the source still **enabled** and asserts the
adapter's `aclose()` was called exactly once, that it is gone from the
push-gauge snapshot, and that the source is still not restarted. At HEAD all
three fail: `refresh` returns without touching either dict, so `aclose` is
called zero times and `push_snapshots()` still names the source. Positive
control: the same assertions run against a *live* lane must show the adapter
open and the source in the snapshot, because a `refresh` that closed everything
would pass the first half of this case and break push entirely.

**Acceptance:**
- `docs/prd/08-operations.md:298` is rewritten so the counterfactual cannot be
  read as a statement about shipping code — the reset rule, then *"if it reset
  on connection instead"*, then the consequence — and it names
  `tests/unit/test_services_push.py`'s ceiling case and M5's 4-case sweep
  result as the evidence that the row fires. The spec's `:155–157` bullet is
  struck and replaced with the correction, in this plan's own diff, because a
  spec is a historical record and the plan is where the refutation belongs.
- `refresh`'s docstring at `lanes.py:299–300` no longer claims to drop crashed
  lanes, or it does and the code matches; the two must agree and the choice is
  named in the commit body.
- `crashed_sources()` has a caller in `src/` after F2 lands, and this task's
  acceptance says so rather than adding a second surface: S10 releases the
  resource, F2 reports the state.
- `uv run pytest tests/unit` collects and passes at least the **4,067** cases
  collected at HEAD (measured 2026-08-13) plus the new ones.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: `failures = 0` moved from the
  `if delivering` arm to just after `async with adapter.events()` must fail
  **4** cases (M5 measured it; re-measure against today's suite and record the
  number, because a survivor list is only true of the selection it ran
  against); `self._lanes.pop(source_id)` deleted from the new release path must
  fail the new case alone; the equivalent-mutant control is swapping the two
  independent `dict.pop` calls in `_stop_lane` (`lanes.py:384–390`) — measured
  against all five gate steps, not against `pytest` alone.

**Risks:**
- **Closing the adapter changes what `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status` reports
  for a degraded source, from `false` to `null`.** That is the honest answer and
  it is a wire change; `tests/unit/test_api_sources.py` and PRD 07's Admin table
  have to agree with it in the same commit or the fix ships a second untruth.
- A `refresh` that pops a *live* lane's adapter is the loudest possible
  regression and the only thing separating it from this change is the
  `task.done()` predicate. The positive control above is what makes that a red
  rather than a review comment.

### Task S11 — the Phase 1 gate: five fixes, one live server, bounded requests, no walk

**Depends on:** S1–S9   **Files:** `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` (the sweep ledger), `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md`, `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (the status paragraph only)

**The standard gate, every step green on a fully restored tree**, with the numbers
quoted rather than asserted to have passed:

- `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .` — and `[tool.ruff]
  extend-exclude = ["docs"]` is why an unscoped `ruff format .` does not rewrite
  this plan's own prose.
- `uv run mypy src tests`, strict, over the file count it reports.
- `uv run lint-imports` — **10 kept, 0 broken**, measured at HEAD `3adb5b0` on
  2026-08-13. A different number is a finding, not a rounding.
- `uv run pytest` — at least the **4,071** unit cases collected at HEAD (measured
  2026-08-13) plus everything this group added, and the integration suite, with
  both counts quoted. ⚠️ **`tests/integration/test_rows_refresh.py::test_the_route_serves_stale_and_the_refresh_runs_on_a_session_of_its_own`
  is intermittent under whole-suite load** — 2 failures in 7 runs, measured by M9's
  H7 and reproduced the same day (`docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:1032-1078`) — and it is
  deselected **by node id for a mutation sweep and by nothing else**, because a
  sweep scored on *"did the run fail"* cannot run against a suite holding a flaky
  case. 🔴 **Do not inherit `test_sse_end_to_end.py`'s deselection**: H7 measured it
  green 5 of 5 and absent from all fifteen sweep runs' failure lists, and *a
  deselection inherited from a ledger is a deselection nobody measured*.
- The PRD link check, scoped to `docs/prd/` plus the two root documents and **not**
  `docs/plans/` — the correction `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md` records, because
  scoped to all of `docs/` it never once printed `OK` in four milestones.
- A mutation sweep for the phase, logged to `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` as a
  **three-way split** — behavioural targets killed, equivalent-mutant controls
  surviving as designed, unintended survivors — with every control measured against
  **all five gate steps separately**, the harness **outside the working tree**
  (V1's finding: `ruff check .` and `mypy src tests` walk the whole repository and a
  harness at the root makes every control read FAIL), the three `.pyc` defences in
  force, the landing check spelled as **byte equality with the intended mutant**
  (F3's repair — this phase has additive and multi-hunk plants and B6's substring
  form is wrong for both), and `git status` asserted clean after every plant.

**And Phase 1's own condition, which only this phase can prove: all four issues
verified against the live shared Emby, in bounded requests, with no walk.** The
discipline is M9's H4/H5, which did the whole playback and write-back chain in
**23 bounded requests** and is worth reading before this is attempted
(`.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:100-180`): a throwaway driver outside the
tree reading the operator's own secrets file; the token swap in a
**`sitecustomize.py` on `PYTHONPATH`** rather than an in-process monkeypatch,
because a subprocess is the thing under test and a patched parent cannot reach it;
**a marker file the caller asserts on**, because *a plant that did not land looks
exactly like a check that passed*; base URL, token, user id and device id redacted
from everything printed; and every write recorded before and restored after,
confirmed by reading it back rather than by the suite going green.

The four, each with what would count as a pass and each ≤ 10 requests:

1. **#19** — the shipped app, `USHER_SOURCE_REQUESTS_PER_SECOND` set deliberately
   low, issuing N single-item reads: the observed inter-request spacing matches
   `1/rate` and `usher.source.throttle.wait` shows a non-zero p50 in Grafana. **The
   control is a second run at rate 0**, where the spacing collapses and the
   histogram reads zero — a limiter that cannot be seen to *not* bind has not been
   observed binding.
2. **#9** — the app started with `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=true` against the real source,
   which is the thing nobody has ever been able to do safely. Either the WARNING
   fires and **no walk begins** (a fresh source), or the delta runs and stops at
   S6's ceiling with a `FAILED` run naming it. The evidence is the request count:
   **≤ 10, against 5,634 pages if the fix is absent.** This is the single most
   valuable observation in the phase, because it is the exact scenario every rule
   in this project has been working around by turning the lanes off.
3. **#13** — S7's numbers restated with their denominators, and the entries pinned
   by value.
4. **#20** — S8's drift probe run once against the live server, ≤ 6 requests, with
   the reading recorded and S9's decision pointing at it.

**Failing test first:** the gate has no new test of its own and this is the
sentence that says so deliberately. What it has instead is **a pre-registered
checklist at `/var/tmp/m10-gate/GATE-S.md`, written and hashed before the live
session opens**, naming the four observations, the per-observation request budget,
and — for each — *what result would mean the phase is not done*. A gate whose
success criteria are written after the run is a report, not a gate.

**Acceptance:**
- The live session's total is **≤ 40 requests**, printed by the driver, quoted with
  its window and the statement that the server was idle from the last one, and the
  status distribution given whole.
- Issues **#19, #9 and #13** are closed on the strength of a measurement each, and
  **#20** is closed on the strength of a recorded decision plus a reading — which is
  the outcome that issue explicitly permits.
- `CLAUDE.md`'s status paragraph gains one row's worth of truth: Phase 1 done,
  what it measured, and — the part the M9 gap taught this project —
  **what it did not**.
- Every number this group produced is in a rules file with its date and its sample,
  never laundered into a constant, and every default that moved carries the
  arithmetic that moved it. **What is still unverified is named rather than
  implied**: any Emby build but 4.9.5.0, a real 429 from anywhere, this server
  under a paging load, and N > 1 Usher processes against one source — which is the
  one bound this whole group cannot express, because every limiter here is per
  process.
## Group J — the one migration this milestone gets, and the one new component

Phase 2. Seven tasks: the single `m10a` revision, the suggest analytics writer
the PRD 10 amendment asks for, an ADR, the scheduler, its two registrations,
and the one overnight run that cannot happen inside any of them.

**Four numbers in the spec are wrong and are corrected here rather than
carried.** Each was re-measured on 2026-08-13 against this host's live
`usher-postgres-1` (1,272,401 titles, 130,720 embeddings, 3,268,000 neighbour
rows) or against the tree at HEAD:

1. **Head is `m09f`, not `m09d`/`m09e`.** `uv run alembic heads` prints
   `m09f (head)`, `tests/unit/test_db_migration_status.py:12` pins
   `code_head_revision() == "m09f"`, and the chain is
   `m08b → m09a → m09c → m09d → m09e → m09f` — **there is no `m09b`**; it was
   pre-allocated in M9's first drafting pass and never minted, exactly as
   Group M's own header said would happen to `m09c` (it was minted; `m09b` was
   not). So `m10a`'s `down_revision` is `"m09f"`.
2. **The neighbour rebuild is 3.33 hours, not 21.6.** The 594.7 ms/seed figure
   the spec quotes is `m09e`'s cost, and `m09f` — the commit the spec's own
   `m09f` paragraph cites — is what repaired it. The completed walk ran
   2026-08-13 18:31:26Z → 21:51:07Z: **130,720 seeds, 3,268,000 rows,
   11,981 s, 91.7 ms/seed** (`.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md:1241-1242`,
   and confirmed independently here from `min(computed_at)`/`max(computed_at)`
   on the live table). `docs/prd/08-operations.md:666` still carries the 21.6
   and is corrected by J7. **The overnight task survives the correction** — a
   3.33-hour job is still not runnable inside a normal task — but every
   sentence in this group that prices it uses 3.33 h.
3. **The neighbour table is *not* stale at HEAD.** See the block below; this is
   the fact the spec could not have had, because the rebuild that discharged it
   was run from the working tree before `e2241a9` was committed.
4. **"Nothing in `src/` schedules anything" is very slightly too strong**, and
   the precise version is what J4 is built on: nothing runs a **named job on a
   period**. Two periodic loops do exist —
   `LaneSupervisor._refresh_loop` (`src/usher/api/lanes.py:325-339`, sleeping
   `push_source_refresh_seconds`, default 60) and the worker lane's idle poll
   (`:595`) with its lease-throttled `requeue_running`. Both are *pollers for a
   condition*, neither has a job name, a period a job declares, or a
   resumption story. The scheduler is new; the `asyncio` shape it copies is not.

### The neighbour table at HEAD, measured rather than assumed

The question the spec could not answer: `e2241a9` added `embedding_model` to
`blend_fingerprint`'s payload (`src/usher/services/similar.py:254-258`), and per
ADR-0020 a fingerprint change makes every stored row read stale. **It did not,
here, because the rebuild that repaired it had already been run from the
working tree before the commit landed.** Measured 2026-08-13 against
`usher-postgres-1`:

| | |
|---|---|
| `title_neighbors` rows | **3,268,000** (130,720 distinct seeds × 25 — a complete walk) |
| distinct `blend_fingerprint` values | **one**: `a7013154c014e0ff1b60ef5d8534a115` |
| `blend_fingerprint(embedding_model="openai:BAAI/bge-m3")` at HEAD | **`a7013154c014e0ff1b60ef5d8534a115`** |
| `title_embeddings.model_name` | **`openai:BAAI/bge-m3`** on all 130,720 rows |
| `alembic_version` | `m09f` |

So the stored digest is the **four-key** one, not the `78f3ecd2…` three-key
digest H7 wrote (`docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:946`, `docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md:727-728`).
H7's 3,266,175 rows are gone — `m09e` deleted them — and what is in the table is
the post-`m09f` rebuild, which is also why its 91.7 ms/seed is the number to
plan against.

⚠️ **And it is fresh only for a process configured with that model string.**
`Settings.embedding_model`'s default is `fastembed:BAAI/bge-large-en-v1.5`
(`src/usher/config.py:352`), whose digest is `afd00fffaf6946c0d8dcf87faf966614`
— a different 32 hex characters, so a process reading the default would report
**all 3,268,000 rows stale** against embeddings that are all `bge-m3`. The
deployment's own `~/code/usher/.env` sets only `USHER_SECRET_KEY` and
`USHER_TMDB_API_KEY`; `USHER_EMBEDDING_MODEL` is supplied per-run from the
environment. This is `e2241a9`'s own "it takes the *configured* model rather
than the stored one" cost arriving on the first deployment to have both — and
it is a real hazard for J6, whose scheduled job would otherwise start a
3.33-hour rebuild every period on a correct table. **J6 carries the guard.**

**What this group deliberately does not deliver.** No dashboard, no alert, no
`LLMCallRepository` read method, and no retirement of
`test_the_cost_ledger_has_no_read_method` — J1 ships the two `llm_calls`
indexes and nothing that reads them, for the reason in J1's own text. No
backup manifest, no rotation, no runbook. No second migration: anything this
group cannot derive from PRD 02, 08 or 10 is **requested**, never minted as a
second head.

**Files shared with other groups, declared so the orchestrator can sequence
rather than discover:** `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (J1 owns the one
`-1` re-point in M10; any other group editing it is a merge conflict by
construction), `src/usher/composition.py` (J2, J4, J5, J6 — and four other
groups), `src/usher/api/lanes.py` (J4, and Phase 1's #9 task at `:401`),
`src/usher/cli.py` (J4, J5, J6, and the backup/rotate group),
`src/usher/telemetry.py` (J4, and the suggest-metrics task),
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (J1 and J2 touch the
`search_queries` block under `## Analytics tables`; J4 adds three metric rows
to the catalogue at `:136-177`).

**One cross-group edge, stated because the spec states it the other way.** The
spec's *Data model* says the two `llm_calls` indexes *"ship with the read method
and the panel that reads it"*. They cannot: this milestone gets one migration
and one head, and a reader task in another group authoring DDL is the serial
spine across groups that Group M's header exists to refuse
(`docs/plans/2026-08-10-m9-api-surface.md:598-609`). **The indexes ship in J1;
the reader task depends on J1.** Corrected here rather than left to be
discovered at the merge.

### Task J1 — `m10a`: two columns, three indexes, and the one whose reader is in another group

**Depends on:** nothing
**Files:** `src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m10a_suggest_surface_and_retention_indexes.py` (new),
`src/usher/db/models/analytics.py`, `src/usher/db/models/curation.py`,
`src/usher/ports/search.py`, `src/usher/services/search.py` (**only** the
`SuggestTier` declaration at `:218-241` and its import line),
`src/usher/api/dto/search.py` (`:36`), `src/usher/api/routers/search.py` (`:124`),
`src/usher/cli.py` (`:80`),
`tests/unit/test_db_migration_status.py` (`:12`),
`tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (the `-1` half at `:412-600`, docstring included),
`tests/integration/test_m10_schema.py` (new),
`tests/unit/test_db_models.py`, `docs/prd/02-data-model.md` (**only** the
`search_queries` and `llm_calls` rows), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`
(**only** the `## Analytics tables` block at `:498-648`),
`.claude/rules/db-and-sql.md`, `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`

`revision = "m10a"`, `down_revision = "m09f"`. `m09f` is verified head three
ways: `uv run alembic heads` prints `m09f (head)`,
`src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m09f_vectors_stay_inline.py:110-111` declares
`revision = "m09f"` / `down_revision = "m09e"` and nothing revises it, and
`tests/unit/test_db_migration_status.py:12` pins it. The id follows
`m08a_curation.py`'s convention — milestone-prefixed, **zero-padded to two
digits**, which that docstring argues for at length precisely because
`sorted(["m8a", "m9a", "m10a"])` puts `m10a` first. M10 is the milestone that
convention was written for; `m10a` sorts after `m09f` correctly.

**Four DDL items, and one of them is a correction to nothing — PRD 10 is right.**
`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:638-648` says `search_queries` has **no index on
`at`**, so an operator's pruning `DELETE` is a sequential scan. Verified twice:
`m09a_api_surface_tables.py:286` carries the comment *"No index on
`search_queries` beyond its primary key"* and creates none, and `\d
search_queries` on the live database lists exactly one index,
`pk_search_queries PRIMARY KEY, btree (id)`. The claim stands as written and is
discharged here rather than corrected.

**`surface` and `tier` are `VARCHAR`, not a Postgres enum and not a CHECK, and
the precedent is `credits.source` one migration back.** `usher.db.base.enum_column`
(`src/usher/db/base.py:124-150`) compiles `native_enum=False` to `VARCHAR(length)`
with `values_callable` binding each member's `.value`, and `create_constraint`
defaults to `False` in SQLAlchemy 2.0 — **so no membership CHECK is emitted
either**; Pydantic owns membership, matching every other constraint in this
schema. There is no `CREATE TYPE` anywhere in this database and `m10a` adds
none. `search_queries.mode` is already `character varying(16)` with no CHECK on
the live table, which is the same column shape one field over. So: `surface
varchar(8)`, `tier varchar(6)`.

**The backfill is three statements, and `server_default` is refused with its
reason.** `m09d_people_provenance.py:139-142` is the exact precedent, in the
exact spelling: *"Three statements, and the order is the point: nullable,
backfilled, then NOT NULL. Never `server_default`, which would outlive this
migration and supply a plausible wrong value to a writer that forgot."*
`surface` follows it verbatim — `ADD COLUMN` nullable, `UPDATE search_queries
SET surface = 'search'`, `SET NOT NULL` — because every row F2 has written came
from `GET /search` or `usher search` and `'search'` is the **true** value for
them rather than a convenient one (`SearchService._record_search` is reachable
from exactly those two callers; `suggest` writes nothing, `src/usher/services/search.py:919`).
`tier` is **nullable and stays nullable**: a `search` row has no tier, and PRD 10's
amendment 2 spells it *"a nullable `tier`"* (`:618`). Nine rows exist on this
deployment, so the backfill is instant here; `ALTER TABLE … SET NOT NULL` scans
the table and `ADD COLUMN` with no default does not rewrite it on PostgreSQL 11+,
which is `m09d:48-52`'s "land the column before the volume" argument and the
reason this revision is not deferred until the suggest writer has filled the
table.

**`m09f`'s storage lesson does not apply here, and it is priced rather than
waved through.** `m09f`'s finding is about `halfvec` and a threshold: pgvector
declares `halfvec` storage `EXTERNAL`, 1024 lanes is **2,052 bytes** against
`TOAST_TUPLE_THRESHOLD`'s **2,032**, and crossing it cost 6.1× on the whole
rebuild job. Neither column here is a vector. Measured on the live table, a
`search_queries` row is **avg 113.1 bytes, max 133 bytes**, longest `query` 45
characters; `surface` and `tier` add at most 15 bytes including varlena headers,
leaving the row an order of magnitude below the threshold. **The one column that
could ever TOAST this table is `query`**, which is `text` with only
`ck_search_queries_query_not_empty` bounding it, and that is true at HEAD
independently of this revision. No `SET STORAGE` is needed and none is added.

**The two `llm_calls` indexes, quoted from `m08a`'s docstring rather than
re-derived.** `docs/prd/02-data-model.md:699` says *"the two that will be right
are written into `m08a`'s docstring"*; that docstring is
`src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m08a_curation.py:197-221`, and the two are:

    -- dashboard 5's "LLM spend per day and month" and the cost-anomaly alert
    -- ("daily spend > 3x the trailing 7-day median"), both WHERE at >= :since
    CREATE INDEX ix_llm_calls_at ON llm_calls (at);

    -- dashboard 5's "cost per curated row", joining curated_rows on
    -- generation_id. PARTIAL: query-expansion rows carry NULL and, once Task
    -- 20 ships, are the majority of the table -- they are exactly the rows
    -- this join never wants.
    CREATE INDEX ix_llm_calls_generation_id ON llm_calls (generation_id)
        WHERE generation_id IS NOT NULL;

Both ship exactly as written, partial predicate included. **`m08a`'s objection to
shipping them early is answered with a number rather than overruled:** it refused
them because *"an index nothing reads is `ix_titles_popularity` again:
maintained on every write, for a consumer that does not exist."* `llm_calls`
holds **0 rows and 16 kB** on this deployment (measured 2026-08-13) and gains
one row per generation per household per night thereafter
(`08-operations.md:618`), so the maintenance cost of both is bounded by the
curation cadence and is not measurable. The reader's task, in another group,
depends on this one and carries the measurement against a real ledger that
`m08a` asked for.

**The index on `at` is a btree, and BRIN was considered and refused.** `at` is
append-only and physically correlated (UUIDv7 primary key, inserts in time
order), which is the shape BRIN is for — but `record_outcome`'s `UPDATE`
(`src/usher/ports/repository/search_query.py:168`) writes a new heap tuple for
every row that gains a click or a play, and whether those stay on their original
page is a `fillfactor` question nobody in this project has measured. A btree also
matches `ix_llm_calls_at` above, which is the same shape for the same
`WHERE at >= :since` query family, and on a table measured at 32 kB the index
size saving BRIN exists for is worth nothing. Named `ix_search_queries_at`.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_db_migration_status.py::test_code_head_revision_matches_the_head_migration_on_disk`
— re-pointed from `assert code_head_revision() == "m09f"` to `== "m10a"`. It is
the cheapest genuine red in the repository: it reads
`usher/db/migrations/versions/*.py` off disk, needs no Docker and no database,
and goes green only once a file exists declaring `revision = "m10a"` with
`down_revision = "m09f"` and no other head. Write it, watch it fail, create the
empty revision. Its positive control is that it is a *comparison against a
scan*, not a membership check — a versions directory that globbed nothing would
make `code_head_revision()` return `None` and the assertion fails loudly rather
than vacuously.

**Second red, before any DDL:** in a new `tests/integration/test_m10_schema.py`,
**one assertion per artefact**, five of them — `surface` and `tier` in
`_column_set(url, "search_queries")`, `ix_search_queries_at`, `ix_llm_calls_at`
and `ix_llm_calls_generation_id` in `_index_set(url)`. One per artefact and not
one combined case, because a migration that ships four of five passes a check
naming only the first — the rule `m08a` needed per table and `m09c` generalised
per artefact kind (`tests/integration/test_migrations.py:534-540`).

**Third red, and the one with teeth:** the planner probe on the retention
`DELETE`. An index that exists proves nothing about what it serves, so the case
runs `EXPLAIN` of `DELETE FROM search_queries WHERE at < now() - interval '90
days'` under `SET LOCAL enable_seqscan = off` and asserts the plan names
`ix_search_queries_at`; run against the pre-migration schema the same probe
still reads `Seq Scan`, because Postgres falls back when there is no
alternative — so the two arms differ observably and the premise is proved rather
than assumed. This is the discipline
`test_both_new_foreign_keys_have_an_index_the_referential_check_can_use`
(`tests/integration/test_migrations.py:238`) already applies. **Watch the
pre-migration arm fail before writing the DDL.**

**Acceptance:**

- `code_head_revision() == "m10a"`; `m10a.down_revision == "m09f"`; exactly one
  head, so `ScriptDirectory.get_current_head()` does not return `None`. The file
  is `src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m10a_suggest_surface_and_retention_indexes.py`.
  **No `m10b` file is added by this task**, and nothing in Phase 2 mints one.
- `search_queries` gains exactly two columns and **no third**. In particular no
  `keystroke_index`, no `session_id`, no `debounced` flag: PRD 10's amendment
  names `surface` and `tier` and nothing else, and a tenth-and-eleventh column
  is the whole change.
- `surface` is `varchar(8) NOT NULL` with every pre-existing row reading
  `'search'` and **no server default surviving the migration** — asserted off
  `information_schema.columns.column_default IS NULL`, not off the model.
  `tier` is `varchar(6)` **nullable**, and a case asserts a `surface='search'`
  row with `tier IS NULL` round-trips, because the nullability is the design
  and not an oversight.
- The backfill is asserted on **rows that existed before the migration ran** —
  the case inserts a `search_queries` row against the `m09f` schema, upgrades,
  and reads `surface` back as `'search'`. An empty-table upgrade satisfies a
  three-statement backfill exactly as well as a correct one; the row is what
  makes the `UPDATE` observable.
- `SuggestTier` moves from `src/usher/services/search.py:218` to
  `src/usher/ports/search.py`, beside `SearchMode`, and `SearchSurface` is
  declared there new. **This is forced by the layering contract and is not a
  tidy-up.** `SearchQueryRecord` lives in `src/usher/ports/repository/search_query.py`
  and already imports `SearchMode` from `usher.ports.search` (`:14`); a `tier`
  field typed on the services-layer enum would make `usher.ports` import
  `usher.services`, which is an upward import under contract 1
  (`layers = ["usher.api", "usher.services", "usher.ports", "usher.domain"]`,
  `pyproject.toml:155`) and reports BROKEN. The move also satisfies that enum's
  own stated condition for staying put — *"**no port method anywhere takes a
  tier**, because a tier *is* the choice of implementation and an implementation
  cannot be told which implementation it is"* (`services/search.py:231-234`).
  A port method now takes one, and it takes it as a **record of which
  implementation ran**, never as an instruction, which is the distinction that
  licenses the move. Three importers update — `cli.py:80`,
  `api/routers/search.py:124`, `api/dto/search.py:36` — and **no alias is left
  behind in `services/search.py`**: a second name for one vocabulary is what the
  two-vocabularies-under-one-column hazard looks like one layer up. mypy strict
  is `no_implicit_reexport`, so the move is visible to the type checker either
  way.
- `SearchSurface` is a two-member `StrEnum` (`SEARCH = "search"`,
  `SUGGEST = "suggest"`) and **`SUGGEST` has a named emitter inside this
  milestone** — J2's writer. This repository forbids an enum member nothing
  emits (`LLMPurpose.QUERY_EXPANSION` sat unemitted for two milestones); J2 is
  in the same phase and the member does not ship without it.
- Both new columns use `enum_column`, and
  `tests/unit/test_db_models.py::test_enum_columns_are_real_enums_not_bare_strings`
  (`:168`) gains a case each asserting `native_enum is False`,
  `create_constraint is False`, and that the stored values are each member's
  `.value`. **There is no Postgres enum type to drop in `downgrade()`** — this
  schema creates none, in any migration.
- `ix_search_queries_at`, `ix_llm_calls_at` and `ix_llm_calls_generation_id`
  exist with the shapes above, asserted off `pg_indexes.indexdef` and not off
  `Base.metadata`; the partial predicate `WHERE generation_id IS NOT NULL` is
  asserted **as text in `indexdef`**, because `compare_metadata` is blind to it
  and a full index answers every membership check a partial one does.
- `test_migration_matches_the_orm_metadata` is green: `SearchQueryRow` gains the
  two mapped columns, `LlmCallRow` gains the two `Index` declarations, and
  `compare_metadata` reports `[]`.
- `tests/integration/test_migrations.py`'s `-1` half is re-pointed at `m10a`'s
  artefacts, in the direction `m10a.downgrade()` establishes — `m10a` **creates**,
  so the artefacts are asserted **absent** after one step back
  (`not in`), the `m08a` spelling and not the `ffc` one. **One assertion per
  artefact, five of them**, because a `downgrade()` that drops four and forgets
  the fifth passes a check naming only the first.
- **`m09f`'s three storage assertions are displaced into a named stop at
  `m09e`, and displaced because they have teeth.** `-1`-from-`m10a` lands on
  `m09f`'s applied state where `attstorage` is already `'p'`, so the inherited
  `== "e"` assertions **fail loudly** — the eleventh landing in a row to do so.
  Run the suite with `m10a` present and the inherited block untouched, and
  **record that it failed** before touching it. A `-1` half that stays green
  after a new head means the assertion it inherited never had teeth, which is a
  defect in the previous author's assertion and is reported as such.
- ⚠️ **The docstring of `test_a_full_down_and_up_cycle_restores_every_index` is
  stale at HEAD and this task corrects it.** Lines 442-450 read *"**Head is
  `m09c`** and the `-1` half is re-pointed at its artefacts"* and *"That is the
  **seventh** landing in a row to do so (`ffa`, `ffb`, `ffc`, `m08a`, `m08b`,
  `m09a`, `m09c`)"*, while the body of the same function at `:545-546` says
  `m09f` and *"the tenth landing"*. Three heads landed and each updated the
  inline comment and not the docstring. Both move to `m10a` / **eleventh**, the
  full chain is spelled out once, and the count is grepped for and moved
  everywhere it is stated — the test docstring, the inline comment at `:546`,
  and `.claude/rules/db-and-sql.md`. This is the same family as A1's corrected
  acceptance sentence: **a counted fact restated in three places goes stale in
  the two nobody re-reads.**
- `alembic downgrade base` then `upgrade head` on a throwaway database restores
  the whole index set (`after == before`), and `-1` then back up is green.
  `run_alembic` is called with an explicit `direction=` for every bare revision
  id (`tests/integration/conftest.py:109-116`) — left to infer, a bare id runs
  `upgrade`, which is a silent no-op.
- `downgrade()` mirrors `upgrade()` statement for statement: drop the three
  indexes, drop `tier`, drop `surface`. **The `DROP INDEX` lines here are
  load-bearing**, unlike `m08a`'s (which preceded a `drop_table` that took the
  index anyway) — nothing else removes them, and a `downgrade()` that dropped
  only the columns would leave two `llm_calls` indexes behind on a table that
  still exists. Said explicitly in the migration because the two spellings look
  alike.
- The gate is green whole, run as `uv run pytest` and not one directory at a
  time: `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run ruff format --check .`,
  `uv run mypy src tests`, `uv run lint-imports` reporting **10 kept, 0 broken**
  (measured at HEAD 2026-08-13; ten `[[tool.importlinter.contracts]]` blocks in
  `pyproject.toml`, the tenth being *no aggregate port module imports another*
  at `:419`), `uv run pytest`.
- Scope proof: `git diff --name-only` touches nothing under `src/usher/api/routers/`
  but the one `SuggestTier` import line, nothing under `src/usher/services/`
  but the `SuggestTier` declaration being deleted, and no repository, service
  or route gains behaviour. **The writer is J2's.**
- PRD edits are partial where the truth is partial, two documents, two anchors:
  `docs/prd/02-data-model.md`'s `search_queries` and `llm_calls` rows (the
  latter's *"No index beyond the primary key"* becomes the two named indexes,
  with *"and still no read method"* kept, because it is still true after this
  task); and `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`'s `## Analytics tables`
  block — the 🔴 *"`GET /search/suggest` writes no row"* paragraph and the ⚠️
  *"the table has **no index on `at`**"* sentence at `:646-648`. The first
  becomes *"the columns exist and the writer is J2's"*, not *"suggest is
  recorded"*: a stale "verified" fact is worse than none.
- A mutation sweep over the migration and its cases, plant list stated before it
  runs: `downgrade()` body replaced by `pass`; the `UPDATE … SET surface =
  'search'` deleted (must fail the pre-existing-row case and only that one); `WHERE
  generation_id IS NOT NULL` dropped from the partial index; `SET NOT NULL`
  dropped from `surface`; `ix_search_queries_at` renamed by one character. The
  equivalent-mutant control, stated first: reordering the two `llm_calls`
  `create_index` calls must pass all five gate steps. Ledger appended to
  `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`.

**Risks:**

- **`--autogenerate` will produce a migration that looks complete and is not.**
  Blind to CHECK bodies and to partial index predicates in the direction that
  matters. Hand-write both `create_index` calls and read the compiled DDL.
- **`tests/integration/test_migrations.py` has one `-1` re-point in M10 and it
  is here.** Any other group editing that file is a merge conflict by
  construction and probably a second, wrong re-point.
- **Moving `SuggestTier` is a four-file rename that mypy will catch and
  `lint-imports` will not.** The contract that forces the move only fires once
  `SearchQueryRecord` names the type; until J2 adds the field, both placements
  pass all ten contracts. Do the move here, with the migration, or J2 discovers
  it mid-task.
- **The nine live rows are not a test population.** The backfill case must
  insert its own row against the `m09f` schema; asserting against whatever the
  developer's database happens to hold is the "a run that did not run is not a
  pass" family wearing a `SELECT`.

### Task J2 — the suggest writer, and the two things the suggest path deliberately does not have

**Depends on:** J1
**Files:** `src/usher/services/search.py` (`suggest` at `:888-950` and
`_record_search` at `:801-887`), `src/usher/api/routers/search.py` (the
`suggest` handler at `:316-401` and its docstring at `:96-118`),
`src/usher/ports/repository/search_query.py` (`SearchQueryRecord`),
`src/usher/db/repositories/search_query.py`, `src/usher/cli.py` (`_suggest` at
`:920-958`), `src/usher/config.py`, `.env.example`,
`tests/unit/test_services_search.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_search.py`,
`tests/integration/test_search_analytics.py`,
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the 🔴 suggest paragraph
and the "which absence means what" table at `:561-648`),
`docs/prd/07-client-api.md` (**only** `GET /search/suggest`)

PRD 10 names two ways to close this and says they are *"named here so M10 plans
one rather than rediscovering the choice"* (`:609`). **M10 takes the column.**
The spec's reasoning is right and is restated once so an implementer does not
re-open it: `SearchMode`'s three members describe **how a query was answered** —
lexical, semantic, fused; a `SUGGEST` member would describe **where it came
from**, a different axis, and would still need a fifth member or a second field
to say *which tier*, which is the half ADR-0031 actually wants measured. It is
also the larger wire change, because `SearchMode` is `GET /search`'s `?mode=`
and two fields of `SearchAnswer`, so a member no search lane can serve becomes
reachable on a route that would have to refuse it.

**Two structural facts the route's own docstring states as reasons not to do
this, and both have to be paid rather than argued away.**

**One: the suggest path has no household, and `search_queries.user_id` is
`NOT NULL` behind `ON DELETE RESTRICT`.** `routers/search.py:102-105` says
*"**No household, unlike `GET /search`.** `SearchService.suggest` takes no
`user_id` … A `DefaultUserIdDep` here would be a `SELECT` (and, on a first run,
an `INSERT`) **per keystroke** to resolve an id nothing downstream reads."*
After this task something downstream reads it. PRD 10 forbids the alternative
explicitly — *"A search with no household. `user_id` is `NOT NULL` behind a real
foreign key, so a search nobody is speaking for has no row rather than a row
with a hole in it"* — so the column does not become nullable and the row is not
written without one. `DefaultUserIdDep` joins the suggest handler
(`api/deps.py`, the same dependency `GET /search` uses at
`routers/search.py:244` and `PUT /watch/…` uses at `routers/watch.py:83`), and
`SearchService.suggest` grows `user_id: uuid.UUID | None` keyword-only,
mirroring `search`'s. `usher suggest` resolves it the same way `usher search`
does. **The per-keystroke `SELECT` that docstring objects to is real and is
measured** — see the acceptance bar below.

**Two: the write is 6.6× the query it records, on the tier that matters.**
`record()` plus the commit is p50 **3.957 ms** / p95 4.738 ms over 2,000
iterations against a real `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`, of which the INSERT is
0.9 ms and the WAL flush 3.0 ms (`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:629-635`).
Tier 1 — the btree `lower(name) text_pattern_ops` probe — is p50 **0.6 ms**
(`.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md:65`). So an unconditional synchronous
row would make the analytics write **6.6× the cost of the thing being measured**
on the path ADR-0031 exists to make cheap, before the household lookup. Tier 2
is p50 33.6 ms (`:59`, re-measured at 39.59 ms in B3's gate at `:230`), where
the same row is 11.8% — the same fraction PRD 10 already accepted for full text.

**So this task's decision is which tiers write, and it is decided by a
pre-registered measurement rather than by this paragraph.** The bar is written
to `/var/tmp/m10-J2/BAR.md` with a `sha256` **before** the run — `/tmp` on this
host is tmpfs and a bar whose value is that it predates the numbers cannot live
in RAM. Registered position, to be refuted: **both tiers write, unconditionally,
gated by one setting `USHER_SEARCH_SUGGEST_ANALYTICS` defaulting `true`**, on
the ground that the seam already exists — `_record_search` returns `None` when
`self._analytics is None` (`services/search.py:866`), so an analytics-free
`SearchService` is a shipped, tested configuration and not a new branch. The bar
that would refute it: **tier 1's p50 must stay under 5 ms end to end** (route
in, route out, against the real 1,272,401-title catalog). If it does not, the
default flips to `false` and the setting ships documented, and *that* is the
result rather than a failure.

**Sampling is refused, and the reason is a denominator.** PRD 10's table of
*"which absence means what, because a reader computing a no-click rate needs all
three and only one of them is a real zero"* (`:561-570`) has five rows and every
one of them reads a **count** of rows. A sample rate makes every count an
estimate and adds a sixth row to that table meaning *"the row that was not
written"*, which is indistinguishable in the data from every other absence. A
whole-or-nothing setting keeps the table's own contract; the volume is bounded
by J5's retention instead, which is exactly why the spec bundles them.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_search_analytics.py::test_a_suggest_records_its_surface_and_the_tier_that_answered`
— drives `GET /search/suggest?q=…&tier=fuzzy` through a real app against a
migrated database and asserts exactly one `search_queries` row exists with
`surface = 'suggest'`, `tier = 'fuzzy'`, a non-null `user_id`, and
`mode` set to the value J2 chooses for a tierless path (below). At HEAD it fails
on `assert 0 == 1` — the route writes nothing, stated in its own docstring at
`:377`. **Its positive control fires first**: the same case drives
`GET /search?q=…` and asserts that row appears with `surface = 'search'` and
`tier IS NULL`, which is J1's backfill semantics on a *new* row rather than an
old one, and which fails at HEAD too (there is no column). A second arm asserts
`tier='prefix'` for the prefix tier, because one tier passing is also what a
writer hard-coding `'fuzzy'` produces.

**Acceptance:**

- One `search_queries` row per answered suggest request that clears the
  min-length gate, on **both** tiers, with `surface='suggest'` and `tier` equal
  to the tier that ran. **The short-`q` arm writes nothing** — it returns before
  the service (`routers/search.py:389-390`) and there is no answered query to
  record; PRD 10's *"A blank or whitespace-only query"* exclusion (`:581`)
  extends to it and is stated in the route rather than inferred.
- **`tier` is the parameter that selected the index, never a tier the service
  chose.** The route already makes this argument for its own echo
  (`routers/search.py:396-398`); the row obeys the same rule, so a response and
  a row can never disagree about which index answered.
- `mode` for a suggest row is `SearchMode.LEXICAL` — both tiers are btree/GIN
  reads with no embed and no fusion, which is what that member already means —
  and the choice is stated in the port docstring beside `surface`, because
  `mode` is the column every dashboard 1 and 4 panel groups by and a suggest
  row landing under `FUSED` would attribute a keystroke to a lane that has never
  run. **Every mode-split panel now has to filter on `surface`**, and that is
  recorded in PRD 10 in this commit rather than discovered from a skewed panel:
  the amendment keeps the two vocabularies apart in the schema and hands the
  dashboards a `WHERE surface = 'search'` they did not previously need.
- `SearchQueryRecord` gains `surface: SearchSurface` (required, no default) and
  `tier: SuggestTier | None = None`. **`surface` is required and not defaulted
  to `SEARCH`**: a default is the "supply a plausible wrong value to a writer
  that forgot" failure `m09d:139-142` names, one layer up from the database.
- A model-level invariant, asserted in a unit case rather than in the database:
  `surface == SEARCH` implies `tier is None`, and `surface == SUGGEST` implies
  `tier is not None`. No CHECK constraint — this schema's constraints are
  Pydantic's (`db/base.py:133-136`) — and the case carries both directions,
  because a validator that only refuses one of them passes a test that only
  tries one.
- The pre-registered bar is met or the default flips, and **the measurement is
  committed rather than the data**: tier-1 and tier-2 p50/p95 end-to-end, with
  and without the writer, over the same query set `scripts/measure_suggest_tiers.py`
  uses, on the real catalog. The report is refutation-first — the bar, then the
  numbers, then which guesses died. It uses that script's own quiet-check, which
  matches argv **tokens**, skips any process whose `comm` is a shell or `sleep`,
  and compares CPU **drift** between two idle moments — the two obvious
  quiet-checks are both wrong and both have condemned a clean run here before.
- The household lookup is measured separately and stated with its denominator:
  `DefaultUserIdDep` on the suggest path is one `SELECT` per request against
  `users`, and the first request on a fresh deployment is an `INSERT`. If it is
  a material fraction of tier 1's budget, say so with the number — the route's
  docstring objection is retired **by a measurement or not at all**, and its
  text is rewritten in this commit either way.
- `usher suggest` writes the row too, and the case proves it — the CLI is the
  one caller for which `cli._session_for` yields a session and disposes the
  engine **without committing**, which is why `_record_search` commits in the
  service rather than leaving it to the caller (`services/search.py:848-856`).
  A suggest writer that inherited the commit would be correct on the route and
  silently lose every CLI row.
- An analytics failure never fails a suggest response: `except UsherPortError`
  and deliberately **not** `except Exception`, matching `_record_search`
  (`services/search.py:839`, the code at `:880`) and `api/analytics.py:82`. Exercised by injecting a
  repository that raises — the guard is defence in depth, so the only way to
  test it is to make the promise breakable.
- `uv run lint-imports` reports **10 kept, 0 broken**. No new contract; the
  `SuggestTier` move in J1 is what keeps it that way.
- PRD 10's 🔴 paragraph (`:595`) and the fifth row of the "which absence means
  what" table (`:570` — *"**no rows from `GET /search/suggest`** — by design, on
  either tier"*) are both rewritten, and the ⚠️ *"the denominator is answered
  searches, never plays"* sentence gains the second denominator this creates.
  `docs/prd/07-client-api.md`'s `GET /search/suggest` gains the household it now
  resolves.
- A mutation sweep: `surface` hard-coded to `SEARCH` in the suggest writer;
  `tier` hard-coded to `PREFIX`; the short-`q` early return moved *after* the
  write; the `except UsherPortError` widened to `except Exception`. Each with
  its expected verdict written first.

**Risks:**

- **The route's own docstring is the counter-argument and it is well argued.**
  Two of its three objections are paid here with measurements; the third — that
  a client drives this per keystroke — is paid by J5 and by nothing else. If J5
  slips, this task ships a table that grows per keystroke with no pruner, which
  is strictly worse than the state PRD 10 complains about. **J2 and J5 land in
  the same phase or neither does.**
- **`SearchMode.LEXICAL` on a suggest row is a decision that looks like an
  implementation detail** and will be read as one by every panel author who does
  not filter on `surface`. It is the reason the PRD edit is not optional.
- **The min-length gate is per tier** (`_MIN_CHARS_FOR_TIER`), so "answered"
  means different things on the two tiers and the row counts are not directly
  comparable. Say so in the PRD edit rather than letting a panel divide them.

### Task J3 — ADR-0039, and the interesting claim is that the scheduler stores nothing

**Depends on:** nothing
**Files:** `docs/prd/decisions/0039-the-scheduler-stores-nothing.md` (new),
`docs/prd/decisions/README.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** a new
`### Scheduled work` subsection), `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md` (**only** the M10 row)

**0039 is the next number.** `docs/prd/decisions/` holds `0001`–`0038` with no
gaps at the top; `0038-the-embedding-width-is-deployment-wide-ddl.md` is the
highest and was minted 2026-08-13 by the `m09e` work. M9 minted six
(0029–0032, 0034, 0035) and 0036/0037 followed. **This group mints exactly one
and does not pre-allocate others** — Group M's rule, and M9's own experience of
pre-allocating ids across groups and getting a serial spine for it.

The ADR this project writes is for a **contested** call, and there are three
here, each with a reasonable person on the other side.

**Context.** Issue #17: *"Nothing runs `usher similar --rebuild`, and neighbours
go stale with no per-row predicate."* A title's neighbours go stale when some
*other* title gets an embedding, which no per-row predicate can decide, so
`usher index --backfill`'s staleness query cannot cover it. The issue's own
"Done when" is deliberately narrow — *"staleness is at least observable … 
Automating the rebuild is a larger call and needs its own decision"* — **and
this is that decision.** The second customer is `search_queries` retention,
which PRD 10 says has *"no retention job and no scheduler anywhere in `src/`"*
(`:638-644`) and prices as part of the same amendment.

**Decision, three parts.**

1. **A small set of named jobs with a period, not a general cron.** The
   contract is a name, a period, a `last_done()` and a `run()`. There is no
   crontab expression, no calendar, no timezone, no per-job concurrency and no
   dependency graph, because both customers that exist take hours and neither
   needs any of it. `JobKind` is the thing this is deliberately not: a queue
   whose unit of work is one row, whose trigger is an event, and which
   `SimilarityService.rebuild`'s own docstring already refuses on exactly these
   grounds — *"a job kind whose trigger is a timer is a cron entry with a queue
   and a park path bolted on"* (`services/similar.py:371-376`).
2. **The scheduler holds no state and needs no table.** This is the contested
   part. The obvious design persists a last-run timestamp per job, which needs
   a row, which needs a migration — and this milestone has one migration whose
   contents are settled. The alternative, taken here: **each job answers "when
   were you last done" from the artefact it maintains.** The neighbour rebuild
   reads `SimilarityService.computed_at()`, which is already
   `max(title_neighbors.computed_at)` and whose docstring already says *"The
   artefact's age. `None` means it has never been built"*
   (`services/similar.py:364-366`). Retention reads `min(search_queries.at)`.
   Both are one query, both are exact, and **both survive a restart because the
   state was never in the scheduler.** A scheduler table would be a second copy
   of a fact the artefact already carries, and the two would drift the first
   time somebody ran the CLI command by hand — which is exactly how the rebuild
   is run today.
3. **It is off by default.** `USHER_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=false`. Two reasons, both
   measured. A fresh deployment has no embeddings, so the first tick of an
   enabled scheduler would start a walk over an empty table and then, once
   `usher index --backfill` drained, a **3.33-hour** job nobody asked for. And
   there is no lease: `JobQueue`'s lease is per job **row** and this component
   has no rows, so two processes with the scheduler on — the server lane and a
   separate `usher work` container, which is a documented deployment
   (`.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`) — would both start the same
   rebuild and both write the same pages. **What would reverse this is a lease,
   which needs a row, which needs a migration M10 does not have.** Stated so the
   next milestone knows the price of flipping the default rather than
   rediscovering it.

**Consequences, including the ones that cost something.**

- #17 is closed on its own "Done when" — staleness is observable — plus more
  than it asked for, and **the rebuild is automated only where an operator turns
  it on.** Say that in the issue close rather than claiming automation.
- `last_done()` from the artefact means a job that produces **no** artefact
  cannot be registered without a design change. Neither of the two does. The
  third one will be the test of this ADR.
- A period is a **minimum interval since last completion**, not a wall-clock
  schedule. "Every night at 3am" is not expressible and is not offered. An
  operator who wants that runs `usher schedule --once` from their own cron,
  which is the pre-M10 arrangement kept as a supported path rather than
  replaced.
- The `last_done()` reads are not free and are priced in J4.

**Failing test first:** none — this is a document. Its gate is the PRD link
check, run over `docs/prd/` plus `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` and **not** over
`docs/plans/` (`.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md:75-110`), which must print
`OK`. **Run it before the edit as well**, so a break it finds is known to be
this task's.

**Acceptance:**

- `docs/prd/decisions/0039-the-scheduler-stores-nothing.md` exists in the house
  format — context → decision → consequences → evidence — and the evidence
  section carries J4's three measured read costs and the 3.33-hour walk with its
  date.
- `docs/prd/decisions/README.md` gains the row, in number order, and the link
  check prints `OK`.
- `docs/prd/08-operations.md` gains a `### Scheduled work` subsection naming the
  two registered jobs, the default-off setting and the `usher schedule --once`
  path. It sits beside the backup section rather than inside it.
- ADR-0020 is **not** silently contradicted: it is linked, because
  `blend_fingerprint` is what makes the rebuild's staleness a query rather than
  an inference, and that is the mechanism `last_done()` depends on.
  ADR-0037 (*the worker is a bounded pool of scopes*) is linked from the
  no-lease consequence, because it is the component that has a lease and the
  contrast is the argument.
- No other ADR number is allocated by this group. Anything else that turns out
  to want one is a **request**.
