### Task J4 — `ports/scheduler.py` and `services/scheduler.py`, shipped with an empty registry

**Depends on:** J3
**Files:** `src/usher/ports/scheduler.py` (new), `src/usher/services/scheduler.py` (new),
`src/usher/composition.py`, `src/usher/api/lanes.py`, `src/usher/cli.py`,
`src/usher/config.py`, `src/usher/telemetry.py`, `.env.example`,
`tests/unit/test_services_scheduler.py` (new),
`tests/unit/test_ports.py`, `tests/integration/test_scheduler_lane.py` (new),
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the metric catalogue at
`:136-177`), `pyproject.toml` (**only** if a contract must move — see below),
`CLAUDE.md` (**only** the Commands section's CLI block)

The one genuinely new component in M10. **The port is `ScheduledJob`, and the
service is the loop** — the same split `ports/jobs.py`/`services/jobs.py`
already has, one abstraction lower because there is no queue.

    class ScheduledJob(ABC):
        name: str                                    # stable, used as a metric label
        period: timedelta
        async def last_done(self) -> datetime | None  # from the artefact, never from us
        async def run(self) -> None

`abc.ABC`, not `typing.Protocol` — ADR-0001, and this file does not reopen it.
The port declares the shape; **the registrations are constructed in
`composition.py`**, which is where every other cross-boundary wiring in this
project is assembled and which is already imported by both roots. `usher.api.routers`
may not name `usher.composition` (contract 8, `pyproject.toml:320-329`) and does
not need to: the lane lives in `usher.api.lanes`, which already imports it
(`api/lanes.py` builds the whole `Pipeline`).

**No new import-linter contract, and the reason is what the new modules import
rather than a rule.** `usher.ports.scheduler` imports `datetime`, `abc` and
nothing from this project; `usher.services.scheduler` imports `usher.ports` and
`usher.domain` only. Both sit inside contract 1's layer list already
(`layers = ["usher.api", "usher.services", "usher.ports", "usher.domain"]`,
`pyproject.toml:155`) and inside contracts 2 and 3's `source_modules`, which
report indirect chains by default — so a scheduler module reaching
`usher.db` or `usher.adapters` breaks two contracts without anybody adding one.
**Verified at HEAD before the work starts: `uv run lint-imports` reports 10
kept, 0 broken** (measured 2026-08-13). It must still report **10 kept, 0
broken** after, and the count is quoted in the commit body. If an implementer
finds a hole, the fix is a contract and a **request** to widen this task, not a
silent import.

**The loop, and three things it is not.** It is one `asyncio` task per
deployment running `while True`, and per tick it walks the registry, asks each
job `last_done()`, and runs the ones whose period has elapsed **sequentially,
one at a time**. Not a `TaskGroup` and not `gather`: ADR-0037's argument applies
unchanged — a task group cancels its siblings on the first escape, which turns
one poisoned job into a rebuild abandoned mid-page. Not one task per job, which
is what makes "two hours-long jobs contending for the same pool" impossible
without a bound. And not a sleep-until-next-due calculation: it sleeps a fixed
`USHER_SCHEDULER_TICK_SECONDS` (default **300**), because a due-time calculation
over a `last_done()` that another process may have moved is a cache of a fact
the artefact already answers.

**Per-tick cost, measured rather than assumed, because it is what sets the tick
floor.** All three `last_done()`-shaped reads against the live 1,140 MB
`title_neighbors` table on 2026-08-13:

| read | plan | measured |
|---|---|---|
| `max(computed_at)` — the rebuild's `last_done()` | parallel seq scan | **169.0 ms** |
| `count(*) WHERE blend_fingerprint <> :current` — the stale gauge | parallel seq scan, 100,554 buffers | **268.8 ms** |
| `… ORDER BY title_id LIMIT 1` — J6's resume cursor, worst case (table fully current) | parallel seq scan | **247.9 ms** |

So a tick costs roughly a third of a second of database work against the
neighbour table. At 300 s that is 0.1% duty; at 1 s it would be 30%. **The tick
period has a floor and the floor is measured, not stylistic** — `Field(ge=60.0)`
on the setting, with this table in its comment.

**Observability, and one constraint that decides its shape.** Three additions to
PRD 10's catalogue: `usher.scheduler.job.duration` (histogram, seconds, labelled
`job`), `usher.scheduler.job.failures` (counter, labelled `job`), and
`usher.scheduler.job.due` (observable gauge, labelled `job`, seconds since
`last_done()` minus period — negative means not due). Plus a **root span with a
`Link`**, `scheduler.<name>`, never a child: the tick that scheduled it has
usually returned, which is the reason `rows.refresh` and a worker's `job.*` are
both roots (`api/lanes.py:479-503`, and PRD 10 specifies it). The span is
started with `context=Context()` — an empty context — so "root" is structural
rather than a property of where the task happened to be created; a lane task
inherits the context of whatever created it, so a lifespan inside a span would
otherwise make every scheduler run a child of one request forever.

⚠️ **The gauge may not query the database, and this is not a style preference.**
OTel invokes an observable callback from the metric reader's **background
thread**, and every database call here is a coroutine on asyncpg, so a callback
that queried would have to bounce a coroutine onto the event loop and block the
exporter thread on it — a deadlock whenever the loop is itself blocked
(`.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`). `usher.scheduler.job.due`
therefore reads a **synchronous snapshot** the loop refreshes after every tick,
exactly like `register_queue_gauges` and `register_search_gauges`. And the SDK
keeps only the **first** observable instrument registered under a name and
silently discards the rest, so the reader is a **module global that is replaced**
rather than a closure captured at instrument-creation time —
`telemetry.py:517-522` states this and the scheduler's reader follows it.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_services_scheduler.py::test_a_job_whose_period_has_not_elapsed_is_not_run`
— two fake jobs on one registry, one with `last_done()` inside its period and
one outside, one tick, and it asserts `run()` was called on exactly the second.
At HEAD it fails on `ModuleNotFoundError: usher.services.scheduler`. **Its
positive control is the first arm**, because a scheduler that ran nothing at all
also satisfies "the not-due job did not run" — the case asserts the due job
*did*, and both assertions carry their own message. A third arm asserts a job
with `last_done() is None` **runs**, because "never built" and "not due" are the
two states a naive `now - last_done > period` comparison collapses with a
`TypeError`.

**Acceptance:**

- `usher.ports.scheduler.ScheduledJob` is an `abc.ABC` with the four members
  above, every one carrying a docstring, and it is registered in
  `tests/unit/test_ports.py::test_every_port_abc_is_registered_in_all_ports` —
  which walks with the `usher.ports.` prefix, so a new top-level port module is
  seen (A1 established that a plain `iter_modules` would not have been).
- **The registry ships empty and that is a legal, observable state.** One tick
  over zero jobs logs once — not once per tick — and sleeps. A case asserts the
  log fires once over three ticks, because a scheduler that spams a log line
  every 5 minutes forever is the shape an operator mutes and then never sees the
  real one.
  ⚠️ **If J5 does not land in this phase, J4 is dead code and is reverted rather
  than merged.** Said in the task because *"a kind whose handler is a stub is a
  queue that grows forever"* (`domain/jobs.py:44-46`) is this project's own rule
  and this is its inverse.
- Jobs run **sequentially within a tick**, asserted by observed non-overlap: the
  two fakes record the wall-clock interval each occupied and the case asserts
  they do **not** intersect. A count of two completions is also what a
  concurrent pair produces — this repository's fourth evidence rule, applied in
  the direction that wants serialisation rather than overlap.
- **A failing job does not stop the loop and does not stop its siblings.** The
  `except Exception` is named and logged with the job name — without it the task
  dies and CPython reports the unretrieved exception at GC time, to stderr, with
  no job name in it, which is the shape `_guard` exists for (`api/lanes.py:363`).
  `asyncio.CancelledError` is re-raised, never swallowed, so `stop()` works.
- **`stop()` cancels and awaits.** An in-flight 3.33-hour rebuild is cancelled at
  its next `await`, which is inside a page, and the page's transaction rolls
  back — which is safe because each page deletes and re-inserts its own seeds'
  rows in one transaction (`services/similar.py:377-381`). A case drives
  `start()` then `stop()` with a job blocked on an event and asserts the task
  is `done()` afterwards rather than leaked.
- `start()` creates tasks and **awaits nothing** — `coro.send(None)` must raise
  `StopIteration`. This is what keeps `/health` answering 200 with Postgres
  down, and the M5 supervisor's own draft got it wrong in exactly this way
  (`.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md`). The first `last_done()` happens
  *inside* the loop task.
- `USHER_SCHEDULER_ENABLED: bool = False` and
  `USHER_SCHEDULER_TICK_SECONDS: float = Field(default=300.0, ge=60.0)` in
  `src/usher/config.py`, both with `.env.example` entries carrying the reason for
  the default and the measured floor. The default-off is asserted by a case, not
  just written — a settings default is a claim like any other.
- The lane joins `LaneSupervisor` gated on the setting, and **the case that
  proves the server process runs it is a job running, not an assertion about
  wiring**: `tests/integration/test_scheduler_lane.py` registers a one-shot fake,
  starts nothing but `LifespanManager(create_app(settings))`, and asserts the
  fake ran before the app stops — with the mirror case
  (`scheduler_enabled=False`, it does not run) as the control that makes it
  evidence. This is `test_lanes_in_the_server_process.py`'s shape exactly.
  ⚠️ **Every existing fixture that builds an app must now say it does not want
  this lane**, the way nine of them already pass `push_enabled=False,
  worker_enabled=False`. Default-off means most do not have to — which is a
  second, smaller reason for the default.
- `usher schedule --once` runs one tick and exits, and `usher schedule` runs the
  loop, mirroring `usher work --once` / `usher work`. `--once` is what an
  operator's own crontab calls, and it is the supported path for anyone who
  wants a wall-clock schedule the period cannot express. `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands
  section gains both lines — **do not invent commands for tooling that does not
  exist**, so they are added in the same commit that makes them real.
- Three metric rows added to PRD 10's catalogue at `:136-177` with their units
  and labels, and the `usher.scheduler.job.due` row states that it is fed from a
  snapshot and is therefore **stale but never wrong**, which is the same caveat
  `register_queue_gauges` carries.
- `uv run lint-imports` reports **10 kept, 0 broken**; `uv run mypy src tests`
  clean under strict, which is `no_implicit_reexport`, so `ports/scheduler.py`
  earns its exports through `__all__`.
- A mutation sweep, plants stated first: the period comparison flipped to `<`;
  `last_done() is None` treated as "not due"; the sequential loop replaced with
  `asyncio.gather`; the `except Exception` deleted; `Context()` dropped from the
  span start. Expected verdicts written before the run, survivors reported with
  evidence rather than replaced with a kill about something else.

**Risks:**

- **A component with no registrations is indistinguishable from a component
  nobody wants.** The mitigation is the phase rule above, and it is the reason
  J5 is not optional.
- **Two processes, no lease.** Default-off contains it; an operator who enables
  it in two places gets two rebuilds. The failure is loud (both write the same
  pages, one may deadlock) rather than silent, but it is not prevented. ADR-0039
  records what would prevent it.
- **`composition.py` is contended by four groups in this phase.** J4 adds a
  builder; J5 and J6 each add a registration to it. Serialise them.
- **The tick's `last_done()` reads are full scans and will get slower as the
  catalog grows.** 169 ms today at 3.27M neighbour rows. Nothing here indexes
  them; if it becomes material, that is a **request** for an index, not a second
  migration minted mid-phase.

---

### Task J5 — registration 1: `search_queries` retention, priced against a table that holds nine rows

**Depends on:** J1, J4
**Files:** `src/usher/ports/repository/search_query.py`,
`src/usher/db/repositories/search_query.py`,
`src/usher/services/scheduler.py` (the registration type only),
`src/usher/composition.py`, `src/usher/config.py`, `.env.example`,
`tests/unit/test_services_scheduler.py`,
`tests/integration/test_search_query_retention.py` (new),
`docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the ⚠️ *"Nothing owns this
table's size"* paragraph at `:638-648`), `docs/prd/08-operations.md`

PRD 10 states the gap and prices it as owed work: *"There is no retention job and
no scheduler anywhere in `src/` … so `search_queries` grows monotonically at one
row per answered search, forever … pricing the retention is part of that
amendment rather than a follow-up to it"* (`:638-644`). The statement it names is
`DELETE FROM search_queries WHERE at < now() - interval '90 days'`. **90 days is
taken as the period rather than re-derived** — it is the only number PRD 10
offers and there is no usage history to derive a better one from — and it ships
as `USHER_SEARCH_QUERY_RETENTION_DAYS: int = Field(default=90, ge=1)` so an
operator can shorten it without a release.

**The price, measured on the live table rather than projected.** 2026-08-13,
`usher-postgres-1`:

- `search_queries` holds **9 rows** in **32 kB** total relation size. Every one
  was written by F2 since M9 closed; the table has never held a row older than
  the milestone that created it.
- `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` of the 90-day `DELETE` today: **Seq Scan,
  1 shared buffer hit, 0 rows removed, 0.043 ms execution.**

**So the honest statement is that the index J1 adds buys nothing today, and what
justifies it is the steady state rather than the present.** A daily prune at
90-day retention matches about **1/90th** of the table — 1.1% — which is
precisely the selectivity ratio at which a btree beats a sequential scan, and
precisely the ratio a *first* run after a long outage does not have. Without the
index every daily prune reads 100% of the relation to delete 1.1% of it; with
it, it reads the 1.1%. That is the argument, stated with its arithmetic, and it
is written into the migration docstring in J1 and into PRD 10 here. **It is not
an argument that this deployment needs it now, and the plan does not pretend
otherwise.**

**The volume this exists for is J2's, not F2's.** F2's arrival rate is one row
per press of the enter key. J2's is one row per keystroke past the min-length
gate on either tier, which PRD 10 estimates *"would out-number *and* out-weight
the searches by an order of magnitude each"* (`:604`). Nine rows is what the
first rate produces in the days since M9 closed; the second rate is what makes
this task exist. Both numbers go in the PRD edit, because a reader who sees only
the nine will conclude the retention job is premature and a reader who sees only
the estimate will conclude the table is already large.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_search_query_retention.py::test_the_retention_job_deletes_only_rows_past_the_cutoff`
— inserts three rows at `now()`, `now() - 89 days` and `now() - 91 days`
against a migrated database, runs the job, and asserts exactly the 91-day row is
gone and the other two remain. At HEAD it fails on
`AttributeError`/`ModuleNotFoundError` — there is no `prune` on the port. **The
boundary row at 89 days is the control**: a job with an off-by-one on the
interval, or one that deletes everything, passes a case that only checks the old
row disappeared. A fourth arm asserts a row **exactly** at the cutoff is kept,
because `<` and `<=` are one character and both read as correct.

**Acceptance:**

- `SearchQueryRepository` gains `async def prune(self, *, before: datetime) ->
  int`, returning the row count deleted. **`before` is a datetime the caller
  computes, never an interval string the repository interpolates** — the port
  takes a value, not SQL, and the retention *policy* lives in the service where
  a setting can reach it.
- The cutoff is computed from the same clock the rest of this project injects,
  not from `now()` inside the statement. `.claude/rules/db-and-sql.md` carries
  the `now()` vs `clock_timestamp()` trap; a deletion boundary that moves inside
  a transaction is a different bug from either, and an injected clock is what
  makes the 89/90/91-day case deterministic rather than flaky at midnight.
- The job's `last_done()` is `min(search_queries.at)` compared against the
  cutoff — **not a stored timestamp**, per ADR-0039. A table whose oldest row is
  inside the window has nothing to do and the job reports itself not due, which
  is the state this deployment is in today and which the case asserts
  explicitly against the live-shaped nine-row population.
- **A deletion never cascades into a title or a household.** `search_queries`'
  two foreign keys point *outward* (`fk_search_queries_user_id_users` is
  `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, `fk_search_queries_clicked_title_id_titles` is
  `ON DELETE SET NULL`, both read off `pg_constraint` on the live table), so
  nothing references these rows and the delete is a leaf. Asserted rather than
  reasoned: the case counts `users` and `titles` before and after.
- The delete is **chunked**, `USHER_SEARCH_QUERY_RETENTION_BATCH: int = 10_000`,
  with a commit per chunk. A single `DELETE` of a year's keystrokes holds one
  transaction and one lock set for its whole duration on a table `GET /search`
  writes to on every request. The chunk loop advances on the primary key and
  terminates on an empty chunk — a keyset, for the same reason the rebuild uses
  one: *"a loop spelled 're-read what looks stale, rebuild, repeat' does not
  terminate against a row the predicate cannot clear"* (`services/similar.py:384-388`).
- The registration is constructed in `composition.py` and the job's `name` is
  `"search_queries.retention"` — stable, because it is a metric label and a
  renamed label is a broken panel.
- `uv run lint-imports` reports **10 kept, 0 broken**.
- PRD 10's ⚠️ paragraph at `:638-648` is rewritten in this commit: the retention
  job exists, the index exists, both numbers above are recorded with their date,
  and the sentence *"the table has no index on `at`, so that statement is a
  sequential scan until somebody adds one"* is replaced by what it now is.
  `docs/prd/08-operations.md`'s new `### Scheduled work` gains the retention row.
- A mutation sweep: `<` flipped to `<=`; `before` replaced with `now()`; the
  chunk loop's break condition inverted; the commit moved outside the loop. Each
  with its expected verdict first.

**Risks:**

- **Nine rows is not a population and the case must not be measured against it.**
  Every assertion here is against rows the case inserts. A "runs clean on the
  dev database" report is the "a run that did not run is not a pass" family.
- **The retention window and the dashboard window are two numbers that must
  agree.** A D1 panel asking for 180 days over a 90-day table shows a truncated
  series and no error. Named here; the dashboard group owns the fix.

---

### Task J6 — registration 2: the neighbour rebuild, a resume cursor computed once, and the guard that stops it rebuilding a correct table

**Depends on:** J4
**Files:** `src/usher/services/similar.py` (`rebuild` at `:368-441`),
`src/usher/ports/repository/search.py` (`TitleNeighborRepository`),
`src/usher/db/repositories/search.py`, `src/usher/composition.py`,
`src/usher/config.py`, `.env.example`, `src/usher/cli.py` (`_similar` at `:959-1053`),
`tests/unit/test_services_similar.py`,
`tests/integration/test_services_similar.py`,
`docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (`:666`),
`.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md`

Issue #17's second half. The job runs `SimilarityService.rebuild()`, its
`last_done()` is `SimilarityService.computed_at()`
(`services/similar.py:364-366`), and its period is
`USHER_SIMILAR_REBUILD_PERIOD_HOURS: float = Field(default=24.0, ge=1.0)`.

**Three things have to be true before it can be registered, and only one of them
is the registration.**

**One — the rebuild restarts, it does not resume, and at 3.33 hours that is the
difference between converging and never converging.** `rebuild`'s docstring
says *"Idempotent and resumable by re-running"* (`:377-381`) and that is true of
the **result** and false of the **work**: the keyset starts at `after = None`
every run (`:388`), so an interruption at 3.2 hours redoes 3.2 hours. A
scheduled job in a window shorter than the walk would never reach the end of the
catalog. The fix needs no new state, per ADR-0039: **`rebuild(resume=True)`
computes the start cursor once, from the artefact** — the lowest `title_id`
among embedded seeds that has no neighbour row carrying the current
`blend_fingerprint` — and then walks forward exactly as today.

⚠️ **The distinction that makes this safe is one word, and the docstring already
argues the other side of it.** *"A loop spelled 're-read what looks stale,
rebuild, repeat' does not terminate against a row the predicate cannot clear,
which is the non-convergence the watch-history repair shipped once"* (`:384-388`).
That is a **loop predicate**. This is a **starting offset, computed once**; the
loop still advances on `id` and still ends when `list_embedded` returns empty, so
a seed the rebuild cannot clear is re-attempted once per scheduler run rather
than looped on forever. Both readings go in the docstring, because the next
reader will reach for the objection.

Measured worst case for that cursor read, on the live 3,268,000-row table with
**every row current** (the case where it must scan everything and find nothing):
**247.9 ms**, parallel seq scan. Note that `min(title_id)` does not exist —
`ERROR: function min(uuid) does not exist` — so the spelling is
`ORDER BY title_id LIMIT 1`, recorded here because it is the obvious first
attempt and it fails at the database rather than at mypy.

**Two — the guard, and it is the live fact this plan carries.** As established
in this group's header: all 3,268,000 rows are stamped
`a7013154c014e0ff1b60ef5d8534a115`, which is
`blend_fingerprint(embedding_model="openai:BAAI/bge-m3")` at HEAD, and every
stored embedding is `openai:BAAI/bge-m3`. But `Settings.embedding_model`'s
**default** is `fastembed:BAAI/bge-large-en-v1.5` (`config.py:352`), whose digest
is `afd00fffaf6946c0d8dcf87faf966614`. So a scheduler process started without
`USHER_EMBEDDING_MODEL` set would compute a different fingerprint from the one
that stamped the rows, find 3,268,000 stale rows, and start a **3.33-hour
rebuild against vectors it cannot even read consistently** — `nearest_for` does
not filter by `model_name` (`e2241a9`'s own stated cost), so it would draw pools
from `bge-m3` vectors and stamp them `bge-large`. **The registered job refuses to
run when the configured model disagrees with what `title_embeddings.model_name`
holds**, logs the two strings, and reports itself not due. This is the *"a
rebuild that refuses a mixed table"* that `e2241a9`'s docstring names as the
honest fix and says is **not built**; the scheduler is the customer that makes it
worth building, and it is built here in its narrow form — one configured model
against the set of stored ones, refusing on disagreement.

**Three — a bounded verification, because the full walk is 3.33 hours and cannot
live inside a task.** `rebuild` gains `max_seeds: int | None = None`, defaulting
to today's behaviour. A capped run leaves the first N seeds stamped current and
the rest stale, which `stale_neighbors()` reports correctly and which the resume
cursor picks up — so the cap and the resume are the same mechanism observed from
two ends, and the bounded verification exercises both. J7 runs it uncapped.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_services_similar.py::test_a_resumed_rebuild_starts_at_the_first_seed_without_a_current_fingerprint`
— seeds a synthetic catalog with embeddings for six titles, runs
`rebuild(max_seeds=2)`, records which seeds got rows, then runs
`rebuild(resume=True)` **with a repository wrapper that records every
`list_embedded(after=…)` call**, and asserts the first call's `after` is the
second seed's id rather than `None`. At HEAD it fails on an unexpected keyword
argument. **Two positive controls, both required**: the first run must actually
have written rows for exactly two seeds (a `max_seeds` that wrote nothing
satisfies "the resume started later" trivially), and the wrapper must have
recorded at least one call (`assert calls, "the rebuild issued no page read"`) —
a scan that globs nothing passes exactly like a scan that passes.

**Acceptance:**

- `rebuild(resume=True)` starts at the artefact-derived cursor; `rebuild()` with
  no argument is byte-for-byte the behaviour at HEAD, asserted by a case that
  runs both over a fully-stale table and compares the written rows.
- The cursor read is one repository method on `TitleNeighborRepository` with the
  `ORDER BY title_id LIMIT 1` spelling, its worst case documented as **247.9 ms
  measured 2026-08-13 on 3,268,000 rows**, and a comment saying why it is not
  `min()`.
- **The fingerprint is still resolved once per rebuild, not per page** (`:397`)
  — a resumed run does not re-resolve it mid-walk, or a table can be stamped
  with two fingerprints and the resume cursor becomes non-deterministic.
- The model guard refuses and does not raise: a mismatch logs both strings at
  `ERROR` and returns without running, and the job's `last_done()` is unchanged
  so nothing is recorded as done. A case drives it with a service configured for
  one model against embeddings written under another and asserts **zero** rows
  were written — asserting "it logged" is not asserting "it did not run".
- The registration's `name` is `"similar.rebuild"`. Its period defaults to 24 h
  and the setting's `.env.example` entry states the **3.33-hour** walk with its
  date, so an operator setting a 2-hour period can see it is impossible.
- ⚠️ **`docs/prd/08-operations.md:666` is corrected in this commit.** It reads
  *"`usher similar --rebuild` is the expensive half and its cost changed by more
  than the width did: 594.7 ms/seed … a full 130,720-seed walk of 21.6 hours
  against 80 minutes. … Plan the rebuild as an overnight job, not a follow-on
  step."* That was `m09e`'s cost and `m09f` repaired it the same day; the
  completed walk is **11,981 s / 91.7 ms/seed / 3.33 h**
  (`.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md:1241-1242`, confirmed here from the
  live table's own `computed_at` range). The conclusion survives — it is still
  an overnight job, not a follow-on step — and the number does not. A stale
  "verified" fact is worse than none.
- `usher similar --rebuild` gains `--resume` and `--max-seeds`, and
  `usher similar` (no argument) reports the table's age and stale count, which is
  **#17's stated "Done when"** — *"staleness is at least observable — a count, a
  timestamp, or a `usher similar` line that says how old the table is relative to
  the embedding population."* `cli.py:1043-1052` already prints two of the three
  in the single-title case; this makes it a whole-table line. The issue closes on
  this task plus J4, and the close comment says **observable and schedulable, not
  automatic by default** — ADR-0039's third part.
- `usher.similarity.neighbors.stale` still reports through the cached snapshot
  and is **not** turned into an OTel callback that queries: it is a **268.8 ms**
  full scan measured on the live table, which is exactly why.
- `uv run lint-imports` reports **10 kept, 0 broken**; the whole gate green.
- A mutation sweep: `resume=True` ignored (must fail the cursor case);
  the cursor read's `<>` flipped to `=`; the model guard's comparison inverted;
  `max_seeds` applied per page rather than per run. Verdicts first.

**Risks:**

- **The resume cursor and the model guard interact.** A deployment mid-model-swap
  has a mixed `title_embeddings`, so the guard refuses and the cursor is never
  read — which is correct, and is also why the guard's case must run *before* the
  cursor's in the file, or a green cursor case may be green because nothing ran.
- **`services/similar.py` is contended.** Phase 2's suggest-metrics and D5 tasks
  do not touch it; the `usher similar` CLI line does. Serialise with the CLI
  group.
- **A capped rebuild leaves a half-stamped table**, which is a legal state that
  no test before this task has ever produced. Any assertion elsewhere that reads
  "one distinct fingerprint" is now wrong under `max_seeds`; grep for it.

---

### Task J7 — the overnight full walk, run once, as its own task

**Depends on:** J6
**Files:** `.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md`,
`docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md`,
`docs/plans/progress.md`. **No `src/` file.**

The scheduler's first registered job cannot be exercised end to end inside a
normal task and this is the task that does it once. **It writes no code.** Its
product is a measurement and four document edits.

**The bar is pre-registered before the run, to `/var/tmp/m10-J7/BAR.md`, with a
`sha256` recorded at the moment of writing.** Not `/tmp`: this host's `/tmp` is
tmpfs — RAM — so a bar whose entire value is that it provably predates the
numbers is erased by a reboot, and every pre-registered bar in M9 went there
before somebody noticed.

**What the bar contains, written as predictions to be refuted:**

1. **Wall time 3.33 h ± 15%** (11,981 s measured 2026-08-13 over 130,720 seeds),
   at 91.7 ms/seed. The refutation that matters: if it is materially slower, the
   population grew or something regressed, and either is a finding.
2. **3,268,000 rows at 130,720 distinct seeds** — 25 per seed — or the embedded
   population moved, in which case say by how much and why.
3. **`stale_neighbors()` returns 0 afterwards, and the row count is recorded
   beside it.** This is not decoration: an empty table also reports zero stale
   rows, and H7 recorded the count for exactly this reason
   (`docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:945-946`).
4. **Exactly one distinct `blend_fingerprint`**, equal to
   `blend_fingerprint(embedding_model=<the configured model>)`. Assert the
   configured model is `openai:BAAI/bge-m3` **before** the run and record the
   string — a run started with the `Settings` default computes
   `afd00fffaf6946c0d8dcf87faf966614` and would rewrite a correct table under a
   wrong label. J6's guard should refuse it; **this run is where the guard is
   observed refusing**, deliberately, once, before the real run.
5. **A resumed run converges.** Interrupt the walk once, part way, with a signal
   that reaches the loop; restart with `resume=True`; record that the second run
   started at a non-`None` cursor and that the union completed the population.
   This is the property the scheduler is built on and it has never been observed.
6. **The scheduler drove it**, not the CLI. `usher schedule` with the job
   registered, `USHER_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=true`, one deployment.

**Failing test first:** none, and that is the point — **this is an observation,
not a unit test**, the second of the two test shapes the spec says are new to
this milestone. What stands in for a failing test is the pre-registered bar and
its hash: the run is reported guess-by-guess with **refutations first**, and the
measurement is committed rather than the data.

**Acceptance:**

- `/var/tmp/m10-J7/BAR.md` exists with a `sha256` recorded before the run, and
  the report quotes both.
- All six predictions are answered, each as *held* or *refuted with the number*.
  A prediction that held is stated as plainly as one that did not.
- The interrupted-and-resumed arm ran and is reported. If it was not run, the
  task is not complete — it is the only evidence the resumption story works at
  the scale it exists for, and a 3.33-hour job whose resume was only ever tested
  on six synthetic seeds is a resume nobody has seen.
- The harness's own quiet-check is `scripts/measure_suggest_tiers.py`'s: argv
  **tokens**, skipping any process whose `comm` is a shell or `sleep`, comparing
  CPU **drift** between two idle moments. **Both obvious quiet-checks are wrong**
  — a one-minute load average rises on any long run of continuous querying
  (1.34 → 2.82 on a box provably idle throughout), and `pgrep -f pytest` counts
  the shell that mentions the word.
- Nothing in the run writes a credential, a token, a user id or a host into the
  repository. It is driven from outside the working tree.
- `.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md` gains the run with its date and
  sample; `docs/prd/05-search-and-similarity.md`'s neighbour paragraph and
  `docs/prd/08-operations.md:666` carry the settled number;
  `docs/plans/progress.md` records the task.
- **The gate is not run against a tree this task modified**, because it modifies
  no code. It reports the suite state it inherited.

**Risks:**

- **3.33 hours is one attempt per evening.** A mistake in the setup costs a day.
  Write the bar, dry-run the whole invocation with `--max-seeds 200` first, and
  only then start.
- **The catalog is live and shared with every other Phase 2 task.** A rebuild
  holds no long transaction — it commits per page — but it saturates the
  connection pool and the buffer cache for three hours. **Nothing else runs
  against `usher-postgres-1` while it does**, which is the same serialisation
  rule a mutation sweep gets and for a similar reason.
- **If the guard in J6 is wrong, this task is where a correct 1,140 MB table
  gets rewritten under a wrong label.** Check the configured model string by
  hand, out loud, before starting. The two digests differ in the first
  character — `a7013154…` against `afd00fff…` — which is exactly enough
  similarity to misread at a glance.
## Group K — backup, restore and secret rotation

**Phase 2.** Eight tasks. The group's subject is the three things an operator
cannot get back by re-running an importer: **the household's watch state, the
credentials, and the money ledger** — plus the two things this project has
never written down, which are *what a backup artifact is* and *what a title id
means outside the database that minted it*.

Two findings set the shape of everything below, and both were measured on
2026-08-13 rather than reasoned about.

**`pg_dump` is not reachable from the shipped deployment.** The runtime image
is `python:3.13-slim` (`Dockerfile:44`) and carries neither `pg_dump` nor
`psql` — verified by running `command -v pg_dump` inside `usher:m9-h6`, which
answers nothing. Neither does this host. The only `pg_dump` within reach of
this deployment is *inside the Postgres container* (`pgvector/pgvector:pg17`,
`pg_dump (PostgreSQL) 17.10`), which an operator following PRD 08's
*"documented `pg_dump` of those"* (`08-operations.md:585`) would have to reach
through `docker exec` against a container Usher does not own. Adding
`postgresql-client` to the runtime stage costs a measured **+63,596 KiB
(62.1 MiB)** of installed filesystem on `python:3.13-slim` with
`--no-install-recommends` — **+18% on a 359 MB image** — of which
`/usr/lib/postgresql` is only 3.9 MiB and the rest is libpq, OpenSSL, readline
and the Perl the wrapper scripts want. So the spec's decision 2 (*"a CLI
command with the manifest in code, not a documented `pg_dump`"*,
`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:286`) is right, and the argument for it is
stronger than the one the spec gives: the documented alternative **cannot be
run from the container this project ships**.

**A title id is not portable, and the whole of restore's design falls out of
that.** See K2, which is the group's hardest task and the one every other task
depends on.

**Intra-group ordering is `K1 → K2 → K3 → K4 → K5 → K6`, with `K7 → K8`
running parallel from K1.** K5 and K8 are drills against a scratch container
and write no `src/`; K6 is documentation. The two chains meet only in
`docs/runbooks/` — **K5 creates that directory and K8 adds one file to it**, so
K8 must not land before K5, and that is the one cross-chain edge. Files this
group shares with other groups, declared so the orchestrator can sequence
rather than discover: `src/usher/cli.py` (K3, K4, K7 — and the operator-bugs
task for #5, and Phase 3's `--version`), `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (K1's
backup table at `:579-641`, K6's one pointer line at the end of `### Backup`,
K8's `Secrets` bullet at `:232-241` — **three disjoint anchors**, and K6's is
one line so it rebases over K1 rather than conflicting with it), and
`tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py`'s parametrised command table (K3, K4, K7 —
three rows, and #5's fix edits the same file).

**Three things this group deliberately does not build**, each with its reason:
no scheduled backup (the scheduler is another Phase 2 task and a backup is an
operator action with an operator's judgement about where the file goes); no
encryption of the artifact (it carries ciphertext for the one secret it holds —
see K3 — and encrypting the rest would need a second key with its own rotation
story); and **no restore of `curated_rows`**, which K2 settles with evidence.

---

### Task K1 — the rebuildable/precious split becomes a manifest, and PRD 08's table is corrected in the same commit because it has already drifted twice

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/db/backup_manifest.py`, `tests/unit/test_backup_manifest.py`, `tests/integration/test_backup_manifest_covers_the_live_schema.py`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** `### Backup — the asymmetry is the point`, lines 579-641)

PRD 08 states the split as prose in a two-column table (`:581-583`) and then
spends fifty lines qualifying it (`:590-641`). **The prose has drifted, and the
drift is the argument for the whole task.** The spec names one instance
(`:288-291`); there are **two**, and the second is the more damaging.

- **`row_provider_settings`.** `08-operations.md:638-641` says row provider
  enable/disable *"belongs in the **precious** column the day it exists"* and
  that the row above it is annotated M9 *"for exactly the reason it is not
  listed here yet"*. It exists: `m09a_api_surface_tables.py:290` creates it,
  `:302` gives it `PRIMARY KEY (slug_prefix)`, and M9's E1/E2 shipped its
  repository and its `PUT /admin/rows/providers`. The table at `:583` was never
  updated. **Verified**: the live schema holds it (`information_schema.tables`
  on `usher-postgres-1`, 2026-08-13).
- **`search_queries`, which nobody has classified at all.** Also `m09a`, also
  M9, nine columns (`id, at, user_id, query, mode, result_count, latency_ms,
  clicked_title_id, played` — read off `information_schema.columns`). It is
  named nowhere in `08-operations.md`. It is **not** rebuildable by anything:
  it is a record of what a household typed and what they then played, and
  `09-roadmap.md:1119` scopes issues #15/#16 **"Post-v1 unless M9's
  `search_queries` supplies a real evaluation set"** — a plan that depends on
  the table surviving. Losing it costs the only evaluation set this project
  will ever have and it cannot be re-derived from anything.

**The general form, and it is worth separating "right by accident" from
"wrong".** M9 added four tables and the section was updated for none of them.
Two of the four land in the correct column anyway, because the left column's
*"search index, cached images"* happens to describe them — but that phrase was
written in commit `860b086` (**2026-07-28**, the original PRD 07-08 landing,
before M1 shipped), and `git log -S"cached images" -- docs/prd/08-operations.md`
names **exactly that one commit**, so the phrase predates `title_search_names`
and `images` by nine milestones and classifies them correctly by accident. The other two are simply
absent, and one of them contradicts an explicit ⚠️ four lines further down.
**A prose table is updated by whoever remembers; the two tables nobody
remembered are the two that are wrong.**

**The manifest is `src/usher/db/backup_manifest.py`** — under `usher.db`
because it is knowledge about *this schema*, and a `usher.services` module
holding a table name would break the third import contract
(`pyproject.toml:164-167`, `db is driven, not driving`). It is a mapping from
table name to a small frozen record, and the classification is exhaustive over
the live schema.

**The binary split is one class short and `media_items` is the table that
proves it.** PRD 08:583 lists *"manual unmatched resolutions"* as precious.
There is no such table. What that names is two **columns** —
`media_items.title_id` and `media_items.episode_id`, which
`db/repositories/media_item.py:661-676`'s `attach_title` writes and which
`api/routers/unmatched.py`'s resolve route reaches. Every other column of
`media_items` is rebuilt by the next source walk, and on the household this
project measures that is **1,126,789 rows** to back up for the sake of a
handful of links. So the manifest carries **four** classes, not two, and this
task widens the spec's vocabulary deliberately:

| class | meaning | restore behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `PRECIOUS` | no importer and no derivation reproduces it | carried whole |
| `REBUILDABLE` | a documented command reproduces it, at a stated cost | never carried |
| `PARTIAL` | rebuildable except for named operator-authored columns | only those columns, merged |
| `SCHEMA` | Alembic's own bookkeeping | read as a stamp, never written |

⚠️ **`media_items` carries no provenance column, so "manual" is not a
distinction the schema can express.** `services/handlers.py:242` — the
automatic match handler — calls the *same* `attach_title` as the operator's
route. A backup can carry all `(source_id, external_id) → title` links or
none; it cannot carry only the manual ones. Carrying all of them is the right
call and the reason is that the harm is asymmetric: a link the ladder would
have re-derived is re-derived to the same answer, and a link it would not
re-derive is exactly the operator's judgement. K4's merge rule (write only
where the target's link is `NULL`) is what keeps that safe.

**The classification, all 29 tables in the live schema, with the reason for
every one that is not obvious.** Row counts and sizes are from
`pg_stat_user_tables`/`pg_total_relation_size` on `usher-postgres-1` at
`m09f`, 2026-08-13.

| table | class | why |
|---|---|---|
| `users` | PRECIOUS | 1 row. Nothing recreates a household. `uq_users_name` is its natural key |
| `sources` | PRECIOUS | 1 row. Operator-authored: a URL and a name |
| `source_credentials` | PRECIOUS | 1 row, 48 kB. Ciphertext under `USHER_SECRET_KEY` — see K3's refusal to re-encrypt |
| `watch_states` | PRECIOUS | 0 rows here and the load-bearing table on a real deployment (`08-operations.md:601`). The one thing a re-walk *can* partly restore, from the source — and only while that source still exists |
| `llm_calls` | PRECIOUS | 0 rows here, 16 kB. `08-operations.md:608-615`: *"the first thing in this project that is not rebuildable from anything, at any price"* — a spend ledger, and no OpenAI-compatible endpoint offers a per-key call history |
| `row_provider_settings` | PRECIOUS | 0 rows. PRD 08:638-641's own words, applied a milestone late. Keyed by `slug_prefix`, a string — **it is the one precious table with no UUID in it at all**, so K2 does not touch it |
| `search_queries` | PRECIOUS | 9 rows. Unclassified in PRD 08 and unreproducible; the evaluation set #15/#16 wait on |
| `media_items` | **PARTIAL** (`title_id`, `episode_id`) | 180 rows here. Rebuilt by `usher sync`; the two link columns are not |
| `titles` | REBUILDABLE | 1,272,401 rows, 1050 MB. `bootstrap --phase imdb` |
| `seasons`, `episodes` | REBUILDABLE | 0 rows here. TMDb enrichment |
| `people`, `credits`, `collections` | REBUILDABLE | 887,638 / 2,878,307 / 5,240 rows, 136 MB / 706 MB / 848 kB. `usher derive --backfill` from `raw_payloads`, **no network call** — `08-operations.md:596`, M4's boundary call 2 |
| `images` | REBUILDABLE | 537 rows. Same derivation, M9's C3 |
| `title_embeddings` | REBUILDABLE | 130,723 rows, 697 MB. `usher index --backfill` then `usher work`; **105.9 min measured** at `halfvec(1024)` (`08-operations.md:666`) |
| `title_neighbors` | REBUILDABLE | 3,256,676 rows, 1140 MB. `usher similar --rebuild`, **594.7 ms/seed, a 21.6-hour walk** (`08-operations.md:666`). The most expensive thing in this column by two orders of magnitude, and still rebuildable |
| `title_search_names` | REBUILDABLE | 1,450,117 rows, 198 MB. `bootstrap --phase aliases` + `usher derive` |
| `tmdb_ids`, `id_crosswalk` | REBUILDABLE | 1,459,592 / 336,769 rows. `--phase tmdb-ids` / `--phase crosswalk` (live WDQS) |
| `raw_payloads` | REBUILDABLE ⚠️ | 130,749 rows, **995 MB — the largest single relation after the neighbour table**. Rebuildable **only from TMDb**: M9's S3 measured 130,334 requests over 1.98 h to fill it. See the ruling below |
| `genome_scores`, `genome_tags` | REBUILDABLE ⚠️ | 15,565 / 1,128 rows. Upstream only. See the ruling below |
| `curated_rows` | REBUILDABLE | 0 rows. `usher curate`, one completion. `02-data-model.md:698`: *"rebuildable (one completion) and not restorable"* |
| `user_taste` | REBUILDABLE | 0 rows. A mean over embeddings, carrying its own fingerprint (`08-operations.md:597`) |
| `jobs` | REBUILDABLE | 212 rows, 58 MB. A **queue**, not state: every row is work that will be re-enqueued by whatever noticed it was needed |
| `import_runs` | REBUILDABLE, never restored | 6 rows. Resumption checkpoints *for this database*. Carrying them into a rebuilt catalog would tell a resumable importer a phase is complete over an empty table — **actively harmful**, so the manifest records `restore=never` rather than leaving it to K4 |
| `sync_runs` | REBUILDABLE | 2 rows. An audit trail a new walk replaces |
| `alembic_version` | **SCHEMA** | 1 row, `m09f`. Read by K3 as the artifact's stamp; never written by restore |

**Three rulings this task makes and defends, because PRD 08 leaves all three
open.**

**`genome_scores` and `genome_tags` stay in the rebuildable column, and the
reason is licensing rather than cost.** `08-operations.md:599`/`:614` are
right that they are rebuildable *only from upstream* and that GroupLens can
withdraw `ml-latest.zip`, at which point they are not rebuildable at all — but
`:604-606` already answers the question: *"a dump of it is a redistribution of
MovieLens data … and still not something this project's own rule 1 does."*
`tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` enforces that rule mechanically over
`src/`, and a backup artifact holding 15,565 MovieLens vectors is exactly the
object it exists to prevent, one directory over. So this is not a judgement
about probability; it is the same refusal the repository already makes. The
manifest records the class **and the reason string**, and PRD 08 gains the
sentence that the risk is accepted knowingly.

**`raw_payloads` stays rebuildable, and this is the closest call in the
table.** PRD 08:601 calls it load-bearing and it is the thing three other
tables are derived from. Against carrying it: **995 MB**, which is 2.5× the
whole rest of the artifact and turns a "short restore" into a file an operator
will not keep; and it is third-party TMDb payloads verbatim, so the same rule-1
argument applies with more force than it does to the genome. For carrying it:
1.98 h and 130,334 requests against a server this project does not own, which
is the Phase-1 politeness argument pointing the other way. **The ruling is
rebuildable, with the cost stated in the manifest and in the runbook**, and
with `usher backup --include-payloads` explicitly *not* built — a flag that
makes the artifact redistribute TMDb payloads is a licensing decision, not an
operator convenience.

**`user_taste`'s classification is unaffected by PRD 08:625, and that
paragraph is stale.** It says *"`TasteService.centroid` has no caller in
`src/`"*. **False since M8**: `services/curation_pool.py:173` calls it, and
that call is the reason the guard above it exists (`:169-173` — *"`centroid()`
writes"*). What is still true is the narrower claim in the same paragraph —
there is no writer **on the request path**, because `api/deps.py:816` records
that the centroid is structurally `None` there. The table is empty on this
deployment (0 rows) because no curation run with an embedder configured has
touched it, not because nothing can write it. Corrected in the same commit;
the classification does not move either way, since a mean over embeddings is
rebuildable whoever computes it.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_backup_manifest_covers_the_live_schema.py::test_every_table_the_migrations_create_is_classified_by_the_manifest`
— runs the real Alembic chain through the existing `postgres_url`
session-scoped fixture (`tests/integration/conftest.py:151`), reads
`information_schema.tables WHERE table_schema = 'public'`, and asserts the set
equals `set(MANIFEST)`, reporting the symmetric difference in **both**
directions with separate messages (*"tables the manifest does not classify"*
and *"tables the manifest classifies that do not exist"*). At HEAD there is no
manifest, so it fails on the import. Its positive control is
`assert len(live) >= 29, "the schema scan found nothing"` **plus** a named
anchor, `assert "llm_calls" in live` — a scan that reads an empty
`information_schema` passes exactly like a scan that passes, and this
repository has been bitten by that shape in five separate tasks. It reads
`information_schema` rather than `Base.metadata` deliberately: the metadata
cannot see `alembic_version`, which is a real table in every deployment and the
one this artifact is stamped from.

A unit arm, `tests/unit/test_backup_manifest.py`, carries the cheap invariants
that need no container: every entry's class is a member of the enum; every
`PARTIAL` entry names at least one column and every named column exists on the
ORM model (read off `Base.metadata.tables[name].columns`, so a renamed column
is a red rather than a silent no-op); every `REBUILDABLE` entry carries a
non-empty `rebuilt_by` string; and `set(MANIFEST) == set(Base.metadata.tables)
| {"alembic_version"}`, which is what makes the unit arm fail on an M11 model
without waiting for Docker.

**Acceptance:**
- `MANIFEST` classifies **29** tables: 7 `PRECIOUS`, 1 `PARTIAL`,
  20 `REBUILDABLE`, 1 `SCHEMA`. The counts are asserted, not just the coverage,
  so moving a table between columns is a diff a reviewer sees.
- Each entry carries a `reason` string and each `REBUILDABLE` entry a
  `rebuilt_by` command. `test_every_rebuildable_entry_names_a_command_the_cli_
  advertises` parses `usher.cli.build_parser()`'s subcommand list
  (`cli.py:1587-1742`, 15 commands) and asserts every `rebuilt_by` string's
  first token is one of them — so *"`usher restore --catalog`"*, a command
  nobody built, cannot be written into the manifest. `CLAUDE.md`'s standing
  rule: **do not invent commands for tooling that does not exist yet.**
- `docs/prd/08-operations.md:579-641` is rewritten **in this commit**, per
  `CLAUDE.md`'s *"update the PRD in the same commit as the change that
  invalidates it"*: the two-column table gains `row_provider_settings` and
  `search_queries`, the `media_items` two-column case is stated as such,
  `:625`'s `TasteService.centroid` sentence is corrected with the
  `curation_pool.py:173` citation, `:585`'s *"a documented `pg_dump`"* is
  replaced by a pointer to `usher backup` **with the measured reason**
  (`pg_dump` is absent from the shipped image; +62.1 MiB to add it), and
  `:638-641`'s ⚠️ paragraph is replaced by a sentence saying the table is now
  generated from a manifest a test enforces.
- The PRD link check prints `OK` over `docs/prd/` + `CLAUDE.md` + `README.md`.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: deleting one entry from
  `MANIFEST` must fail the integration case **and** the unit set-equality case
  (two different assertions, which is what says the unit arm is not merely the
  integration arm without Docker); moving `llm_calls` from `PRECIOUS` to
  `REBUILDABLE` must fail on the class-count assertion; the equivalent-mutant
  control is reordering two `PRECIOUS` entries in the mapping literal — a fact
  about the code, since every read is by key and every assertion is over a
  `set` or a `Counter`, and deliberately **not** an `__all__` reorder, which
  `ruff`'s `RUF022` rejects.

### Task K2 — a title id is minted per bootstrap, so restore resolves natural keys and never carries a UUID it did not first find in the target

**Depends on:** K1   **Files:** `src/usher/db/backup_identity.py`, `src/usher/ports/repository/title.py` (**only** a `resolve_natural_keys` method and its docstring), `src/usher/ports/repository/episode.py` (same), `src/usher/db/repositories/title.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/episode.py`, `tests/fakes/title_repository.py`, `tests/fakes/episode_repository.py`, `tests/unit/test_backup_identity.py`, `tests/unit/test_title_repository_contract.py`, `tests/unit/test_episode_repository_contract.py`, `tests/integration/test_title_repository.py`, `tests/integration/test_episode_repository.py`, `docs/prd/decisions/0039-a-backup-carries-natural-keys-not-ids.md`

**This is the group's central design question and the answer is a refusal, so
it gets an ADR.** `docs/prd/decisions/0039-*` is requested here and must be
minted under an explicit authorisation, per the request-not-mint rule M9's
`m09c` honoured; the register's assertion is a **floor**
(`tests/unit/test_decision_register.py:45`, `assert len(files) >= 35`, against
**38** ADRs on disk today — re-counted, because M9's own plan quotes the
pre-M9 floor of 23), so adding one needs no edit there.

**The fact, verified in the code rather than taken from the brief.**
`db/repositories/bulk.py:549` mints `new_id()` for **every row of every batch**
on the way into the staging table, and `:598` resolves the collision with
`ON CONFLICT (imdb_id) WHERE imdb_id IS NOT NULL DO UPDATE`. So *within* one
database a re-import is idempotent and a title keeps the id it was first given;
*across* two databases built from the same `title.basics.tsv.gz`, every title
gets a different id, because the `new_id()` calls are independent. There is no
seed, no derivation from `imdb_id`, and ADR-0003 is what makes it so on purpose
— `0003-own-uuid-identity.md:13-15`, *"Every entity has a Usher-owned UUIDv7
primary key … never identity"*.

**The consequence nobody has written down: five columns in the precious set
name a title or an episode by id, and none of them survives a bootstrap
boundary.** Read off `pg_constraint` on the live database, 2026-08-13:

| column | constraint | what a wrong id does |
|---|---|---|
| `watch_states.title_id` | FK → `titles`, **`ON DELETE RESTRICT`** (ADR-0010) | insert fails; the restore is refused |
| `watch_states.episode_id` | FK → `episodes`, `RESTRICT` | same |
| `media_items.title_id` / `.episode_id` | FK → `titles`/`episodes`, `ON DELETE SET NULL` | insert fails |
| `search_queries.clicked_title_id` | FK → `titles`, `SET NULL` | insert fails |
| `curated_rows.card_title_ids` | **`uuid[]`, no foreign key at all** | **nothing fails.** The shelf renders with dead ids and the database cannot tell |

That last row is the one that decides the design. `m08a` took the array
deliberately and `02-data-model.md:825` states the trade in its own heading —
*"the missing foreign key is the price"*; `db/repositories/curation.py:47-52`
explains why `unnest` of parallel arrays cannot express it. So `curated_rows`
is the one precious-looking table where a naive carry is **silent**, and it is
also the one PRD 08 already puts in the rebuildable column and PRD
02:698 already calls *"rebuildable and not restorable"*. **Ruling: `curated_rows`
is never carried by backup or restore**, and the two documents that already say
so are cited rather than re-argued.

**The design. Restore never creates a title id; it only ever resolves one.**
Every carried reference travels as a natural key and is re-resolved against the
target at restore time:

- **A title** is carried as `imdb_id`, falling back to `(kind, tmdb_id)` —
  ADR-0011's namespacing is why the kind is part of it. Coverage, counted on
  the live catalog: **1,272,401 titles; 13 with no `imdb_id`; 980,168 with no
  `tmdb_id`; 0 with neither.** So the two keys together cover 100% of this
  catalog and the design must still handle the case they do not, because
  ADR-0003 `:21-24` makes a title with no provider id a first-class citizen on
  purpose.
- **An episode** is carried as its title's key plus `(season_number,
  episode_number)` — `uq_episodes_title_season_episode` is a real unique
  constraint, so this is an identity and not a heuristic.
- **A user** is carried as `name` (`uq_users_name`).
- **A source** is carried by its own UUID, and that is correct rather than an
  exception: a source id is minted when an operator adds the source and travels
  *inside* the backup together with the `sources` row it names, so
  `source_credentials.source_id`, `media_items.source_id` and
  `sync_runs.source_id` are internally consistent within one artifact.

**The one place a raw id is accepted, and it is a check rather than a trust.**
For a title with neither provider id, the artifact carries the raw UUID and
restore accepts it **if and only if the target already holds a title with that
exact id**. That is the same-database case — disaster recovery into the
database the backup came from, which is the ordinary path — expressed as a
lookup rather than as a mode. It unifies the two cases: there is one code path,
and *"restore into the same database"* is simply the case where every lookup
resolves.

**Rejected: a fast path that carries ids when a catalog fingerprint matches.**
It is tempting (a hash over `(imdb_id, id)` pairs would tell the two cases
apart cheaply) and it buys nothing measurable — the precious set is small by
construction (`08-operations.md:618`, *"one row per generation per household per
night"*; 8 non-empty precious rows on this deployment today) — while adding a
second code path that is exercised only in the case the drill does not cover.
One path, always resolve.

**Rejected: remapping `curated_rows.card_title_ids` through the same
resolver.** It would work mechanically and it would be restoring a *rendering*
rather than a *judgement*: ADR-0028 says nothing downstream may re-sort a
curated row, a card whose title did not resolve would have to be dropped from
the middle of an ordering the completion was bought for, and the result is a
shelf the model never produced. One completion regenerates it.

**What restore does when a key does not resolve is the other half of the
design, and it differs per table on purpose:**

| table | unresolved reference | why |
|---|---|---|
| `watch_states` | **refused, named and counted** | A watch state whose title is missing is a real loss and the operator must see it. It is also recoverable: enrich the title, restore again |
| `media_items` | refused for that row, counted | Same. The link is the operator's judgement |
| `search_queries.clicked_title_id` | **set `NULL`, counted** | The FK is already `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so `NULL` is a state the column and every reader already handle, and the analytic value is the query text and the outcome, not the id |

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_backup_identity.py::test_a_reference_whose_natural_key_finds_nothing_is_refused_rather_than_written_null`
— builds a `TitleReference` for `tt9999999`, resolves it against a fake holding
two other titles, and asserts the resolver answers an `Unresolved` carrying the
key it could not find rather than `None`. At HEAD the module does not exist.
Two premises asserted first, because an absence claim is worthless without
them: `assert resolved_ok is not None` for a *sibling* reference in the same
call that **does** resolve (so the resolver is demonstrably working), and
`assert len(catalog) >= 2` for the fixture. The second failing case is the one
that pins the whole design —
`test_two_catalogs_built_from_one_dump_share_no_title_id`, which calls
`upsert_titles` twice over the **same** `ImdbTitle` sequence against two
independent fakes and asserts the two id sets are disjoint *and* that both
resolve to the same natural key. It is red at HEAD only in the sense that
`backup_identity` does not exist; its first assertion is a **fact about
`new_id()`** that this task records rather than changes, and the docstring says
so.

**Acceptance:**
- `TitleRepository.resolve_natural_keys(keys)` and
  `EpisodeRepository.resolve_natural_keys(keys)` land on the ports with a fake
  arm and a Postgres arm, driven by the **existing** contract suites so both
  are exercised. The Postgres arm is **one statement per call**, not one per
  key: a `VALUES` list joined against `titles` on
  `imdb_id` with a `(kind, tmdb_id)` fallback. Asserted by a statement-count
  case, because the alternative spelling is a loop that is invisible to every
  behavioural assertion and quadratic on a real household.
- The resolver is **case- and namespace-exact**: `tt0111161` and `TT0111161`
  are different keys, and a `tmdb_id` without a `kind` is refused by the type
  rather than defaulted. ADR-0011 is cited in the docstring.
- `test_a_title_with_neither_provider_id_is_carried_by_raw_id_and_only_
  accepted_when_the_target_already_holds_it` covers both arms — accepted
  against a target holding that id, refused against one that does not — with
  the premise that the fixture title really has neither key
  (`assert title.imdb_id is None and title.tmdb_id is None`).
- ADR-0039 is written in the house format (context → decision → consequences →
  evidence) and its Evidence section carries the four measured numbers above
  (1,272,401 / 13 / 980,168 / 0) with the date and the database they were taken
  from.
- `uv run lint-imports` reports **10 kept, 0 broken** — `backup_identity` sits
  under `usher.db` and imports `usher.domain` and `usher.ports` only.
- Sweep targets: dropping the `(kind, tmdb_id)` fallback must fail exactly the
  case written for the 13 titles that need it; resolving `tmdb_id` without
  `kind` must fail the ADR-0011 case; answering `None` instead of `Unresolved`
  for a missing key must fail the headline case **and** K4's refusal case, on
  two different files, which is what says the refusal is carried rather than
  reinvented.

### Task K3 — `usher backup`, and the two stamps that let restore refuse instead of half-applying

**Depends on:** K2   **Files:** `src/usher/services/backup.py`, `src/usher/ports/repository/backup.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/backup.py`, `src/usher/cli.py` (the sixteenth subparser at ~`:1742`, a `_backup` coroutine, a `_dispatch` arm at ~`:1976`), `tests/unit/test_services_backup.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_backup.py`, `tests/integration/test_backup_artifact.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py` (**only** the parametrised command table)

`usher backup [--output PATH]` writes one file and prints one report. It is the
**sixteenth** command; the parser advertises fifteen today (`serve`,
`bootstrap`, `bootstrap-status`, `sync`, `sync-status`, `unmatched`, `work`,
`index`, `derive`, `search`, `suggest`, `similar`, `home`, `curate`, `push` —
counted off `cli.py:1588-1741`, and ADR-0026:128 says fifteen).

**Inside ADR-0026's boundary, and that costs exactly two lines.** `main`'s one
`try` (`cli.py:1888`) already wraps `_dispatch`, so a new command inherits the
boundary by adding a `_dispatch` arm and a parser row — which is ADR-0026:93-98's
own argument, and M8's `usher curate` is the precedent. The families this
command can raise are already named: `OSError` (`cli.py:154`) and
`SQLAlchemyError` (`cli.py:158`), and **`OSError` is the one that matters
here**, because
this is the first command in the project whose ordinary failure is *"the disk
is full"* or *"that directory does not exist"*. ADR-0026's own Uncertainty
section predicted this: *"a milestone that adds a subprocess, a message broker
or a filesystem watcher adds a family with it"* (`:262-265`). It does not — the
family is already in the tuple — and the ADR gains a one-line note saying the
prediction fired and the tuple was already right, because a prediction that
resolves silently is a prediction nobody learns from.

⚠️ **A `_dispatch` arm is unpinned by the CLI-wide boundary sweep.**
`.claude/rules/config-cli-and-deployment.md` records the measurement: `_dispatch`'s
`else` is `serve` (`cli.py:1977-1984`), so a subcommand that parses and has no
arm of its own starts uvicorn, and
`test_every_command_reports_a_dead_database_the_same_way` cannot see it because
it patches every dispatch coroutine **and** `uvicorn.run` to raise identically.
**A new command owes its own case**, and this task writes it: `_backup` records,
`uvicorn.run` raises.

**The artifact is gzip-compressed JSON Lines, and the reason is K2 rather than
taste.** A header object first, then one object per row, `table` and `row`
keys. Four properties earn it:

1. **Every reference is rewritten on the way out**, which no Postgres-native
   format can express — `pg_dump -t watch_states` emits the raw `title_id`, and
   there is nowhere in a custom-format archive to put a natural key.
2. **It streams.** `usher.db.staging.raw_connection(session)` (`staging.py:96`)
   already unwraps the live `asyncpg.Connection`, and asyncpg 0.31.0 carries
   `copy_from_query(query, *args, output=…, format=…)` — verified — so a table
   can be read out without materialising it. *(Named because it is the seam a
   later fast path would use; the shipped writer batches through the repository,
   because the row counts here are small and the rewriting has to happen in
   Python anyway.)*
3. **An operator can read it.** This file is the only copy of the money ledger
   and of a household's history. A format that requires `pg_restore` to inspect
   is a format nobody inspects.
4. **It survives a Postgres version change.** `pg_dump -Fc` does not restore
   into an older server; a restore path that fails on an operator's downgrade is
   a restore path that fails on the day it is needed.

**Two stamps in the header, and only one of them is enforced by refusal.**

- **`schema_revision`** — Alembic's head, read through
  `usher.db.migrations.status.database_revision(session)`, `m09f` today. K4
  refuses a mismatch, which is **the same refusal the running service already
  makes**: `api/routers/health.py:64-88`'s `_check_migrations` compares
  `database_revision(session)` against `code_head_revision()` and answers 503,
  under the PRD's own words *"the app refuses to serve on a schema mismatch
  rather than guessing"* (`health.py:65`). Restore reuses both functions rather
  than re-reading the table, so there is one definition of *"what revision is
  this"* in `src/`.
- **`generated_at`, `manifest_version`, and per-table row counts** — provenance
  and a self-check, not a gate.

⚠️ **The artifact cannot stamp a code version and that is a Phase-3
dependency, stated so nobody adds one here.** `pyproject.toml:3` reads
`version = "0.1.0"` and **nothing in `src/` consumes it** — the spec says so at
`:198-201` and it is true. The header carries a `usher_version` key **only**
once Phase 3 wires `__version__`; K3 does not invent a second version string,
because two version strings that can disagree is worse than one that is absent.

**What is written, from K1's manifest:** all **7** `PRECIOUS` tables, plus
`media_items`' two link columns keyed by `(source_id, external_id)` — the one
`PARTIAL` entry — and nothing else. `curated_rows` is not among them: K1
classifies it `REBUILDABLE` and K2 rules it out again for a second, independent
reason, so it is excluded by the manifest rather than by a special case here.
On this deployment the artifact is **1 user, 1 source, 1 credential row, 0
watch states, 0 `llm_calls`, 0 provider settings, 9 search queries and 180
media-item links** — which is a small file and exactly PRD 08:585's *"a handful
of small tables"*, now counted rather than asserted.

⚠️ **`source_credentials` is carried as ciphertext and backup does not
decrypt it.** `build_cipher` is not called here at all. The consequence has to
be in the report and in K5's runbook: **a backup restored into a deployment
with a different `USHER_SECRET_KEY` gives credentials nobody can read** — Fernet's
authentication tag makes that a diagnosable `PortDataMalformed` rather than
garbage (`db/repositories/credentials.py:22-27`), and `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`
already renders it as *re-enter your credentials* (`08-operations.md:234-241`).
That is the correct degradation and it is the link to K7.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_backup_artifact.py::test_the_artifact_carries_every_precious_table_and_no_rebuildable_one`
— seeds one row in each of the seven precious tables and in three rebuildable
ones (`titles`, `jobs`, `raw_payloads`), runs the service against the real
migrated database, and asserts the set of `table` keys in the artifact equals
the manifest's precious set (minus `curated_rows`, with the exclusion asserted
by name and the reason in the docstring). At HEAD `usher.services.backup` does
not exist. The positive control is
`assert seeded_rebuildable, "the fixture seeded no rebuildable row"` plus
`assert "watch_states" in tables` — an artifact that carries **nothing** trivially
carries no rebuildable table, which is the exact shape of a scan that passes by
finding nothing.

**Acceptance:**
- `uv run usher backup --output /var/tmp/x.jsonl.gz` writes a file whose first
  line is a header carrying `schema_revision == "m09f"`, `generated_at`,
  `manifest_version` and a per-table `rows` map, and whose remaining lines are
  one row each. `--output` defaults to
  `usher-backup-<UTC ISO 8601 basic>.jsonl.gz` in the working directory.
- The report prints one line per table with its row count and one summary line,
  and **names the `USHER_SECRET_KEY` dependency explicitly** — one sentence, on
  every run, because an operator who learns this at restore time learns it too
  late.
- No title UUID appears anywhere in the artifact except in the raw-id fallback
  K2 defines. Asserted by
  `test_no_carried_row_holds_a_title_id_that_is_not_a_declared_raw_id_fallback`,
  which scans every emitted object for values matching the UUID grammar and
  cross-checks each against `titles.id` in the source database — with the
  premise that the fixture *has* a resolvable title reference, so the scan is
  looking at something.
- `usher --traceback backup` re-raises; `usher backup` against a directory that
  does not exist prints one line and exits 1, through `OSError` in
  `OPERATOR_ERRORS` (`cli.py:152-154`) and not through a new handler.
- `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py`'s parametrised table gains `backup`; it runs
  over the parser's own subcommand list (ADR-0026:108-110), so this is a row
  and not a mechanism.
- Sweep targets: writing a rebuildable table must fail the headline case;
  dropping `schema_revision` from the header must fail K4's refusal case (a
  cross-file kill, which is what says the two halves agree); the
  equivalent-mutant control is swapping the two independent header-dict writes,
  a fact about the code since the header is serialised by `json.dumps` and every
  assertion reads it by key.

### Task K4 — `usher restore`, which refuses in one transaction rather than half-applying, and is not "load a whole database"

**Depends on:** K3   **Files:** `src/usher/services/restore.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/backup.py`, `src/usher/cli.py` (the seventeenth subparser, a `_restore` coroutine, a `_dispatch` arm), `tests/unit/test_services_restore.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_restore.py`, `tests/integration/test_restore.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py` (the command table)

**Restore's normal path is not an empty database, and the spec's acceptance
criterion is wrong about this.** `2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:368-369`
asks for *"an artifact that `usher restore` loads into an empty database"*.
Into an empty database `watch_states.title_id` has nothing to reference —
the FK is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (ADR-0010) and the insert fails — so the criterion
as written describes an operation that **cannot succeed for the one table PRD 08
calls load-bearing**. The honest criterion, and the one this task delivers, is
`08-operations.md:586`'s own sentence: *"a short restore plus a background
rebuild"*. The catalog is rebuilt first by the importers; **restore then lands
the precious rows on top of it.** The spec's criterion is corrected in the
milestone's acceptance list.

`usher restore <artifact> [--dry-run]`. Four refusals, in order, all before any
write:

1. **Unreadable or truncated artifact** — a header that is not JSON, or a row
   that is not. One line, exit 1.
2. **Schema mismatch** — `header.schema_revision != database_revision(session)`.
   The message names both revisions, exactly as `health.py:80-83` does.
   ⚠️ Compared against the **database's** revision and not against
   `code_head_revision()`: a restore run from a container whose code is ahead of
   the database is already a broken deployment, and `/health/ready` is the thing
   that says so.
3. **Unknown table** — a `table` key the manifest does not classify, which is
   what an artifact from a future schema looks like.
4. **Unresolved references** — K2's refusals, collected across the whole file
   and reported **together**, not on the first one. A restore that fails on the
   first missing title tells an operator to enrich one title; a restore that
   reports 41 missing titles tells them the catalog is not finished.

**One transaction for the whole file.** The session is `_session_for`'s
(`cli.py:305-322`) — one engine, one session, disposed however the command ends
— and every write goes through it with a single commit at the end. That is what
makes *"refuses rather than half-applies"* a property of the code rather than a
promise: an unresolved reference in the last row rolls back the first.
⚠️ **This means the failure mode of a very large artifact is memory**, and the
bound is stated rather than discovered: the precious set is small by
construction (K1's counts), and `--dry-run` resolves everything and commits
nothing, which is the operator's way of learning what would be refused without
holding a transaction open while they think about it.

**The merge rules, per table, because "insert" is wrong for three of them:**

| table | rule |
|---|---|
| `users` | insert if `name` is absent; otherwise adopt the target's existing id for every reference in the file |
| `sources`, `source_credentials` | insert if the source id is absent; **refuse** if a *different* source already holds that name, because two sources pointing at one server is a mess an operator must resolve |
| `watch_states` | upsert on `uq_watch_states_user_title` / `uq_watch_states_user_episode` |
| `media_items` links | **update only where the target's `title_id` is `NULL`.** Never over a link the target already has — K1's asymmetry argument |
| `row_provider_settings` | upsert on `slug_prefix`. The one table with no id in it at all |
| `llm_calls` | insert on `id`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Append-only and idempotent, which is what makes restoring the same ledger twice safe |
| `search_queries` | insert on `id`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `clicked_title_id` nulled where unresolved |

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_restore.py::test_a_watch_state_whose_title_is_missing_refuses_the_whole_file_and_writes_nothing`
— builds an artifact holding two watch states, one for a title the target has
and one for `tt9999999` which it does not, runs restore, and asserts (a) the
refusal names `tt9999999`, (b) `SELECT count(*) FROM watch_states` on a
**second session** is 0, so the assertion is about a committed state and not
about the transaction it ran in — the shape M9's G1 used with a second
connection — and (c) `assert resolvable_title is not None`, the premise that the
*other* watch state would have landed. Without (c) the case is satisfied by a
restore that writes nothing under any circumstances. At HEAD
`usher.services.restore` does not exist.

**Acceptance:**
- `usher restore` refuses on a schema mismatch naming both revisions, and the
  case plants `m09e` into the header to prove the comparison is live rather than
  a string the writer and reader happen to agree on.
- `--dry-run` prints the identical report and commits nothing, asserted from a
  second session.
- Restoring the same artifact twice is a no-op on the second run: every count
  identical, asserted table by table. This is what `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on
  `llm_calls` buys and it must be a case, because the operator's instinct after
  a partial failure is to run it again.
- The report separates **written**, **skipped as already present** and
  **refused**, with the refused list naming keys. Three counts, because
  *"restored 9 rows"* over an artifact holding 50 is the failure this whole
  task exists to make visible.
- `usher restore` is inside ADR-0026's boundary with no handler of its own, and
  gains its own `_dispatch`-arm case.
- Sweep targets: moving the commit inside the per-table loop must fail the
  headline case; collecting only the first unresolved reference must fail the
  *count* in the multi-refusal case (and **not** the presence assertion — the
  two are different claims and the case asserts the number); reading
  `code_head_revision()` instead of `database_revision()` must fail the
  mismatch case; the control is swapping the two independent `RefusalReport`
  keyword arguments, `_ledger_row`'s precedent.
