### Task K5 — the restore drill, against a scratch container, with the dev database untouched

**Depends on:** K4   **Files:** `docs/runbooks/restore.md`, `docs/runbooks/disaster-recovery.md`, `.claude/rules/db-and-sql.md` (the drill's findings), `/var/tmp/m10-K5/` (the transcript, outside the repo)

**Nothing in `src/` changes.** This is Phase 2's own gate line — *"the restore
drill succeeds into a scratch `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` container"*
(`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:342`) — run for real and written up.

⚠️ **`usher-postgres-1` is the development database and holds 1,272,401 titles,
3,256,676 neighbour rows and 130,749 cached payloads.** It publishes **no host
port** (`docker ps` shows `5432/tcp`, unmapped) and is reachable only at its
container IP, `172.26.0.2` on this host today. Rebuilding it costs
`bootstrap --phase all` plus **105.9 minutes** of embedding backfill plus a
**21.6-hour** neighbour walk (`08-operations.md:666`). **The drill does not
write to it.** It reads it once, to take a real backup, and everything else
happens in a scratch container started for the drill and removed afterwards.

**The drill has two arms and the second is the one that proves the design.**

**Arm 1 — the synthetic arm, which is where the refusals live.** Start a
scratch `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`, `alembic upgrade head` into it, seed a small
catalog (a dozen titles with known `imdb_id`s, one series with seasons and
episodes, one title with **neither** provider id, and one household), take a
backup, drop and recreate the schema, rebuild only the catalog, restore.
Deliberately included: a watch state on a title the rebuild **does not**
produce, so the refusal path is exercised end to end and the count in the
report is checked against a number written down first.

**Arm 2 — the real artifact.** `usher backup` against `usher-postgres-1`,
read-only, then `usher restore` of that file into the scratch container after a
minimal catalog build. Predicted outcome, **written to `/var/tmp/m10-K5/BAR.md`
with a `sha256` before the run** (`/tmp` is tmpfs on this host and a bar whose
value is that it predates the numbers may not live there): 1 user, 1 source, 1
credential row, 9 search queries and up to 180 media-item links carried; 0
watch states and 0 `llm_calls`, because both tables are empty today; and
**every one of the 180 links refused**, because the scratch catalog will not
hold the titles they point at. That last prediction is the interesting one — if
the links land, the resolver is matching something it should not.

**Two things the drill must confirm rather than assume**, both of which are
cheap and both of which have bitten this project:

- **`usher.__file__` resolves under this worktree** before any run. A `uv run`
  reaching another checkout produces a complete, plausible, wrong result — M9's
  G2 recorded it.
- **The scratch container's `alembic_version` is `m09f`** and the artifact's
  header says the same, read back from the database rather than from the file
  that wrote it.

**Failing test first:** this task's deliverable is an observation, not a unit
test, and it says so — the same treatment the spec gives panel verification
(`:392-394`). What it *does* commit is
`tests/integration/test_restore_drill.py::test_a_backup_of_a_seeded_household_restores_into_a_rebuilt_catalog`,
which is arm 1 compressed into the existing `postgres_url` fixture: seed,
backup to a `tmp_path`, `TRUNCATE` the precious tables, restore, assert every
carried row is back **and that the ids are different from the ones backed up**
— which is the assertion that says the natural-key path ran rather than a raw-id
copy. Its positive control is `assert restored.written >= 4` plus
`assert original_ids != restored_ids`, and it is red at HEAD because
`usher restore` does not exist. ⚠️ The truncate-and-restore shape is
deliberately **not** drop-and-rebuild: `tests/integration/conftest.py:178`'s
session runs in a rolled-back transaction, and a case that re-ran migrations
inside it would be testing the fixture.

**Acceptance:**
- `docs/runbooks/restore.md` exists — the directory does not today — and is
  written **from the drill's transcript**, with the real commands and the real
  output, not from the design. It states the `USHER_SECRET_KEY` dependency, the
  rebuild-then-restore order, and what a refusal report means.
- `docs/runbooks/disaster-recovery.md` states the recovery time honestly:
  restore is seconds, the catalog is `bootstrap --phase all`, and **the
  neighbour table is 21.6 hours** — so the sequence is restore → serve →
  backfill, and search is degraded rather than absent in between (`08-operations.md:304`).
- Both arms are recorded with their predictions and their outcomes, refutations
  first, and the drill's findings are appended to `.claude/rules/db-and-sql.md`.
- `docker ps` shows the scratch container removed and `usher-postgres-1`
  untouched — its `alembic_version` and its `titles` count read back identical
  to the values in this task's own opening paragraph.

### Task K6 — `docs/runbooks/` gets the two remaining documents, and the README's backup sentence is prepared but not landed

**Depends on:** K5   **Files:** `docs/runbooks/README.md`, `docs/runbooks/upgrade.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** a pointer to `docs/runbooks/`), `tests/unit/test_runbooks.py`

The spec asks for four runbooks — *"restore, upgrade, disaster recovery,
rotation"* (`:193-194`). K5 writes two, K8 writes rotation, and this task
writes the fourth plus the index.

**`upgrade.md` is the one with a real finding in it, and it is not about
backup.** The container's `CMD` is `alembic upgrade head && exec python -m usher`
(`Dockerfile:96`) and the Dockerfile's own comment says it has **no
distributed lock** and that `/health/ready`'s mismatch check *"would at least
surface a lost race as a 503 rather than silently serving against the wrong
schema, but it does not prevent the race itself."* So the upgrade procedure for
this deployment is: back up first, `docker compose down`, pull, `up`, and check
`/health/ready` — and the reason to back up first is now a command rather than
a `pg_dump` an operator has no client for. `m09e` is the worked example the
runbook uses, because it is the migration in this project's history that
**deleted every embedding, centroid and neighbour row** (ADR-0038) and is
therefore the one where *"back up first"* would not have helped and *"know what
you are about to lose"* would.

**A test over prose, and its bar is deliberately low.**
`tests/unit/test_runbooks.py::test_every_runbook_the_index_names_exists_and_every_runbook_is_indexed`
reads `docs/runbooks/README.md`'s links, resolves them, and asserts the set
equals the `.md` files in the directory minus the index. It is the same shape as
the PRD link check and it exists for the same reason: a runbook index that
points at a file somebody renamed is worse than no index. Its positive control
is `assert len(indexed) >= 4`.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_runbooks.py::test_every_runbook_the_index_names_exists_and_every_runbook_is_indexed`
— fails at HEAD because `docs/runbooks/` does not exist, which the case reports
as a named `FileNotFoundError` rather than as an empty-set comparison that
would pass. That distinction is the case's whole design: `set() == set()` is
true, and a scan over a missing directory must be a failure and not a pass.

**Acceptance:**
- Four runbooks and an index. Each names the commands it uses and each command
  exists in `build_parser()` — asserted by
  `test_every_command_a_runbook_names_is_a_command_the_cli_advertises`, which
  extracts `uv run usher <word>` occurrences and checks the subcommand list,
  with a premise that it found at least six such occurrences.
- The README sentence PRD 08:585-588 asks for (*"state this loudly in the
  README"*) is **drafted in `docs/runbooks/README.md` and not yet added to
  `README.md`** — Phase 3 owns the README pass (`:208-211`), and two groups
  editing `README.md` in one phase is a merge conflict for no benefit. The
  draft is one paragraph and Phase 3 moves it.
- `docs/prd/08-operations.md` gains a single pointer line to
  `docs/runbooks/README.md`; the PRD link check prints `OK`.

### Task K7 — `usher rotate-secret`, holding two ciphers, over the one table that has ciphertext in it

**Depends on:** K1   **Files:** `src/usher/services/rotation.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/credentials.py` (a `list_refs` method only), `src/usher/ports/credentials.py`, `src/usher/cli.py` (subparser, `_rotate`, `_dispatch` arm), `tests/unit/test_services_rotation.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_rotation.py`, `tests/integration/test_rotation.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py` (the command table)

**What `USHER_SECRET_KEY` actually protects, surveyed rather than assumed.**
`grep -rn "secret_key" src` finds every reader, and there are exactly **two**
derivations, both HKDF-SHA256 over the same input with different `info`
strings:

| derivation | `info` | what it encrypts | persisted? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `db/repositories/credentials.py:56` `build_cipher` | `b"usher.source-credentials.v1"` (`:53`) | a JSON `{username, password}` blob → `source_credentials.ciphertext` (`:75-82`) | **yes** |
| `services/playback_ticket.py:79` `build_ticket_cipher` | `b"usher.playback-ticket.v1"` (`:76`) | a playback target URL inside a 300-second ticket | **no** |

**So rotation touches exactly one table and, on this deployment, exactly one
row.** `SELECT count(*) FROM source_credentials` on `usher-postgres-1` is
**1**, and the table is 48 kB. The upper bound is one row per configured
source; PRD 08's deployment shape is a household with a small number of media
servers. **This is a command whose whole job is measured in single-digit rows,
and saying so plainly is what stops it being over-built.**

The ticket cipher needs no rotation and that is a fact about its lifetime, not
an omission: a ticket lives 300 seconds (`TICKET_TTL_SECONDS: Final = 300`,
confirmed live in M9's H4 at 127 s honoured / 312 s refused), it is never
stored, and rotating the key invalidates every outstanding one — which renders
as a `404 ticket_invalid` the client answers by asking `/play` again. The
rotation report says so in one line; the command does nothing about it.

⚠️ **`api/cursor.py:23` records that `Settings.secret_key` is deliberately
*not* what signs a keyset cursor**, so cursors are outside this entirely.
Checked rather than assumed, because a cursor is the other opaque string in
this API and the obvious wrong guess.

**`build_cipher` was made public for exactly this** — its docstring says so
(`credentials.py:57-62`: *"Module-level and public so a rotation command (PRD
08's 'a documented rotation command handles the bulk case') can build both the
old and the new cipher without instantiating two repositories"*) — and it has
had **no caller in `src/`** since M3, which assigned this command to M10 by name
(`docs/plans/2026-07-30-m3-emby-adapter.md:7926`). This task is the seam's first
caller.

**The design. Two ciphers, one row at a time, and a re-read before every
write.** `usher rotate-secret --new-key-env USHER_NEW_SECRET_KEY` reads the new
key from the **environment** and never from an argument, because a key on a
command line is in the shell history and in `ps` output; `--new-key-env` names
the variable, so the value never appears in `argv`. For each `ref`: read the row,
decrypt with the old cipher, re-encrypt with the new, write. The rotation
service holds two `Fernet` objects and no plaintext key — `build_cipher`
unwraps `get_secret_value()` exactly once inside itself and retains only the
HKDF output (`credentials.py:28-32`), which is the property that makes holding
two ciphers safe.

**Resumability, which is the part the brief asks for and the part the shape
makes almost free.** *"What happens if rotation is interrupted halfway"* has a
good answer here and a bad one, and the design picks the good one:

- **Per-row commit, not one transaction.** The opposite of K4, deliberately.
  A single transaction over N rows means an interrupted rotation leaves *every*
  row on the old key while the operator has already changed `USHER_SECRET_KEY`
  in their `.env` — which is total credential loss on the next restart. Per-row
  commit means an interruption leaves a **mixed** state, and a mixed state is
  recoverable.
- **A mixed state is diagnosable, not silent.** Fernet's authentication tag
  makes a wrong key an `InvalidToken` → `PortDataMalformed`
  (`credentials.py:22-27`), and `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status` already renders
  it as an unreachable, unauthenticated source with a re-enter-your-credentials
  detail (`08-operations.md:234-241`). So the half-rotated deployment degrades
  into the exact screen it was designed to degrade into.
- **Re-running is the recovery, and it needs no ledger.** Each row is tried
  with the **new** cipher first and skipped if it already decrypts; only then is
  the old one tried. So a second run over a half-rotated table finishes it, and
  a run over a fully-rotated table is a no-op that reports *N already rotated*.
  **The resumption story is a decrypt attempt, and there is no state to keep.**
- **A row that decrypts under neither key is refused, named and counted**, and
  the command exits 1 having rotated the rest. That is the row an operator has
  to re-enter, and it must not be hidden behind a success.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_services_rotation.py::test_a_row_already_written_under_the_new_key_is_skipped_rather_than_double_encrypted`
— seeds two credential rows, rotates the first by hand, runs the rotation, and
asserts (a) both rows decrypt under the new key afterwards, (b) the report says
`rotated=1, already=1`, and (c) the ciphertext of the pre-rotated row is
**byte-identical** to what it was before. (c) is the assertion with teeth: a
service that re-encrypts an already-rotated row still leaves it readable, so
(a) and (b) alone are satisfied by the double-encryption defect this case is
named for. Its premise is `assert before != after_first_row_ciphertext` for the
row that genuinely moves, so *"the ciphertext did not change"* is a claim about
this row rather than about a service that writes nothing. At HEAD
`usher.services.rotation` does not exist.

**Acceptance:**
- `usher rotate-secret --new-key-env <VAR>` rotates every `source_credentials`
  row, commits per row, and prints `rotated / already / refused` with the
  refused refs named. **The new key never appears in `argv`**, asserted by a
  case that runs the parser and greps the namespace.
- The new key is validated by the same rules as the old before anything is
  written: `min_length=32` and the placeholder rejection
  (`config.py:152`, `:891-893`). A rotation to a key `Settings` would refuse is
  a rotation that bricks the next start, and the refusal must arrive **before**
  the first row is touched, not from pydantic at the next boot. Asserted from a
  second session: nothing written.
- No credential and no key reaches stdout, a log line or an exception message.
  `test_the_rotation_report_names_refs_and_never_a_credential` asserts the
  report contains the `ref` and asserts a canary password is absent — with the
  canary's *presence* in the seeded row asserted first, so the absence claim is
  about a value that could have appeared.
- `usher rotate-secret` is inside ADR-0026's boundary with no handler of its
  own, has its own `_dispatch`-arm case, and its row in
  `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py`'s parametrised table.
- ⚠️ `PortDataMalformed` is **not** in `OPERATOR_ERRORS` (`cli.py:136-138`) and
  this task does not add it. A row that decrypts under neither key is handled
  by the command itself and rendered as a counted refusal — which is the
  per-command handling ADR-0026 permits (`:234-240`, the `_vocabulary_line` and
  `_curate` precedent), as distinct from the per-command *boundary* it rejects.
- Sweep targets: trying the old cipher first must fail the headline case;
  committing once at the end must fail the interruption case in K8; validating
  the new key after the first write must fail the validation case on a second
  session; the control is swapping the two independent `build_cipher` calls in
  the service's `__init__`, a fact about the code since both are pure HKDF
  derivations over two distinct parameters with nothing between them.

---

### Task K8 — the rotation drill, and PRD 08:232's sentence corrected from "re-encrypts on next write" to what the code does

**Depends on:** K7, and K5 for the directory   **Files:** `docs/runbooks/rotation.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** the `### Secrets` bullet at lines 232-241), `tests/integration/test_rotation.py`, `.claude/rules/config-cli-and-deployment.md`

**PRD 08:232 says something the code does not do, and it is the sentence this
whole task pair was written from.** *"Rotating `USHER_SECRET_KEY` re-encrypts on
next write; a documented rotation command handles the bulk case."* The first
clause is not true in the sense a reader takes it. There is no write path that
notices a key changed: `PostgresCredentialStore.put` (`credentials.py:74-99`)
encrypts with whatever cipher the store was built with, and it is called by the
admin source routes when an operator **submits a credential**. So *"re-encrypts
on next write"* means *"a credential you re-type is stored under the new key"* —
which is true and is not rotation, and reads as a mechanism that keeps a
deployment working while an operator gets around to it. It does not: until that
write happens the row is unreadable, which the **second half of the same bullet**
(`:233-241`) already says clearly. The two halves of one bullet describe
different worlds and this task reconciles them.

**The drill.** Against a scratch `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`, not against
`usher-postgres-1` — whose single `source_credentials` row is a real credential
for the operator's real Emby, and re-encrypting it under a throwaway key and
back is a write to production state for no benefit. Seed three credential rows
in the scratch container, then run four arms:

1. **The happy path.** Rotate, restart nothing, read all three back through
   `PostgresCredentialStore` built with the new key. All three decrypt; the
   canary passwords are byte-identical to what was seeded.
2. **The interruption.** Kill the command after the first row commits —
   spelled as an injected failure on the second row, not as a signal, because
   *"a run that did not run is not a pass"* and a killed process leaves nothing
   to assert on. Then: read back with the **old** key (rows 2 and 3 decrypt,
   row 1 raises `PortDataMalformed`), read back with the **new** key (row 1
   decrypts, rows 2 and 3 raise), and confirm both raises name the `ref` and
   neither names the payload (`credentials.py:113-118`).
3. **The resumption.** Re-run the same command over the mixed state. Report
   reads `rotated=2, already=1`; all three decrypt under the new key; row 1's
   ciphertext is unchanged.
4. **The unrecoverable row.** Corrupt one row's ciphertext, rotate, and assert
   the command names it, exits 1, and **still rotates the other two** — because
   a rotation that aborts on one bad row leaves an operator with a worse mixed
   state than the one they started with.

Arms 2 and 3 are the ones that go in the runbook, because they are the states
an operator will actually be in when they read it.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_rotation.py::test_an_interrupted_rotation_leaves_a_mixed_table_that_a_second_run_finishes`
— arm 2 and arm 3 as one case against the real migrated database: three rows,
an injected failure on the second, an assertion that row 1 is on the new key
and rows 2-3 on the old **read through two separately-constructed stores** (one
per key, so the claim is about the ciphertext and not about a cached cipher),
then a second rotation and an assertion that all three decrypt under the new key
with row 1's ciphertext byte-identical. At HEAD `usher.services.rotation` does
not exist. Its premise, asserted before the injected failure: all three rows
decrypt under the **old** key, so the mixed state is a thing the run produced
and not a thing the fixture was.

**Acceptance:**
- `docs/runbooks/rotation.md` exists, written from the drill transcript, and
  its ordering is explicit and load-bearing: **generate the new key → run
  `usher rotate-secret` while `USHER_SECRET_KEY` is still the old one → only
  then change `.env` and restart.** An operator who changes `.env` first has a
  deployment that cannot read its own credentials and a rotation command that
  cannot decrypt anything, which is the failure this order exists to prevent
  and which the runbook states in those words.
- The runbook names the two things rotation does **not** cover: outstanding
  playback tickets (invalidated, 300-second lifetime, client re-asks) and
  **backup artifacts** — a file taken under the old key restores ciphertext the
  new key cannot read (K3's warning, arriving from the other direction). The
  documented sequence is: rotate, then take a fresh backup, then discard the
  old one.
- `docs/prd/08-operations.md:232` is rewritten in this commit: the misleading
  first clause replaced by *"nothing re-encrypts an existing row on its own;
  `usher rotate-secret` is the only thing that does, and until it runs the old
  rows are unreadable"*, with the existing `:233-241` degradation text kept
  intact and now agreeing with the sentence above it. The PRD link check
  prints `OK`.
- `.claude/rules/config-cli-and-deployment.md` gains the drill's findings under
  the existing settings/CLI material — specifically the `.env`-first ordering
  trap, which is a deployment fact and not a rotation fact.
- The scratch container is removed; `usher-postgres-1`'s `source_credentials`
  row is byte-identical to what it was before the task, verified by comparing
  `md5(ciphertext)` before and after. **A drill that touched production state
  is a drill that has to prove it did not.**
# Group D — the telemetry gaps, the five dashboards, and the seven alerts

Phase 2. Fifteen tasks: one audit, five instrumentation gaps, five dashboards, four
alert tasks covering seven rules.

**Three corrections to the spec and the PRD, made here rather than left standing.**

1. **PRD 10's metric table is 35 rows, not the 34 the spec claims twice**
   (`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:32`, `:86`). Counted by AST-free `grep -c` over
   `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:143–177`: **35 metric names**, of which **34 carry the
   `usher.` prefix** and one — `http.server.duration` — deliberately does not
   (`:179–192`). The spec's 34 is almost certainly the count through
   `tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py:79`'s `_ROW` regex, which is anchored on
   `` `(usher\.[a-z0-9._]+)` `` and cannot see the one row that matters most to Group O's
   semconv pinning. **The two numbers to carry are "35 rows, 34 of them `usher.`-prefixed"**,
   and every catalogue-parsing test in this group must state which it counted.
2. **The spec's other two "wrong numbers" are not wrong.** `.env.example:290` ships
   `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` empty and `:291` ships `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME=usher` set —
   exactly what the spec says at `:48`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is **46 lines**, exactly
   what the spec says at `:205`, and `:46` is also the `uv run pytest --cov=usher` line, so
   the spec's `ci.yml:46` at `:179` is a correct line reference that happens to equal the
   file length. Both were re-measured before being reported as correct, because a
   correction asserted against a document that was right is the same defect as a number
   nobody checked.
3. **`provider_cache_meta` does not exist and was refused by name.** The spec's risk
   paragraph (`:424`) says *"`provider_cache_meta` tracks a live ≤ 6-month compliance
   obligation"*. [ADR-0016](../prd/decisions/0016-raw-payloads-cache-providers-not-sources.md)`:26`
   decided **"No `provider_cache_meta` table is created"**, and
   `src/usher/db/models/sync.py:90–94` restates it at the column that replaced it. The
   compliance series is `raw_payloads.fetched_at`, served by `ix_raw_payloads_fetched_at`
   (`sync.py:115`), whose own comment names PRD 10 dashboard 5. D5 carries the correction.

**Group O owns the semconv pin and this group consumes it.**
`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:193–199` records that `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http`
renames `http.server.duration` → `http.server.request.duration`, moves its unit from `ms`
to seconds and swaps `http.target` for `http.route`. Nothing in `.env.example`, `compose.yml`
or `config.py` sets it. **Every panel in D9 and every alert threshold in D11–D14 is
authored against the convention Phase 0 records, and none of them may be written before
that record exists.** The dependency is stated per task rather than assumed.

**Files this group shares with others, declared so the orchestrator can sequence rather
than discover:** `src/usher/telemetry.py` (D1, and Group O's exporter work),
`src/usher/services/home.py` (D2 only, the propose loop),
`src/usher/ports/repository/llm_call.py` and `src/usher/db/repositories/llm_call.py` (D3),
`tests/unit/test_ports.py` (D3's guard retirement — **only** the case at `:338` and the
parametrisation entry at `:208–219`), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (D0, D4,
D5 and every dashboard task, at disjoint anchors named per task), and the new
`dashboards/` directory, which does not exist today and is created by D6.

**Migration `m10a` belongs to Group J and this group does not carry it.** D3 needs
`ix_llm_calls_at` and the partial `ix_llm_calls_generation_id`, both written out as
copy-pasteable DDL in `m08a`'s own docstring
(`src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m08a_curation.py:206–216`). D3 depends on J's migration
landing; it adds no revision of its own.

---

### Task D0 — audit D1 and D2's panels against the live catalogue, and the interesting half is that the two dashboards fail for different reasons

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** none in `src/`; writes `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the two paragraphs appended after `:802` and after `:811`) and `tests/unit/test_prd_10_panel_backing.py`

PRD 10 annotates per-panel backing for three of its five dashboards and not for the other
two. D3 carries it at `:830–839`, D4 at `:848–873`, D5 at `:885–902`, and all three are ✅
or carry a named caveat. **D1 (`:795–802`) and D2 (`:804–811`) carry no backing statement
at all**, so a panel with no series behind it is indistinguishable from one nobody has
written yet — which is this document's own first principle (`:136–139`) applied to panels
instead of to metrics. This task is scheduled first so the answer arrives as a task list
rather than as an empty panel at the Phase 2 gate.

**The audit was run while scoping this plan and its result is stated here rather than left
for the implementer to rediscover**; the task is to *verify* it, write it into PRD 10 in
the shape D3/D4/D5 already use, and pin it with a test. **D1's eleven panels are all
backed. D2's eight are not: three have no series, one is mis-titled, and one has a
nullable denominator.**

**D1 — Library & Catalog, eleven panels, eleven backed.** Titles by enrichment state is
`titles.enrichment_state` (`src/usher/db/models/title.py:166`); owned-vs-catalog is
`media_items.available`/`title_id` (`src/usher/db/models/source.py:72`, `:99`); genre,
decade, language and runtime are `titles.genres` (`:98`), `titles.year` (`:68`),
`titles.original_language`/`spoken_languages` (`:104`, `:107`) and
`titles.runtime_minutes` (`:74`). **The quality ladder is the one a reader expects to be
missing and it is not**: `media_items` carries `container`, `video_codec`, `audio_codec`,
`width`, `height`, `hdr_format`, `audio_channels`, `file_size_bytes`
(`source.py:85–92`), all filled by `src/usher/adapters/emby/mapping.py:417–423` from
Emby's `MediaStreams`, and a real `hdr_format = DV` row from a live `VideoRange:
"DolbyVision"` is recorded in `.claude/rules/emby-push-and-ingest.md:545`. Franchise
completeness is `titles.collection_id` (`title.py:131`) joined to `collections`; most-
represented directors and actors are `credits.kind`/`department`/`job` joined to `people`;
library growth is `media_items.added_at` (`source.py:95`); unmatched depth is
`ix_media_items_unmatched` and `list_unmatched`, already annotated at `:836–839` with the
`OFFSET` measurement (43.7 ms at offset 0 against **388.9 ms at 1,126,574**).

**Two caveats D1's paragraph must state, because both are denominators rather than
absences.** `titles.collection_id` and every `credits` row arrive from TMDb enrichment, so
franchise completeness and the credits panels are bounded by the enriched tier — **130,647
of 1,272,367 titles, 10.3%**, on the catalog `08-operations.md:657` measures. A franchise
whose other entries were never enriched reads as complete. And `media_items.added_at` is
nullable (`source.py:95`), so a growth curve silently omits any item whose source reported
no `DateCreated`.

**D2 — Taste & Watching, eight panels, three with no series.** The root cause of two of
the three is one fact worth stating once: **this schema has 28 tables and none of them is
a play-event log.** `watch_states` (`src/usher/db/models/watch.py:40–106`) is one row per
`(user, title)` or `(user, episode)` — unique-constrained as such at `:110–111` — carrying
`position_seconds`, `runtime_seconds`, `played`, `play_count` and a single
`last_played_at`. It is a *current state*, not a history.

- **"Watch time by day and user" has no backing series.** Minutes attributable to a day
  require a row per play; the only date any row carries is the last one, and `play_count`
  carries none at all.
- **"Taste drift as genre affinity in a stacked area over months" has no backing series**,
  for the same reason and with a sharper failure mode: a rewatch **overwrites**
  `last_played_at`, so the panel is not merely coarse, it **systematically empties toward
  the past** — every item watched again since has moved forward out of its own month. A
  stacked area over it reads as taste that did not exist before last quarter.
- **"Row effectiveness: plays attributed per `RowProvider`" has no backing series, and PRD
  10 already says so in its own words at `:778–787`**: *"This column is joined to a
  **search**, not to a row: `search_queries` has no row slug, no `generation_id` and no
  provider, and a play launched from a home shelf carries no `search_id` at all"*. The
  audit's contribution here is to move that admission from a paragraph about `played` into
  D2's own backing statement, where the panel is specified.
- **"Time-of-day heatmap" is backed and mis-titled.** `last_played_at` gives one hour per
  item — the hour of its *last* play — so the honest panel is *"when each item was last
  played"*, not *"when this household watches"*. Backed, retitled.
- **"Abandonment cliff" is backed with a nullable denominator.**
  `position_seconds / runtime_seconds` over `played = false`, and `runtime_seconds` on
  `watch_states` is nullable by [ADR-0014](../prd/decisions/0014-absence-is-not-zero.md)
  because a walk's listing cannot determine it. The fallbacks are
  `media_items.runtime_seconds` (`source.py:93`) then `titles.runtime_minutes` × 60, and
  the panel must state which it used or a null denominator silently drops the row.
- Completion rate (`watch_states.played`, `:84`), rewatches (`play_count > 1`, `:87`) and
  longest unwatched (`media_items.added_at` with no `watch_states` row) are backed
  outright.

**The three unbacked panels do not become Group D build tasks and this is the audit's
actual output.** A play-event log is a schema change with a writer, a retention policy and
a size argument — `POST /titles|episodes/{id}/play` is the only surface that could write
one — and row attribution needs a handle `GET /home` does not hand out, which
`:786–787` already calls *"a PRD amendment rather than a follow-up"*. **Both are M11
issues opened by this task, and D7 ships Dashboard 2 with five panels and a stated
absence rather than with three permanently empty ones.** Shipping an empty panel is
precisely the failure PRD 10's preamble exists to prevent, one layer up from the metric it
was written about.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_prd_10_panel_backing.py::test_every_dashboard_section_carries_a_backing_statement`
— parses `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` for the five `^### \d — ` headings under
`## Dashboards` and asserts each section contains at least one paragraph opening with a
bolded backing claim (the shape D3/D4/D5 use: `**… backed by real data as of …**` or
`**… is still unbacked**`). At HEAD it fails naming sections 1 and 2. Its positive control
is `assert len(sections) == 5, "the dashboard section scan found nothing"` plus a named
anchor (`"Cost & Compliance" in headings`) — **a scan that globs nothing passes exactly
like a scan that passes**, and this file's headings are markdown, which the next reformat
can move.

**Acceptance:**
- PRD 10 gains exactly two paragraphs, one after `:802` and one after `:811`, in D3/D4/D5's
  own shape. No other line of the file moves; `git diff --stat` shows one file plus two
  new test/issue artefacts.
- D1's paragraph names all eleven panels, ticks all eleven, and states the two denominator
  caveats (the 10.3% enriched tier, `added_at`'s nullability) with their measurements.
- D2's paragraph names all eight, ticks five, marks **three as unbacked with the reason and
  the issue number**, and retitles the time-of-day panel. The word "⏳" is not used for any
  of the three — ⏳ means *owed by a named milestone*, and none of these is owed by M10.
- Two issues opened: one for a play-event log (naming `watch_states`' unique constraints at
  `watch.py:110–111` as the reason the current table cannot carry one), one for row
  attribution on `POST /play` (citing `:786–787`).
- **The report back to the plan names which panels have no backing metric.** That list is
  this task's deliverable; the PRD paragraphs are its written form.

---

### Task D1 — a metric series for the suggest path, and the number PRD 10 quotes for it belongs to a tier the route does not default to

**Depends on:** Group O's semconv record   **Files:** `src/usher/services/search.py` (the instrument block at `:95–116` and `suggest` at `:888–957`), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the metric table at `:141–177` and the bullet at `:216–223`), `tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py`

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:216–223` records that **there is no series for the
type-ahead path at all** — `SuggestIndex` is its own port
([ADR-0021](../prd/decisions/0021-the-suggest-path-is-its-own-port.md)) and M6
emitted nothing for it — and argues the gate's measured latency makes it worth closing:
*"p50 33.6 ms / p95 211 ms / max 730 ms at 1.27M names, against the 50 ms as-you-type
budget"*. **The three numbers are real and the sentence around them is stale, and that is
what decides this task's design.**

Verified against source: 33.6 / 211 / 730 is the `GIN % @0.3 cap 200 + vote tiebreak` row
of `.claude/rules/search-and-embeddings.md:59`, i.e. **tier 2's whole-name figures**, and
[ADR-0031](../prd/decisions/0031-the-two-tier-suggest.md)`:304` says so in exactly
those words — *"Its 33.6 ms p50 / 211 ms p95 / 730 ms max are whole-name figures"*. M9's
route defaults to **tier 1** (`src/usher/api/routers/search.py:317–400`;
`services/search.py:906–907` states the two boundaries' opposite defaults and why), and
tier 1's cost is a function of prefix length that spans three orders of magnitude:
`ADR-0031:192–193` measures the shipped union at **2,707 ms p95 at one character, 809 at
two, 303 at three, 112 at four, 2.3 ms at seven**. Four is the shipped minimum precisely
because 112 ms is the shortest length at which tier 1 is cheaper than the tier it exists
to be cheaper than.

**So a single unlabelled histogram over this route would be a mixture of two populations
whose ratio is decided by client debounce behaviour, differing by ~50× at p50 and ~24× at
p95.** The series carries `tier`, and it carries it as a *label* rather than as two
instrument names for the reason `usher.row.build.duration` carries `provider`
(`:112`): a group-by is one query and two names are two panels that cannot be summed.

**Two instruments, mirroring the pair M6 already shipped for search**, and the shape is
copied deliberately rather than invented: `services/search.py:106` and `:115` declare
`usher.search.duration` (histogram, `unit="s"`, labelled `mode`) and
`usher.search.results` (histogram, `unit="1"`), and that file's comment block at `:97–105`
records that the near misses which cost M4 three corrections are singular/plural and
by-analogy names. The near misses this pair invites are `usher.suggest.latency` (by
analogy with `usher.enrichment.latency`), `usher.search.suggest.duration` (which would put
the type-ahead box inside every `usher.search.*` panel — the exact objection `:600–604`
raises against reusing `SearchMode`), and `usher.suggest.result` singular.

**The names are `usher.suggest.duration` and `usher.suggest.results`, both labelled
`tier`.** `tier`'s vocabulary is `SuggestTier`'s own two lower-case members, `prefix` and
`fuzzy` — written into PRD 10's table beside `mode`'s vocabulary at `:201`, because a label
whose vocabulary is undocumented is a label two call sites spell differently.

**Where they are recorded is the decision the task's tests exist to pin.**
`SearchService.suggest` (`:888`) opens with `if not prefix.strip(): return ()` at `:945`
and then makes exactly three awaits — the tier's `suggest`, `list_by_ids`, and
`owned_title_ids` (`:946–950`). **The measured interval is the whole method, not the tier
call**, because hydration is what a client waits for and it is the same two reads for both
tiers by construction (`:912–917`); and **a blank prefix records no point**, on the rule
`:224–226` states for the search histograms and `:698` enforces one method over — *"a
search box sends one between every keystroke"*, and a blank one counted would make the p50
a measure of how fast somebody types.

⚠️ **This task adds a metric and does not add a `search_queries` row.** The amendment that
does — the `surface`/`tier` columns of Key design decision 3 — is Group J's migration and
another group's writer. The two are independent on purpose: a histogram answers *"is the
box fast"* and the table answers *"what did people type"*, and `:606–610` is explicit that
only the second needs the schema change.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py::test_a_suggest_records_its_duration_and_its_result_count_under_the_tier_that_answered`
— drives `SearchService.suggest` twice through the real service over `FakePrefixSuggestIndex`
and `FakeSuggestIndex` (both already in `tests/fakes/search_index.py` and already imported
by this file at `:44–48`), once per tier, and reads the points back out of the
`meter_reader` fixture at `:96`. It asserts **two** things a weaker case would not: that
each point carries `{"tier": "prefix"}` / `{"tier": "fuzzy"}` respectively, and that the
`usher.suggest.results` point equals the hydrated result count rather than the raw hit
count — the two differ whenever a hit's title is missing from `list_by_ids`, which
`:953–956`'s `if hit.title_id in by_id` filter makes reachable. At HEAD it fails because no
instrument of either name exists. Its positive control is the existing
`_instrument_names(meter_reader)` helper's non-emptiness plus
`"usher.search.duration" in names` — **a reader that recorded nothing gives an empty set
that `<=` accepts silently.**

Two further cases, each pinning a decision rather than a value:
`test_a_blank_prefix_records_no_suggest_point`, which is the `:229`-shaped mirror of the
existing blank-query case and asserts the *port* was not called either; and
`test_the_suggest_series_are_histograms_and_not_counters`, on `:383`'s precedent, because a
different instrument type under a documented name is the same class of failure as a
near-miss name.

**Acceptance:**
- PRD 10's table gains two rows, `usher.suggest.duration` and `usher.suggest.results`,
  both `histogram`, both labelled `tier`, both marked `✅ M10` — and
  `test_prd_10_marks_the_m6_rows_as_shipped` (`:464`) is generalised to every milestone's
  rows rather than copied for M10, so the maintenance rule holds for M11 without a third
  copy. **The table is 37 rows after this task, 36 of them `usher.`-prefixed**; both
  numbers are quoted in the commit body, because the group preamble's correction exists
  precisely so the next reader does not re-derive them.
- `:216–223`'s bullet is rewritten: the gap is closed, the three numbers are re-attributed
  to tier 2 with `ADR-0031:304` as the citation, and tier 1's per-length curve
  (`ADR-0031:192–193`) is named as the reason the label exists. **The claim "the shipped
  suggest path measures p50 33.6 ms" is deleted rather than softened** — the shipped route
  defaults to `prefix`, which that number is not about.
- `uv run mypy src tests` clean; `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py`
  collects and passes at least the cases it collected at HEAD plus the three new ones.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the `tier` label replaced by a constant
  must fail the two-tier case and **nothing else** (predicted, because no other case in the
  repository reads a suggest label); the record moved inside the `if not prefix.strip()`
  guard must fail the blank case alone; the results point recorded as `len(hits)` rather
  than `len(results)` must fail on the hydration arm — ⚠️ **and this one is predicted to
  need a fixture that has never existed**: no case in `tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py`
  seeds a suggest hit whose title `list_by_ids` does not return, so if the mutation
  survives, that is coverage rather than equivalence and the case costs four lines. The
  equivalent-mutant control is swapping the two `create_histogram` calls' definition order
  — a fact about the code (two module-level assignments referencing neither each other nor
  anything between them, in a module with no import-time side effect), deliberately **not**
  an `__all__` reorder, which `RUF022` rejects.

---

### Task D2 — the `propose` span, placed where the report already times it and inherited by two roots rather than one

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/services/home.py` (**only** `_compose` at `:386–429`), `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the bullet at `:113–117` and the span tree at `:39–66`), `tests/unit/test_telemetry_pipeline.py`

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:113–117` records the gap and the reason it was recorded
rather than drawn: *"There is no `propose` span. Proposal runs inside `home.compose` and is
untraced individually, so a provider that is slow to *propose* and cheap to *build* shows
up only in the parent's duration."* D4's per-row breakdown wants it and the number that
makes it worth paying for is already measured: at the scale ceiling
(`.claude/rules/rows-and-genome.md`, 1,277,878 owned items) compose is **p50 710.3 ms /
p95 783.4 ms** with `next-up` costing **302.9 ms to propose** — 39% of the p50 in a phase
the trace cannot see at all. At the 5,200-copy household the same composer is **p50
23.9 ms**, which is the other half of why this is a span and not a decision: the two
households differ by 30× on one composer and only a per-provider breakdown distinguishes
them.

**The placement is exact and it is not where a reader would guess.** The propose loop is
`services/home.py:395–402`, and it already holds the timing this span duplicates:
`entry.propose_seconds += time.perf_counter() - at` at `:399`, feeding
`ProviderReport.propose_seconds` (`:188`) which `usher home` prints. So the span wraps
`await provider.propose(ctx)` at `:397` inside the existing `at`/`entry` bracket, and
carries **`usher.row.provider`** — the `slug_prefix`, the same attribute
`_build` sets at `:452` — plus `usher.row.proposed`, the length of the returned tuple.

Three properties of that placement, each a fact about the code rather than a preference:

- **`start_as_current_span`, never `start_span`**, for the reason `_build`'s docstring
  gives at `:434–437`: the child-of-composition nesting is what makes a trace answer
  *"what did this request do"*. Verified as parentage rather than existence by
  `tests/integration/test_pipeline_spans.py`, which is where this span's assertion belongs
  if it is to mean anything (`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:68–74`).
- **`_compose` is called from two roots and the span inherits both**, which is the
  surprising part. `compose_report` (`:336`) opens `home.compose` and calls `_compose`
  inside it; `rebuild` (`:369–384`) calls `_compose` **with no `home.compose` span at
  all**, deliberately, so that the serve-stale lane's `row.build` spans hang off
  `rows.refresh` instead (`:369–376`, and `:120–129` in PRD 10 records the same asymmetry
  for `row.build`). So a `propose` span emitted from `_compose` has a `home.compose`
  parent on the request path and a `rows.refresh` parent on the refresh path — **and the
  PRD's span tree at `:39–66` must gain it in both places, not one.**
- **The span name is `propose`, matching the tree's existing bare-verb children**
  (`row.build`, `curation.generate`, `index.embed`), and the provider is an attribute
  rather than part of the name for `:104–112`'s reason: a provider-keyed name would put
  ten values where two belong, and `because-you-watched-<seed>` would make it
  catalog-sized.

**A cached screen produces no `propose` span at all**, and the panel must say so. `:327`
short-circuits on a screen hit before `_compose` runs — *"A screen hit does not re-propose,
stale or fresh"* — so the population of `propose` spans is *compositions that happened*,
which is the same population `usher.home.compose.duration` measures and **not** the same as
`row.build`'s, whose population is misses within those compositions.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry_pipeline.py::test_every_provider_that_proposes_opens_a_propose_span_under_the_composition`
— composes a screen over a two-provider registry through a real `TracerProvider` with an
`InMemorySpanExporter`, and asserts (a) one `propose` span per **registered** provider, not
per provider that returned something — a provider proposing nothing must still have a span,
for the reason `ProviderReport` has a line for it at `:171–182` — (b) each span's
`usher.row.provider` is that provider's `slug_prefix`, and (c) **each span's parent is the
`home.compose` span**, asserted through `parent.span_id`, not merely that both exist. At
HEAD it fails on (a) with zero spans found. Its positive control is
`assert exported, "the exporter collected no spans"` plus
`"home.compose" in {span.name for span in exported}`, because an exporter that collected
nothing satisfies every per-span assertion vacuously.

A second case, `test_a_refresh_opens_propose_spans_with_no_home_compose_parent`, drives
`rebuild` and asserts the `propose` spans exist and have **no** `home.compose` ancestor —
the mirror `:120–129` makes for `row.build`, and the assertion that fails if somebody
"fixes" the refresh by minting a `home.compose` nobody asked for.

**Acceptance:**
- PRD 10's span tree at `:39–66` gains `propose` under **both** `home.compose` and
  `rows.refresh`; the bullet at `:113–117` is rewritten from a recorded gap into a shipped
  span with its two attributes named, and the sentence *"a provider that is slow to propose
  and cheap to build shows up only in the parent's duration"* is deleted rather than
  softened.
- `git diff --name-only` names `services/home.py`, PRD 10, and the two test files. **No
  metric is added** — the per-provider propose cost is already a span attribute and a
  `ProviderReport` field, and a third copy as a histogram would be the
  `usher.http.server.duration`-beside-`http.server.duration` mistake `:186–192` refuses.
- `uv run pytest tests/integration/test_pipeline_spans.py` still passes: that file walks
  parent chains and a new intermediate span is exactly the change that breaks such a walk
  if it was written against depth rather than against ancestry.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: `start_as_current_span` → `start_span`
  must fail the parentage arm and nothing else; the span moved outside the `for` so one
  span covers all ten providers must fail the per-provider count; `usher.row.provider` set
  to the class `__name__` rather than the `slug_prefix` must fail the attribute arm — and
  `:242–244` is the reason that mutation is worth planting, since `BASE_SCORES` really is
  keyed by `__name__` one module over and the two vocabularies are one copy-paste apart.
  The equivalent-mutant control is swapping the two `span.set_attribute` calls — a fact
  about the code (an OTel span's attributes are a map; both calls are side-effect-free
  reads), and the same control `complete_json`'s pair already carries in
  `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`.

### Task D3 — `llm_calls` gains its read, and the guard that forbade one retires in the same commit

**Depends on:** Group J's migration `m10a` (the two indexes)   **Files:** `src/usher/ports/repository/llm_call.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/llm_call.py`, `tests/fakes/llm_call_repository.py`, `tests/unit/test_ports.py` (**only** the parametrisation entry at `:208–219` and the case at `:338–355`), `tests/unit/test_llm_call_repository_contract.py`, `tests/integration/test_llm_call_repository.py`, `docs/prd/02-data-model.md` (**only** the `llm_calls` row at `:699`)

**M8 withheld the read and the indexes together and said which milestone would pay for
them.** `docs/plans/2026-08-06-m8-curation.md:798–808`: *"No read method, because `m08a`
shipped `llm_calls` with its primary key and no other index on the strength of that, and
every reader PRD 10 names is a Grafana panel M10 builds… The read and its index arrive
together, in the milestone with the panel."* `docs/prd/02-data-model.md:699` says the same
and points at where the DDL is written down. The two statements are in
`src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m08a_curation.py:206–216`, verbatim and
copy-pasteable — `CREATE INDEX ix_llm_calls_at ON llm_calls (at);` at `:208` for
dashboard 5's spend-per-day panel and the cost-anomaly alert, and
`CREATE INDEX ix_llm_calls_generation_id ON llm_calls (generation_id) WHERE generation_id
IS NOT NULL;` at `:214` for the `curated_rows` join, **partial** because query expansion
writes `NULL` there.

**The guard is `tests/unit/test_ports.py:338`,
`test_the_cost_ledger_has_no_read_method`**, and its docstring names its own exit
condition at `:349–353`: *"Adding `list_since` **and** the index it needs, in the milestone
that adds the panel reading them, is a decision; adding it without deleting this case is a
failing test."* There is a second copy of the same claim as a parametrised entry at
`tests/unit/test_ports.py:208–219`, which pins `LLMCallRepository.__abstractmethods__ ==
{"record"}` as an **exact set** — so the retirement is two edits, not one, and a task that
finds only the named case leaves the port pinned by the other. **The guard is deleted in
the same commit that adds the read.** A guard left standing against a shipped reader is
worse than either state: it is a red that everyone learns to ignore, which is the failure
`.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md` records about the PRD link check.

**The read is one method and its shape is decided by the two panels that consume it**, not
by generality. D10 needs spend per day and month grouped by `model` and `purpose`, tokens
in/out, and the `generation_id` join; D14's alert needs a daily total and a trailing
seven-day median. Both are `WHERE at >= :since` over a window, which is exactly what
`ix_llm_calls_at` serves and exactly what `m08a`'s comment predicts. So:
`list_since(since: AwareDatetime, *, until: AwareDatetime | None = None) ->
Sequence[LLMCall]`, returning the domain model `record` already takes — **symmetric on
purpose**, because `record`'s own docstring
(`ports/repository/llm_call.py`, the `record` block) argues at length that a parts-shaped
signature raises the same `ValidationError` one frame deeper, and a parts-shaped *read*
would put the aggregation in the repository where a panel author cannot see it.

**Grafana reads Postgres directly and this port is not on that path.**
`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:10–14` puts *"what did it cost"* on **Postgres, queried
directly by Grafana** — so D10's panels are SQL in the dashboard JSON, not calls to this
method. **The read exists for the caller in `src/` that the indexes cannot be justified
without**, and D14's cost-anomaly evaluation is that caller. Say so in the port's
docstring, because "the read arrives with the statement that reads it" is the rule this
port was written under and a read whose only consumer is a dashboard would re-commit
`ix_titles_popularity` with extra steps.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_llm_call_repository_contract.py::test_the_ledger_reads_back_every_attempted_call_in_the_window_including_the_failures`
— the shared contract, run against the fake and against Postgres, which seeds four rows:
two inside the window (one `ok=True` with a `generation_id`, one `ok=False` with an
`error` and `generation_id=None`), one before it and one after. It asserts the answer is
exactly the two inside, **in `at` order**, and that the `ok=False` row is present —
because a ledger of successes alone understates spend by exactly the failures
(`02-data-model.md:699`), and a `WHERE ok` that nobody wrote is the cheapest way to lose
that property. At HEAD it fails at import: `LLMCallRepository` has no `list_since`. Its
positive control is `assert len(seeded) == 4` before the read, so a fixture that wrote
nothing cannot satisfy an empty-equals-empty comparison.

**Acceptance:**
- `LLMCallRepository.__abstractmethods__ == frozenset({"record", "list_since"})` — the
  parametrised entry at `test_ports.py:208–219` is **updated**, not deleted, so the exact
  set stays pinned; `test_the_cost_ledger_has_no_read_method` at `:338` is **deleted**, and
  the commit body quotes M8's own sentence licensing it.
- `ports/repository/llm_call.py`'s module and class docstrings are rewritten rather than
  appended to: the module opens *"which is a write path and deliberately has no reader"*
  and the class calls the absence *"the port's central decision"*. **Both sentences are
  false the moment this task lands**, and a stale "verified" claim is worse than none.
- The two indexes exist under the names `m08a`'s docstring wrote — `ix_llm_calls_at` and
  the partial `ix_llm_calls_generation_id` — asserted by
  `tests/integration/test_migrations.py`'s existing index comparison, and the partial
  predicate is asserted as a predicate rather than by name, because a non-partial index of
  the right name inverts on the day an operator enables `USHER_QUERY_EXPANSION_ENABLED`
  (`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:684–694`).
- An `EXPLAIN` on `SELECT … WHERE at >= :since` shows an `Index Scan using ix_llm_calls_at`
  against a seeded ledger, and the measurement is committed rather than the data. ⚠️ **On a
  ledger of a few hundred rows Postgres will prefer a `Seq Scan` and that is correct** —
  the assertion is on the plan at a seeded size where the index is chosen, and the row count
  used is quoted beside the verdict. A plan assertion at a size where either plan is
  reasonable is a flaky test wearing a measurement's clothes.
- Sweep targets: `WHERE at >= :since` narrowed to `>` must fail a boundary case whose
  timestamp is exactly `since` (written with **literal** offsets, not `since ± 1`, for
  D4's `TICKET_TTL_SECONDS` reason); the `ORDER BY at` dropped must fail the ordering case,
  which carries its own premise (`assert seeded_ids != sorted(seeded_ids)` — UUIDv7 makes
  `ORDER BY id` and `ORDER BY at` agree by accident, and this repository has paid for that
  five times); a `WHERE ok` added must fail the failure-row arm.

### Task D4 — "cost per play attributed to an LLM row" is marked ⏳ M9 against a column M9 shipped, and the panel it blocks is one join wide

**Depends on:** D3 (the `generation_id` index), D0   **Files:** `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** `:889–902`), `tests/unit/test_prd_10_panel_backing.py`

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:896–898` reads: *"**Cost per play attributed to an LLM row
is still unbacked** and stays ⏳ M9: it needs `search_queries`' `played`, which needs a
client."* **M9 shipped it.** `src/usher/db/models/analytics.py:86` declares
`played: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False)`, and the comment above it
at `:83–85` states the writer contract — *"The writer sets it at insert (nothing has been
played yet) and attribution updates it"* — with `POST /titles|episodes/{id}/play` as the
second writer (`:530–534` of PRD 10 itself, and `src/usher/api/analytics.py:79`'s
`record_outcome`). The marker is stale and collectable.

**And collecting it does not deliver the panel the paragraph names, which is the half this
task exists to state.** The blocker was never only `played`. `:778–787` records the other
half in the same document: *"This column is joined to a **search**, not to a row:
`search_queries` has no row slug, no `generation_id` and no provider, and a play launched
from a home shelf carries no `search_id` at all, so it is one of the *unattributed* plays…
the same shape [06]'s 'cost per play attributed to an LLM row' panel needs, and a PRD
amendment rather than a follow-up to this one."* So a play that came from a curated shelf
is, today, indistinguishable from a play that came from a bookmark. **`played` unblocks the
denominator and not the attribution.**

**What ships is therefore the honest panel and the corrected marker, not the aspirational
one.** The join that exists is
`llm_calls ⋈ curated_rows USING (generation_id)` — `:669–675` calls it *"what makes
dashboard 5 a join"* and `:889–896` records it verified live on 2026-08-07 with
`cost_usd 0.01658700` reconciling to `Decimal((4359×3 + 234×15) / 1e6)` to eight decimal
places. What that join answers is **cost per curated row**, which is already ✅. The panel
this task adds beside it is **cost per curated row that was subsequently played**, computed
by joining `curated_rows.card_title_ids` (`src/usher/db/models/curation.py:274`, an ordered
`uuid[]` on the row) against `watch_states` for the same `user_id` — *not* against
`search_queries`, which cannot see a home-shelf play at all.

⚠️ **State the panel's weakness in the panel, because it is a correlation and not an
attribution.** A household that played a title which happened to appear on a curated shelf
did not necessarily play it *from* that shelf; `watch_states` carries no origin for the
launch (`src/usher/db/models/watch.py:106`'s `origin` is `WatchStateOrigin`, which
discriminates *source-pushed vs user-originated*, not *which shelf*). The panel is an
upper bound on the LLM's contribution and must be titled as one. **The true attribution is
D0's second M11 issue** and this task cites it rather than pretending to close it.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_prd_10_panel_backing.py::test_no_dashboard_panel_is_marked_owed_by_a_milestone_that_has_shipped`
— parses PRD 10's dashboard sections for `⏳ M<n>` markers and asserts every named
milestone is unshipped, reading the shipped set from `docs/plans/progress.md`'s table
rather than from a literal in the test. At HEAD it fails naming `⏳ M9` at `:897` against
M9's `complete` row. Its positive control is `assert milestones_seen, "no ⏳ markers
parsed"` **plus** a negative-control fixture asserting the parse would have caught a
planted `⏳ M11` — because a regex that matches nothing passes this assertion exactly as a
document with no stale markers does, and the failure mode here is a scan that stopped
matching after a reformat.

**Acceptance:**
- `:896–898` is rewritten: `played` is ✅ M9 with its writer named
  (`SearchQueryRepository.record_outcome`, `api/analytics.py:79`), the ⏳ marker is gone,
  and the sentence *"the one that answers 'was it worth it' is not"* is replaced by the
  narrower true one — the correlation panel is backed, the attribution is not, and the
  issue number for the attribution is named.
- The stale-marker test is generic over the document, not special-cased to this line, so
  the next milestone that ships a ⏳ and forgets the marker is a red rather than a
  discovery.
- **`git log -S"⏳ M9" -- docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` shows exactly one
  removal.** If it shows more, the task removed a marker that is still true.
- No `src/` file is touched. This is a documentation correction plus a panel specification
  that D10 implements.

### Task D5 — the ≤6-month TMDb cache ceiling, which is a live licensing obligation with no panel, and the table PRD 10 attributes it to does not exist

**Depends on:** D0, Group O   **Files:** `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** `:880–881` and the D5 backing paragraph at `:885–902`), `docs/specs/2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md` is **not** edited (a spec is a point-in-time record — `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md`), `tests/integration/test_raw_payload_cache_age.py`

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:880–881` specifies *"**oldest `enriched_at` against the
6-month TMDb cache ceiling**, a licensing-compliance panel given [ADR-0005]"*. The M10
spec's risk section (`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:423–425`) says the panel is backed
by `provider_cache_meta` and *"must not be dropped if D5 is trimmed"*. **Both the spec and
the PRD name the wrong column, in different ways, and this task fixes the PRD and reports
the spec as stale rather than editing it.**

**There is no `provider_cache_meta` table.**
[ADR-0016](../prd/decisions/0016-raw-payloads-cache-providers-not-sources.md)`:17–26`
records that PRD 02 once listed one *"holding fetch timestamps — enforces TMDb's ≤6-month
cache term"*, and decided against it: *"`raw_payloads` holds **provider** responses only,
keyed `(provider, kind, reference)`, and carries its own `fetched_at`. No
`provider_cache_meta` table is created."* `:36–38` states the consequence in the exact
words this panel needs: *"`fetched_at` is the compliance answer directly: 'the oldest cache
entry against the 6-month ceiling' is `min(fetched_at)`, which `ix_raw_payloads_fetched_at`
serves."* Verified in code: `src/usher/db/models/sync.py:104–106` declares the column,
`:112–115` declares the index with a comment naming *"PRD 10, dashboard 5"* and explaining
that it is ascending **because the query asks for the minimum**, and `:90–94` restates the
refusal at the column that replaced the table. `grep -rn provider_cache_meta src/` returns
one hit and it is that refusal.

**`enriched_at` is the wrong column and the difference is the obligation.**
`src/usher/db/models/title.py:175` declares `titles.enriched_at`, which is *when Usher last
enriched a title*. TMDb's term is about **how long a provider's response may be cached**,
which is `raw_payloads.fetched_at` — the timestamp on the payload itself. The two diverge
in the direction that matters: a title enriched from a cached payload advances
`enriched_at` and leaves `fetched_at` where it was, so a panel on `enriched_at` reports
freshness the cache does not have. `sync.py:92–94` already warns about the mirror of this —
*"an upsert that refreshes a payload must set it explicitly, because a stale timestamp on
fresh data is precisely the compliance answer the column exists to give"* — and this is the
same sentence read the other way.

**This panel gets weight because it is the only one in the five dashboards whose failure is
a licence breach rather than a blind spot**, and the spec is right about that even where it
is wrong about the table. `04-catalog-bootstrap.md:619–680` establishes the posture and
`tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` enforces the *redistribution* half mechanically;
**nothing enforces the retention half**, and this panel plus its threshold line is the whole
of the enforcement. It ships even if D10 is trimmed to one panel.

**The panel is three numbers, not one.** `min(fetched_at)` alone is satisfied by a cache
holding one ancient row and answers nothing about how much of the cache is out of term. The
panel reads: the oldest entry, the **count** of entries older than 6 months, and that count
as a share of `count(*)` — with a threshold line at `now() - interval '6 months'`. The
measured baseline to record beside it: **129,131 cached payloads at 995 MB** on the
1,272,367-title catalog (`08-operations.md:657–661`), which is the denominator the share is
taken over.

**Failing test first:** `tests/integration/test_raw_payload_cache_age.py::test_the_cache_age_query_uses_the_index_and_counts_the_rows_past_the_ceiling`
— seeds a real `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` with payloads straddling `now() - interval
'6 months'` (three inside, two outside, one exactly on the boundary), runs the panel's own
SQL, and asserts (a) the count past the ceiling is 2 — **the boundary row is not past it**,
which is what makes `>=` versus `>` a decision rather than a detail — and (b) `EXPLAIN`
shows `ix_raw_payloads_fetched_at` serving the `min()`. At HEAD it fails because the file
does not exist and neither does the query. Its positive control asserts the six rows were
committed and that at least one lies on each side, so a fixture that seeded nothing cannot
answer `0 == 0`.

⚠️ **The `EXPLAIN` arm is the one to write carefully.** At six rows Postgres seq-scans and
is right to; the arm seeds to a size where the index is chosen, quotes that size in the
docstring, and the *count* arm runs at six rows where it is deterministic. Two claims, two
fixtures — the shape `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` records for D4's TTL and B9's cast
limit.

**Acceptance:**
- `:880` is corrected from *"oldest `enriched_at`"* to *"oldest
  `raw_payloads.fetched_at`"*, with ADR-0016 cited and the distinction from
  `titles.enriched_at` stated in one sentence, so the next reader does not re-conflate
  them.
- D5's backing paragraph gains this panel as ✅ M2 (the column and the table are `m04`-era
  and the index ships with the model), with `129,131 payloads / 995 MB` as its measured
  denominator and the date of that measurement.
- **The plan reports to the milestone that the spec's `provider_cache_meta` sentence at
  `:424` is wrong**; the spec is not edited, per the PRD/spec rule, and the correction
  lives in PRD 10 where it is authoritative.
- The panel is specified with all three numbers and the threshold line, and D10 implements
  it. A single-number version is a rejected design, stated in the panel's own description
  so it is not "simplified" back.
- Sweep target: the ceiling spelled `interval '6 month'` against `'180 days'` — measured,
  not assumed, because they differ by up to 4 days and a compliance panel that is
  four days optimistic is a compliance panel that is wrong in the only direction that
  matters. The expected verdict is that the boundary case fails on the exact-boundary row.

### Task D6 — Dashboard 1 as provisioned JSON, and the invariant harness the other four inherit

**Depends on:** D0, Group O   **Files:** `dashboards/README.md`, `dashboards/01-library-and-catalog.json`, `dashboards/provisioning/dashboards.yml`, `tests/unit/test_dashboards.py`, `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** `:791–793`)

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:791–793`: *"Five, shipped as provisioned JSON in this
repository so a fresh deploy has them without clicking. They live with the code that emits
the data, so they version together."* Nothing exists — `ls dashboards grafana
observability docs/dashboards` returns nothing, and `.github/` holds one workflow. **This
task creates the directory, the provisioning file, the first dashboard, and the test the
other four are graded by.**

**The asymmetry PRD 10 sets up and this task honours: the dashboards are an asset of this
repository; the stack that renders them is not** (`:904–922`, and the M10 spec restates it
at `:271–273`). So `dashboards/` holds JSON and a provisioning YAML that
`~/code/observability/`'s compose project bind-mounts. **Usher's `compose.yml` gains
nothing** — its only coupling to telemetry stays the two environment variables at
`.env.example:290–291`.

**Dashboard 1 is eleven panels and every one is Postgres**, which is the first principle at
`:5–14` doing exactly what it was written for: *"Most of what is worth knowing about a
media catalog is **not a metric**… The catalog *is* the record."* There is no Prometheus
panel on this dashboard at all, and saying so up front is what stops an implementer
reaching for a metric that does not exist. The eleven panels, their statements, and their
two denominator caveats are D0's output; this task implements them.

**The harness is three invariants and each closes a distinct way a committed dashboard
lies.**

1. **Schema-valid, without adding a dependency.** `pyproject.toml:8–33` carries no
   `jsonschema` and this task does not add one — a validator pulled in for a test that has
   five inputs is the shape `litellm` was refused under (`+146 MB and 29 distributions
   against +0 and 0`, ADR-0027). The check is structural over the parsed JSON: every file
   parses, carries `title`/`uid`/`schemaVersion`/`panels`, every panel carries a
   non-empty `title` and a `datasource`, every panel has at least one target, and every
   `uid` is unique across the five files. `uid` uniqueness is the one that bites: Grafana
   silently overwrites a dashboard whose `uid` collides, so two files and one visible
   dashboard is the failure mode, and it has no error anywhere.
2. **Every Prometheus target's metric name is in PRD 10's catalogue.** The extractor is
   `tests/unit/test_telemetry_search.py:79`'s `_ROW` regex generalised to drop the `usher\.`
   anchor — **which is the fix the group preamble's row-count correction implies**, since a
   catalogue check that cannot see `http.server.duration` cannot grade D9's API-latency
   panel at all. Names are pulled out of each target's `expr` by a
   `[a-z][a-z0-9_.]*` scan filtered against the catalogue's own set, and any token that
   looks like a metric and is not in the catalogue fails, naming it. **Prometheus mangles
   dots to underscores and appends `_bucket`/`_count`/`_sum`/`_total`**, so the comparison
   normalises both sides — `usher.suggest.duration` in the PRD against
   `usher_suggest_duration_bucket` in the panel — and the normalisation is asserted by its
   own case, because a normaliser that lower-cased everything to nothing would make the
   whole check vacuous.
3. **Every Postgres target's referenced table and column exists.** Not by parsing SQL —
   that is a parser this repository does not want — but by scanning each `expr`/`rawSql`
   for `\b(\w+)\.(\w+)\b` pairs and checking them against `Base.metadata`, which is the
   live schema the ORM already holds. Unknown pairs fail naming the panel. ⚠️ **This one
   needs its false-positive escape stated in the test's own docstring**: a SQL alias
   (`t.name`) is not a table name, so the check is over pairs whose left side is a real
   `__tablename__`, and a panel using aliases is checked on nothing. That is a real
   limitation and it is written down rather than papered over — the alternative is a SQL
   parser, and a check that silently covers a third of the panels is worse than one whose
   coverage is stated.

**Verification** (a panel is not unit-testable and this slot says so explicitly, per the
spec's `:392–394`): each of the eleven panels is opened in the running Grafana against the
live catalog and the observation is recorded in `dashboards/README.md` beside the panel —
**the query as issued and the data it returned**, not "it rendered". For the quality
ladder that is the 4K share by decade with its row count; for franchise completeness it is
one named franchise with its listed missing entries; for unmatched depth it is the depth
and the wall time of the query at that depth, against `:836–839`'s 388.9 ms at offset
1,126,574. **No panel is committed before its observation exists**, which is Phase 2's gate
(`:342`).

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_dashboards.py::test_every_committed_dashboard_is_structurally_valid_and_names_only_metrics_the_catalogue_holds`
— globs `dashboards/*.json`, applies the three invariants, and fails at HEAD because the
glob is empty. **Its positive control is the whole point of the case**:
`assert files, "the dashboard glob found nothing"` plus
`assert catalogue, "no metric rows parsed out of PRD 10"` plus a named anchor
(`"usher.jobs.queued" in catalogue`) — a glob that matches nothing and a catalogue that
parses nothing both pass every downstream assertion silently, and this repository has paid
for that failure in an import-contract verification (*7 kept, 0 broken* against a
substitution that was a no-op) and in a suggest-index scan.

**Acceptance:**
- `dashboards/` exists with the provisioning YAML, one JSON file, and a `README.md` whose
  structure is one section per panel holding its recorded observation.
- `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_dashboards.py` passes with all three invariants live, and
  each invariant is demonstrated red first against a planted defect: a duplicate `uid`, a
  panel targeting `usher.suggest.latency` (the near-miss name D1 names), and a panel
  selecting `titles.enrichment_status` (the near-miss column). **All three plants are run,
  because an invariant demonstrated only by reasoning is an invariant nobody has seen
  fail.**
- PRD 10 `:791–793` gains the path (`dashboards/`) and the provisioning mechanism, so the
  sentence names a thing that exists.
- `ruff`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy src tests`, `lint-imports` clean. **The JSON files
  are not Python and `[tool.ruff] extend-exclude` does not cover `dashboards/`** — checked,
  not assumed; ruff ignores `.json`, and the risk this bullet exists for is the opposite
  one, that a `README.md` full of SQL fences gets reformatted. `CLAUDE.md`'s note about
  `docs/plans` and `docs/prd` is the precedent, and `dashboards/README.md` is added to
  `extend-exclude` for the same reason.

### Task D7 — Dashboard 2, five panels and three stated absences

**Depends on:** D0, D6   **Files:** `dashboards/02-taste-and-watching.json`, `dashboards/README.md`, `tests/unit/test_dashboards.py`

D0 establishes that three of PRD 10's eight D2 panels have no backing series: **watch time
by day and user**, **taste drift over months**, and **row effectiveness per
`RowProvider`**. This task ships the five that are backed and does **not** ship the three
as empty panels, because *"a documented metric nothing emits is a dashboard panel that is
permanently empty, and nothing distinguishes that from a healthy zero"* (`:136–139`) is
the same argument one level up, and an empty panel on a shipped dashboard is worse than an
absent one: it looks like a household that watches nothing.

**What ships, with the statement each panel is over.** Completion rate is
`count(*) FILTER (WHERE played) / count(*)` over `watch_states`
(`src/usher/db/models/watch.py:84`). Rewatches is `play_count > 1` (`:87`). Longest
unwatched is `media_items.added_at` (`source.py:95`) with no `watch_states` row for the
household, sorted by age. The abandonment cliff is
`position_seconds::float / runtime_seconds` bucketed over `played = false`, **with the
denominator coalesced in the stated order** — `watch_states.runtime_seconds` (`:83`,
nullable by ADR-0014), then `media_items.runtime_seconds` (`source.py:93`), then
`titles.runtime_minutes * 60` (`title.py:74`) — and the panel description names the order,
because three fallbacks silently applied are three different populations plotted as one.
The time-of-day panel ships **retitled**: *"when each item was last played"*, over
`date_part('hour', last_played_at)` (`watch.py:90`), which is what the column can answer.

**The three absences are a panel of their own — a text panel naming each, its reason and
its issue number.** That is not decoration: a dashboard whose specification lists eight
panels and whose JSON holds five reads as a half-finished commit to anyone who has not read
D0's PRD paragraph, and the person reading it at 2 a.m. is the operator. The text panel
carries the same three sentences the PRD paragraph does, which is duplication chosen
deliberately over a link, on `:201`'s rule for label vocabularies: the reader of the
artefact must not have to hold the document.

**Verification:** each of the five panels observed against the live catalog, with the
household named. ⚠️ **The abandonment cliff needs its own note in the recorded
observation**: on a household whose watch states came from a walk rather than from a push,
`runtime_seconds` is null across the board and the panel is running entirely on the third
fallback — so the observation records **which fallback supplied the denominator and for
what share of rows**, or the panel's shape is a fact about the ingest path rather than
about viewing habits.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_dashboards.py::test_dashboard_two_ships_no_panel_the_audit_found_unbacked`
— reads D2's JSON and asserts none of its panel titles matches the three unbacked panels
named in PRD 10's D2 backing paragraph, **with the forbidden list parsed out of the PRD
rather than retyped**. At HEAD it fails because the file does not exist; against a
"complete" dashboard that shipped all eight it fails naming the three. Its positive
control is `assert forbidden, "no unbacked panels parsed out of PRD 10's D2 paragraph"`
plus `assert titles, "the dashboard has no panels"` — a parse that found no forbidden
names and a dashboard with no panels both satisfy the disjointness assertion.

**Acceptance:**
- Five panels, one text panel, six total; D6's three invariants pass over it.
- `dashboards/README.md` gains a D2 section with five recorded observations, and the
  abandonment cliff's observation names its denominator source and coverage share.
- The text panel's three sentences are byte-identical to PRD 10's, asserted by the test
  above, so a correction to one is a red on the other rather than a drift.
- No `src/` file is touched.
