### Task D8 — Dashboard 3, and two of its panels read a series whose label is not the one the panel's name implies

**Depends on:** D6, Group O   **Files:** `dashboards/03-pipeline.json`, `dashboards/README.md`

D3 is the mixed dashboard — Prometheus for rates and depths, Postgres for the queue's own
breakdown — and PRD 10 already annotates it at `:830–839`. Two of its ten panels need a
statement the section does not make.

**"Queue depth by priority" is a Postgres query and PRD 10 says so twice.** `:489–496`:
*"M5 introduces demand promotion and the label stays `kind`: a priority band needs a
second `GROUP BY` on `JobQueue`… The panel reads `jobs` directly."* Verified:
`usher.jobs.queued` is registered in `src/usher/telemetry.py:298–303` with a single `kind`
attribute (`:332`), and `QueueSnapshot` (`:236–246`) holds two `Mapping[str, int]`s keyed
by `JobKind.value` and nothing else. So the panel is `SELECT priority, count(*) FROM jobs
WHERE status = 'pending' GROUP BY priority`, and **the Prometheus gauge beside it is the
by-kind panel, not the same panel with a different datasource.** Two panels, two questions.

**"Push connection uptime" plots delivery, and the gauge's label is a source *name*.**
`telemetry.py:335–353` documents the first half — `PushSnapshot.delivering`, not
`connected`, because *"a gauge fed by the socket's state would read 1 for the failure
ADR-0004 measured"*. The second half is not documented anywhere and a panel author will
get it wrong: `src/usher/api/lanes.py:273–287` builds the reader as
`{self._names[source_id]: PushSnapshot(...)}` over `self._open_adapters`, so the `source`
label is **the operator-typed source name** (`"Living Room Emby"`) and not a UUID, and
**only sources with a running lane appear at all**. A source that is disabled, or that does
not support push, produces *no observation* rather than a zero — `_push_observations`
returns `[]` with no reader (`telemetry.py:419–424`) and the comprehension simply omits
anything without an open adapter. That is the honest behaviour and it decides D11's
Push-down alert: the condition is `== 0`, never `absent()`.

**"Enrichment throughput and p50/p99" is backed and its label is `outcome`.**
`src/usher/services/enrich.py:62–70` records the correction M4 made: *"Labelled `outcome`
rather than PRD 10's original `trigger`: nothing in M4 enriches on demand… while a
failure's latency and a success's are genuinely different populations."* The panel splits
on `outcome`; **the demand-versus-background split D12 wants does not exist on this
series** and the panel says so rather than implying it.

The remaining panels are unremarkable and backed: parked jobs
(`usher.jobs.parked`, `telemetry.py:304–309`), sync run outcomes and duration
(`usher.sync.run.duration`, plus `sync_runs` for the outcome breakdown), promotion latency
against the 5 s target (the join `:816–819` describes, from the `traceparent` on the
promoted job — `telemetry.py:31–50`'s `current_traceparent` is what puts it there), push
events by kind (`usher.source.push.events`), Emby request latency
(`usher.source.request.duration`), and TMDb requests/sec against the ~40 ceiling with 429
count — the last from `usher.provider.requests`, whose `status` label is **the HTTP status
code as a string** (`src/usher/adapters/tmdb/client.py:180`, `status = str(response.status_code)`)
or the literal `"error"` for a transport failure that never reached a status line
(`:172`, `:184–190`). So the 429 panel is `status="429"`, and the ceiling line is drawn at
40 rps against `USHER_TMDB_REQUESTS_PER_SECOND`.

**Verification:** all ten panels observed against the live deployment. Three need traffic
to be non-empty and the observation records how it was generated: a bounded delta sync for
the ingest and sync panels, a `usher work --once` pass for the job panels, and — for the
push panels — a source with a running lane, which means the observation run does **not**
set `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false`. ⚠️ **And that is the one dangerous observation in this
group.** `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` records that starting the shipped app
against a real source is itself an unbounded walk: the push lane's reconnect gap-closer
calls `reconcile(source, DELTA, adapter)`, which against the 1,126,789-item household this
project measures is exactly the walk that is forbidden. **The push observation runs against
a bounded scratch source, never the operator's real Emby**, and the README records which.

**Failing test first:** D6's three invariants, plus
`tests/unit/test_dashboards.py::test_the_queue_depth_panels_are_two_panels_over_two_datasources`
— asserts D3 carries both a Postgres panel selecting `jobs.priority` and a Prometheus panel
on `usher_jobs_queued`, because the failure this closes is a single panel claiming
"by priority" while querying a series labelled `kind`, which renders perfectly and answers
a different question. At HEAD it fails on the missing file. Positive control: the panel
list is non-empty and contains a known title.

**Acceptance:**
- Ten panels; the queue-depth pair is two panels with two datasources and two titles.
- The push panels' description states the `source` label is the operator-typed name and
  that a source with no lane produces no series — **the sentence D11's alert is written
  against**.
- The enrichment panel's description states its label is `outcome` and that no
  demand/background split exists on this series, citing `enrich.py:62–68`.
- The TMDb panel's 429 series is `status="429"` and its denominator includes
  `status="error"`, per `:422–426` — *"a denominator that omitted the failures would read
  low exactly during an outage."*
- `dashboards/README.md` gains ten observations; the push one names the scratch source and
  states that the run did not point a lane at the operator's library.

### Task D9 — Dashboard 4, whose every panel is now backed and two of which read differently after M9

**Depends on:** D1, D6, Group O   **Files:** `dashboards/04-performance.json`, `dashboards/README.md`

`:848–873` is the most complete backing statement in PRD 10 and it ends *"so every panel
on this dashboard now has a writer behind it"*. Three things it says that a panel author
must not lose, plus one this task adds.

**API latency by endpoint is `http.server.duration` and the semconv opt-in is the hazard
that empties it.** `:179–192` records that `FastAPIInstrumentor` (wired in `create_app`,
`api/app.py:127`) emits it with `http.target` set to the **route template** —
`/titles/{title_id}`, so two title ids collapse into one series — and `:193–199` records
that `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN=http` renames it, re-units it and swaps the label,
*"any one of which empties a dashboard panel built against the names above, silently, with
no error anywhere."* **This panel is authored against Group O's recorded convention and the
recorded convention is quoted in the panel's own description**, so the day somebody sets
that variable the panel that goes quiet is not a mystery. This is the single strongest
reason the semconv dependency is stated per task in this group rather than assumed.

**The home-composition breakdown comes from two sides and the histogram's population is
misses.** `usher.home.compose.duration` carries **no labels** by decision (`:375–379`) and
`usher.row.build.duration` carries `provider` — the ten `slug_prefix` constants
(`:228–232`), never the row slug, because `because-you-watched-<seed>` would make the label
catalog-sized. `:866–868`: *"the build histogram's population is cache misses only, so a
p50 that rises after a deploy may be a colder cache rather than a slower provider."* The
panel description carries that sentence. **D2's `propose` span is the drill-down beside
it**, and this task is where it earns its keep: the breakdown answers *which provider is
slow* and the span answers *in which phase*, which `:113–117` records as previously
unanswerable.

**Cache hit rates split on `freshness` and the split is the trade, not a detail.**
`usher.cache.hits` carries `cache` (`row`/`screen`/`image`) **and** `freshness`
(`fresh`/`stale`); `usher.cache.misses` carries `cache` only, because *"a miss served
nothing, so it has no freshness to report"* (`:262–270`). The instruments are declared once
in `src/usher/telemetry.py:634–642` for three callers — the row cache, the screen cache and
the image proxy — with the module-level comment at `:613–633` recording that they moved
there because a second `create_counter` under one meter for one name is *"either a
duplicate-instrument warning or a second stream, and either way a dashboard's hit rate
silently stops covering a cache."* **So the hit-rate panel groups by `cache` and does not
sum across it**, and it plots stale as its own series rather than folded into the good one.

**Zero-result rate and search→play conversion are `search_queries` and the absence table
is what makes them readable.** Zero-result is `result_count = 0` over answered searches
(`:854–858`); conversion is `played` over the same denominator with the no-click rate
beside it (`:859–865`). ⚠️ **The denominator is answered searches, never plays**
(`:572–575`), and the panels are titled accordingly: *"of searches, how many led to a play
Usher was told about"*, never *"of plays, how many came from a search"* — a play carrying
no `search_id` is not in this table at all (`:569`).

**What this task adds: the suggest panels D1 makes possible**, split by `tier`. Two panels
— `usher.suggest.duration` p50/p95 by tier against the 50 ms as-you-type budget, and
`usher.suggest.results` — with the budget line drawn and the two tiers plotted separately
rather than summed, for the reason D1 gives: they differ by ~50× at p50, and a merged
series is a measure of client debounce behaviour.

**Verification:** all nine panels observed. The suggest panels need real traffic and the
observation records the prefix lengths it drove, because tier 1's cost spans **2,707 ms
p95 at one character to 2.3 ms at seven** (`ADR-0031:192–193`) — a panel observed at one
prefix length says nothing about the panel. The image-proxy panels need a cold and a warm
request; the hit-rate observation records **one cold and two warm**, not one of each, on
`.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`'s C5 finding: a fixture with one hit and one miss makes
the two counters symmetric, so a panel that swapped them renders identically.

**Failing test first:** D6's invariants, plus
`tests/unit/test_dashboards.py::test_the_cache_panel_groups_by_cache_and_never_sums_across_it`
— asserts D4's hit-rate targets carry a `by (cache` grouping and that no target sums the
hits counter without one. At HEAD it fails on the missing file. Positive control: the
target list is non-empty and at least one names `usher_cache_hits_total`.

**Acceptance:**
- Nine panels including the two new suggest ones; every metric name in every target passes
  D6's catalogue check, which after D1 holds 37 rows.
- The API-latency panel's description quotes Group O's recorded semconv convention and
  names what changes if it moves.
- The build-histogram panel's description carries `:866–868`'s cache-miss caveat verbatim;
  the conversion panel's carries `:861–865`'s direction caveat verbatim.
- `dashboards/README.md` gains nine observations; the suggest one names its prefix lengths
  and the cache one names its 1-cold/2-warm shape and why.

### Task D10 — Dashboard 5, and the one panel on it whose failure is a licence breach

**Depends on:** D3, D4, D5, D6   **Files:** `dashboards/05-cost-and-compliance.json`, `dashboards/README.md`

Eight panels, and PRD 10 annotates six of them at `:885–902`. Five are Postgres over
`llm_calls` and `curated_rows`, one is Prometheus, one is `import_runs`, and one is the
compliance panel D5 specifies.

**The spend panels are exact and were verified live.** `:889–896`: spend by model and
purpose, tokens in/out, and cost per curated row are `llm_calls` and
`llm_calls ⋈ curated_rows USING (generation_id)`, *"both of which exist and were verified
live on 2026-08-07: `cost_usd` is `0.00000000` against a local model — the honest value —
and `0.01658700` with prices 3/15 per Mtok configured, exactly
`Decimal((4359×3 + 234×15) / 1e6)`, with the column `numeric` and `SUM()` agreeing to 8
decimal places."* The panel's own description carries the second half of that finding,
which is the operator-facing one: `cost_usd` defaults to `0` because that is honest for a
local model and **wrong for a hosted model an operator forgot to price** (`:702–706`), and
the mitigation is that `tokens_in`/`tokens_out` are exact so spend is recomputable after
the fact. **A dashboard reading $0.00 is not evidence of a free deployment**, and the panel
says so rather than leaving the operator to infer it.

**The join is partial-indexed and this task is downstream of D3 for that reason.**
`ix_llm_calls_generation_id` is `WHERE generation_id IS NOT NULL` because query expansion
writes `NULL` there (`:680–694`), and on the shipped default —
`USHER_QUERY_EXPANSION_ENABLED=false` even where `USHER_LLM_ENABLED` is true — the table is
**100% `curation`** and the partial index degenerates to a full one. The panel is correct
under both populations; the description records which the deployment is in, because the
same panel over an expansion-enabled deployment is reading a minority of rows.

**Cost per play is D4's corrected panel**: `curated_rows.card_title_ids` against
`watch_states` for the same `user_id`, titled as the upper bound it is, with the
attribution issue cited. The ⏳ M9 marker is already gone by D4.

**The compliance panel is D5's three numbers** — oldest `raw_payloads.fetched_at`, the
count past `now() - interval '6 months'`, and that count as a share — with the threshold
line drawn. **It is the panel that does not get trimmed**, and the reason is stated in the
dashboard rather than only in this plan: every other panel here is a blind spot when it
breaks and this one is a licence term. `tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` enforces
the redistribution half of `04-catalog-bootstrap.md:619–680` mechanically; nothing enforces
the retention half, and this panel plus its threshold is the whole of it.

**Embedding compute time is `usher.embedding.duration`** (histogram, no labels), the only
Prometheus panel here. **Data freshness is `import_runs.heartbeat_at` and `finished_at`**,
✅ M2 per `:885–887`, one row per bulk dataset. **Postgres size by table with a
disk-exhaustion projection** is `pg_total_relation_size` per table plus a linear fit —
and it is the panel D13's alert is drawn from, so the two are authored together and the
projection window is stated in both.

⚠️ **The size panel must not quote `08-operations.md:643–674`'s resource table as a
budget.** That table carries a red warning in its own text: *"These are **sizing estimates
for an operator provisioning a disk**. Nothing in this project reads them, no host enforces
them and no policy is derived from them"*, and it records that M9's Track 2 treated one row
as a budget, derived a 2.0 GB ceiling from it, and **withdrew a design** measured at
2.702 GB against a number with no forcing function (ADR-0036). The panel plots measured
sizes against **measured free disk**, and its description names the trap.

**Verification:** eight panels. The spend panels need a real generation — `uv run usher
curate` against the local vLLM, which `CLAUDE.md` records as served for development — and
the observation records the tokens and the cost, reconciled against the ledger row by hand
the way the 2026-08-07 run did. The compliance panel's observation records the oldest
`fetched_at` and the count past the ceiling **on the real cache**, which at 129,131 payloads
is the first time anyone has looked; if any row is past six months, that is a finding
reported to the milestone and not a panel bug.

**Failing test first:** D6's invariants, plus
`tests/unit/test_dashboards.py::test_the_compliance_panel_exists_and_reads_the_column_adr_0016_named`
— asserts D5's JSON holds a panel whose target names `raw_payloads` and `fetched_at`, and
that **no panel on any dashboard reads `titles.enriched_at` for a cache-age question**,
matched by the panel title rather than by the SQL. At HEAD it fails on the missing file;
against a dashboard built from PRD 10's uncorrected `:880` it fails on the second arm.
Positive control: `assert panels` plus a named anchor title.

**Acceptance:**
- Eight panels; the compliance panel carries all three numbers and the threshold line.
- The spend panel's description states the zero-cost trap and the recomputability
  mitigation; the join panel's states which population the deployment is in.
- The size panel's description names `08-operations.md:643–674`'s warning and plots against
  measured free disk, not against that table.
- `dashboards/README.md` gains eight observations, including the first real reading of the
  cache's age distribution.
- **If D5 or this dashboard is trimmed for time, the compliance panel is the one that
  ships**, and the trim order is written into the README.

### Task D11 — the alert file, and the three alerts whose series already exist — one of which must fire on a zero and not on an absence

**Depends on:** D6, D8, Group O   **Files:** `dashboards/alerts/usher.yml`, `dashboards/README.md`, `tests/unit/test_alerts.py`

`10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:924–936` specifies seven alerts and opens *"Kept few, so
they mean something."* This task creates the rule file, the test that grades every rule in
it, and the three whose backing series ship today and need no decision. **The Phase 2
acceptance is that each rule has been fired at least once, deliberately**
(`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:367`), so every rule below carries its firing recipe as
an acceptance criterion rather than as a note.

**Ingest stalled — "Queue depth rising for 30 min with zero completions" (`:930`).**
Depth is `usher.jobs.queued`, an observable gauge labelled `kind`
(`src/usher/telemetry.py:298–303`). Completions are the **count** series of
`usher.jobs.duration`, recorded at `src/usher/services/jobs.py:478` labelled `kind` —
and the exact reach of that point is load-bearing: line 478 sits *after* the
`with _tracer.start_as_current_span(...)` block, so it records for a completed job, for a
retryable failure and for a park, but **not** when a handler raises something that is not a
`UsherPortError`, which propagates past both `except` arms by design (`:448–455`). So the
series is *jobs that settled*, and a crashing handler produces neither a completion nor a
duration point — which means this alert fires correctly on the crash-loop case, and the
rule's description says "settled" rather than "completed" so nobody reads the panel as a
success rate.

Rule: `increase(usher_jobs_queued[30m]) > 0 and increase(usher_jobs_duration_count[30m])
== 0`, `for: 30m`. **The `and` is per-`kind` by label matching**, which is what makes it a
statement about a lane rather than about the queue: a busy `enrich` lane must not mask a
stalled `curate` one.

⚠️ **`usher.jobs.queued` reports *no observation* rather than zero before anything has read
the table** (`telemetry.py:320–332`, `_observations` returns `[]` with no reader, and the
docstring names this alert as the reason: *"a fabricated zero is the one value that makes
the alert quietly wrong"*). So the depth half must tolerate a gap without firing, and the
rule uses `increase()` over the gauge rather than `min_over_time(... ) > 0`, which a gap
would satisfy vacuously.

**Jobs parking — "Parked count increasing" (`:932`).** `usher.jobs.parked`, same registration
(`telemetry.py:304–309`), same no-reader-no-observation rule. Rule:
`increase(usher_jobs_parked[15m]) > 0`, `for: 5m`, by `kind`. **No absolute threshold**, because
PRD 10's condition is the derivative and a deployment that has carried three parked jobs
for a month does not need paging about them every evaluation.

**Push down — `push.connected == 0` for 15 min on a source that supports it (`:931`).** The
series is `usher.source.push.connected`, a gauge registered at
`telemetry.py:387–392`, and **three properties of it decide the rule's spelling, all
verified in `src/usher/api/lanes.py:273–287`**:

- The value is **delivery, not connection**. `PushSnapshot.delivering` (`telemetry.py:335–353`)
  is `adapter.supports_push`, the adapter's message ledger — a channel that upgraded, is
  held open and delivers nothing reads 0, which is *exactly* the failure ADR-0004 measured
  and the condition this alert exists for.
- The `source` label is **the operator-typed source name**, not a UUID:
  `{self._names[source_id]: PushSnapshot(...)}`. So the alert's annotation renders
  `"Living Room Emby"`, which is the right thing for an operator and the wrong thing for
  anything that joins on identity.
- **A source with no running lane produces no series at all**, because the comprehension
  iterates `self._open_adapters`, and a lane only starts for an *enabled* source
  (`lanes.py:298–300`). So PRD 10's qualifier *"on a source that supports it"* needs no
  join against `sources.supports_push` (`src/usher/db/models/source.py:48`) — **the series
  is already scoped to exactly those sources by construction.** The rule is
  `usher_source_push_connected == 0`, `for: 15m`, and it is **not** `absent()`: an absent
  series is a source nobody is running a lane for, which is a configuration state and not
  an incident.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_alerts.py::test_every_alert_prd_10_names_exists_and_every_rule_names_a_series_the_catalogue_holds`
— parses `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md:928–936`'s table for the seven alert names, parses
`dashboards/alerts/usher.yml`, and asserts (a) the two name sets are equal in **both**
directions — a rule PRD 10 does not name is as much a defect as an alert with no rule,
because *"kept few, so they mean something"* is a claim a seventh unnamed rule falsifies —
and (b) every metric token in every `expr` normalises to a name in the catalogue, reusing
D6's normaliser rather than a second copy. At HEAD it fails on the missing file. Positive
control: `assert prd_alerts, "no alert rows parsed out of PRD 10"` **and**
`assert len(prd_alerts) == 7`, because a regex that matched three rows would make the
equality assertion a comparison of two small wrong sets.

**Acceptance:**
- The rule file exists with three rules; the bidirectional name check is live and the other
  four rules are added by D12–D14, so the test is **red until D14 lands** — stated here
  deliberately, and the four missing names are listed in the test's own failure message so
  the red says which task owes what rather than merely that something is missing.
- **Each of the three fired once, deliberately, with the firing recorded in
  `dashboards/README.md`.** Ingest stalled: enqueue a batch of `enrich` jobs against a
  worker that is not running (`USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false`, no `usher work`), wait out the
  window, record the alert instance and its labels, then start the worker and record the
  resolve. Jobs parking: park a job by handing the enrich handler a payload that raises
  `PortDataMalformed` — the arm at `jobs.py:450–453` that sets `usher.job.parked` and calls
  `_fail(retryable=False)` — and record the transition. Push down: point a lane at a source
  whose websocket upgrades and delivers nothing, which is ADR-0004's own measured failure
  and is reproducible against a stub; record that the gauge read 0 while the socket was
  open, because **a firing produced by stopping the source proves the wrong thing** — it
  fires the alert through a path the series was deliberately built not to use.
- Each rule's `annotations.description` names the series, the label vocabulary and the
  panel it corresponds to on D3, so an operator paged at 2 a.m. lands on a panel rather
  than on a query.
- ⚠️ **The push firing runs against a stub, never the operator's Emby.**
  `.claude/rules/api-telemetry-and-lanes.md` records that a push lane's reconnect
  gap-closer issues a full `DELTA` reconcile, which against the measured household is
  1,126,789 items — the walk M9's live runs set `USHER_PUSH_ENABLED=false` to avoid.

### Task D12 — the two alerts whose PRD condition names a dimension the shipped series does not carry

**Depends on:** D11, D8   **Files:** `dashboards/alerts/usher.yml`, `src/usher/services/enrich.py`, `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` (**only** the `usher.enrichment.latency` row at `:153`, the correction bullet at `:416–421`, and the alert table row at `:933`), `tests/unit/test_telemetry_pipeline.py`, `tests/unit/test_alerts.py`

**"Enrichment SLA missed — Demand-triggered p99 > 5 s for 15 min" (`:933`) cannot be
expressed on the shipped series, and the reason is a correction M4 made on purpose.**
`src/usher/services/enrich.py:62–70`: *"Labelled `outcome` rather than PRD 10's original
`trigger`: nothing in M4 enriches on demand (`JobPriority.DEMAND` is defined and unused
until M5), so a `trigger` label would carry one constant value, while a failure's latency
and a success's are genuinely different populations. PRD 10 is corrected rather than
approximated."* PRD 10 carries the same correction at `:416–421` — **and the alert table
three hundred lines further down was never updated to match.** So the milestone's own
document specifies an alert against a label it also records as not existing. Found while
scoping; this task is where it is settled rather than approximated a second time.

**The decision: add the `trigger` label, because M4's stated reason for withholding it has
expired.** The withholding argument is explicit and conditional — *"nothing in M4 enriches
on demand… so a `trigger` label would carry one constant value"* — and M5 shipped demand
promotion, which is what `:415` predicted would make it a real series
(*"M5 introduces demand promotion and is where the band becomes a real series"*, said of
`usher.jobs.queued`'s priority band, on identical terms). `usher.enrichment.latency` gains
a second label, `trigger`, whose vocabulary is `demand` | `background` — **the same two
values the spans already carry** (`:131–132`: *"Spans carry `title_id`, `source`, and
`trigger` (`demand` vs `background`) as attributes"*), so this is one vocabulary reaching a
second signal rather than a new one being minted.

⚠️ **Two labels on one histogram is 2 × |outcome| series and the cardinality claim is
stated rather than assumed**: `outcome`'s vocabulary is closed and small, `trigger`'s is
two, and neither is catalog-sized — which is the test `:239–241` applies to every label in
this document. Recorded in the row's own PRD entry so the next label added to this series
is argued rather than appended.

Rule: `histogram_quantile(0.99, sum by (le) (rate(usher_enrichment_latency_bucket{trigger="demand"}[5m]))) > 5`,
`for: 15m`. **The `sum by (le)` is not optional** — a quantile taken without collapsing the
other labels is a quantile per outcome, and the p99 of the *failures* is a different claim
from the p99 an operator was promised.

**"Provider degraded — TMDb 429 or 5xx rate above threshold" (`:934`) is backed and its
threshold has never been measured.** The series is `usher.provider.requests`, labelled
`provider` and `status` (`src/usher/adapters/tmdb/client.py:84–86`, `:196`), and
**`status` is the HTTP status code rendered as a string** — `status = str(response.status_code)`
at `:180` — or the literal `"error"` for a transport failure that never reached a status
line (`:172`, and the `finally` at `:184–190` is what makes the failure counted rather than
silently absent). `:422–426` states the denominator rule: *"a denominator that omitted the
failures would read low exactly during an outage."*

Rule: `sum(rate(usher_provider_requests_total{provider="tmdb",status=~"429|5..|error"}[5m]))
/ sum(rate(usher_provider_requests_total{provider="tmdb"}[5m])) > 0.05`, `for: 10m`.
**`error` is in the numerator as well as the denominator**, which is the half `:422–426`
implies and does not say: a transport failure is a degraded provider by any reading, and
counting it only below the line makes the ratio fall during a total outage.

⚠️ **5% is a bar with no measurement under it, and it is labelled as one.** The live run
this project has is M9's S3 crawl — **130,334 requests over 1.98 h with no 429 and one
400** (`CLAUDE.md`'s M9 row; `.claude/rules/tmdb-and-enrichment.md` carries the taxonomy) —
so the observed rate at the one measured load is `1/130,334 ≈ 0.0008%`, four orders of
magnitude below the bar. That is the argument for 5% being *safe*, not for it being
*right*, and the rule's description says which. `.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md`
records what a bound with no measurement under it is, and this one is written down as
provisional with the re-measure named: the first real 429 this deployment sees.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_telemetry_pipeline.py::test_a_demand_enrichment_and_a_background_one_are_two_series_on_the_latency_histogram`
— drives `EnrichService` twice through the real code path, once from a `DEMAND`-priority
job and once from `BACKFILL`, and reads both points back out of an `InMemoryMetricReader`,
asserting the attribute sets differ on `trigger` and agree on `outcome`. At HEAD it fails:
the recorded attributes carry `outcome` alone. Its positive control is that **two** points
were recorded — a case that recorded one and compared it against itself would pass a
weaker "the label exists" assertion — plus `assert points, "no latency points recorded"`.

**Acceptance:**
- `usher.enrichment.latency` carries `outcome` **and** `trigger`; PRD 10's table row at
  `:153` names both, `:416–421`'s correction bullet gains a dated amendment saying the
  withholding condition expired with M5 rather than being reversed, and `:933`'s alert row
  is now expressible against the row above it. **The correction is amended, not replaced** —
  `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md`: *"Update the existing ADR's status and add the new
  evidence — never silently contradict it."*
- Two rules in the file; D11's bidirectional name check now names two missing rather than
  four.
- **Both fired once, deliberately.** Enrichment SLA: enqueue a `DEMAND` enrich against a
  provider stub that sleeps past 5 s, hold it for the window, record the instance and its
  `trigger="demand"` label, then remove the delay and record the resolve. **The firing must
  show the label** — an alert fired by making *all* enrichment slow proves the quantile
  works and not the scoping, which is the whole content of this task. Provider degraded:
  drive the TMDb client against a stub answering 429 above the rate for ten minutes;
  record the numerator and denominator series separately, so the `error`-in-both decision
  is observed rather than asserted.
- Sweep targets: the `trigger` label dropped from the record call must fail the two-series
  case alone; `sum by (le)` removed from the rule must fail a rule-shape assertion in
  `test_alerts.py` (a quantile expression over a multi-label histogram with no `by (le)` is
  a static defect and is checkable without a Prometheus); `error` dropped from the
  numerator must fail a rule-shape assertion that the numerator and denominator selectors
  differ only by the status match. The equivalent-mutant control is swapping the two
  attribute keys in the record call's dict literal — a fact about the code, since OTel
  aggregates on the attribute *set*.

### Task D13 — Disk projection, the only alert with no series at all, and the two documents that describe its subject are both wrong

**Depends on:** D10, D11, Group O   **Files:** `dashboards/alerts/usher.yml`, `dashboards/README.md`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** the `Image cache` row at `:668`), `tests/unit/test_alerts.py`

*"Disk projection — Postgres or image cache on track to fill within 14 days"* (`:935`).
**Neither half has a series today**, and the two halves fail differently.

**The Postgres half has an exact query and no metric.** `pg_total_relation_size` per table
is D10's size panel, and the projection is a linear fit over it. **It is not a metric Usher
emits and must not become one**: this document's first principle puts *"what is in the
library"* on Postgres (`:10–14`), a database's own size is the most Postgres-shaped
question there is, and an observable OTel callback **cannot query this database at all** —
`src/usher/telemetry.py:274–283` records why, in the register that has already cost this
project a design: OTel invokes the callback from the metric reader's background thread,
every database call here is a coroutine on asyncpg, and a callback that queried would have
to bounce onto the event loop and block the exporter thread on it. So the alert is a
Postgres-datasource rule, evaluated by Grafana against the same query D10's panel plots.

**The image-cache half has neither a query nor a bound, and `08-operations.md:668` says it
has both.** That row reads *"Image cache | Grows with use; capped by a configurable LRU
ceiling"*. **There is no LRU and no ceiling.** `src/usher/services/images.py:7–9`:
*"There is no resize, no format decision and **no eviction** — the provider serves every
width a client needs, the bytes are stored exactly as they arrived, and the ladder bounds
the cache at four entries an image by construction."* The four image settings in
`src/usher/config.py` are `image_cache_dir` (`:720`), `image_max_bytes` (`:731`, a
**per-image** 5 MiB refusal, not a cache ceiling), `image_fetch_timeout_seconds` (`:739`)
and `image_cdn_base_url` (`:755`). `DiskImageBlobStore` (`src/usher/adapters/images/disk.py:58`)
has `get`, `put`, `_forget_other_media_types` and `_path` — **no eviction method exists to
configure.** This task corrects that row.

**And the correction changes the alert rather than just the prose.** The cache is bounded
by *catalog images × four rungs × their sizes*, which is a slow-moving function of how much
of the catalog has been browsed — so a 14-day fill projection over it is a real question,
and the answer is a directory size, which nothing measures. **Two honest options and the
task takes the second**: emit a gauge from Usher (a periodic `du` over
`image_cache_dir`, which is I/O on a lane and a new instrument for one consumer), or read
the filesystem from the stack. The stack already has to see free space for the Postgres
half — a projection against total size with no free-space denominator is a number, not an
alert — so **the disk facts come from the observability stack's node exporter and not from
Usher**, and Usher's `compose.yml` gains nothing, per `:906` and the spec's `:76–78`.

🔴 **The threshold is not derived from `08-operations.md:643–674`'s resource table, and
this bullet exists because doing so is a documented, expensive mistake.** That table's own
header says *"These are **sizing estimates for an operator provisioning a disk**. Nothing
in this project reads them, no host enforces them, and no policy is derived from them"*,
and records that M9's Track 2 derived a 2.0 GB ceiling from one row, measured a design at
2.702 GB and **withdrew the design** — against a number with no forcing function
(ADR-0036). The M10 spec restates the prohibition at `:250–256`. So the rule is grounded in
two **measured** series — `pg_database_size` and the filesystem's own free bytes — and in
neither case in a row of that table. The measured baseline recorded beside the rule is
`08-operations.md:657`'s **5,025,650,355 B (4,793 MB) at 1,272,367 titles with 130,647
enriched, 2026-08-12**, quoted as *what the database was on that date*, never as a budget.

Rule: `predict_linear(usher_disk_free_bytes{mountpoint=~"/|/var/lib/postgresql|/data"}[7d],
14 * 86400) < 0`, `for: 1h`, with a companion Postgres-datasource rule on the growth of
`pg_database_size` so an operator can tell *which* consumer is filling the disk. **A
7-day window predicting 14 days** rather than a shorter window, because this deployment's
growth is dominated by events that are days apart — a bootstrap phase, a crawl, a
`similar --rebuild` — and a 1-hour window extrapolated to a fortnight would page on every
`VACUUM`.

⚠️ **`predict_linear` over a filesystem that a migration transiently doubles will fire
spuriously and that is stated rather than tuned away.** `08-operations.md:660–663` measures
`m09d` at **+637 MB transient on `credits`** (794 → 1,431 MB, settled at 740 after a
`VACUUM FULL` the migration does not run) and `titles.credit_names` at **+624 MB settled,
+1,368 MB transient**. The rule's description names those two as known false-positive
sources, and the runbook entry says to silence during a migration rather than to widen the
window — widening it is how an alert stops seeing the thing it is for.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_alerts.py::test_the_disk_rule_is_grounded_in_a_measured_series_and_not_in_the_resource_table`
— asserts the disk rules' `expr` reference only series in an allow-list of *measured*
sources (`usher_disk_free_bytes`, `pg_database_size`) and that **no rule anywhere in the
file carries a literal byte threshold matching any figure in `08-operations.md`'s resource
table**, the figures parsed out of that table rather than retyped. At HEAD it fails on the
missing rule. Positive control: `assert table_figures, "no figures parsed out of the
resource table"` **and** a planted rule carrying `8589934592` (the old `~8 GB` row)
demonstrated red before the real rule is written — because a scan for numbers that finds
none passes exactly like a file with no bad numbers in it.

**Acceptance:**
- `08-operations.md:668` is corrected: no LRU, no configurable ceiling, no eviction; the
  bound is the ladder's four rungs an image (`services/images.py:7–9`), the growth driver
  is browse coverage, and the four real settings are named with `image_max_bytes` called a
  per-image refusal rather than a cache cap. **This is the only line of that file this task
  touches.**
- Two rules in the file (free-space projection, database growth); D11's name check now
  names one missing.
- **Fired once, deliberately, and the firing is the awkward one.** Filling a real disk is
  not acceptable and shrinking the threshold to fire is testing the threshold rather than
  the rule. The recipe: point the rule at a small scratch filesystem (a loopback or a
  tmpfs mount with a stated size), write into it at a measured rate, and record
  `predict_linear`'s output crossing zero with the wall-clock times and the fill rate. The
  recorded observation names the filesystem, its size and the rate — **a firing whose
  recipe is "we lowered the number until it fired" is recorded as not having fired.**
- The rule's description names the two transient-migration false positives with their
  measured sizes and points at the runbook's silence procedure.

### Task D14 — Cost anomaly, whose series is a ledger nothing could read until this milestone

**Depends on:** D3, D10, D11   **Files:** `dashboards/alerts/usher.yml`, `dashboards/README.md`, `tests/unit/test_alerts.py`, `tests/integration/test_cost_anomaly_query.py`

*"Cost anomaly — Daily LLM spend > 3× trailing 7-day median"* (`:936`). **This is the alert
`m08a` deferred an index for by name.** `src/usher/db/migrations/versions/m08a_curation.py:206–210`
writes the DDL out and says what it is for: *"dashboard 5's 'LLM spend per day and month'
and the cost-anomaly alert ('daily spend > 3x the trailing 7-day median'), both WHERE at >=
:since — `CREATE INDEX ix_llm_calls_at ON llm_calls (at);`"*. D3 ships that index with the
read; this task ships the rule that closes the loop M8 opened.

**It is a Postgres-datasource rule and there is no `usher.llm.*` metric to write it
against, deliberately.** `:309–312`: *"This document's own first principle puts LLM spend
on Postgres — `llm_calls` is the record — so there is no `usher.llm.*` series at all"*, and
M8's boundary calls record the same refusal. The rule is SQL:

```sql
WITH daily AS (
  SELECT date_trunc('day', at) AS day, sum(cost_usd) AS spend
  FROM llm_calls WHERE at >= now() - interval '8 days' GROUP BY 1
)
SELECT (SELECT spend FROM daily WHERE day = date_trunc('day', now()))
     > 3 * (SELECT percentile_cont(0.5) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY spend)
            FROM daily WHERE day < date_trunc('day', now()))
```

**Four properties of that query, each a decision the panel beside it does not have to
make.**

- **Median, not mean**, as PRD 10 specifies — and the reason is this deployment's shape:
  one generation per household per night (`:353–357`), so a single failed night at $0 and a
  single re-run at 2× would drag a mean far enough that 3× stops meaning anything.
- **Eight days, not seven.** The trailing median excludes today, so the window has to hold
  seven complete days *plus* the partial one being judged. A seven-day window including
  today compares today against a median it is a member of, which is a bar that moves toward
  whatever fired it.
- 🔴 **A `0` median makes `3 × median` zero and any spend at all fires.** That is the
  default state of this deployment: `cost_usd` defaults to `0` because both per-million-token
  prices default to `0` (`:702–706`), which is *"the honest value for a local model and the
  wrong one for a hosted model an operator forgot to price"*, and this host's LLM is a local
  vLLM. **So the rule carries an absolute floor** — it does not fire below a configured
  minimum daily spend — and the alert's description says that an unpriced deployment is
  silent by construction, pointing at the two price settings. Without the floor this alert
  pages on the first cent ever spent, forever, on the majority of deployments.
- **`cost_usd` is `NUMERIC(12, 8)` and the comparison stays in `numeric`**
  (`02-data-model.md:699`: *"never a float: `$3/Mtok × 1,200 tokens` is exactly `0.0036` and
  at scale 4 a `$0.02/Mtok` call stores as `0.0000` — measured"*). A rule that casts to
  `float8` for the ratio reintroduces exactly the rounding that column exists to refuse.

**The alert reads a ledger of *attempts*, which is wider than a ledger of calls and is what
makes it honest about a failing provider.** `record()` is called on the failure path too
(`ports/repository/llm_call.py`'s `record` docstring, and `02-data-model.md:699`), so a
night of 200 failed completions that each spent tokens before validating to nothing appears
here as spend — which is the anomaly an operator most wants to see, and which a ledger of
successes would hide by exactly the failures.

**Failing test first:** `tests/integration/test_cost_anomaly_query.py::test_the_anomaly_query_fires_on_a_tripled_day_and_not_on_a_doubled_one`
— seeds a real `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` with seven trailing days at a fixed spend and a
today row, parametrised over **literal** multiples: `2.9×` must not fire, `3.1×` must. The
literals are the point — a case spelling its fixture as `3 * median ± 1` moves both sides
together and pins that a multiplier is in force while pinning nothing about its value,
which is the failure `.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md` records at `TICKET_TTL_SECONDS`,
`CAST_LIMIT` and `SimilarityService._WEIGHTS` — three constants in one milestone. A third
arm seeds a **zero** trailing median with a nonzero today and asserts the floor suppresses
it. At HEAD the file does not exist. Positive control: `assert len(seeded) == 8` and an
assertion that the trailing median is the value the fixture intended, read back out of the
database rather than computed in Python — the two disagree the moment a timezone does.

**Acceptance:**
- One rule; **D11's bidirectional name check is now green**, seven rules against PRD 10's
  seven rows, and that transition is this task's completion signal for the whole alert
  half of the group.
- The floor is a stated constant with its reason in the rule description, and the
  description names the two price settings whose defaults make an unpriced deployment
  silent.
- **Fired once, deliberately**: run four `usher curate` generations in one evening against
  the local vLLM with prices configured (`3`/`15` per Mtok, the pair the 2026-08-07 live
  verification used and reconciled to eight decimal places), against a seeded trailing
  week; record the ledger rows, the computed median, the ratio and the alert instance.
  **The firing records the `cost_usd` values it was computed from**, because a firing whose
  inputs were not written down cannot be distinguished from a threshold that was lowered.
- `EXPLAIN` on the query shows `ix_llm_calls_at` serving the window, at a seeded size where
  the index is chosen, with that size quoted — the same care D3's plan assertion takes, and
  for the same reason.
- Sweep targets: the window narrowed from 8 days to 7 must fail an arm seeding a spike on
  day 7 (which a 7-day window includes in its own median and a 4-day one does not);
  `percentile_cont(0.5)` replaced by `avg` must fail an arm whose trailing week holds one
  zero day and one double day — **the two agree on a flat week, so a flat fixture ratifies
  the mean**; the floor deleted must fail the zero-median arm alone.
## Group F — the failure-mode gaps, the operator bugs, and #8's diagnosis

Phase 2. Ten tasks over five issues (#5, #6, #7, #8, #10) and three rows of PRD
08's degradation table. **Three of this group's ten tasks exist because a
recorded claim is wrong**, and each says so where the claim lives rather than
only here: the spec's `supports_push` bullet inverts PRD 08's own counterfactual
(S10, now in Phase 1), #10's five headline counts are an M8-head measurement that M9 moved and
one of which cannot hold under the rule that produced its sibling (F8), and the
carried-debt entry's prescribed fix for #7 — *"observed through a session log"*
— **describes what the case has done since the day it landed** (F7).

Baseline at HEAD `3adb5b0`, measured 2026-08-13: **4,067 unit passed / 4
skipped** (42.8 s), and **1,232 integration / 22 skipped** — the integration
figure is derived rather than read, because two of three runs printed no summary
line at all (see F6's `-qq` note) and the third reported *3 failed, 1,229 passed,
22 skipped*. Quote it as derived until somebody runs it without a verbosity
flag.

Files this group shares with others, declared so the orchestrator can sequence
rather than discover: `src/usher/api/lanes.py` (**S10 in Phase 1**, then F2 here),
`docs/prd/08-operations.md` (S10's row `:298`, F2's paragraph `:447–461`, F3's
row `:301` — three disjoint anchors), `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md`'s carried-debt
section (F5 at `:1314–1330`, F7 at `:1031–1072`, F9 at `:974–1004` — three
disjoint bullets), `docs/prd/decisions/0030-*.md` (F3 only), `src/usher/cli.py`
(F4's `_unmatched`, F10's `_work` loop — disjoint functions, but a mutation
sweep of either invalidates the other, so they are serialised like everything
else in the phase).

---

### Task F2 — orphaned claims reach `/health/ready`'s body, and the number has been computed and discarded at both call sites since W1

**Depends on:** S10 (Phase 1 — both edit `src/usher/api/lanes.py`)   **Files:** `src/usher/api/lanes.py:544–580` (`_run_worker`), `src/usher/api/dto/health.py:30–55` (`LaneReport`), `src/usher/api/routers/health.py:91–140`, `src/usher/cli.py:505–530` (`_work`'s `_measure`), `tests/unit/test_api_health.py`, `tests/integration/test_health.py`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** the readiness paragraphs at `:447–461`)

**The status code does not move and this task is written to respect that.**
`08-operations.md:447–456` gates readiness on Postgres and migration state
alone and gives the reason: *"a readiness probe that failed because a source was
unreachable would take the process out of a load balancer for a reason
restarting it cannot fix."* `routers/health.py:123` computes
`is_ready = all(checks.model_dump().values())` over `ReadinessChecks`, whose
docstring (`dto/health.py:18–24`) says every field added to *that* model becomes
part of the status code automatically — which is exactly why `LaneReport` is a
separate model (`:30–51`). **This task adds fields to `LaneReport`, never to
`ReadinessChecks`**, and `tests/unit/test_api_health.py:193
test_no_lane_state_can_change_the_readiness_verdict` is extended to cover them.

**The condition is invisible today and the number already exists.**
`JobWorker.recover()` (`services/jobs.py:264–293`) returns the count of claims
it took back from a process that stopped heartbeating. **Both callers throw the
return value away**: `api/lanes.py:575` is `await worker.recover()` and
`cli.py:520` is the same statement. The only trace is a `logger.warning` at
`jobs.py:287–292`, and only when the count is non-zero. `/health/ready`'s body
carries `LaneReport(push=lanes.running_sources(), worker=lanes.worker_running())`
(`routers/health.py:140`) and nothing about the queue at all. So M9's S3
condition — one of three workers dead at 78 minutes holding 20 claims — is now
*survivable* (ADR-0037's lease plus `touch()`) and still *unobservable*: an
operator watching `/health/ready` sees `worker: true` throughout.

**Report what `recover()` measured, not a fresh query, and the reason is a
cost.** The compose healthcheck polls this endpoint **every 2 s**
(`08-operations.md:466–467`), and readiness makes *no upstream request at all*
because everything it reports is in-memory. A `SELECT count(*) FROM jobs WHERE
status = 'running' AND updated_at <= clock_timestamp() - …` per poll is a third
statement per 2 s over a table with **no index on `status = 'running'`** —
`db/models/jobs.py:76,84` declare `ix_jobs_claim` partial on `pending` and
`ix_jobs_parked` partial on `parked`, and nothing else — so it is a scan of a
table M4 measured at 1,126,674 rows. The in-memory alternative costs nothing and
is what `recover()` already knows.

**`null` is the honest value for a process that runs no worker, and it is not
`0`.** With `USHER_WORKER_ENABLED=false` beside a `usher work` container —
the topology `08-operations.md:306` prices — this process never calls
`recover()`, so `0` would assert *"no orphans"* about a question it never
asked. `SourceStatus.push_available: bool | None` (`ports/source.py:363`) is the
precedent and the docstring reuses its wording: `None` means *not probed*.

**What the field is, precisely:** `recovered_claims: int | None` — the total
this process has taken back since it started — and `recovered_at: datetime |
None`, the instant of the last recovery pass that found something. A rate is
what an alert wants and a total is what a probe can honestly carry, since a
readiness body is read by a poller with no memory. `crashed_sources: list[str]`
joins it in the same model, which is what gives S10's `crashed_sources()` its
first caller in `src/`.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/integration/test_health.py::test_a_recovered_orphan_is_reported_in_the_body_and_moves_no_status_code`
— plants a `jobs` row directly at `status = 'running'` with `updated_at` older
than `USHER_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS` (a raw `INSERT`, for `db-and-sql.md`'s reason:
the trigger and `clock_timestamp()` make this the only way to own the column),
drives one worker pass through the lifespan's own lane, then reads
`/health/ready` and asserts `status_code == 200`, `body["status"] == "ready"`,
`body["lanes"]["recovered_claims"] == 1` and `body["lanes"]["recovered_at"] is
not None`. At HEAD it fails on `KeyError: 'recovered_claims'` — the key does not
exist. Its positive control is the assertion that the planted row really was
`running` and really was older than the lease **before** the pass, asserted from
a second session, because a row the recovery could not see produces `0` and
`0` is also what a broken report produces.

**Acceptance:**
- `/health/ready` answers **200** with a non-zero `recovered_claims`, proved by
  the case above and by the extension of
  `test_no_lane_state_can_change_the_readiness_verdict` to the three new
  fields — the extension is demonstrated red first, against a mutant that folds
  any one of them into `ReadinessChecks`, which is the mutation
  `dto/health.py:33–41` exists to make loud.
- `recovered_claims` is `null` — not `0` — in an app built with
  `worker_enabled=False`, asserted in `tests/unit/test_api_health.py` against
  the existing no-lanes fixture.
- `usher work`'s `_measure` (`cli.py:505–530`) records the same total; the CLI
  has no readiness route, so it prints it in the pass line beside
  `f"{ran} jobs"` rather than growing a surface. `recover()`'s return value has
  a reader at **both** call sites, which is the grep that closes this task:
  `grep -rn "worker.recover()" src/` must show no bare-statement call.
- `08-operations.md:447–461` gains one sentence naming the three fields and
  restating that none of them is in the status code, with the reason it already
  gives for the lanes.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: `recovered_claims` moved into
  `ReadinessChecks` must fail the verdict case; the counter incremented before
  `recover()` rather than from its return must fail the integration case on the
  number; `crashed_sources` reported from `running_sources()` instead must fail
  S10's own case. Equivalent-mutant control: the two independent field
  declarations `recovered_claims`/`recovered_at` swapped in `LaneReport` — a
  fact about the code, since every construction is by keyword and every
  assertion reads the serialised mapping by key — measured against all five gate
  steps.

**Risks:**
- **A per-process total cannot see a peer's orphans that a peer recovered.** Two
  workers each report what *they* took back, and the sum is the truth. Stated in
  the field's docstring rather than solved, because the alternative is the
  per-poll query this task priced and refused. The dashboard that wants the
  deployment-wide number reads it from Postgres on its own beat, which is Phase
  2's alerting work and not a readiness probe's.

### Task F3 — ADR-0030's image amendment is answered, and closure is what makes answering it the work rather than the edit

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `docs/prd/decisions/0030-the-problem-code-vocabulary-is-designed-against-a-real-503.md` (**only** the `### Amendment 2026-08-11 — GET /images/{image_id}…` section at `:434–490`, and the vocabulary table between `<!-- vocabulary:begin -->` and `<!-- vocabulary:end -->` **if and only if** the answer is to mint), `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** the `Provider image CDN unreachable` row at `:301`), `src/usher/api/routers/images.py:80–120,222–270`, `src/usher/api/dto/problem.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_problem_vocabulary.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_images.py`

**The vocabulary is closed at seven members by design, and that is why this is
an amendment and not a patch.** ADR-0030's rule 1 admits no member without an
emitter in `src/usher/api/` at the commit that adds it; its stability rule fixes
one status per code forever, enforced by
`test_every_code_carries_one_status_everywhere_it_is_raised`; and its closure is
checked **in both directions** — `test_api_openapi.py::test_every_member_of_the_vocabulary_has_a_route_that_can_emit_it`
walks each route's call graph and `test_api_problem_vocabulary.py` compares
`ProblemCode` against the markered table as sets. **Closure means an eighth
member cannot be added quietly**: the enum, the table row, the emitter and both
closure directions land in one commit or the suite is red. It also means the
*answer* is the deliverable — the record's own text says a request left open
while the table states an answer flatly is *"how an unanswered question quietly
becomes an answered one"*, and it names two files that had already cited the
sibling amendment as precedent.

**What ships today, verified.** `routers/images.py` has three upstream arms
(`:231` `PortUnavailable` → `503 source_unavailable` **with** `Retry-After`;
`:241` `MediaTypeNotServable` → `404 not_found`; `:253` residual
`PortDataMalformed` → `503 source_unavailable` with **no** `Retry-After`), and
its own table at `:86–88` says so. `08-operations.md:301` closes with *"That
second one's honest status is a 502 and ADR-0030's closed vocabulary has no code
for one"* — a sentence that reads as an open defect, which is exactly how the
M10 spec picked it up at `:160–161`.

**The measurement the amendment lacks, and it is this task's first step.** The
404 arm is measured — an `image/svg+xml` logo, roughly **1 title in 17**
(`.claude/rules/ports-and-error-taxonomy.md`, and C4's follow-up corrected its
*stated reason* while confirming the rate). **The residual arm's rate has never
been measured at all.** A 4xx from a withdrawn rung, a body past
`USHER_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` and a captive portal's HTML are three different
populations, and ADR-0030's bar — *"does an existing member already carry this
meaning, and would a client branch differently on it?"* — cannot be applied to a
population nobody has counted. Measure it against the live TMDb image CDN in
**bounded** requests with no walk, on the same discipline Phase 1 uses: a sample
of stored `images` rows across kinds and rungs, each fetched once, classified by
which arm the shipped `ProviderCdnImageFetcher` takes.

**The two answers and what each costs, written before the measurement so the
result cannot choose the argument.** *Mint `upstream_unusable` at 502*: the
naming rule gives `<subject>_<state>`, rule 1 is already met (`images.py:253`
is an emitter at this commit), and the case for it is that **the two arms differ
in whether retrying can ever work** — which a cache or a retry layer in front of
a client does branch on. *Decline*: ADR-0030's own text says an image client
*"paints a placeholder either way"*, `Retry-After`'s presence and absence
already carry the distinction on the wire in a standard, machine-readable form,
and C5's sweep measured **both** halves pinned (`Retry-After` added to the
malformed arm fails 1 case; removed from the transient arm fails 1). The
declining answer is the cheaper one and the record already contains most of its
argument; it is not the pre-registered answer, because the rate has not been
measured.

**Failing test first:** one per outcome, and the task writes the one its
measurement earns — the answer is not knowable before the measurement, so
pre-registering both is what stops the result choosing the argument. If the
answer is **mint**:
`tests/unit/test_api_images.py::test_a_cdn_answer_that_will_never_become_usable_is_a_502_and_not_a_503`
— drives the residual `PortDataMalformed` arm and asserts `status_code == 502`,
`body["code"] == "upstream_unusable"`, and no `Retry-After`; at HEAD it fails on
`assert 503 == 502`. If the answer is **decline**:
`tests/unit/test_api_problem_vocabulary.py::test_the_image_proxys_amendment_is_no_longer_open`
— parses ADR-0030 for the amendment's `Status of the amendment:` line and
refuses the literal `Open`; at HEAD it fails naming `Open`, and it carries the
positive control every scan in this repository needs (`assert
statuses, "the amendment scan found no status lines"` plus the anchor that the
*accepted* `not_playable` amendment is found and reads `Accepted`), because a
regex that matches nothing passes exactly like one that passes.

**Acceptance:**
- The amendment's `Status of the amendment:` line is `Accepted` or `Declined`,
  never `Open`, and carries the measured residual-arm rate with its denominator
  and its date.
- `08-operations.md:301`'s closing sentence states the answer instead of the
  question. If declined, it says `Retry-After` **is** the contract rather than
  an interim; if minted, the row's second arm reads 502 and the table matches
  the router.
- If minted: `ProblemCode` gains one member, the markered vocabulary table gains
  one row, `images.py:253–265` raises it, and **both** closure directions stay
  green without either being weakened — demonstrated by planting the member in
  the enum alone (must fail naming it) and in the table alone (must fail naming
  it), which is the pair V1's ledger records as the only honest proof that a
  both-ways check is both ways.
- If declined: the second open amendment (`GET /search`'s `?mode=semantic`,
  `:492–538`) is **left open and untouched**. It is a different question with a
  stronger case for minting by the record's own reading, and answering it here
  would be the fan-out failure ADR-0030 exists to prevent.
- Live measurement follows the house rule: the bar is written to `/var/tmp`
  (**not** `/tmp`, which is tmpfs on this host) with a `sha256` before the first
  request, and the measurement is committed rather than the data.

### Task F4 — #5's "one-line change" is refused, and the taxonomy argument is why the fix belongs at the call site

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/cli.py:409–439` (`_unmatched`), `tests/unit/test_cli.py`, `tests/integration/test_cli_pipeline.py`, `docs/prd/decisions/0026-the-cli-boundary-names-families.md` (**only** a new bullet under `## Consequences`), closes #5

**The debt entry calls this "a one-line change, carried rather than taken
because ADR-0026's family is an argued taxonomy, not a list — answering 'how
often does an operator hit it' is the work." This task answers that question and
the answer refuses the one-line change.**

**The defect, precisely, and it is one of three shapes.** `cli._unmatched`
(`:420–431`) calls `media_items.attach_title(_as_uuid(resolve, "media item id"),
title_id=_as_uuid(title, "title id"), episode_id=None)`. Three bad `--title`
values give three different answers: a value that is **not a UUID** is caught by
`_as_uuid` (`cli.py:1567–1571`) and exits with `title id is not a uuid: …`; a
`--resolve` naming **no media item** makes `attach_title` return `rowcount == 0`
and the command prints `no such media item` (`:431`); and a **well-formed UUID
naming no title** violates `fk_media_items_title_id_titles`
(`a8a0e10ff464_core_schema.py:264`), which
`db/repositories/media_item.py:676–680` translates to `RepositoryConflict`. That
type is not in `cli.OPERATOR_ERRORS` (`cli.py:151–175`), so `main` re-raises and
the operator gets the stack. **Two of the three arms are handled and the third
is the one an operator hits by pasting the wrong column of a listing.**

**The enumeration the debt entry asks for, done by reading every raise site.**
`grep -rn "raise RepositoryConflict\|raise _conflict" src/usher/` finds **22
raise sites across 14 modules**, one of which is `_errors.py:178`'s
`refusals_as_conflict` and therefore stands for seven more callers. Of all of
them, the ones a **CLI argument** can reach:

- `usher unmatched --resolve … --title …` → `media_item.py:680`, the FK above.
  **The only site where an operator's own argument is the direct and sole
  cause.**
- `usher bootstrap --phase …` → `import_run.py:111`, *"an import run for
  {dataset} already exists under a different id"*, `uq_import_runs_dataset`.
  Reachable, but by running two bootstraps at once rather than by a typo — and
  `BootstrapService._concede_to_other_owner` already answers it by touching
  nothing and returning the winner's row (`domain/jobs.py:194–198`), so it does
  not reach the boundary.
- Everything else is unreachable from an argument. `usher similar --rebuild`
  reaches `search.py:409,717`, whose own docstrings call them *"a bug in the
  blend"*; `usher derive` reaches `people.py:450,613` and
  `collection.py:255,288`; `usher curate` reaches `curation.py:227`; `usher
  search`/`suggest` reach `search_query.py:124,137` and
  `services/search.py`'s `except UsherPortError` absorbs them (F2's M9 sweep
  measured widening that arm to `except Exception` as a 1-case kill); `usher
  work` reaches most of the rest. **`sources.py:57,71` is reachable only from
  `POST /admin/sources`, because the CLI has no add-source command** — the
  fifteen subparsers at `cli.py:1588–1737` are `serve`, `bootstrap`,
  `bootstrap-status`, `sync`, `sync-status`, `unmatched`, `work`, `index`,
  `derive`, `search`, `suggest`, `similar`, `home`, `curate`, `push`.

**So the frequency answer is: one command, one argument.** And that is the
argument against the tuple change, because `OPERATOR_ERRORS`' own comment
(`cli.py:136–141`) names the cost precisely — *"`RepositoryConflict`,
`RepositoryNotFound` and `PortDataMalformed` stay out because several of their
raise sites are deliberate tripwires for bugs in this project's own code
(`title_neighbors`' bounds, the credits delete's scope, a curated batch this
project assembled wrong), and a one-line message is exactly what those must not
become."* Adding `RepositoryConflict` to the tuple collapses **all 22 sites** to
one sentence to fix one. ADR-0026's stated bar is *"a family belongs in the
tuple when an operator can act on it"*, and 1-of-22 is not a family. **The fix
is a lookup at the call site**, which is also what issue #5's own *Done when*
asks for: *"an unknown `--title` prints a one-line message naming the id."*

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_cli.py::test_resolving_to_a_title_that_does_not_exist_names_the_id_and_keeps_the_stack_out_of_it`
— runs `usher unmatched --resolve <a media item the fake holds> --title <a
well-formed uuid the fake's `TitleRepository.get` answers `None` for>`, asserts
`SystemExit` with the id in the message, that the word `Traceback` is absent
from stderr, and — the arm that stops the fix being a bare `except` — that
`attach_title` was **not called**, so nothing was written. At HEAD it fails
because the fake has no foreign key and `attach_title` succeeds, which is the
honest red: the unit arm proves the *pre-check*, not the translation. Paired
with
`tests/integration/test_cli_pipeline.py::test_an_unknown_title_id_is_a_sentence_against_real_postgres`,
which drives the same command against a real `pgvector/pgvector:pg17` where the
FK exists and fails at HEAD with `RepositoryConflict` and ~60 frames.

**Acceptance:**
- `usher unmatched --resolve <id> --title <unknown uuid>` prints one line naming
  the title id and exits 1, with no traceback and no write, verified against
  real Postgres.
- **`cli.OPERATOR_ERRORS` is unchanged**, and
  `tests/unit/test_cli_errors.py::test_the_port_taxonomy_is_split_and_the_base_class_is_not_in_the_tuple`
  — which reads the set off `UsherPortError.__subclasses__()` — gains
  `RepositoryConflict`'s exclusion **reason** in its assertion message, so the
  next reader meets the argument rather than the omission.
- ADR-0026 gains one bullet under `## Consequences` recording the enumeration —
  22 raise sites, one CLI-argument-reachable — because that count is what the
  next request to widen the tuple has to beat, and a count nobody wrote down is
  a count somebody re-derives wrongly.
- The third arm is not silently changed: `--resolve` naming no media item still
  prints `no such media item`, pinned by a case, so the fix does not
  accidentally make one message serve two conditions.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the pre-check deleted must fail
  both cases; the pre-check spelled as `except RepositoryConflict` around
  `attach_title` must fail the unit case's *nothing-was-written* arm alone,
  which is the assertion that separates a lookup from a swallow;
  `RepositoryConflict` added to `OPERATOR_ERRORS` must fail the taxonomy case.
  Equivalent-mutant control: one sentence of `_unmatched`'s docstring reworded —
  checked first against `grep -rln "getdoc\|__doc__\|ast.unparse\|getsource"
  tests/`, whose scans do not read `cli.py`'s prose (M8 Task 18's ledger
  measured this and it is re-checked, not inherited).

### Task F5 — `/openapi.json` names the problem media type, and the obvious spelling produces a dangling `$ref`

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `src/usher/api/app.py:52–130`, `tests/unit/test_api_openapi.py` (the module docstring's `One bounded untruth` paragraph at `:40–49`, plus a new case), `tests/unit/test_api_playback.py:1017–1039`, `tests/unit/test_api_watch.py:470–496`, `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md` (**only** the carried-debt bullet at `:1314–1330`), closes #6

**Measured at HEAD 2026-08-13, by building the app and reading its own
document: 35 operations, 92 response bodies, of which 56 carry
`ProblemResponse` — and all 56 are keyed `application/json`, none
`application/problem+json`.** The wire is not in doubt: `PROBLEM_MEDIA_TYPE` is
`"application/problem+json"` (`api/dto/problem.py:50`) and `api/errors.py:153`
sets it on every problem `JSONResponse`. FastAPI 0.140.13 renders
`responses={404: {"model": ProblemResponse}}` under the *route's* response media
type, so the document describes the one header RFC 9457 makes load-bearing at
the wrong key, 56 times.

**Three spellings, measured on a two-route probe rather than reasoned about, and
two of the three are wrong.**

| spelling | what `/openapi.json` renders |
|---|---|
| `{"model": P}` | `application/json` only — HEAD's behaviour |
| `{"model": P, "content": {"application/problem+json": {}}}` | **both** keys, and the problem one carries **no schema** |
| `{"content": {"application/problem+json": {"schema": {"$ref": "#/components/schemas/P"}}}}` | the right key with the right `$ref` — **and `P` never reaches `components/schemas`** |

The third is the spelling a reader reaches for and it is the dangerous one: with
`model` gone, FastAPI never registers the model, so the document ships a
`$ref` pointing at nothing while an assertion spelled
`schema["$ref"].endswith("/ProblemResponse")` passes. Verified directly —
an app carrying only that spelling renders `components.schemas == ["Ok"]`.

**So the mechanism is a custom `openapi()` on the app, keyed on the schema
rather than on the status.** `create_app` (`api/app.py:52`) installs no
`openapi` override today. The rewrite walks every operation's `responses` and,
for any content entry whose schema `$ref` ends `/ProblemResponse`, moves it from
`application/json` to `application/problem+json`. `model=` stays at all 20
`_*_FAILURES` constants across 14 router modules, so registration is untouched;
no route decorator changes. **`GET /health/ready`'s 503 is excluded by
construction rather than by an exemption list** — its declared model is
`ReadinessResponse` (`routers/health.py:33–38`), which the predicate does not
match — and that is the argument for keying on the schema: ADR-0030's exemption
set already exists as data (`PROBLEM_EXEMPTIONS`) and a second, differently
spelled exemption is the drift that record exists to prevent.

**The two known-wrong markers are this task's obligation and one of them
misnames its own twin.** `tests/unit/test_api_playback.py:1020–1029` and
`tests/unit/test_api_watch.py:482–491` carry identical 🔴 comments added
2026-08-12, each saying the fix *"would fork this assertion and its twin in
`test_api_watch.py`"* — correct in the playback file, and a self-reference in
the watch file, whose twin is in the playback file. Both markers are deleted by
this task, and the second's misdirection is worth naming in the commit body:
**a marker that points at itself is a marker that survives a grep for the thing
it was meant to make findable.** The assertions themselves fork narrowly — the
problem bodies at `test_api_playback.py:1031`, `:1037` and
`test_api_watch.py:492` move to `content["application/problem+json"]`, while
`test_api_playback.py:1017` and `test_api_watch.py:494` stay at
`application/json`, because a **200** really is `application/json`.

**Failing test first:**
`tests/unit/test_api_openapi.py::test_every_problem_response_is_described_at_the_media_type_the_wire_sends`
— walks every operation through the file's existing `_IncludedRouter` descent,
collects every response body whose schema resolves to `ProblemResponse`, and
asserts each is keyed `PROBLEM_MEDIA_TYPE` and none is keyed
`application/json`. At HEAD it fails with 56 at `application/json` and 0 at
`application/problem+json`. Two positive controls, and the second is the one
this repository has been bitten without: `assert len(problem_bodies) >= 50, "the
walk found no problem responses"` (a walk that globs nothing passes exactly like
one that passes — and FastAPI 0.140's `include_router` makes that the default
failure here), and `assert "ProblemResponse" in document["components"]["schemas"]`,
which is the only thing that separates the working fix from the dangling-`$ref`
spelling measured above.

**Acceptance:**
- `/openapi.json` names `application/problem+json` for **56** problem bodies and
  `application/json` for **0** of them; the 36 non-problem bodies are unmoved,
  asserted as a count so a rewrite that touched a 200 is a red.
- `ProblemResponse` is still in `components/schemas`, asserted in the same case,
  with the dangling-`$ref` spelling planted and watched to fail it — the
  measurement above is why this is an acceptance criterion rather than a note.
- `GET /health/ready`'s 503 still describes `ReadinessResponse` and is still
  exempt through `PROBLEM_EXEMPTIONS` alone; **no second exemption list is
  minted**, and `test_the_readiness_probe_stays_exempt_and_answers_its_own_shape`
  passes unmodified.
- Both 🔴 markers are gone, the five assertions are forked as listed above, and
  `test_api_openapi.py`'s module docstring loses its `One bounded untruth`
  paragraph (`:40–49`) rather than keeping a paragraph describing a defect that
  no longer exists.
- `docs/prd/09-roadmap.md:1314–1330` is struck through as ✅ with the date and
  the mechanism, in the shape that section uses for a paid debt.
- Sweep targets, expected verdict written first: the rewrite keyed on `status`
  rather than on the schema must fail on `/health/ready` (the exemption case);
  the rewrite that **adds** the problem key without removing the JSON one must
  fail the new case's `none is keyed application/json` arm; `model=` deleted
  from one `_*_FAILURES` constant must fail on the components assertion.
  Equivalent-mutant control: the two independent entries of `_PLAY_FAILURES`'s
  `404`/`409` dict swapped — a fact about the code, since FastAPI merges it into
  a `responses` object keyed by status and every case reads it as a mapping
  (M9's D4 ledger measured this control against all five gate steps and it is
  re-measured, not inherited).
