### Task R8 — a tag-triggered release job, and the licensing guarantee that has to extend to the artifact

**Depends on:** R1, R3   **Files:** `.github/workflows/release.yml` (new), `tests/unit/test_release_workflow.py` (new), `README.md` (a `Running it` note naming the published image)

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is **46 lines with a single `check` job** (verified:
`wc -l`), triggered on `push: branches: [main]` and `pull_request` (`:3–6`).
There is no tag trigger, no release job, no container build or push, and no
artifact upload. `git tag -l` is empty. **The image has never been built by
anything but a hand-run `docker build`.**

**A separate workflow file, not a job in `ci.yml`.** `ci.yml`'s trigger has no
`tags:` entry, and adding one would run the whole 5,325-case suite on every tag
as well as on the merge that preceded it — the same commits, twice. A
`release.yml` on `push: tags: ['v*']` is one file, one trigger, one job, and it
can be read without reading the gate.

**The image name is `ghcr.io/anirudhlath/usher`**, i.e.
`ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}` lower-cased. GHCR rather than Docker Hub:
`GITHUB_TOKEN` already authenticates to it with a `packages: write` permission
and no secret to manage, which for a single-maintainer project is the whole
argument.

**Action pins, all four re-measured 2026-08-13 with
`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/ref/tags/<tag>` — the check
`.claude/rules/config-cli-and-deployment.md` records M1 learning the hard way,
when `astral-sh/setup-uv@v9` resolved to nothing and the job died in 2 s at *Set
up job*:**

| action | latest release | floating major tag exists |
|---|---|---|
| `actions/checkout` | v7.0.1 | `v4`, `v5`, `v6`, **`v7`** |
| `docker/login-action` | v4.6.0 | `v3`, **`v4`** |
| `docker/metadata-action` | v6.2.0 | `v5`, **`v6`** |
| `docker/build-push-action` | v7.3.0 | `v5`, `v6`, **`v7`** |
| `actions/attest-build-provenance` | v4.2.2 | **`v4`** |
| `astral-sh/setup-uv` | **v10.0.0** | `v4`–**`v7` only; `v8`/`v9`/`v10` do not exist** |

⚠️ **The last row is the finding and it applies to `ci.yml` as well as to this
file.** `setup-uv`'s floating major tags stop at `v7` while its releases have
reached `v10.0.0`, so `@v8`, `@v9` and `@v10` all resolve to nothing.
`ci.yml:21`'s `astral-sh/setup-uv@v9.0.0` is an **exact release tag** and is
therefore fine — but anyone "modernising" it to `@v10` gets a job that fails
before checkout. Say so in a comment beside the pin. This workflow needs no
`setup-uv` at all: the image build is `docker build`, and `uv` runs inside it.

**Multi-arch is declined, explicitly, with the reason and the reversal.** This
host is `x86_64` (`uname -m`), there is no arm64 machine here, and
`platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64` under QEMU would publish an arm64 image
that has never been started — a `uv sync` of 100+ wheels plus an editable install
under emulation, verified by nothing. **Nothing in the Dockerfile blocks it:**
`python:3.13-slim` is multi-arch and every dependency resolves to a prebuilt
`manylinux` wheel (`Dockerfile:3–8`). So the reversal is two lines —
`docker/setup-qemu-action@v4` plus a `platforms:` key — the day there is a
machine to test on. Record that in the workflow's own comment, not only here.

⚠️ **The licensing guarantee extends to the release artifact, and the honest
statement is narrower than "the guard covers it".**
`04-catalog-bootstrap.md:623` says *"The repository and its release artifacts
contain zero third-party metadata"*, and
`tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` is what enforces it. Its scope is
`_SCANNED_ROOTS = ("src", "tests")` for three of its checks (`:88`), and the
fourth — `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` — walks the **whole
repository** (`:220–233`). The runtime image's own-repo payload is exactly three
things (`Dockerfile:58–60`): `/app/.venv`, `/app/src`, and `alembic.ini`. Two
consequences, both measured:

- **`src/` is inside all four checks** — the module docstring already says so
  (`:13–14`) — and `alembic.ini` is inside the whole-repo scan only. Both are
  covered; the coverage is not uniform and the sentence should say which.
- 🔴 **`README.md` reaches the runtime image and the docstring does not mention
  it.** `pyproject.toml:5` declares `readme = "README.md"`, so hatchling embeds
  the whole file as the distribution's long description:
  `.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/usher-0.1.0.dist-info/METADATA` is **744
  lines** and `# Usher` starts at line 31. `LICENSE` rides along in
  `dist-info/licenses/`. So the image ships the README verbatim, inside the venv,
  and the README is covered by the **whole-repo dataset-row scan and by nothing
  else** — the three `src/`+`tests/` checks do not read it. That is adequate (a
  dataset row is the licence-relevant shape wherever it sits, which is that
  check's own argument at `:395–404`) and it is not what the docstring claims.
  Correct the docstring in this task.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_release_workflow.py::test_a_tag_push_builds_and_publishes_the_container_image`
— loads every file under `.github/workflows/` with `yaml.safe_load` and asserts
exactly one declares `on.push.tags` matching `v*`, that its job requests
`permissions: {contents: read, packages: write}`, and that some step's `uses`
starts `docker/build-push-action@` with `push: true` and a `tags:` input derived
from `docker/metadata-action`'s output rather than a literal. At HEAD
`.github/workflows/` holds one file with no `tags:` trigger and the case fails on
the count. Its positive control is `assert workflows, "the workflow scan found
nothing"` plus the named anchor `ci.yml` — and note the YAML trap this scan will
hit: **`on:` parses as the boolean `True` under `yaml.safe_load`**, because YAML
1.1 treats `on` as a truthy scalar, so the key to read is `document[True]` and a
naive `document["on"]` returns `None` and makes every assertion vacuous. Check
that before writing the assertions, not after.

**Second case, a guard rather than a discovery — verified true at HEAD before it
is written:** `test_the_image_copies_only_paths_the_licensing_guard_scans` parses
the `Dockerfile`'s `COPY` instructions (skipping `--from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv`,
which is a third-party image) and asserts every source path is either under
`src/` or is one of a named, reasoned set — `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`,
`alembic.ini`, `README.md` — each of which the whole-repo scan reads. A path
outside both fails with the reason in the message. **This is the assertion that
makes `04-catalog-bootstrap.md:623`'s "and its release artifacts" a check rather
than a hope**, and it is the one that goes red the day somebody adds
`COPY data/ ./data/`.

**Acceptance:**
- `.github/workflows/release.yml` exists, triggers on `push: tags: ['v*']`, and
  publishes `ghcr.io/anirudhlath/usher` tagged with both the semver tag and
  `latest` via `docker/metadata-action`.
- The job requests `contents: read`, `packages: write`, and — if provenance
  attestation is included — `attestations: write` and `id-token: write`, which is
  the permission set GitHub's own published-images guide names. Attestation is
  **optional here and worth taking**: it is one step and it is the only thing that
  lets a consumer verify the image came from this repository.
- Multi-arch is declined **in a comment in the workflow**, with `uname -m` and
  the two-line reversal named.
- `tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py`'s module docstring is corrected: the
  image copies `src/` *and* `alembic.ini`, and the installed distribution embeds
  `README.md` and `LICENSE`. The correction is a sentence, not a rewrite.
- `ci.yml` gains one comment beside `astral-sh/setup-uv@v9.0.0` recording that
  the floating majors stop at `v7` and that `@v10` resolves to nothing. **This is
  the only line of `ci.yml` this task touches.**
- The workflow is **not** verified by this task. It cannot be — nothing executes
  it until a tag exists — and R13 is where it runs. Do not report a green
  static test as a working release pipeline.

### Task R9 — the README says what is rebuildable and what is not, and it is the shorter of the two lists that matters

**Depends on:** Group K (`usher backup` / `usher restore`)   **Files:** `README.md` (a new `## Backup` section after `## Command line` ends at `:673`), `docs/prd/08-operations.md` (**only** `:585–588`)

`grep -ni backup README.md` returns **nothing** — verified, exit code 1. The
README describes ten commands across 500 lines and never says what to do before
a disk dies.

`08-operations.md:587` is explicit about whose job this is: *"State this loudly
in the README — it is the difference between 'lost everything' and 'lost an
afternoon of indexing'."* The asymmetry it wants stated is at `:581–583`:
**rebuildable** — catalog, embeddings, search index, neighbour tables, cached
images, curated rows; **precious** — watch state, users, source config, manual
unmatched resolutions, and `llm_calls`.

**`llm_calls` is the sentence that makes the section worth reading**, and it is
`08-operations.md:609`'s own words: *"the first thing in this project that is
not rebuildable from anything, at any price."* It is a spend ledger; it cannot
be recomputed from the catalog, from `curated_rows` (replaced nightly), or from
the provider, because no OpenAI-compatible endpoint offers a per-key call
history and the price applied is a setting at the time of the call
(`:615`'s row). It is also small and append-only, which makes it the cheapest
thing in the precious set to back up and the most complete loss if nobody does.

**Two more that a reader will get wrong without being told.**
`genome_scores`/`genome_tags` are rebuildable **only from upstream** — re-download
`ml-latest.zip` and re-run `bootstrap --phase movielens` — so they depend on
GroupLens still serving a file (`08-operations.md`'s M7 table). And
`curated_rows` is rebuildable *cheaply* but **not to the same rows**: a curated
row has no oracle and is not deterministic at `temperature > 0`, so "rebuildable"
there means *a screen appears*, not *the screen comes back*.

**This task writes the README section around Group K's commands and does not
invent them.** `CLAUDE.md` is explicit: *"Do not invent commands for tooling that
does not exist yet."* Read Group K's landed `usher backup --help` and
`usher restore --help` and quote them; if Group K has not landed, this task
blocks rather than guessing at flags.

**And `08-operations.md:585` gets one correction in the same commit.** It says
*"A documented `pg_dump` of those turns disaster recovery into a short restore"* —
which is the design M10 departs from. The spec's key decision 2
(`2026-08-13-m10-hardening-design.md:286–295`) is that backup is **a CLI command
with the manifest in code**, and the reason is that the prose version has already
drifted: `:638–641` says row provider enable/disable *"belongs in the precious
column the day it exists"*, M9 shipped `row_provider_settings`, and the table was
never updated. Point `:585` at the command and the manifest.

**Verification:** — a README section, so there is no unit test and the slot is
this instead. Three checks, and the second is the one that says something.
(1) `grep -ni backup README.md` now returns the section. (2) **The section's list
of precious tables is read back out of Group K's manifest, not transcribed** —
run the manifest's own listing and diff it against the README's list by eye,
because a hand-copied list is the exact drift `08-operations.md:638–641` already
demonstrates. If Group K's manifest exposes the classification programmatically,
prefer a test asserting the README names every precious table over doing this by
eye. (3) The PRD link check prints `OK`.

**Acceptance:**
- `README.md` has a `## Backup` section naming the two columns, the `llm_calls`
  sentence, the `genome_*` upstream caveat, and `curated_rows`' "a screen
  appears, not the same screen".
- It gives a working command for the precious set and a one-line statement of
  what restoring costs — the rebuildable half is `usher index --backfill`, then
  `usher work`, then `usher similar --rebuild` (the same three
  `CLAUDE.md` already names for the `m09e` deletion, in that order).
- `08-operations.md:585`'s `pg_dump` sentence names the command and the manifest.
- The section says what is **not** backed up by the command, if anything is. A
  backup section that lists only what it covers is the shape that loses the
  table nobody classified — which is what the manifest test in Group K exists to
  prevent, one layer down.

### Task R10 — the unauthenticated-admin posture, stated as a posture and pinned as a fact

**Depends on:** R3   **Files:** `README.md` (a new subsection under `## Requirements` at `:99`, before `## Running it` at `:113`), `tests/unit/test_api_openapi.py`

**Verified, not taken from the spec.** `grep -rn
"fastapi.security\|HTTPBearer\|APIKeyHeader\|OAuth2\|HTTPBasic" src/ tests/`
finds one hit and it is prose (`adapters/emby/session.py:15`, *"Emby has no
OAuth2"*). `grep -rn "dependencies=" src/usher/api/` finds nothing — no router
and no `create_app` call carries a router-level dependency. There is no
`current_user` and no token check anywhere. **Every route in this API is
unauthenticated**, and the twelve under `/admin` are where that matters:
`sources.py` (5 operations, `:83,105,110,122,136`), `unmatched.py` (2,
`:184,227`), `bootstrap.py` (2, `:69,92`), `rows.py` (3, `:192,241,267`) —
counted from the decorators, not recalled.

**Two of those twelve are not merely readable, they are expensive.**
`POST /admin/bootstrap/{phase}` and `POST /admin/sources/{id}/sync` put the two
longest units of work in the system on the single sequential `JobWorker` lane —
`08-operations.md:406–412` states it plainly: *"`enrich`, `index`, `derive`,
`curate` and `match` are unavailable for the duration of either, hours in the
sync case, triggered by an unauthenticated route."* And `POST /admin/rows/regenerate`
enqueues a `curate` job, which is *where this project spends money*
(`README:316–318`).

**This is a documented posture, not a defect, and the README has to make the
distinction land in one sentence.** It is M9's boundary call 1
(`.claude/rules/milestone-boundary-calls.md`) and it sits under *Post-v1
candidates* (`09-roadmap.md:1338–1339`). Usher is a LAN-local self-hosted
backend, its threat model is a home network, and building authorization against
routes landing in the same milestone is the mistake PRD 07 avoided four times
with the error envelope. **A stranger will not infer any of that from an API
that answers.** What the README owes them is: the fact, the reason, the
consequence (do not publish this port to the internet; put it behind whatever
already fronts your other services), and the pointer to #18.

⚠️ **The section belongs in `## Requirements`, before `## Running it`.**
`README:113–124` is `docker compose up -d --build` and a `curl` — a reader who
meets the posture *after* running that has already exposed it. This is the one
README statement whose position is part of the content.

**Failing test first:** `tests/unit/test_api_openapi.py::test_no_route_declares_a_security_scheme_and_the_readme_states_the_count`
— builds the OpenAPI document, asserts `components.securitySchemes` is absent and
no operation carries a `security` key, counts the operations whose path starts
`/admin`, and asserts that count appears in `README.md`'s posture section. At
HEAD the security half **passes** (it is a guard, and its docstring says so) and
the README half **fails**, because the section does not exist. Its positive
control is `assert admin_operations >= 10, "the operation walk found nothing"` —
a walk that globs nothing agrees with any number the README happens to state.

**The README half is a derived-agreement assertion, not a prose check, and the
distinction is why it is worth having.** The number is computed from the app on
both sides of the comparison in the sense that matters: the day an eleventh
admin route lands, the case goes red and the sentence has to move. That is
H6's finding — *a static contract configured by an enumeration needs a test that
the enumeration is complete* — arriving at a README sentence instead of a
`pyproject.toml` list. It pins the **number**, not the prose; say so in the
docstring so nobody reads a green as "the README explains this well".

**Acceptance:**
- `README.md` states, before its first `docker compose up`, that no route
  requires authentication, that twelve of them are `/admin`, why that is the
  posture, and what an operator should do about it.
- The count in the README equals the count the test computes. Twelve at HEAD,
  measured 2026-08-13.
- The security-scheme half of the test is labelled in its docstring as a guard
  verified true before it was written, so a later reader does not mistake it for
  a discovery — and it is the case that will fail the day #18 ships, which is
  exactly when the README sentence has to change.
- Issue #18 is linked from the section. A posture with no ticket reads as an
  oversight.

### Task R11 — the two things a client developer has to know, and neither is on the page they will read

**Depends on:** R3   **Files:** `README.md` (`## Attribution`, at `:674–711`, and a new `## Building a client` subsection)

Two statements, one audience — somebody writing a client against this API — and
both are currently in a place that audience does not read.

**1. The playback-token caveat, and it must not be overstated.** Read both ADRs
before writing a word: ADR-0012 accepted the grant, ADR-0029 built the ticket.
The accurate three-sentence version:

- `POST /titles/{id}/play` now hands back an **opaque, short-lived ticket URL**
  (`/stream/{ticket}`), not a source URL. The ticket is a Fernet token over an
  HKDF-SHA256 subkey of `USHER_SECRET_KEY`, stateless, with a **300-second** TTL
  (`TICKET_TTL_SECONDS`, deliberately not a setting) — measured live on
  2026-08-12 at honoured 127 s, refused 312 s.
- **Redeeming it is a `302` whose `Location` is the real source URL, and that
  URL carries the source's session token.** The client reads `Location` by
  definition, so the token still reaches it. ADR-0029's own framing, which is
  also ADR-0012's: **what changed is the artifact, not the grant.**
- The token is the whole capability grant for whatever the configured source
  account can do, it is **not minted per request** (cached for the adapter's
  lifetime, re-minted only on a 401), and **nothing about the response ending
  ends the grant** (`08-operations.md:261–264`). There is no revocation before
  expiry for the ticket either — the coarse revocation that exists is rotating
  `USHER_SECRET_KEY` (ADR-0029's *Decision*, part 3).

⚠️ **Do not write "the token no longer reaches the client".** ADR-0029 opens by
recording that an earlier draft of this ADR *and* of PRD 07 *and* of PRD 08 each
said the M9 work "removes" or "closes" this, and **none of them does**. Repeating
that in the README would be the fourth instance. **And name the `deep_link`
asymmetry**, because it is the case a client developer actually hits: the deep
link hands the ticket to a third-party player, which follows the redirect and
then holds the real URL exactly as before — *"for that target the reduction is
close to nil"*.

**2. TMDb's logo obligation.** `README:696–698` already says it — *"TMDb's table
row also asks for a logo, which a string cannot carry and this project does not
ship — that half stays a client obligation."* It is correct, it is three lines
into a paragraph about why `GET /meta/attribution` is unfiltered, at line 696 of
a 714-line file. **A stranger building a client will not read that line**, and
the obligation is theirs, not this project's. `04-catalog-bootstrap.md:642`'s
table row is `Logo + disclaimer` and `:666–668`'s hard rule 4 is *"Render
attribution in clients"*.

The fix is not to move it — the paragraph at `:692–698` is a good argument in the
right place — but to **surface it** in a short `## Building a client` section
that a reader meets before `## Attribution`, listing the three obligations a
client takes on: render all four attribution strings from
`GET /meta/attribution`, render TMDb's logo (which this project does not ship and
cannot), and handle the playback caveat above. Link down to `## Attribution` for
the strings themselves.

**Verification:** — README prose, so no unit test. Four checks.
(1) Every factual claim in the new section is traceable to ADR-0012, ADR-0029,
`08-operations.md:242–264` or `04-catalog-bootstrap.md:639–671`; the citation
goes in the README text, not only in this plan. (2) The section contains no
sentence claiming the token was removed — grep the diff for "removes", "closes",
"no longer carries" and read each hit. (3) The `deep_link` asymmetry is named.
(4) The PRD link check prints `OK`.

**Acceptance:**
- `README.md` has a `## Building a client` section naming three obligations, each
  in one sentence, each linking the document that owns it.
- The playback paragraph states the ticket, the `302`, the grant that survives
  it, the 300-second TTL, and the `deep_link` case — and states no more than
  that.
- `README:696–698` is left where it is. This task adds a signpost; it does not
  relocate a working argument.
- The TMDb obligation says explicitly that Usher **cannot** discharge it — a
  string cannot carry a logo — so a client author does not wait for a future
  release to do it for them.

### Task R12 — PRD 04 gains the git-history paragraph, and the counts in it are three files, not one

**Depends on:** nothing   **Files:** `docs/prd/04-catalog-bootstrap.md` (**only** an insertion between `:637` and the licensing table at `:639`)

`04-catalog-bootstrap.md:626–637` already records that the "ship importers, never
data" rule was broken from M1 to M4 and fixed on 2026-08-01. **It records the
working tree and stops there.** The pre-fix commits are reachable on the public
remote, and this paragraph is what says so.

**Every number below was re-measured on 2026-08-13 against `origin/main`, and
three of them correct the spec.**

- **`1162346` is an ancestor of `origin/main`** (`git merge-base --is-ancestor
  1162346 origin/main` → 0). Its `tests/fixtures/bulk/title.ratings.slice.tsv`
  holds `tt0111161  9.3  2900000` and `tt0944947  9.2  2200000` — two real
  ratings rows with real vote counts, which the fix commit itself identifies as
  the most licence-restricted part of that dataset.
- 🔴 **It is not one file.** `tests/fixtures/bulk/title.basics.slice.tsv` at the
  same commit holds **four** real title rows — `tt0000001`, `tt0073045`,
  `tt0111161`, `tt0944947` — with real titles, years, runtimes and genres. And
  `docs/plans/2026-07-30-m2-bootstrap.md` prescribed the same rows **verbatim**,
  which is the shape `tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py:395–404` calls worse
  than a fixture: *"it is data and the instruction that recreates it."*
- 🔴 **113 of 678 commits, not 96.** Each fixture blob is in the tree of **96**
  commits; the M2 plan's copy is in **113**, and the union is **113** (the plan
  landed first, at `7dde52a`, 2026-07-30). Total commits on `origin/main`: **678**.
- 🔴 **The fix is two commits, not one.** `1196a9d` (2026-08-01 13:41:56 −0500)
  replaced the fixtures and added the guard; **`969dcbf`** (2026-08-01 13:59:12,
  eighteen minutes later, *"fix(fixtures): a dataset row is a dataset row
  wherever it sits"*) is the one that removed the rows from the M2 plan and is
  the reason the fourth check scans the whole repository. Both are ancestors of
  `origin/main`.
- **The repository was created public on 2026-08-02T23:43:14Z, MIT**
  (`gh repo view --json createdAt,visibility,licenseInfo`) — after both fixes,
  so the working tree has never been public in a broken state. **The history went
  with it.**
- **HEAD is clean.** `tests/fixtures/bulk/title.ratings.slice.tsv` is now
  `tt99000020 / tt99000030 / tt99000090` and the basics slice is nine `tt990000xx`
  rows, all inside the reserved band `_SYNTHETIC_IMDB_ID` enforces
  (`test_no_third_party_data.py:101`).

**The decision is to document rather than rewrite, and the paragraph must carry
the argument rather than the conclusion.** Six real rows across three files are
*de minimis* against IMDb's non-commercial clause. Against them sits **678
commits of cross-referenced engineering record** — measured findings, ADRs, plans
and sweep ledgers, referenced by hash from `CLAUDE.md`, from every file under
`.claude/rules/`, and from `docs/plans/`. A `filter-repo` rewrites every hash and
breaks every one of those references, which is a large certain loss against a
small theoretical risk. **And it would not even achieve the thing it costs that
for**: GitHub retains unreachable objects and serves them by SHA until a support
request purges them, and any fork or clone keeps its own copy. The decision is
**reversible** — a rewrite is available any day — and the inverse is not, which
is the asymmetry that settles it.

**Verification:** — a PRD paragraph, so no unit test, and the slot is this.
(1) Every number in the paragraph is reproduced by the commands above, run again
by the implementer rather than copied from this plan; a count in a plan is a
claim about the day the plan was written. (2) `uv run pytest
tests/unit/test_no_third_party_data.py` is green, which is what makes the
paragraph's "the working tree has been clean since 2026-08-01" a statement rather
than a hope — six test functions, **24 collected cases** (measured 2026-08-13;
nineteen of them are one parametrized coverage guard). (3) The PRD link check prints
`OK`.

**Acceptance:**
- `04-catalog-bootstrap.md` carries a paragraph between the existing correction
  (`:626–637`) and the licensing table (`:639`) naming: what is in history, which
  three files, which commits (`1162346` in, `1196a9d` and `969dcbf` out), how
  many commits carry it, that the repository went public after the fix, and why
  it was left.
- It states the decision is reversible and the inverse is not, in those terms.
- It does **not** reproduce a dataset row.
  `test_no_dataset_row_is_committed_anywhere` scans `docs/` (`:395–404`), so a
  paragraph quoting `tt0111161\t9.3\t2900000` in a tab-separated line would fail
  the very guard it is about. Naming an id in prose is fine and is explicitly
  permitted (`:1–19` of that module's docstring); a *row* is not. **Run the guard
  before committing the paragraph, not after.**
- `docs/prd/README.md`'s status table is untouched — this amends a section, it
  does not add one.

### Task R13 — the quickstart, the first tag, and the clean-checkout run that is Phase 3's exit condition

**Depends on:** R1–R12   **Files:** `README.md` (a `## Quickstart` section immediately after `## Requirements` ends at `:112`), `CHANGELOG.md` (the `[0.1.0]` date), and the tag itself

**What is missing is not setup instructions — it is a path.** `README:113–166`
already gets a stranger to a running container: `cp .env.example .env`,
`openssl rand -hex 32`, the `mkdir -p data/images && sudo chown 1000:1000` line
(with the best paragraph in the file explaining why), `docker compose up -d
--build`, and two `curl`s. **That works and stops at an empty catalog.** The next
thing a reader needs — populate it, connect a source, see a screen — is at
`README:197`, eighty lines later, under `## Command line`, interleaved with
measurement findings and ordering warnings that are correct and are not a
tutorial. A stranger following the file top to bottom meets ADR-0015's retraction
ceiling before they have a title.

So the quickstart is a **short numbered path with links out**, not new material:
container up → `bootstrap --phase imdb` → register a source → `usher sync` →
`usher work --once` → `GET /home`. Seven steps, each one command, each linking
the section that explains it.

**What it legitimately shortcuts, and the honesty is the design.** `README:197–203`
prices a full bootstrap at *"about three minutes and 1.27M titles"* for the three
core phases, plus 1.49 GiB and *"rather longer"* for the two IMDb expansion
phases, and `04-catalog-bootstrap.md`'s Cost table puts the whole thing at
**~3–5 h wall-clock, mostly the TMDb crawl**. A quickstart that runs that is not a
quickstart. So it runs `--phase imdb` only, and **says at that step what it is
skipping and what the deployment is therefore missing** — no TMDb enrichment, so
every title is a skeleton; no embeddings, so search is full-text and type-ahead
only; no genome. Then it links `## Command line` for the full path. **The
shortcut is stated at the step that takes it, not in a footnote**, which is the
same rule `usher search`'s `semantic_coverage` line follows (`README:370–383`):
a degraded answer that looks like a working one is worse than an error.

**The verification is the exit condition and it is designed here, concretely.**

1. `git clone https://github.com/anirudhlath/usher.git` into a fresh directory
   under `/var/tmp` — **not `/tmp`, which is tmpfs on this host**
   (`CLAUDE.md`), and not a copy of this worktree: a `cp -a` carries `.venv`,
   whose `bin/pytest` shebang points at the source interpreter, and the run then
   measures the wrong tree (`.claude/rules/mutation-sweeps.md`'s `cp -a` entry).
   Clone from the **remote**, so the thing verified is what a stranger gets.
2. A scratch database, on a port nothing else on this host uses. Set
   `USHER_COMPOSE_HOST_PORT` to something other than `8100`, because
   `usher-postgres-1` and other containers are already running here.
3. Run the quickstart **verbatim, in order, pasting each command**, in **fish** —
   the shell a reader on this host has. Any command in the README that is
   bash-only (`export FOO=bar`, `set -a; . ./.env; set +a`) is a defect this run
   finds; `README:174–176` already routes environment through
   `docker compose exec`, which is shell-agnostic, and the quickstart should stay
   that way.
4. Bound the run: `--phase imdb` only, one source, `usher work --once` rather
   than a daemon, and `curl /home`. Record the wall-clock of each step in the
   commit body. **If the honest number is not "a few minutes", the quickstart is
   wrong and this task changes the quickstart, not the claim.**
5. Tear down: `docker compose down -v` and delete the clone.

**Then the tag, and only then.** `git tag -a v0.1.0 -m "…"` on the merge commit,
annotated rather than lightweight so it carries an author and a date, and pushed.
R8's workflow is what that executes, and **this is the first time it runs** —
a workflow verified only by a YAML-parsing test is not a verified workflow.

**Verification:** — the milestone's exit condition, so the slot is a procedure
rather than a test. Five things are checked, in this order, and a failure at any
one is a red for Phase 3:

- The clean-checkout run above reaches a `GET /home` that returns rows, from a
  clone made from the public remote, with every command pasted as written.
- `git tag -l` shows `v0.1.0` and `git ls-remote --tags origin` shows it on the
  remote.
- The release workflow's run is **green**, and `docker pull
  ghcr.io/anirudhlath/usher:v0.1.0` succeeds **from a shell that has done no
  `docker login`** — a package published private pulls fine for its owner and not
  at all for a stranger, and those two states look identical from here.
  `gh api /users/anirudhlath/packages/container/usher --jq '.visibility'` is the
  second reading.
- `docker run --rm ghcr.io/anirudhlath/usher:v0.1.0 python -m usher --version`
  prints `usher 0.1.0` — which is R1 and R8 checked against each other, and is
  the cheapest end-to-end proof that the tag, the metadata and the image agree.
- `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[0.1.0]` heading carries the tag's own date and its link
  target now resolves.

**Acceptance:**
- `README.md` has a `## Quickstart` between `## Requirements` and `## Running
  it`, seven steps or fewer, every step one command, every step linking the
  section that explains it.
- The bootstrap step names what it skips and what the deployment lacks as a
  result, at that step.
- The whole path was followed on a clean clone in `/var/tmp`, in fish, against a
  scratch database on a non-default port, with per-step wall-clock recorded in
  the commit body.
- `v0.1.0` exists locally and on the remote; the release workflow ran green from
  it; the image pulls anonymously and answers `--version`.
- ⚠️ **Nothing in this task is reported from a dry run.** The two failure modes
  this exit condition exists to catch — a README command that is bash-only, and a
  workflow that has never been executed — are both invisible to reading.
